# Project: MCP Factory

DFY (Done-For-You), BYOK (Bring Your Own Keys) automation platform on MCP. User gives URL + budget; the platform handles lead finding, outreach, email generation, reporting.

## Commands

```bash
pnpm dev                    # All services via Turbo
pnpm dev:dashboard          # Dashboard only (Next.js, port 3001)
pnpm dev:<service-name>     # Any individual service
pnpm build                  # Build all (Turbo-orchestrated)
pnpm lint                   # Lint all packages
pnpm generate:readme        # Regenerate README.md from shared/content

# Per-package testing — scripts use `test`/`test:unit`, NOT `vitest` directly
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test                              # all tests
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test:unit                         # unit only
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test tests/unit/specific.test.ts  # single file
```

**Verify frontend with `tsc --noEmit`, NOT lint — `next lint` is GONE in Next 16.** Any `next lint` invocation reads `lint` as a directory arg and fails; no standalone `eslint` binary in the repo. Real check: `cd apps/<app> && node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` (dashboard has ~3 pre-existing test-file `TS1501` regex errors — ignore, only care about your files). (#1223) **The suite passing is NOT a substitute: the guards are source-substring, so a file that does not even PARSE can keep them all green.** A `{/* comment */}` dropped directly inside `{cond && ( … )}` above the single root element is two children in one expression — 10 `TS1005`/`TS1382`/`TS17015` errors, 1702 tests still passing. Put the comment ABOVE the `{cond &&` line or INSIDE the element, and run `tsc` on every JSX edit however cosmetic.

**Don't trust RTK's `next build` summary** — the hook replaces stdout with a `Errors: N | Warnings: M` one-liner that can be wrong (phantom `Errors: 1` on a clean run). Real run: `rtk proxy -- npx next build` (the `--` is required), trust the log + exit code.

**Build shared workspace packages before app tests/build.** Vitest/Vite resolve workspace deps via `dist/`; an unbuilt `shared/*` surfaces as `Failed to resolve entry for package "@distribute/<name>"`. Run `pnpm -r build` after install or after pulling `shared/` changes.

**Source-substring page tests: a `not.toMatch(/X/)` guard trips on the fix's own explanatory comment** (a comment that writes the forbidden literal fails the test). Reword the comment, or assert against a comment-stripped copy (`pr-expert-public-report.test.ts` ships `stripComments(src)`). **Before adding a `not.toContain("<literal>")` guard, grep the WHOLE target file for that literal, not just the JSX you edited — a big page carries PRE-EXISTING comments mentioning it far from the code you touched, and each one fails the guard.** Reword every hit in the same commit. (#1235, DIS-112; #3033 removed an "AI-suggested" badge from `onboarding.tsx` and two unrelated comments ~1600 and ~300 lines away still wrote the literal.) **When the grep shows the literal also lives in LEGITIMATE code elsewhere, rewording is wrong — SCOPE the guard to a slice of the function you actually changed** (`sliceFrom("async function saveX(", N)` then `expect(body).not.toContain(...)`), because a file-wide `not.toContain` on a common expression asserts something you do not mean. #3039 forbade restating `replyToMeetingPct: nextRates.r2m` in the two economics SAVE paths, but the same expression legitimately builds the projection helper's econ overrides one screen away — a read, not a write; the file-wide guard failed on correct code until it was scoped to the two save functions. **The trap also runs in the OTHER direction, and that one has no target file to grep: some guards SCAN ALL of `src/` for a co-occurrence heuristic, so a DOC COMMENT you add to any file can trip a guard you never opened.** `api-proxy.test.ts` flags any file containing both `getToken` and `apiCall` as token-passing to the api client; the SWR rewrite of `use-tenant-switcher.ts` (which legitimately calls `getToken` for the org-switch re-mint) failed it purely because a comment explained that its reads "go through `apiCall`" — the code passes no token. Rewording the comment ("the shared api client") fixed it. So when a guard you didn't touch goes red on a file you only commented, read the guard's predicate before assuming a real violation. **Third face: the `sliceFrom(marker)` ANCHOR itself collides with a doc comment, silently slicing prose instead of code.** `indexOf` takes the FIRST hit, and a good doc comment above a component names the very token you want to anchor on (`role="button"`, a field name, a class), so the guard then asserts against explanatory English and fails with a confusing "expected 'role=\"button\">`, NOT a `<button>`. Se…' to contain 'tabIndex={0}'" — the quoted haystack is your own comment. Use `lastIndexOf`, or anchor on a JSX-only string (`aria-label="More info"`, `onKeyDown={(e) => {`). This bit twice on 2026-07-30 alone, once as a `not.toContain` on a comment and once as a mis-anchored slice. **Fifth face: a `const` declaration is a bad anchor, because a later change can HOIST or duplicate it and `indexOf` then slices from somewhere else entirely — silently, on a guard nobody touched.** A guard anchored on `const onePath = selectedFunnels.length === 1;` inside the budget step kept passing until that flag was hoisted to the top of the flow for a second reader; the first hit moved ~2800 lines up, so every assertion (including a `not.toContain("—")`) ran against unrelated code. Anchor on something only the target can contain: the function signature, or a string literal from its own copy. **Fourth face, the slice LENGTH: compute the offset, never guess it.** A `slice(at, at + N)` whose `N` falls short cuts the assertion's target out of the haystack, and the failure message quotes the SLICE (which starts with the function signature you anchored on), so it reads as "the code is missing" for code that is right there — and every comment you add to the function pushes the boundary further, so a guard that passed at commit time breaks on the next edit. Measure it once (`python3 -c "s=open(f).read(); a=s.index(anchor); print(s.index(target)-a)"`) and give the real distance real headroom. Cost 2026-07-30 (leads-table mobile fit): a 5000-then-7000-char slice and a 900-char slice each dropped their last assertion, two wasted suite runs. **And the danger flips with the assertion's POLARITY, so "when unsure, give it headroom" is wrong half the time: a `toContain` fails when the slice is too SHORT (the target got cut out), a `not.toContain` fails when it is too LONG (the slice ran past the function into a NEIGHBOUR that legitimately contains the forbidden literal — very often the next function's doc comment).** So for a `not.toContain` guard, measure to the function's CLOSING BRACE and do not pad; write the measured number in a comment beside it so the next editor does not "fix" it by widening. Cost 2026-07-31 (#3167): a 400-char slice from `function continueFunnelStats()` — asserting the step persists nothing — reached the next function's comment *"Saved via sales-economics"* and failed on correct code; the body is 188 chars. **Fifth face, the slice DIRECTION: `sliceFrom` only ever looks FORWARD, so an anchor that sits AFTER its target can never see it.** Gating existing copy behind a new condition puts the gate BEFORE the string, so a guard anchored on the string and asserting the gate reads the wrong side of the file and fails no matter how long the slice is — widening it looks like the fix and is not. Anchor on the GATE and assert the copy, not the reverse. The same `python3` measurement that sizes a slice also reveals the sign (`print(s.index(target) - s.index(anchor))` going NEGATIVE is the tell), so measure before writing either direction. Cost 2026-08-24 (service-suggest-loading): a guard anchored on `We drafted these from` asserting `servicesDrafted` — the gate is 70 chars EARLIER. **Sixth, cheapest face: a guard asserting user-facing COPY must use the SOURCE spelling, not the rendered one.** JSX escapes an apostrophe as `&apos;`, so `toContain("We couldn't read your site")` fails against source reading `We couldn&apos;t read your site` while the rendered page is exactly what you typed. Copy-paste the literal out of the file rather than out of the design.

**Run `pnpm --filter @distribute/dashboard test` before pushing a dashboard change — `tsc` alone misses source-substring guards** (many tests assert a page contains a literal expression/copy string). `tsc` stays green while the guard goes red in CI and silently blocks auto-merge. Run it (~3s, source-only) and update the matching guard in the same commit. (#1252) **Run the package SCRIPT, never the raw `vitest` binary — the script is `vitest run --exclude 'tests/e2e/**'`, so a bare `node_modules/.bin/vitest run` also collects the three Playwright specs under `tests/e2e/`, which cannot run under vitest and report as 3 FAILED FILES. Every actual test still passes (`1504 passed / 0 failed` beside `3 failed` files), so the summary reads as a regression you just introduced when it is only the missing exclude.** Same shape in `apps/admin`. Tell them apart by the failed-FILE list being entirely `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` and the failed-TEST count being zero.

**`apps/admin` tests are NOT a CI merge gate — only `apps/dashboard`'s are (`.github/workflows/test.yml` = build + `--filter @distribute/dashboard test` + lint; NO `--filter admin test` job).** So admin source-substring guards ROT SILENTLY: a rename/nav change that doesn't update the matching admin test ships red and nothing blocks it (#2689's Signups-tab rename left 4 stale assertions across `public-metrics-page.test.ts` + `context-sidebar.test.ts` + `last-brand.test.ts`, all red on main). Because there's no gate, you MUST run `pnpm --filter @distribute/admin test` locally before pushing an admin change and update every matching guard in the same commit — CI will not catch it for you. When editing an admin test file, also fix any pre-existing red assertions in THAT file (they're this same rot); sibling test files reading code you didn't touch are follow-ups, not scope. (#2698)

## `apps/admin` is the STAFF GOD-MODE console — a dashboard fork, NOT a customer surface, and it runs on the HETZNER BOX

**It is a container on `167.233.196.79` beside the whole backend fleet (#3298), reachable at `admin.distribute.you`.** It left Vercel on 2026-08-07 (#3293/#3297) because the allowlist held one person's two addresses and a hosted build cost 1,231 build minutes a month to serve nobody outside the team; `apps/admin/vercel.json` is vestigial, kept in case something goes back. `pnpm dev:admin` (port 3002) still works locally — seed `apps/admin/.env.local` from the box: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/oracle-distribute root@167.233.196.79 'cat /root/distribute/env/admin-app.env'` (and `admin-app.build.env` for the `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`), which carries every var admin needs including `ADMIN_DISTRIBUTE_API_KEY`.

- **`apps/admin/Dockerfile`, built from the MONOREPO ROOT.** `output: "standalone"` with `outputFileTracingRoot` pinned to the repo root — `next build` runs with `apps/admin` as its cwd, so without it the output silently misses everything pnpm hoisted above the package. The image installs only admin's subtree (`--filter @distribute/admin...`).
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is inlined at BUILD time** from `apps/admin/.env.production` (written on the box, gitignored). Changing one needs a rebuild, not a restart. Server-side secrets arrive at run time via the compose `env_file`.
- **Two runtime secrets did NOT survive the Vercel cutover, and a `process.env.X` grep cannot find that class of hole — `requireEnv("X")` reads the name as a STRING.** `env/admin-app.env` reached the box with 16 keys while `apps/admin/src` needs 18: `POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY` (the `/metrics` PostHog visitor + signup queries) and `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (the paid-users tab's per-customer payment-method fan-out) were both absent. They fail LOUD and correctly, so **four of the five `/metrics` tabs threw on render** — every one but `?view=active-users`, which is the only tab that skips `fetchPublicStatsSummary`. The symptom is a bare `digest` on screen and nothing else; the actual line is in `docker logs distribute-admin-app-1` (`⨯ Error: [public-stats] POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY is required`). Both values already sat on the same box in `env/dashboard-app.env`, so the recovery was two appended lines plus `docker compose up -d admin-app` — a RECREATE, not a rebuild, since neither is `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`. **Generalise: after moving an app between platforms, diff what it READS against what landed, and enumerate the reads with `grep -rhoE 'requireEnv\("[A-Z0-9_]+"\)'` ALONGSIDE the `process.env.` grep** — the env-var name inside a `requireEnv()` helper is a string literal, invisible to the obvious search, so the obvious search reports a clean bill of health on a container that is missing credentials. The existing "a deleted platform takes its secrets with it" rule below covers the values with no second source; this is the other half, where a second source existed and nobody checked whether the copy had been made.
- **The box is NOT a git repo** — `/root/distribute` is hand-managed like the rest of the fleet: `docker-compose.override.yml` holds `admin-app` (the base compose file is generated from the Railway bundle and says not to hand-edit it), `env/admin-app{,.build}.env`, a Caddy vhost, and `deploy-admin.sh` (fetch main → refresh `.env.production` → rebuild → restart).
- **Two gates, and the second one is the load-bearing one.** A Cloudflare Access application allows `kevin.lourd@gmail.com` only, so the email allowlist in middleware is now the second gate rather than the only one. But **Access protects a HOSTNAME, not an origin** — a request straight to the box's IP carrying the right `Host` and SNI sails past it, and it did (verified 200 before the fix). The Caddy vhost therefore 404s any peer outside Cloudflare's published ranges. Residual gap: an attacker proxying through their OWN Cloudflare zone arrives from a Cloudflare IP. Closing that needs a managed Transform Rule injecting a shared header (or Authenticated Origin Pulls), and **no token on this machine has the Transform Rules scope** — the MCP and the `cloudflare` skill's token both 403. **Every other service on that box has the same origin bypass and no allowlist at all.**
- **Admin REGISTERS NOTHING — `apps/admin/src/instrumentation.ts` is DELETED, and the dashboard is the single registrar for every shared platform store.** Do NOT re-add one. Both apps used to write email templates, platform keys, chat configs, the `cold-email` prompt and the welcome-gift pin, keyed by name and last-writer-wins; 6 of the 9 template names differed, so each boot silently overwrote the other app's copy (read at the destination: the dashboard wrote its 14 at 10:17:52, admin replaced 9 of them at 10:19:53). The dashboard's set is a strict SUPERSET for templates and platform keys and byte-identical for the rest, so deleting admin's copy removed nothing from the store. **A change to a chat config, the platform prompt, an email template or the gift amount therefore goes in `apps/dashboard`, even when the surface consuming it is admin-only** — admin's duplicate guards for those were deleted with it (the dashboard's own `workflow-chat-context` / `platform-config-startup` / `welcome-gift-pinned` cover the same ground). Owner-decided when the clobber was found: **the customer dashboard wins any conflict between the two.** (#3302)

⚠️ **Before deleting a deployment, diff its boot registrations against the app that survives.** `apps/admin/src/instrumentation.ts` was the ONLY registrar of the `posthog` platform key, which features-service consumes — a staff console with one user was supplying a backend credential. That entry moved to the dashboard first (#3292). The same class of gap applies to email templates and platform prompts: a registration that stops running fails SILENTLY, because the stored row survives and nothing re-writes it until the store moves. Verify at the DESTINATION (`SELECT … FROM platform_keys` on key-service's Neon), never by reading the registration code.

`apps/admin` is the dashboard cloned for internal staff. Two load-bearing facts before touching it:
- **The admin BRAND MARK is intentionally DISTINCT from the customer dashboard's GREEN charter dot — do NOT re-sync it in a fleet-wide rebrand.** Admin uses a WHITE mark on the theme-neutral surfaces (tab favicon `src/app/icon.svg`, `src/app/apple-icon.png`, `public/favicon.jpg`) and a theme-responsive header mark (`components/header.tsx`, inline SVG dot — **blue in light mode, white in dark mode**, no dark square). This is so a staff tab reads distinct from a customer tab at a glance. A "swap the mark across all apps" PR (like #2614 which green-ified everything) MUST skip admin or re-clobbers this — keep the customer dashboard green, admin white/blue. The green `text-brand-600` "distribute" wordmark text was left as-is (only the mark differs). (#2625)
- **Access = email allowlist, single source `apps/admin/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`** (`ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS` + `isAdminEmail`). Edge-gated in `proxy.ts` off the session `email` claim (403 for any signed-in non-staff on every non-public route); the `/api/admin/*` routes re-check it (defense-in-depth). Add/remove staff in that ONE file — don't re-inline the list. **The api-service `requireStaff` gate (staff-only `/v1/billing/credits/*` grant proxies + `/v1/billing/usage-discount` + admin-brands/features-admin, on the forwarded `x-email`) HARDCODES the same allowlist in `src/middleware/auth.ts` — the `STAFF_EMAILS` Railway env var was REMOVED (api-service v0.84.2, #698) because Kevin doesn't want a Railway var for this + it silently drifted. It is still a THIRD copy that must stay byte-equal with the admin/dashboard allowlists, but now via a code edit (no env). Whenever you add/remove staff here, edit the hardcoded array in api-service `src/middleware/auth.ts` to the SAME set + ship it, or the missing staff get a 403 on credit-granting / discount-setting. Do NOT re-introduce a `STAFF_EMAILS` env read.** (admin free-credits #2059; hardcode #698)
- **Cross-org "god-mode" = option A (real auto-join), NOT read-only impersonation.** The breadcrumb org switcher lists EVERY platform org via `GET /api/admin/orgs?q=` (Clerk Backend `getOrganizationList`, `CLERK_SECRET_KEY`), not `useOrganizationList({ userMemberships })`. On switch, `handleOrgSwitch` first `POST /api/admin/orgs/:orgId/join` (makes the admin a real `org:admin` member, idempotent) THEN `setActive` — because Clerk `setActive` rejects a non-member org. This keeps the whole **DIS-143 membership-scoped data path unchanged** (Clerk active org → JWT → gateway `x-external-org-id`); brands/features/writes all follow the active org with zero extra change. Accepted tradeoff: staff emails appear in each customer's Clerk member list. Why A over a read-only `x-admin-view-org-id` impersonation header: the gateway admin path (`api-service auth.ts`) trusts `x-external-org-id` with no membership check BUT resolves the (org,user) PAIR via client-service `/internal/resolve`, which **upserts the caller's `users.orgId` to the viewed org** (single org per user) — so a naive "forward URL org + staff user id" would churn/mis-attribute the staff user across customer orgs. Real membership (A) makes that churn legitimate + lets staff act, which is what's wanted. (#1643)
- **The SAME god-mode also lives in the customer `apps/dashboard`, but STAFF-GATED — and there the gating model flips.** `apps/dashboard/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts` (byte-equal copy) + the same `GET /api/admin/orgs` + `POST /api/admin/orgs/:orgId/join` routes; breadcrumb shows the all-orgs switcher only when `isAdminEmail(useUser().email)` (else the unchanged `userMemberships` list — customers see only their own orgs). CRITICAL DIFFERENCE vs the admin app: the dashboard is the customer surface with **NO edge allowlist**, so the route-level `isAdminEmail` **403** is the SOLE security boundary — it's what stops a logged-in customer from enumerating/joining every other tenant. The client `isStaff` flag is cosmetic (hides the UI); never rely on it for access. Keep the dashboard allowlist + routes byte-equal with admin's. (#1655)
- **The god-mode join route MUST be idempotent via Clerk's RESPONSE, never via a `getOrganizationMembershipList` pre-check — that pre-check reads only the FIRST PAGE and silently breaks for staff who joined many orgs.** Symptom: god-mode switch into a (real, onboarded) customer org lands on a **blank org page** (`/orgs/:id` renders, sidebar + header fine, main empty). Causal chain (took a full session to trace, documented so it's instant next time): `POST /api/admin/orgs/:id/join` 502s → `OrgActivator` (dashboard, the URL-driven god-mode variant) does `if (!res.ok) throw` BEFORE `setActive`, so the active org never switches → URL org ≠ Clerk active org → `useAuthQuery`'s org-consistency gate (`urlOrg === activeOrg`) DISABLES every org-scoped read → the org-landing page's `["brands"]` query stays `undefined` → `if (!brandsData) return null` → blank; meanwhile every `/api/v1/*` returns **409 org_desync** (proxy `checkProxyOrg`: client-sent URL org ≠ JWT active org). The 502 root cause: the join route did `getOrganizationMembershipList({userId})` (FIRST PAGE only) → for a staff member of many orgs an existing membership beyond page 1 read as absent → re-`createOrganizationMembership` → Clerk **400 `already_a_member_in_organization`** → route wrapped it 502. **Tell it apart from the legit case:** an org the staff is NOT meant to enter (incomplete/never-onboarded org, no brand) bouncing to `/onboarding` is CORRECT (that's the first-run gate, not this bug) — THIS bug is a blank page on a COMPLETE org (has brands). **Fix (KevinLourd/godmode-join-idempotent):** drop the paged pre-check; `createOrganizationMembership` directly and treat a Clerk error whose `errors[].code === "already_a_member_in_organization"` as success (`{ok:true}`) — trust Clerk (server-side source of truth) over a paged list. Applied byte-equal to BOTH `apps/dashboard` + `apps/admin` join routes. Hardening in the same PR: `OrgActivator` now `await session?.getToken({skipCache:true})` AFTER `setActive` (mirrors `handleOrgSwitch`, CLAUDE.md DIS-143 item 3) to close the stale-token 409 window. **Diagnostic approach that nailed it:** the route only returned `err.message` ("Bad Request", Clerk's generic) — the real `errors[].code` was in the **Vercel runtime logs** (`get_runtime_logs query:"admin-join"` → `already_a_member_in_organization`); and the "is this org actually complete?" question was answered by querying **brand-service** (`org_brands` for the org → 2 brands = onboarded → blank is a bug, not the onboarding gate). Org NAMES live only in Clerk (client-service `orgs.name` is null for everyone), and the prod `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` Backend API is IP-restricted from a dev machine (403 code 1010) — so map a Clerk-org-name to data via brand-service, not Clerk. (godmode-join-idempotent)

- **The admin WORKFLOW-EDIT chat (`apps/admin/.../workflows/[workflowId]/page.tsx` → `WorkflowChat`) MUST NOT inline the full workflow DAG (`workflow.dag`) in the chat `context` — api-service caps the JSON request body (`express.json({ limit: "10mb" })` today; it was the 100KB Express default when this shipped, and a large workflow's DAG serializes to >300KB) → `PayloadTooLargeError: request entity too large` on api-service → 500 → `{"error":"Internal server error"}` in the chat panel on EVERY message.** The `context` is re-sent on every turn (chat-service `buildSystemPrompt` JSON.stringifies it fresh each turn from the client payload — it is NOT persisted per session), and it travels through the api-service gateway, so the 100KB cap bites on the very first message. The model reads the DAG ON DEMAND via its existing `get_workflow_details(<uuid>)` tool (already the HARD RULE for edits); the page's `instructions` point at that tool, and only small fields (`summary`, `feature`, identity) stay inline for a tool-free overview. Guarded by `apps/admin/tests/workflow-chat-context.test.ts` (`not.toContain("dag: workflow.dag")`). RULE for ANY chat/Edit-with-AI surface (admin OR dashboard): never inline a large payload (DAG, full lead list, big profile) into `context` — fetch it via a tool on demand. Persisting context server-side by session does NOT fix this (the first message already carries the full payload). (#2978)

- **An IDENTIFIER a downstream service validates against a closed set (`model`, prompt-template `type`, `service`, an endpoint `path`) is READ from that service's schema, NEVER transcribed from the user's words — and `validate_workflow` does not catch the difference, so a validated DAG is not a proven one.** The user speaks product language ("DeepSeek Flash v4"); the service accepts an exact token; turning one into the other produces a slug that looks right, passes every check, and 400s on the first live send. **Answering from memory is the specific failure mode** — training data lags the platform, so a model/template/provider shipped last week is absent from what the LLM "knows" and present in the schema, one `get_endpoint_details` call away. The rule lives in the `workflow` chat's system prompt (`apps/dashboard/src/instrumentation.ts`, the single registrar, so it covers the org-scoped editor AND the feature-stats panel) and in the panel's own context (`workflowCatalogueInstructions`): read the permitted set, copy the exact string, and if what the user named is absent **say so BEFORE writing anything**. Do NOT "fix" this by inlining a model list into the prompt — it goes stale on the next alias and reproduces the bug with extra steps. **`validate_workflow` checks DAG structure, body field NAMES against the request schema, and referenced output paths — never whether a VALUE is one the service accepts**, so it returns `valid: true` on a DAG that cannot run; never report such a workflow as "tested". Cost 2026-08-15: asked to duplicate three cold-email workflows onto DeepSeek, the chat wrote `deepseek-flash-v4` (real alias: `deepseek-flash`, provider `vercel`, chat-service #376) into three PRODUCTION DAGs, called them "testé, 100% valide", and three turns later asserted DeepSeek has no Flash V4 — wrong in both directions. Zero blast radius only because no campaign referenced them; all three dynasties were deprecated. Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/platform-config-startup.test.ts` + `apps/admin/tests/feature-stats-workflow-ai.test.ts`. Sibling fix spawned in workflow-service (make the validator reject a static body literal outside the target schema's enum) and content-generation-service (accept the DeepSeek alias at all — its `/generate` model set is its OWN, and had no DeepSeek entry on either branch, so the request was unsatisfiable however the DAG was authored).

- **An image in an investor update is UPLOADED, never linked — staff pick a file and it lands on our own R2.** `InvestorUpdateComposer` reads the file as a data URL and posts it to api-service `POST /v1/platform-uploads` (staff-gated) → cloudflare-service `POST /internal/upload/base64` → back comes the public URL, which is what goes in the markdown. An image hosted somewhere else can be moved, expire or start blocking hotlinking long after the update has landed in forty inboxes, so there is no URL field left (guarded: the composer must carry no `type="url"`). PLATFORM-owned on purpose — the file is ours, so it is billed to no customer org and the route carries no org identity. Three gates, each catching a different failure: `imageFileProblem` before the upload (SVG is refused by Gmail, Outlook and Yahoo; WebP is deliberately absent because Outlook on Windows renders through Word; 5MB cap), `imageUrlProblem` on the URL that came BACK (the public domain is resolved at upload time from key-service, so a misconfiguration surfaces as an unusable link rather than a broken picture), then an actual decode. The accept list and the gate read ONE catalogue (`ACCEPTED_IMAGE_TYPES` in `lib/investor-update-html.ts`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests). Guards: `tests/investor-update-html.test.ts` + `tests/investors-surface.test.ts`.

- **CHOOSING the file is the whole action, and the send is BLOCKED while a picked file is not in the body.** A separate confirm button stood between the two, and that is how the first real update went out with no picture at all: the file sat chosen in the form, the button was never pressed, nothing was ever uploaded, and the send said nothing. Diagnosed against prod, not the code — `body_markdown` carried no `![…]`, `html_body` no `<img>`, and R2 `investor-updates/` held only the two verification files, so the producer (which renders `![alt](url)` through a dedicated renderer) was never in it. Generalise: **a two-step control whose FIRST step looks like the action will be left half-done, and if the surrounding form can then be submitted, the omission is silent.** Fix both halves — make step 1 do the work, and let the entity's own gate refuse while the half-state exists (`investorUpdateBlocker`'s `pendingImageName`). The insertion states that it landed (the line drops below the fold of a 14-row textarea) and alt text falls back to the filename, which is what an investor reads while their client blocks remote images.
- **The update AUTOSAVES to `localStorage` (`lib/investor-update-draft.ts`, alias-free, versioned key), and the hydration latch is STATE, never a ref.** With a ref the saving effect's next pass still holds the EMPTY values of the render it was created in, decides they differ from what was restored, and schedules a write of the blank form over a stored draft — only the effect cleanup happens to cancel it, and the line sits on `Saving draft...` on a page nobody typed into. React batches the restore's four updates into one commit, so a state flag in the deps sees the restored values and the flag together. Verified by REPRODUCTION (esbuild-bundled harness + Playwright over a real http origin — `localStorage` is unavailable on `about:blank` and `file://`, which would make every assertion vacuously pass), not by reading the classes. Draft never reaches the network, which is what makes autosaving safe beside a button that mails forty investors; it is purged on a successful send, and the line says it is local to that browser. NOT a cookie: the remembered-state rule is about the FIRST frame and pays for it on every request, which a multi-KB markdown body has no business doing. Guards: `tests/investor-update-draft.test.ts`.
- **A filename the author picked becomes a PUBLIC URL, so it is sanitized at the upload boundary (`sanitizeUploadFilename`) — and `imageMarkdown` wraps a destination containing a space or a parenthesis in angle brackets.** cloudflare-service builds the object key from the filename we send, and macOS names every screenshot `Screenshot 2026-08-02 at 10.46.50.png`; a bare markdown destination ends at the FIRST SPACE, so the line emitted no `<img>` at all and the update went out displaying its own markup, in the preview and the send alike. Angle brackets rather than `encodeURI`: the body doubles as the email's plain-text part so it stays readable, and re-inserting an already-encoded URL cannot turn `%20` into `%2520`. Alt text keeps reading the ORIGINAL name, not the slug. **Generalise, and this is the deeper miss: proving an artifact was PRODUCED is not proving it WORKS — reproduce it through the real consumer.** The insertion was verified end to end (the file uploaded, the line landed in the body) and the one thing never checked was the line through the producer's own `marked`, which is where it failed. Whenever a surface emits input for another system to parse, run the emitted value through that system's actual parser in the guard, not a shape assertion.

## The Claude config console edits a REPO, not a machine — and markdown is the only thing it writes

`/audit/config` (`audit/config/page.tsx`, rules in `lib/config-files.ts`, GitHub in `lib/github-config.ts`) reads and edits the global CLAUDE.md, RTK.md, the 26 skills, the hooks and each repo's own CLAUDE.md. The config Claude Code actually loads lives on a laptop under `~/.claude` and `~/.agents/skills`, which are SYMLINKS into a clone of `shamanic-technologies/agent-config`; admin is a container on the Hetzner box and cannot see that laptop. So every read and write goes through the GitHub Contents API against the repo, and a save is a commit. **That indirection is the design, not a limitation to route around** — do not add a filesystem path, and do not try to reach the machine.

- **A save does NOT reach the laptop until it pulls.** The page prints `git -C ~/conductor/repos/agent-config pull` with a copy button for exactly that reason: a commit that lands on the remote and never reaches the machine that reads it is the one way this feature quietly does nothing. The durable fix is a `SessionStart` hook in agent-config that pulls; it lives in that repo, so it is a separate ship.
- **Hooks and `scripts/` are READ ONLY, and that is a security decision, not an omission.** A hook is code that runs on the laptop, unattended, on every Bash call, so editing one from a browser is arbitrary local execution one click away. Cloudflare Access and the email allowlist bound WHO reaches the page; they do not make the two actions the same class. `settings.template.json` is read-only for a different reason: `install.sh` copies it into `~/.claude`, so an edit here would not reach the live settings. Everything unwritable is still fully readable.
- **The write rule is markdown-only in agent-config, and exactly `CLAUDE.md` in a product repo. It is enforced in the ROUTE, never by the UI** — a control the page does not render is still a request anyone can send, so `configWriteProblem` runs before any network call and 403s. Read is narrow too: widening it past one file per product repo turns a config console into a source browser for the whole organisation.
- **A save sends the blob `sha` it read**, so a file that moved in between is REFUSED rather than overwritten. That 409 is the feature; do not retry past it. `configFile` is therefore in `EPHEMERAL_QUERY_ROOTS` — a restored copy carries a stale sha, so every save in that session would conflict.
- **Saving a product repo's CLAUDE.md commits to its default branch, so the box rebuilds and redeploys that service** within ~5 min (health-checked with rollback, so bounded). The page states it on those files.
- **MCP servers, the live `~/.claude/settings.json` and `.credentials.json` are machine-local and unreachable.** The page says so in its own card rather than omitting them — an absent section reads as a gap someone forgot.
- Auth is `GITHUB_CONFIG_TOKEN` (fine-grained PAT) read from admin's own container env, like `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` in the god-mode routes; not a key-service platform key, which exists for secrets several services resolve. **Absent token is a first-class state**: the page names what is missing and the command to supply it. With a fine-grained PAT the repo list IS the grant, so what shows up is exactly what can be touched.
- `config-files.ts` is alias-free (real unit tests) and carries no em-dash anywhere, comments included: nearly every string in it is copy a person reads. Guards: `tests/config-files.test.ts` + `tests/audit-config-surface.test.ts`.

## A brand Overview states MONEY; a channel- or funnel-scoped figure belongs to the campaign

The brand Overview answers one question — what did the whole brand return — and a brand runs several acquisition channels and several sales funnels AT ONCE. So a figure whose meaning is scoped to one channel or one funnel is narrower than the page it sits on, and putting it there labels a sum with one member's vocabulary. The campaign Overview is where those belong: it runs exactly one channel and, since #3344, states exactly one funnel.

Concretely, the brand Overview shows `Outreach` plus four money cards — **Pipeline revenue** (`headline.totalPipelineUsd`), **ROI** (`costEconomics.roiMultiple`), **$ CAC** (`costEconomics.costPerAcquisitionUsd`), **% CAC** (`costEconomics.costOfAcquisitionPct`) — charts **Return on spend** (`roiHistory`) where the one-funnel signal line used to be, and ranks its **Top-audiences** card on `projection.returnPerDollar`. It does NOT show the per-day `Outreach activity` bars (they count the emails cold outreach sends) nor the funnel step pairs (`Website Visits` / cost per visit, and the goal's outcome pair). All of it is the SAME components, prop-gated (`showActivityChart`, `showEconomics`, `showFunnelMetrics`, `showRoiTrend`) — never a second copy, or the two Overviews drift into describing one result two ways.

⚠️ **`$ CAC` reads `costPerAcquisitionUsd`, NOT the lens-only `costPerConversionUsd`.** The latter is served on a `?lens=` response only and is ABSENT on the brand Overview's un-lensed read, which is what left the card on a dash for a while; features-service pins the two equal for the same scope. Whichever is absent, do NOT "fix" a dash by dividing — neither `committedCostUsd ÷ an outcome count` nor `pipeline ÷ roiMultiple` is the figure features-service computes, and putting either on screen is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug.

**`roiHistory` is CUMULATIVE on both legs, its spend leg is COMMITTED and its pipeline leg is what actually landed** (spend dated by runs' own cost buckets, pipeline by the per-lead event timestamps). The spend leg rides the same single basis `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` does, so the curve's last point reconciles with the ROI card above it rather than charting a different currency. Do not re-derive it per-period: spend on a day buys outcomes that land days later, so a period-grain ratio oscillates between 0 and absurd. A day whose cumulative spend is still 0 carries `roiMultiple: null` and is DROPPED, never plotted at 0. Pipeline whose outcome has no timestamp (`undatedPipelineUsd`) counts in the ROI card but cannot be in the line, so the card STATES that gap rather than letting the two disagree silently. `roiHistory` is fail-soft server-side (null on a blip) and null on `?lens=` — render the reason, never a skeleton.

**Every ROI figure on the page prints ONE decimal** (`11.7×`), byte-equal across the ROI stat card, the chart headline, the chart's y-axis and the Campaigns table. Coarsening above 10× was tried and reverted: at a real 11.7 the headline read `12×` two inches under a stat card reading `11.7×`.

**Rank audiences on RETURN, never on cost per outcome** — cost per outcome ranks by cheapness, so an audience converting to nothing outranks an expensive one that pays. `projection.returnPerDollar` is the same definition `funnel-ranking` uses, so an audience's return and the brand's are one statistic at two grains.

**The Top-3 card is BRAND-scoped by default and states NOTHING but the return there** (`TopAudiencesCard`, `campaignScoped` opts the campaign Overview out). Two things were removed from it, and both are the same rule:
- **It states no brand-wide return of its own.** It used to render `brandProjection.returnPerDollar` above the rows, so a reader could see an audience beating the brand or not. That number is **PROJECTED** (conversion rates × lifetime revenue) while the ROI stat card two inches above is **REALIZED** (`costEconomics.roiMultiple`, actual pipeline ÷ actual spend) — so one page carried two different numbers under one word (prod: `2.5×` under `2.7×`) and read as contradicting itself. Both were correct; printing both was the bug. **One return per page.** Do NOT re-add a second brand-grain figure here, and do NOT "reconcile" them — they answer different questions and a card is the wrong place to explain that.
- **No cost per outcome at brand level.** `$45 cppr` under an audience names ONE funnel's step on a surface that sums several — the same reason the brand Overview drops the funnel step pairs. A row with no projection prints `-` rather than falling back to a funnel cost. The campaign Overview passes `campaignScoped` and keeps both the cost subtitle and the cost fallback, because it sells exactly one funnel.

Guard: `tests/brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts`. (#3349, #3355; features-service v0.127.0)

**The rule runs one level down too: the brand AUDIENCES table and the brand Overview's campaigns section.**

`customer-audiences-page.tsx` is shared by the brand route and `campaigns/[id]/audiences`, and `brandLevelMoney = !campaignScoped` is the switch. At BRAND level it shows **ROI / % CAC / $ CAC** (all three read off `projection.{returnPerDollar, costOfAcquisitionPct, costPerPaidClientUsd}`, features-service v0.128.0) sorted by RETURN DESC, and every funnel-scoped pair is off — `Positive replies`/`CPPR`, `Website Visits`/`Cost per website visit`, and the signup / form-submission / sale variants. Cost per outcome ranks by CHEAPNESS, so an audience converting to nothing outranks an expensive one that pays; that is why the default flips to desc-on-return rather than asc-on-cost. The campaign route keeps every column: it sells exactly one funnel, so its own steps ARE what it buys. ⚠️ **`% CAC` is READ, never derived by inverting the return beside it** — `100/cacPct` is on the banned-browser-math list, and the producer implements it AS the reciprocal so the two null together. It is PROJECTED (like the return) and must not be paired with `/revenue`'s REALIZED `costEconomics.costOfAcquisitionPct`.

**Each funnel-scoped pair carries its own `&& !brandLevelMoney`, so a NEW pair added here is off by default only if you remember to write it — and the visit pair proves that is not a safe assumption.** `Website Visits` / `Cost per website visit` shipped gated on `isPositiveReplies` ALONE, which reads as a complete rule (a click is genuinely not in the reply→paid funnel) and hides the second, larger reason: at brand level a visit names one funnel's first step while the rows beside it are attributed across every funnel the brand sells. So the pair kept rendering for every brand on any other goal, months after #3358 dropped its four siblings. It is now ONE named flag, `showVisitCols = !isPositiveReplies && !brandLevelMoney`, pinned by name in `brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts` + `customer-audiences-table.test.ts`. Generalise: when a sweep turns N sibling conditions off at a scope, grep for the ones that were ALREADY conditional on something else — those read as done and are the ones it misses.

**`$ Invested` sits right of `$ CAC` and is the one REALIZED figure in that group.** It renders `evidence.totalCostInUsdCents` verbatim (features-service, `pricing=net`, the same net spend billing charges and the same field the CPPR floor reads), sorted on the field it displays. The three columns to its left are PROJECTIONS of what the audience is worth going forward; this is what it has cost so far. That is not the one-return-per-page contradiction — the labels differ and they answer different questions — but the tooltip states it outright, because a reader who assumes one basis will try to multiply `$ Invested × ROI`.

**It renders at BOTH scopes, and at both it sits immediately left of `Outreach`** — at brand level that falls out for free, since every funnel pair above it is off there. So the campaign copy is a separate `{campaignScoped && …}` header/cell pair placed before `Outreach` rather than a widening of the `brandLevelMoney` block: moving the brand column would have re-ordered a table nobody asked to change. Two things differ from the brand column and only two — the campaign read is `campaignId`-scoped (its own cache key), and the TOOLTIP is its own string, because the brand copy says "the three columns to its left are projections", which is false beside per-outcome costs. The rest is byte-equal: same `evidence.totalCostInUsdCents`, same `col="invested"` sort case, same skeleton. ⚠️ The campaign `min-w` widths count this column — a new campaign-only column means bumping all four campaign branches, and the brand branch stays put. Guard: `tests/brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts` pins it at both scopes AND asserts the campaign header precedes `Outreach`. (#3377, #3407)

**A FEATURE IS AN ACQUISITION CHANNEL, so `/features/{slug}/revenue` answers for ONE channel — every surface asks at the grain it PRESENTS (campaign → offer → brand), and repointing one surface means sweeping every sibling that reads the same thing.** The per-feature read is correct for a campaign, which runs exactly one channel, and wrong the moment a surface presents its answer as an offer's or a brand's: it describes whichever channel it happened to name and understates the subject by whatever the others did. Nothing errors, every figure is real, and the two surfaces simply disagree — so it is reported as "the stats don't match" rather than as a bug, and the first instinct is to look for a caching lag that is not there.

The grains and their reads: campaign → `getFeatureRevenue(slug, brandId, {campaignId})`; offer → `getOfferRevenue(offerId, brandId)`; brand → `getBrandRevenue(brandId)`; the brand's offers one row each → `getBrandOfferMoney(brandId)`. Keys carry the grain (`["offerRevenue", brandId, offerId]` / `["brandRevenue", brandId]` / `["brandOfferMoney", brandId]`), so surfaces at one grain dedupe to a single poll and cannot print different money for one subject.

**The sweep is the part that gets missed.** #3468 moved the brand Overview onto the brand and offer reads and left three siblings on the per-feature one: the Campaigns header (whose own comment said *"Brand-level (ungrouped) revenue"*), `OutreachStatCardsAuto` (which renders on every brand- and offer-scoped page, so its cost cards described one channel throughout), and the Offers table. Measured on the brand that surfaced it, whose single offer runs four channels — `sales-cold-email-outreach` $2,625.44 / 2.67×, plus $40.94, $2.24 and $0 — against $2,670.44 / 2.62× for the brand and for that offer. The customer read `2.6× / $2,669` on the cards and `2.7× / $2,625` in the table directly beneath. So when you repoint ONE surface to a new grain, grep every remaining caller of the old read in the same PR and decide each one's grain explicitly; a comment claiming a grain is not evidence of one. Guard: `tests/money-asked-at-page-grain.test.ts`.

**Rows do NOT sum to their parent, deliberately, and that is not this bug.** Money adds across an offer's channels because a run belongs to exactly one, but people do not (a lead worked through two channels is one lead) and no ratio does (a ratio of sums is neither the sum nor the average of ratios). Across several offers the rows likewise do not add to the brand, and a campaign stating no offer is in no row at all. features-service combines the parts at each grain; summing them in the browser would be wrong on top of being the client-computed metric this repo forbids.

⚠️ **The brand's per-offer money is `GET /v1/features/brands/:brandId/offers`, NOT `/v1/brands/:id/offers`** — the latter is already the brand's offer CATALOG from brand-service, and `brandRoutes` mounts first, so a same-path declaration would be dead code. Two different questions sharing a noun. (#3473, #3474; features-service#816 → v0.140.0, api-service#855)

**The Campaigns table is a COMPONENT (`components/campaigns/campaigns-table.tsx`), rendered by the Campaigns page AND full-width under the brand Overview's chart** — never a second copy, or a campaign reads one way on the Overview and another one click over. The ordering lives in the shared `useCampaignRows` hook because the Campaigns page's "#1 acquisition channel" tile reads the same sorted list; two sorts would let the tile and the first row name different campaigns. Both queries keep their original keys, so the extra surface costs no network. (#3358, #3359)

**Its money block reads `ROI · % CAC · $ Revenue · $ Invested`, and `$ Invested` is the one already-happened figure among them** — `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` off the `groupBy=campaignId` group the table already fetches with `pricing=net`, i.e. net spend already billed PLUS the holds open on sends the campaign has queued, AND the exact number `ROI` and `% CAC` divide by, so a row cannot contradict its own return. Rendered verbatim (`fmtUsd(revenue?.committedCostUsd)`); a campaign with no group reads `—` rather than `$0`, because "we have no figure" and "it cost nothing" are different statements. Same field, same net basis and near-identical tooltip as the `$ Invested` on both Audiences scopes (#3377, #3407) — one cost question, one answer fleet-wide. The tooltip states the projection-vs-realized split outright, since a reader who assumes one basis will try to multiply `$ Invested × ROI`. `Revenue` was renamed `$ Revenue` in the same pass so the two money columns read as a pair. ⚠️ A new column here means bumping `min-w` (720 → 820 → 900) and BOTH `colSpan`s (6 → 7 → 8 → 9, the mobile identity column included) — the skeleton and the empty state each carry one, and a stale `colSpan` silently narrows those rows. Guard: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` pins the column order, the tooltip copy and that the cell reads the served field rather than deriving one. (#3419)

**On a phone the row narrows to ROI plus ONE identity cell, and that cell carries the funnel above the channel** — a campaign IS (offer × funnel × channel), so naming one without the other names half of it. The `Sales funnel` and `Channel` columns fold into it (`md:hidden` cell ⟷ `hidden md:table-cell` columns, strict complements off the SAME `FunnelCell` / `ChannelCell` bindings, so no value is on screen twice or zero times at any width); `% CAC`, `$ Revenue`, `$ Invested`, `$ Budget` and `Status` fold away. **The floor is `md:min-w-[900px]`, gated — unconditional it re-widens the row past the viewport even with seven columns hidden, pushing the survivors off to the right where they read as missing** (the leads-table case, CLAUDE.md above), and `table-fixed md:table-auto` + a 30/70 split is what makes `truncate` bite at all: in the auto layout a column grows to its content, so one long funnel name widens the whole row. The **Offers** table folds the same way (ROI + Offer, `md:min-w-[720px]`, same 30/70) — its `colSpan` stays 5, since nothing new is added there. Measured rather than grepped: at 360 / 390 / 414px both tables render `scrollWidth === clientWidth` where the pre-change campaigns markup measured 1068px against a 358px viewport. Guards: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` + `tests/brand-offers-page.test.ts`.

**`$ Budget` is the row's fifth number and it sits with the STATUS, not with that money block** — the four above are charges and projections of charges, and a ceiling is neither, so parking it beside them invites a reader to add it to spend. Beside the pill it answers the other half of one question: is this campaign running, and how hard. It is billing's own `(org, brand, funnel, channel, offer)` row — a campaign IS that triple — narrowed by **the ROW's own `campaign.offerId`**, never by the surface's: billing's per-pair figure spans every offer selling that pair, so a row that borrowed the pair total would print a sibling offer's money under this campaign's name, and the brand-scoped list and the offer-scoped one would disagree about one campaign. Whole dollars always (a ceiling is a configured whole-dollar value); `$0` is a real answer meaning stopped, while `—` means billing had none — different statements. The read is `["brandFunnelBudgets", brandId]`, the key Offer Settings and Campaign Settings already poll, so it dedupes to no new network, and it joins the reveal-on-settle gate so a failed read shows dashes rather than an eternal skeleton. Guard: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts`.

**At brand level there is NO goal — only the return.** The brand goal (`salesEconomics.optimizationGoal`) is a RETIRED brand-service column: `NOT NULL` with a server default, so it reads `websitePurchase` for a brand that stated nothing (`schema.ts`: *"Nothing reads it"*), and it collapses `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` onto one word. Do NOT key a brand-level surface on it, and do NOT replace it by PICKING one declared funnel — that answers "which funnel", which is not the brand's question.

- **Leads tabs are the UNION of the funnels the brand's ACTIVE campaigns sell** (`leadTabsForFunnels`, built on #3362's `funnelSteps` so one chain definition serves both scopes). A campaign filters that same list to itself; a brand takes the union. `outreach` is always present and always last — every lead we contacted is in it whatever the funnel, so a brand with no live campaign still has one truthful tab. The auto-select latch is one-shot, so it waits on the CAMPAIGNS query, not the economics one.
- **The reassurance banner says "results", counts outcomes of EVERY kind, and retires on TIME** (`LEARNING_WINDOW_DAYS` = 28, dated from `roiHistory.daily[0]` — the brand's first spend by construction). Website clicks are deliberately NOT in that count: a click is a signal on the way to an outcome, so counting it would retire the banner on the first visit for a brand still waiting on the reply it buys. Time rather than a spend cap because there is nothing honest to price one with — picking a declared funnel is the retired-goal pick wearing a different hat, and cost per acquisition is unresolvable before the first outcome (a brand with no pipeline has no CAC). The CAMPAIGN keeps the outcome-priced window: it sells one funnel, so it can name what it buys.
- **The brand Overview resolves NO workflow.** `/workflow-projection` answers "how many of the GOAL's outcome does this budget buy", and it fed only the Outcome line's dashed forecast (replaced by the return chart) and that spend cap. `getWorkflowProjection` / `salesObjectiveForOptimizationGoal` / `selectWorkflowForOptimizationGoal` / `workflowOutcomeUnitCost` are all off this page; the campaign Overview still resolves one, correctly.
- **Brand-level audience reads name NEITHER a funnel NOR a goal** — features-service v0.129.0 treats that as a first-class request and prices every audience through the best-returning declared funnel (`max`, never a blend), sorting on return. A campaign names its `funnel`. Each row carries its own `basisFunnelKey`, because an audience's best chain is routinely not the brand's.
- **The daily digest fires on the RETURN**: it sends only when the brand's return went UP on the day (two SERVED points of `roiHistory.daily`, never a browser-computed ratio), and names every kind that landed (`"3 positive replies and 1 signup"`), each person badged for what THEY did. A flat or falling day sends nothing — an inbox that gets the email whatever happened learns to ignore the one that matters. An absent curve means "could not measure", never "improved". `OutcomeGoal` / `fetchBrandGoal` / the goal-keyed `GOAL_CONFIG` are deleted. (#3364)

Open follow-up: `audience-stats` now prefers a `funnel` param and marks `goal` deprecated — the dashboard still sends `goal`, which features-service keeps working.

## The Revenue tab counts THREE different monies and must never merge them

`/metrics?view=revenue` (`components/revenue-view.tsx`) shows three figures that are all "revenue" in English and are not the same number. Prod 2026-08-02: **cash $10,351.46**, **consumed $5,758.88**, **committed MRR $1,980**. Someone reading two of them together will try to reconcile them; the page is laid out in named bands so they can't.

- **Cash collected** — Stripe charges minus settled refunds and lost disputes. Served ready-made by `/public/stats/billing`: `total_paid_cents` (gross), `total_returned_cents`, `total_revenue_cents` (net), and `revenue_cents` per period on `monthly_growth`/`weekly_growth`. Nothing here recomputes it. A return is attributed to the period it HAPPENED in, so a past bucket is never rewritten and a net-negative period is real.
- **Revenue consumed** — `getFleetRevenue()` → features-service, summing net actualized cold-email spend off the runs cost ledger. **Stripe is nowhere in this chain.** It trails cash by the prepaid credit not yet burned. **Refunds must NOT be netted into it**: a refund reverses a payment, it cannot un-send an email.
- **Committed run-rate** — Σ active daily budget × 30, a daily snapshot recorded going forward. It does not reach back to inception, so its growth labels say "since the first snapshot".

Two traps:
- **Every "since inception" label holds only while the producer's TRAILING window still contains the first billed day** (2026-03-12 / 2026-W11) — at its defaults the weekly window stopped doing so around Sept 2026. `getFleetRevenue` requests the maximum windows (36 months / 104 weeks) and `trimLeadingZeroBuckets` drops the pre-inception buckets. A zero INSIDE the history is kept; dropping it makes the growth line skip a period.
- **`Number("")` is `0`, not `NaN`** — `centsStringToUsd` rejects blank before finiteness, or an empty field renders "$0" and reads as "we took in nothing".

**NRR** (`retentionSeries`) is features-service-owned (v0.120.0, `netRevenueRetention`), read `.optional()`. Cohort fixed at period start, new logos excluded from both sides — the industry definition, so it is comparable to the >100 / >120 bands. A `retentionPct: null` period is UNMEASURABLE and is dropped, never charted as 0; the headline is the last COMPLETE period (mid-period retention always reads low) with its cohort size beside it. Guards: `tests/revenue-view.test.ts`. (#3243, #3244, features-service #727)

**Chart chrome (`period-compound-chart.tsx`, and any recharts chart with NAMED y-axes): NO `CartesianGrid`, and the only horizontal is the one that means something.** `CartesianGrid` defaults to `yAxisId` 0; these charts name their axes (`"value"` / `"growth"`, or `"capacity"` on `audit/capacity-history-chart.tsx`), so recharts resolves no ticks for it and degenerates into ONE dashed line across the top of the plot — a reference the chart does not have. Reported as *"cette barre horizontale en pointillé blanc dans tous mes graphes qui ne veut rien dire"*. Do not re-add it; a chart on a DEFAULT axis renders a real grid and keeps its own. On retention the 100% line takes that role (`referenceValue`), dashed, coloured from `currentColor` off a `text-gray-400` wrapper — a hardcoded hex is invisible on one of the two themes, and the `html.dark` remap does not reach an SVG `stroke` attribute. **Y ticks are ANCHORED on that reference** (`referenceTicks`, walking up from it rather than from zero) so 100% carries its own label and coarsening a tall axis can never drop it. `niceCeiling`'s steps include 1.5/2.5/3/4 on purpose: under the classic 1/2/5/10 grammar a 255% series rounds to a 500% ceiling and every bar renders half as tall as it should. Guards: `tests/chart-domain.test.ts`. (#3251, #3256)

## The feature-stats surface is THREE pages behind a SECOND sidebar — and "Outreach" is people contacted, never a run count

`/feature-stats/sales-cold-email-outreach` was one 641-line page holding two unrelated questions. It is now Economics (`page.tsx`, the cross-org price of every outcome), Cost details (`details/`, the objective-selectable trend + per-workflow cost split) and Workflow (`workflows/`, every workflow scored across all client brands), with a sub-nav column of its own.

- **The second sidebar renders BESIDE the Platform one, not in place of it.** `getNavigationLevel` leaves `/feature-stats/...` at nav level `app`, so `ContextSidebar` keeps drawing `AppLevelSidebar`; the route's `layout.tsx` mounts `FeatureStatsSidebar` next to `{children}` in a `md:flex-row`. The campaign layout looks identical and is the OTHER case — its level returns `null`, so its sidebar replaces. Adding a `segments[0] === "feature-stats"` branch to `getNavigationLevel` would silently drop the Platform nav (guarded).
- **`SidebarSection` / `SidebarLink` / `SidebarItem` are EXPORTED from `context-sidebar.tsx` for this.** Two sidebar columns on screen at once cannot each own their row markup, or they read as two products. Same reason every shared helper moved out of the page: `lib/feature-stats-format.ts` (the one money format, the null-sinking comparator, the sort toggle, the trend derivations), `lib/feature-stats-objectives.ts` (the outcome catalogue) and `components/feature-stats/primitives.tsx` (`SortableTh` / `GrowthBadge` / `Sparkline` / `OutcomeCard` / `TrendTooltip`). The guard fails if any page redeclares one.
- **Query keys are unchanged across the split**, so the per-objective trend fetched on Economics is the same entry Cost details reads, and Workflow's `positiveReply` / `websiteVisit` cost reads dedupe with the Cost details selector. Splitting a page must not multiply its network.
- **Every Workflow cell is a SERVED field — the join is a display lookup, not a metric.** `buildWorkflowPerfRows` (alias-free, real unit tests) merges three cross-org reads on `workflowDynastySlug`: `workflow-cost-per-outcome?objective=positiveReply` (positive replies + CPPR + spend), the same route at `objective=websiteVisit` (website visits + CPWV) and `public/features/ranked?groupBy=workflow` (outreach). Two calls are needed because that endpoint prices ONE objective at a time. Verified equal on the shared fields in prod (Azalea: `$209.88 / 1 click / 1 positive reply` on both), so the row cannot contradict itself. The rows are the UNION of the three sources — a workflow one source has not caught up on still shows its money, with `—` where a source is silent.
- **⚠️ `completedRuns` is NOT an outreach volume.** A workflow that contacted 638 people logs ~15,000 runs, so labelling the run count "Outreach" overstates it ~23×. The column reads `recipientsContacted`, which is what "outreach" means on every other surface in the fleet (the customer Leads page's Outreach tab is contacted leads). `getCrossOrgWorkflowOutreach` states this and the guard fails if `completedRuns` reaches the page.
- **`limit` on the ranked read is a ceiling, not a page size** — that endpoint defaults to the top 3, and the Workflow page needs every workflow, not a leaderboard.
- The table reveals on SETTLE (`isPending && !isError` per query), so one slow cross-org read cannot skeleton the page forever. Guards: `tests/feature-stats-subnav.test.ts`.
- **The Workflow page EDITS its workflows — `Edit with AI` mounts the SAME `WorkflowChat` the org-scoped editor runs, in a right-side overlay (`components/feature-stats/workflow-ai-panel.tsx`). Never a second chat implementation.** Two things differ, and both follow from this page having no workflow in its URL. (1) The context carries a **name → UUID catalogue** (`lib/feature-stats-workflow-catalogue.ts`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests) instead of one `workflowId`, because the user names the workflow in the sentence ("duplicate Ballad but swap the LLM"). (2) The instructions route a duplication through `get_workflow_details` + `fork_workflow` **explicitly**, because the platform prompt reads an ABSENT `workflowId` as "this feature has no workflow yet, generate one from scratch" — which would discard the very pipeline the user asked to copy. No DAG is inlined (the context is re-sent every turn; same rule as the single-workflow editor). The panel stays MOUNTED while closed — hidden, not unmounted — or closing it to look at the table aborts a streaming answer and loses the thread.
- **The CATALOGUE is the FOURTH row source, and that is what makes a just-created workflow visible.** All three cross-org stats reads are keyed on spend and outcomes, so a workflow that has NEVER RUN is absent from every one of them — without the catalogue in the union, a workflow created from this page would be invisible on it until its first billed run and the page would read as if the creation had not happened. It lands with every earned column blank, which is the honest reading. Its query key is `["workflows", FEATURE_SLUG]` on purpose: `WorkflowChat.onFinish` already invalidates the `workflows` root, so the new row appears with zero extra wiring — do NOT add a bespoke post-chat refresh. Guards: `tests/feature-stats-workflow-ai.test.ts`.
- ⚠️ The chat proxy bills the LLM spend to the staff member's **currently-active Clerk org** — on a platform page that is whatever customer org was last god-moded into. Same as every admin chat surface today; a platform-billed chat route is a separate backend ask.

**The same scorecard exists one grain down, at `.../brands/:id/features/:slug/workflow-stats` — and there EVERY column is THIS brand's own realized figure. There is no fleet half.** Eleven columns: ROI, % CAC, $ CAC, Revenue, Positive replies, CPPR, Website visits, CPWV, Outreach, $ Invested — all features-service fields for this brand.

- **The cross-brand benchmark used to occupy the right six columns of this same table, and taking it out is the fix, not a simplification.** Both halves carried the SAME six labels, so one label meant this brand on one side and every brand on the other, and reading a row across compared one brand against the fleet — two different questions under one heading. The fleet answer keeps its own page (`/feature-stats/<slug>/workflows`), where every row IS the fleet, so nothing was lost. `tests/brand-workflow-scorecard.test.ts` fails if `All client brands`, any `getCrossOrgWorkflow*` read or `buildWorkflowPerfRows` reappears here. The cross-org readers stay — the fleet page still uses them.
- **`$ Invested` is `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` — billed spend PLUS the open provisioned holds, never the billed-only sibling.** ROI and % CAC divide by that same number, so a spend column showing anything else would make one row contradict its own ROI. This line said the exact opposite until features-service#779: ROI rode billed-only while the `spend` block rode committed, so ONE payload answered "how much did this cost" two ways and a brand with a single campaign read **$202** on its Overview beside **$191** here. There is now exactly one basis and it is COMMITTED. The producer still serves `actualCostUsd` (honestly billed-only, so consumers had a gap-free path); nothing in this repo reads it and nothing may fall back to it.
- **Positive replies / CPPR / Website visits / CPWV / Outreach have no per-workflow answer from features-service yet** — it serves them for the WHOLE brand on the un-grouped read. They are declared `.nullish()` under the producer's own established names (`recipientsRepliesPositive`, `recipientsClicked`, `recipientsContacted`, `spend.cpprCents`, `spend.totalCpcCents`) so the columns fill in on their own when it ships, and the page STATES why they are blank rather than leaving five unexplained columns of dashes. Do NOT derive them here: a group is a dynasty folding several versioned slugs, so it would mean summing and re-dividing in the browser.
- **The brand half CANNOT be rolled up from the per-campaign grouping.** `/features/:slug/revenue?groupBy=campaignId` is the only breakdown that existed, and a workflow routinely carries several campaigns (prod brand `75d7e3e8`: 20 campaigns over ~15 workflows, `bronze-2` alone on four). Summing pipeline and re-dividing ROI/CAC in the browser is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug AND would contradict the Overview. features-service owns it: **`groupBy=workflow`** (features-service #772 → v0.130.0), one lean group per workflow. A group is a DYNASTY, keyed `workflowDynastySlug` with `workflowSlugs` listing the versions folded in — the same key the cross-org benchmark uses, so the two halves join directly. Both legs read the producer that FROZE the attribution (runs-service spend by `workflowSlug`, and the `workflowSlug` lead-service froze on each `leads_campaigns` row), never the campaign row's CURRENT workflow: campaign-service switches a live campaign's workflow while keeping its id, so that would mis-attribute everything spent before the switch. Groups deliberately do NOT sum to the brand — a lead served under two workflows belongs to both, and the engine's per-organisation combination is not additive across partitions (the per-campaign grain has the same property). The api-service gateway already forwards `groupBy`, so no proxy PR — but note its whitelist is a fixed list (`brandId, campaignId, workflowSlug, groupBy, lens, pricing`) and `funnel` is MISSING from it, which is the gateway-strips-a-field bug waiting to bite a future reader.
- **The route is `workflow-stats`, not `workflows`, on purpose**: `getNavigationLevel` sends `segments[6] === "workflows"` to the featureSettings level, so a `/workflows/...` sub-route would draw the Settings sidebar instead of the feature one. Guarded.
- **The nav entry reads `Workflows`; the alpha workflow EDITOR one level down under Feature Settings was relabelled `Workflow editor`.** One nav tree with two entries reading the same word for a scorecard and an editor is a nav contradicting itself. The app-level `/features/:id/workflows` entry keeps `Workflows` — different tree, no collision.
- **`buildBrandWorkflowRows` reads the workflow key under either spelling** (dynasty preferred, versioned accepted) because the producer owns how it identifies a workflow; a group carrying neither is DROPPED rather than rendered under a fabricated name. Guards: `tests/brand-workflow-scorecard.test.ts`.

**A field a producer RENAMED, left declared under its old name and `.nullish()`, reads `undefined` forever and nothing anywhere goes red.** This is the quiet twin of the too-narrow-schema rule above (a narrow `z.object()` STRIPS a new field): here the schema is wide enough, the name is simply dead. `safeParse` passes (optional-and-absent is valid), `tsc` passes (the property exists on the inferred type), and every guard passes — the only symptom is a cell that is always `—`, which is indistinguishable from "the producer could not measure it". The brand workflow reader carried `costEconomics.totalCostUsd` for however long after features-service#402 renamed it to `actualCostUsd`, and it surfaced only because a new column finally needed to render the value. So when a producer's own docs describe a field as *renamed from X*, grep your readers for X in the same breath, and prefer reading BOTH (`new ?? old`) so an older body still fills the column. Corollary for a renamed REQUIRED field: declaring it `.nullish()` is what hides it — a field the producer marks required should be required in the reader too, so a rename fails the parse loudly instead of going blank.

**Third face, the LOUD one: a parser shared across GRAINS must accept every grain's BODY, and a field that is required at one grain is routinely absent at another by construction.** The two rules above are silent — a stripped or dead field reads `—`, indistinguishable from "the producer could not measure it". This one throws, and the failure lands somewhere you would not think to look: `safeParse` fails, the reader throws, the query errors, and reveal-on-settle paints the section as HEADINGS WITH NOTHING UNDER THEM. No skeleton, no error UI, nothing on screen saying it broke — so it gets reported as "the stats display wrong" rather than as a crash, and the diagnosis starts at the producer instead of at the parse. The tell is a whole band of labels blank at once while the wire carries real numbers.

Sharing the parser is still right — the money block a consumer renders is identical at all three grains, so a second parser is a second place for it to drift. What has to change with it is the SCHEMA: reread which fields survive a grain change before pointing a second reader at it. Here the field is whatever names the answer's subject, and a subject name almost never survives a widening. `featureSlug` was required for as long as the only revenue read was per-feature; #3468 pointed the brand and offer Overviews at reads whose bodies carry `brandId` / `offerId` and a `channels` breakdown INSTEAD, because a feature IS an acquisition channel here and a brand runs several. Every brand and offer Overview threw for two days on `$7,000` pipeline, `2.62×` ROI and `$953` CAC that were on the wire the whole time.

**Guard it with a REAL PARSE of each grain's body, never a source-substring assertion about the call sites.** `overview-reveal-on-settle.test.ts` shipped in the same PR and stayed green: it pinned the query keys, the shared parser, `pricing=net` and the absence of browser-side summing — every claim about how the readers are CALLED, none about what the parser ACCEPTS. `tsc` is equally blind, since the property exists on the inferred type either way. `revenue-parse.ts` imports only zod and a type precisely so it can carry real unit tests; keep it that way, and add a fixture per grain plus one rotten body that must still throw, so loosening a field cannot quietly turn the parser fail-soft. (#3468 → #3470)

⚠️ **`new ?? old` is ONLY for a RENAME — the same number under two spellings. It is FORBIDDEN the moment the two names mean different things, and a producer that keeps the old name POPULATED beside the new one is usually telling you they differ.** A rename retires the old name (it stops being served, so the fallback can only ever fire on an older body). Two live fields are a MIGRATION between two concepts, and `??` there renders one concept under the other's label on exactly the window where it matters — silently, since both parse and both are numbers. Read the producer's description before writing the `??`: "TRANSITIONAL AND REPORTED ONLY… nothing divides by it" is not a spelling, it is a second answer. Kevin, on `committedCostUsd ?? actualCostUsd`: *"ACTUAL CA VEUT DIRE ACTUAL, COMMITED VEUT DIRE COMMITED, LES DEUX CONCEPTS ONT DU SENS, NE LES MELANGE PAS."* Read the new name alone; absent → null → render `—`, which is the honest "we have no figure". (features-service#779)

## "Queued today" on the Instantly audit is the number the SEND SELECTOR decides on — a step count compared against a daily cap is a wrong number, not a rounding

`GET /internal/audit/account-health` serves two different granularities for the same queue and they are one letter apart: `queuedFirstUnsent` counts every REMAINING STEP of a lead nobody has contacted yet (a 3-step sequence contributes 3), `queuedFirstUnsentSequences` counts the LEADS. A never-started lead owes exactly ONE email today, its first; the other steps are weeks out. So the honest due-today figure — and the exact quantity instantly-service's send selection counts toward an account's daily load — is **`queuedFirstUnsentSequences + queuedNextToday`**, and that is what `queuedTodayFor()` in `audit/instantly/page.tsx` returns.

The step field is not wrong, it is a different question: it is the correct input to the partition that reconciles against `queueSize` (`queueSize === queuedFirstUnsent + queuedNextToday + queuedNextTomorrow + queuedNextLater`), which is why both survive. Mixing them is what breaks.

- **Everything that displays, sorts, buckets or colors "queued today" reads the one helper.** The column, `compareRows`, the `QUEUE_BINS` histogram and the row panel all call it — a table that shows one number and orders by another reads as broken in a new way.
- **The row panel keeps the two totals under SEPARATE headings** (`Due today` / `Queue (all remaining steps)`). They differ on purpose and must never be read as one number.
- **The two tiles above the chart count STEPS** (`queueSize`) and say so; do not relabel them as emails.
- Guard: `apps/admin/tests/instantly-queued-today.test.ts` forbids the old summation and pins a single definition of the value.

Why it mattered: summing the step field put `tatiana@fuseconnectio.com` at 102 against a 45/day cap (227% saturated) while the selector saw ~32 of 45 and correctly kept assigning it leads. Fleet-wide 29 accounts read over cap where 12 are. Every account looking saturated is why a real starvation bug (25 of 39 live mailboxes sending nothing for days) was hard to read off this page. (#3190, #3191, instantly-service#547)

## A sending DOMAIN is the unit that bills and the unit you cancel — the Instantly audit grades it above the accounts table

`DomainHealthCard` (`components/audit/domain-health-card.tsx`, model in `lib/domain-health.ts`) sits above "Sending accounts" and answers what the per-account table cannot: which domains to turn off this month. Mailboxes are sold per domain and a spent one keeps billing, so the delete decision is per domain, not per account. Both read the SAME `["instantlyAccountHealth"]` query, so they share one poll and cannot disagree about the rows.

- **A mailbox must clear `HEALTH_BAR` (95) on BOTH scores** — Instantly's Health Score AND inbox placement. Either one alone answers the wrong question: prod carries mailboxes at `98/0` (perfect health, zero inbox). Below the bar it is **dead** with an empty queue and **dying** while it still owes emails, because deleting a draining mailbox drops queued sends. **The bar between ~85 and ~95 reclassifies nothing** — the scores are bimodal, and measured over all 256 prod accounts on 2026-08-02 the two produce byte-identical verdicts. Do not re-litigate it without re-measuring.
- **The domain roll-up is ORDERED and EXHAUSTIVE** (`domainHealthState`), guarded by a test over every combination of up to three mailboxes: all dead → `To delete now`; all healthy → `Healthy`; **any healthy beside any non-healthy → `Mix state`**; otherwise → `To delete soon`. Mix is deliberately wider than "one dead + one healthy", which left `dead + dying` and `healthy + dying` matching no rule at all. `To delete soon` therefore means NOT ONE mailbox clears the bar — a domain still landing in inboxes must never sit in a tab whose whole promise is that it is on its way out (prod `axionmilestone.com`: four dying mailboxes beside one at 100/100 with 29 queued).
- **An absent score is `ungraded`, never a pass or a fail.** Ungraded mailboxes are excluded from the roll-up rather than counted either way; a domain where nothing could be graded reads `Not graded`, and **that tab renders only when it has rows** so an empty one never advertises a state the fleet is not in. Both scores are non-null for every prod account today — this is the defensive branch, and the honest answer for it is its own word, not a default.
- **Cost is MAILBOX-ONLY, and that is the point**: a registered domain is not refunded when you cancel, so the recurring mailbox subscription is exactly what deleting saves. `MAILBOX_MONTHLY_USD` carries published list prices (google $7 Workspace Business Starter annual, microsoft $6 M365 Business Basic, imap $3 Mailforge, read 2026-08-02). **A provider with no rate returns `null` for the whole domain, never a partial sum** — a partial reads as the real cost while understating it. The tab header states its own total the same way.
- **Colour is keyed on the VERDICT, never on a raw score band** (guarded): a chip coloured by score beside a pill stating the verdict is one row contradicting itself. All tints are in the `html.dark` closed set.
- Rows lead with the most expensive domain (the card exists to answer "what do I turn off"), unstateable costs last. One column per mailbox slot, sized to the widest domain in the ACTIVE tab. `lib/domain-health.ts` is alias-free apart from a type-only import, so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way. Guard: `tests/domain-health.test.ts`.

## Conversion rates and lifetime revenue belong to a SALES FUNNEL — the dashboard writes NO brand-level economics, anywhere
