`brand_sales_economics` is the model the funnel model replaced: ONE set of rates and ONE lifetime revenue for every path a brand sells through, plus an `optimizationGoal` that could not tell the two meeting funnels apart. **features-service no longer reads that goal at all**, and prices its pipeline on the brand's DECLARED funnel — so a dashboard write there persists values nothing consumes. The row survives only as a legacy fallthrough for a brand that declared no funnel, and as the prefill the funnel screens seed from.

**So no dashboard surface writes it. `saveBrandSalesEconomics` has no caller left in `apps/dashboard`** — `buildEconomicsPayload`, `resolveStoredEconomics`'s payload role and `rememberSavedEconomics` are deleted with it, and `tests/onboarding-economics-write.test.ts` fails if any of the three names reappears. (`apps/admin` keeps its own copies; staff fork, deliberately not synced.)

**Why this is a DELETION and not a better restatement.** The write REQUIRED all six core metrics on every call, so a screen editing one had to restate five it never showed — and restating them from client state silently destroys what the user confirmed earlier. Cost 2026-07-29 (brand `EmailToolsHub` / `7604c385-1f02-4016-b42f-344565bcd36d`): the rates step showed the hydrated 8.4% visit→signup / 16.2% signup→paid and the user accepted them. While they were at Stripe the pending-checkout snapshot became unparseable, so `readPendingCheckoutLaunch` rebuilt it via `reconstructCheckoutOnboardingState` — which fabricated `rates: { ...DEFAULT_RATES }`. The lifetime-revenue step then PUT the full six-metric set from that rebuilt state, writing `5%` / `10%` over the confirmed values. The same reconstruction seeded a `$2,500` placeholder LTR, and since `roiMultiple = ltrUsd / costPerPaidClientUsd`, the projection printed a flattering ROI that collapsed to `0.6×` the moment the user's real `$100` landed. Restating from the WIRE fixed that instance; not writing at all removes the class.

Rules (`apps/dashboard/src/components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx`):
- **Picking the primary funnel persists NOTHING.** `savePrimaryFunnelAndContinue` is synchronous: it sets the outcome (which orders the detail screens, prices the budget step and resolves the projection) and advances. What the brand sells through was already stated one step back by `saveFunnelsAndContinue`.
- **The best-model step prices the PRIMARY FUNNEL, through the same `getBrandSalesFunnels` → `buildFunnelPatch` → `declareBrandSalesFunnel` path the funnel's own screen and the Settings card use.** Its fields come from `funnelRateFields(def)` plus the lifetime revenue, so a rate reads the same words in all three places and one funnel is priced the same way wherever it is priced. It kept an editor at all because a sub-1x ROI is almost always a small lifetime revenue rather than a bad model — a block showing the quotient and neither operand is unexplainable.
- **The old field set was the retired goal's, and it was incoherent.** `RATE_KEYS_FOR_OUTCOME[outcome]` held the ENTRY legs of DIFFERENT funnels (the meeting goal asked for reply→meeting AND visit→meeting, one from each meeting funnel) rather than the chain of the one funnel being priced. Deleted; do not reintroduce a per-goal rate list.
- **The Update button arms on a LIVE compare** (`economicsSnapshot !== modelEconomicsBaseline`), never a sticky edited latch — typing a value and undoing it must disarm it. The baseline is captured by an effect on entering the step and refreshed on write, so no entry path (funnel Continue, offer Back, a fresh page load resuming at `model`) can forget it.
- **A refusal renders `funnelWriteErrorMessage(err)`**, never `err.message` — that is the whole downstream body verbatim.
- **`DEFAULT_RATES.ltv` is `0` / `""` on purpose — do NOT re-add an invented lifetime revenue.** An LTR placeholder is divided into the fleet cost-per-outcome and rendered as a projected ROI, so it reads as a promise, not a default.
- **`prewarmStoredEconomics` stays.** The post-payment steps run on a FRESH page load (the Stripe return) and never call `hydrateOnboardingInBackground`, so the warm-up is what seeds the lifetime revenue the funnel screens prefill from. `ltvEditedRef` keeps a user-typed LTR from being overwritten by it.
- Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/onboarding-economics-write.test.ts`. (#3039, retire-brand-level-rates)

**The run-status bar is GONE from the whole customer dashboard, and PAUSING IS DEFUNDING A FUNNEL — `brand-status-control.tsx` is DELETED, not hidden.** It stated three BRAND-level things side by side — the retired optimization goal, the brand pause flag, and the brand's daily budget — and its last home was the campaign Overview, a page scoped to ONE campaign and ONE funnel. Every one of the three was wrong there: the goal word cannot even name which of the two meeting funnels the campaign runs (`sales_meetings` covers both), and the dollar figure is billing's SUM of every funnel's ceiling, not this campaign's. Money is funded per sales funnel on Brand Settings, so **a customer stops a chain by dropping its ceiling to zero** — a brand-wide flag beside per-funnel ceilings is two ways to say one thing, and only one of them is what billing charges. Deleted with it: `setBrandPause` in `lib/api.ts` (there is NO writer left), the `api/brand-status-email` route the mutation fired, the `brand-paused` / `brand-resumed` email templates whose only sender that route was, and the already-orphaned `brand-daily-budget-card.tsx` (the second `setBrandPause` caller). **`getBrandPause` STAYS, read-only** — both Overviews read `paused` so the first-outcome reassurance banner never promises results to a brand that is not running. Choosing how a brand sells, and funding it, are both the Sales Funnels card on Settings. Consequence accepted: `Restart` exists nowhere, so a brand paused by the old button resumes by funding a funnel. The no-website goal coercion (`noWebsite && storedGoal ? "positive_replies" : storedGoal`) went with the bar and is no longer needed as a display rule — it now holds by construction, since every website-led funnel refuses to be declared for a brand with no domain. Guards: `tests/campaign-funnel-steps.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-budget-once.test.ts`.

**Sibling fix, features-service v0.106.0 (#659): the workflow projection reads live economics on every request and caches only the evidence fan-out.** Before that, `servedCached` keyed the whole body on `(org, brand, objective, pricing)` with a 5s TTL / 60s hard-max and no invalidation on an economics change, so a read right after the write could still return the pre-write `roiMultiple`/`cacPct`. Do NOT re-fold economics-derived fields back into the cached body, and do NOT add a consumer-side cache-bypass param — the split (heavy LTR-independent fan-out cached, cheap LTR division live) is what makes read-your-own-write correct.

## The Sales Funnels section is what a brand STATES about how it sells, and every field on it persists per funnel

`BrandSalesFunnelsCard` (`components/settings/brand-sales-funnels-card.tsx`, model in `lib/sales-funnels.ts`) is **GA** and is the brand Settings page's ONLY sales-economics surface: it REPLACED the flat `Click Destination` and `Sales Economics` sections, whose cards were deleted from `apps/dashboard` with them (`apps/admin` keeps its own separate copies — staff fork, deliberately not synced). A funnel owns the conversion rates, the lifetime revenue and the landing page those two held ONE set of for the whole brand, so a brand selling a $200 self-serve plan and a $20k contract prices each one. It renders its own heading and offers FOUR funnels, multi-select, each with its own conversion rates, its own customer lifetime revenue and its own destinations:

| key | name | chain |
|---|---|---|
| `reply_meeting` | Sales Meeting from Conversation | Positive reply → Meeting booked → Meeting attended → Paid client |
| `visit_meeting` | Sales Meeting from Website | Website visit → Meeting booked → Meeting attended → Paid client |
| `visit_signup` | Website Purchase | Website visit → Signup → Paid client |
| `visit_form` | Form Magnet | Website visit → Form filled → Paid client |

**A funnel's NAME is what it is; its CHAIN is what it does — the two are separate fields and the card renders them on separate lines.** Before #3164 the chain WAS the title, which left the second line with nothing to say and made every card read as a wire diagram. The name leads (`text-sm font-medium text-gray-900`), the chain sits under it quieter (`text-xs text-gray-500`), and a `h-11 w-11` icon tile runs alongside BOTH (44px vs the 42px the two lines occupy — a tile sized to the title alone reads as decoration).

**There is no primary funnel.** Several run at once and none outranks another; which one a campaign optimizes for is a campaign concern, not a settings one. `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts` fails if `primary` / `Primary` / `StarIcon` reappears in the card.

**Choosing a funnel, and dropping one, are decisions about how the brand sells — so NEITHER is a checkbox.** There is no checkbox in the card at all (guarded). A closed card is opened by clicking it ANYWHERE: a `role="button"` div wraps the whole header with `tabIndex` + Enter/Space, deliberately not a `<button>`, because the form it reveals contains its own buttons and a nested button is invalid HTML. The choice itself is then a named button inside the open form — `OK` / `Update` to take the funnel, **`Remove this funnel`** to drop it — with the action row right-aligned on desktop (`sm:flex-row sm:items-center sm:justify-end`) and stacked on mobile. **One card is open at a time** (`openKey`), because the list reorders itself around the selection and several open forms would move under the cursor. Verified by reproduction rather than by reading the classes: `document.elementFromPoint` at six points across a closed card (padding, icon tile, name, chain, empty space, the Selected tag) all resolve inside the clickable container at 1280px and on a Pixel 7, and Enter + Space each fire it.

**The chosen funnels render FIRST, then the rest, as two groups** (`partitionFunnelsBySelection`, pure and unit-tested). A chosen funnel carries a green `Selected` pill on the right (`bg-green-50 text-green-700`); the rest sit under a quiet `Not selected` heading on `bg-gray-50` with a `text-gray-500` name and a dimmed tile, so the two sets read apart at a glance. The heading only renders when both groups exist — labelling a list where nothing is chosen yet is noise. The hover tint differs per group (`hover:bg-gray-50` on a white card, `hover:bg-gray-100` on the gray ones), or an unselected card would show no response to the cursor.

**A funnel that is neither chosen nor open shows its name and its chain, and NO numbers** (`showNumbers = state.declared || isOpen`, guarded). An undeclared funnel's rates and lifetime revenue are a prefill nobody has confirmed, and printing them on a row the brand never picked reads as a claim about how it sells.

**The chain is modelled as `steps` + `legs`, one leg per arrow, every arrow priced.** `legs.length === steps.length - 1` is guarded. `funnelLegPct` prints the rate under its arrow once the funnel is chosen or open, and returns `null` for a rate the brand has not filled — a rate we do not have prints nothing rather than a zero. **`Meeting booked → Meeting attended` is the show-up rate, and brand-service stores it ON THE FUNNEL and nowhere else in the fleet** — so `meetingBookedToAttendedPct` saves like every other leg but is the one `SeedlessFunnelRateKey`: `isSeedableRateKey` sends it down a branch that starts it BLANK on every brand instead of indexing `BrandSalesEconomics` (which has no such column). Rate labels live once in `RATE_FIELDS` and funnels point at them by key, so two funnels sharing `meetingToClosePct` cannot drift into two labels for one stored number.

**The lifetime revenue closes the CHAIN, after `Paid client`** (`funnelLifetimeLabel` → `$2,500 lifetime revenue`), not a line of its own: it is what the last step is worth, so it reads in the same sequence. It is dropped when absent or zero.

**It WRITES, through brand-service's own per-funnel routes and nothing else** (`getBrandSalesFunnels` / `declareBrandSalesFunnel` / `undeclareBrandSalesFunnel` in `lib/api.ts`, proxied by api-service under `/v1/brands/:id/sales-funnels`). `OK` / `Update` declares the funnel and prices it; `Remove this funnel` undeclares it, and its economics go with the declaration. The CTA is `OK` on first declare and `Update` after (`state.declared`), never `Save`. **Do NOT wire `saveBrandSalesEconomics` / `saveBrandClickDestination` here** (guarded): those hold ONE lifetime revenue and ONE destination for the whole brand, so they would take four funnels' values and persist one of each — the per-funnel model collapsing back into the brand-level one.

**The write is a PARTIAL patch diffed against what is stored** (`buildFunnelPatch(def, draft, saved)`), because brand-service leaves an omitted field as stored and CLEARS an explicit `null`. Three things follow, all guarded in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`: editing one rate sends only that rate, so a field confirmed elsewhere is never overwritten from a stale copy; emptying a field sends `null` and really clears it, so nothing is validated as "required" client-side any more (`validateFunnelDraft` is shape-only — a blank value is legal everywhere); and the patch can never carry a rate outside the funnel's own chain or a destination the funnel has no use for, both of which brand-service 400s. `sameUrl` compares what two destinations would NORMALIZE to, or every save would re-send an unchanged URL.

**A goal the catalogue prices under a LONGER chain is translated, never read leg by leg** (`orphanGoalSeedFor`). `positive_replies`, `website_visits` and `sales` price ONE end-to-end step; the funnel spells the same journey over several legs. So the brand's rate seeds the LAST leg (the one landing on `Paid client`) and every leg above it seeds at **100%** — the product across the chain then equals exactly what the brand gave us. `positive_replies` → `reply_meeting`, `website_visits` → `visit_signup`, `sales` → BOTH (each from its own rate); `signups` / `sales_meetings` / `form_submissions` / `website_purchase` are absent because a funnel already prices their legs by name. Reading those legs by name CANNOT work: brand-service stores every blended rate NOT NULL with a server default, so a leg the goal never configured reads back a plausible number the brand never stated. Guarded in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`, including that the chain multiplies back to the stated rate.

**The prefill is for a person to confirm and is never written on its own.** An UNDECLARED funnel seeds from the brand's blended economics + click destination (`funnelDraftFromBrand`); a DECLARED one shows its own stored values (`funnelDraftFromDeclared`, `null` → blank, never a zero). Because the patch omits any field still equal to what is stored, and an undeclared funnel's stored state is `NOTHING_DECLARED`, a prefilled field the user left alone is never persisted — brand-service has no defaulting layer, so a value it never received reads back `null`, which is how a consumer knows not to rank on it. Turning that into "the brand told us" is worse than the preview was. **The booking link still starts EMPTY** — nothing seeds one, and borrowing the click destination would put a URL on screen the brand never gave us.

**`declared` is read BEFORE `funnels`, and the two empty answers never collapse.** `declared: true` with no funnels means the brand STATED it sells through none; absent-or-false means it has never told us anything, and the card says so in different words. It is `.optional()` in the reader only because an older brand-service serves the set without the flag; an absent flag reads as "never stated". Undeclaring the LAST funnel does not un-state the set (brand-service keeps the marker), so a brand that dropped everything has still answered.

**A refusal from brand-service is shown, not swallowed** (`funnelWriteErrorMessage`): a 400 renders `body.error`, which is a sentence brand-service wrote for a person ("this funnel starts with a click onto the brand's website…"); 403/404 get their own line; anything else gets one generic line. **Never `err.message`** (guarded) — `apiCall` sets it to the whole downstream body verbatim. Do NOT re-implement brand-service's validation as the source of truth; the client checks exist to make typing pleasant and the server's 400 is the answer.

- **A booking link is OPTIONAL wherever a meeting sits in the chain** (`bookingLink: true` on both meeting funnels), and a page destination only where a click onto the site starts it (`pageDestination`). A brand that books over email still runs the funnel, so `validateBookingUrl("")` returns ok; a malformed one is still rejected. These are two independent fields on `FunnelDraft`, not one `kind`-switched destination — `visit_meeting` legitimately has both.
- **The line under the chain is `funnelDestinationChips`: each destination as its own favicon (`BrandLogo`, logo.dev) beside `shortUrl(...)`** rather than the raw link. A destination the brand never gave us is DROPPED, not printed empty. **`shortUrl` cuts everything from the first `?` or `#`** and the chip is width-bounded + `truncate`: a real click destination carries a UTM tail (`…/level-1-free-assessment/?utm_source=landing_page&utm_medium=email&utm_campaign=…`) long enough to fill the row on its own, and none of it identifies the page. The path stays, because that IS what distinguishes one destination from another.
- **Icons are Phosphor duotone** (`@phosphor-icons/react`, imported per-icon from `dist/csr/<Name>` so the 190KB barrel never enters the bundle; the non-deprecated export is `<Name>Icon`). Heroicons is a single-weight utility set and read as flat inside a colored tile; duotone carries a tinted fill under its stroke in the same `currentColor`, so one `text-<tone>-600` class drives both layers. `ChatsCircle` / `CalendarCheck` / `ShoppingCartSimple` / `Magnet`. The four tints (purple / indigo / blue / orange) are all in the `html.dark` closed set, guarded.

- A booking link is a scheduling page on a third-party domain (Cal.com, Calendly), so it is validated as a plain URL (`validateBookingUrl`), NOT through `validateDestination`'s on-brand-domain check. Conflating the two rejects every real booking link.
- `["brandSalesFunnels", brandId]` is in `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` — an unlisted root is default-OFF and the card cold-skeletons on every visit. The declare response carries ONE funnel, so `cacheDeclared` merges it into the cached set in catalogue order; the undeclare response IS the remaining set and is written whole.
- **A funnel carries the MONEY it is funded with, and that money is billing's, not brand-service's.** The card composes two reads — the declaration and its economics from brand-service, the daily ceiling from billing (`getBrandFunnelBudgets`) — and makes two writes on one button. The ceiling stays OUT of `FunnelDraft`: that draft is exactly what brand-service's partial patch reads, and it has no column for money. It is written only when it MOVED, and BEFORE the nothing-changed exit, because a budget edit alone is a real change even when the economics are untouched. The green tag states the funded ceiling rather than merely that the funnel was picked — the money IS the selection now — and a declared funnel at zero reads `Not funded` instead of a green tag claiming it runs. Guards: `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`.
- **The per-funnel floor governs what a brand may NEWLY state, never what one has already been running — so `funnelBudgetBelowMinimum` reads the STORED ceiling, not the typed value alone.** Per-funnel ceilings shipped after the money did, and billing's single-funnel attribution sweep carried each brand-level ceiling over verbatim rather than raising anyone's spend, so live brands sit under their funnel's floor right now (prod: a reply-to-meeting funnel at $8/day against a $24 floor, charged daily). Checking the typed value alone left those brands two moves — leave it alone, or defund it — and refused the raise, which is the wrong direction to block. It also refused an edit to a **conversion rate** on a funnel whose money nobody was touching, because the gate runs before the whole form saves. A funnel billing funds under its floor is therefore grandfathered: keep it, raise it (even to another figure under the floor), or drop it to zero; lowering it to another funded figure under the floor stays refused, and reaching the floor spends the grandfather through the ordinary rule rather than a branch of its own. The refusal and the tip beside the field (`funnelBudgetFloorMessage` / `funnelBudgetTip`) name what the funnel may actually do instead of a floor it is not allowed to walk down to. **Onboarding passes a stored ceiling of `0` explicitly** — signup is a brand stating its budgets for the FIRST time, so the floor applies in full and no sub-floor ceiling can be created there. billing-service holds the same rule and its 400 is what decides. ⚠️ **The grandfather RATCHETS: it measures against what is stored right now, so a brand that raises $8 to $10 can no longer return to $8, and going through 0 does not help (0 clears the grandfather, so $8 reads as a fresh sub-floor statement). Only a direct DB write gets it back — do not "just test" a raise on a real customer's funnel.** (#3339, billing-service #354 → v0.62.1)

**The funnel key is becoming the fleet's ONE vocabulary, and it is the RICHER word — a surface naming what something buys reads the funnel key, never the goal.** `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` both collapse onto `meetingBooked`, so the goal cannot name a chain on its own; the eight-token goal set is being retired into four funnel tokens the owner fixed verbatim: `form_magnet`, `sales_meetings_from_conversation`, `sales_meetings_from_website`, `website_purchases` (renames of `visit_form` / `reply_meeting` / `visit_meeting` / `visit_signup`). `normalizeSalesFunnelKey` (`lib/sales-funnels.ts`) already reads BOTH spellings ahead of brand-service emitting the new ones, same discipline as `CANONICAL_GOALS`. A campaign carries `funnelKey` on the wire and the Campaigns table reads it and **NOTHING else — there is no goal fallback anywhere, and `primaryFunnelForGoal` was deleted rather than left unused** (`lib/campaign-funnel.ts` exports exactly one function, guarded). Deriving a funnel from a goal prints a chain the campaign never stated, and `meetingBooked` proves it: two funnels answer to it.

**Creating a campaign STATES the funnel it sells — `funnelKey` is REQUIRED on both `createCampaign` signatures, and a feature that sells through no sales funnel states an explicit `null`.** campaign-service 400s a sales campaign that states none: the funnel is what the campaign is paced on (its own daily ceiling in billing) and priced on, so a campaign without one is what left customers funding a funnel and never getting a campaign for it. Required-not-optional is the point — it makes a new caller answer the question instead of quietly shipping a null.
- **Onboarding states a funnel the customer just FUNDED** (`lib/launch-funnel.ts` `fundedLaunchFunnelKey`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests), read off the SAME per-funnel budget map billing was written from a few lines above, so the campaign and its ceiling can never name different funnels. The pick never leaves the funded set and is DETERMINISTIC — the primary funnel when it was funded, else the first funded funnel in catalogue order — because a retry that picked differently would create a second campaign for a second funnel.
- **Several funded funnels create ONE campaign here**, not one per funnel: campaign-service provisions the rest, one per funded funnel, on its next tick. Do NOT loop the client over the funded map.
- **Nothing funded STOPS the launch** rather than defaulting. A funnel nobody funded is neither paced nor priced, so inventing one is worse than the error.
- **Staff state it explicitly**: a picker on both admin create paths (`lib/sales-funnel-keys.ts`, the canonical four), required when `isRevenueFeature(featureSlug)` and absent otherwise; it rides the pending-checkout blob because the resume path creates the campaign on a fresh page load. A relaunch carries `campaign.funnelKey` off the row it relaunches.
- **Never derive it from a goal** (guarded) — same reason as the vocabulary rule above. Writes go out in the canonical spelling (`canonicalSalesFunnelKey`) while reads stay tolerant of both.
- The api-service create proxy re-declares the body, so a new field on this call needs the gateway to forward it before it reaches campaign-service. Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/launch-funnel.test.ts` + `apps/admin/tests/campaign-funnel-key.test.ts`. (#3344, campaign-service #316)

**A brand that has been running outreach for months KEEPS THAT CAMPAIGN when it funds its funnels — campaign-service adopts it into the funnel it was already running instead of parking it and provisioning an empty twin.** The "superseded" state is gone from the fleet, front and back; nothing renders it, nothing computes it, and no campaign is alive-but-never-given-a-turn. It shipped as a 15-minute defer on the keyless pre-funnel campaign plus a new campaign for the same funnel, so the staff table showed two rows on one channel and one funnel: the row holding 100% of the history (11.6×, $1,177) read dead, and the row that read live had produced nothing. The old goal vocabulary is REWRITTEN in campaign-service's own storage, not mapped on read — `form_submissions` → Form Magnet, a booked meeting → **Sales Meeting from Conversation** (owner-decided 2026-08-02: these campaigns are cold email, so the chain that ran is reply→meeting, never the website one), `website_purchase` → Website Purchases.

**Generalise it: when the wire hands a surface two rows that contradict each other, INVENTING A WORD to explain the contradiction is not a fix — it ships the bug with a label on it.** This page did exactly that: it noticed two campaigns reading `ongoing` for one funnel, correctly called that "the page contradicting brand Settings", and then coined `superseded` — a state no service emits — instead of asking why the backend had produced two. The tell is a status/label the wire does not carry, computed client-side from a shape you find surprising, with a doc comment justifying why the shape is surprising. That comment is the diagnosis; act on it upstream. Kevin, reading the result cold: *"WTF IS SUPER SEEDED???"* — a word invented for a customer surface is jargon by construction, because nobody outside the diff has ever seen it. Same reflex to distrust: a read-time fallback that maps a retired vocabulary onto a current one. If a stored value is stale, rewrite the stored value. (#3253, #3258, #3262, campaign-service v0.36.0)

**Picking funnels IS picking goals.** features-service `/goal-arbitration` reads the declared set from brand-service's own `/internal` route (never from here), ranks it, and campaign-service runs the winner — the brand-level `optimizationGoal` is only the FALLBACK for a brand that declared nothing. That is why removing the flat goal picker was safe, and why a dashboard surface that DISPLAYS a goal must read the arbitrated one rather than the stored field: showing the stored goal beside campaigns running the arbitrated one is the self-contradictory-surface bug. The arbitration is reachable from the browser through the api-service proxy (`KevinLourd/goal-arbitration-proxy`); it 502s `authorized_goals_unavailable` for a brand that never declared, which is the signal to fall back to the stored goal, the same chain campaign-service uses. (#3146, #3164, #3169, #3149; brand-service#393 / #396, api-service#784, features-service#704)

## Acquisition Channels sits UNDER Sales Funnels and answers a different question: a funnel is what happens once a lead lands, a channel is where we went to find them

`BrandAcquisitionChannelsCard` (`components/settings/brand-acquisition-channels-card.tsx`, model in `lib/acquisition-channels.ts`) is **GA** on brand Settings, under the funnels card, and it **STATES what we run rather than asking**. brand-service has no field for a channel selection, so a toggle would take the answer and persist none of it, and a control that silently discards a choice is worse than no control. The card therefore has NO control of any kind — `tests/acquisition-channels.test.ts` fails on `useMutation` / `saveBrand*` / `updateBrand*` / `role="button"` / `onClick` / `onKeyDown` / `useState` — and reads as two statements: what runs today (`Running`) and what is coming (`Coming soon`), grouped by `partitionChannelsByAvailability`. The selection model (`initialSelectedChannelKeys` / `partitionChannelsBySelection` / `canSelectChannel` / `removeChannelBlockedReason`) was DELETED with the toggles rather than left dead; it comes back against a real column the day one exists. The two lists are separate on purpose — the same funnel can be fed by cold email today and by paid clicks later.

Eight channels: `cold_email` (Sales Cold Email Outreach) is the ONLY live one and reads `Running`; `google_ads` / `meta_ads` / `linkedin_ads` / `x_ads` / `reddit_ads` / `cold_whatsapp` / `cold_sms` carry `comingSoon: true` and read `Coming soon`.

- **The mark is picked by OWNERSHIP, not by taste.** A channel run on somebody else's platform is `{kind: "vendor", domain}` and wears that platform's real logo through the existing `BrandLogo` (logo.dev) on a WHITE tile, because a provider mark is never tinted and the repo rule is never to hand-roll a provider SVG. A channel that is OURS (email, SMS) has no vendor to borrow from, so it is `{kind: "own", tone}` with a Phosphor duotone mark in a tinted tile. Only `bg-blue-50` / `bg-indigo-50` are used and the guard pins each one to an existing `html.dark` rule — a tint outside that closed set paints a bright block on the dark surface, invisible in the light default.
- **No control, anywhere** (guarded). Choosing a channel is a decision about how the brand sells, and there is nowhere to record it yet, so the card states the answer instead of collecting one. When the column lands, the affordance is the whole row (a `role="button"` div with `tabIndex` + Enter/Space, deliberately not a `<button>`, so the row stays free to hold its own controls) and the last live channel must not be droppable: a brand running no channel is a brand we cannot reach anyone for.
- **Names and copy live once, in the catalogue**; the card maps over it and the guard fails if a channel name is restated in the component. `lib/acquisition-channels.ts` is alias-free so it carries real unit tests, not source-substring guards — keep it that way. **Its em-dash guard covers the WHOLE file, comments included** (`expect(lib).not.toContain("—")`), which is stricter than the global "code comments unrestricted" rule: the catalogue is nothing but user-facing copy, so the guard does not try to tell prose from a doc comment. A JSDoc block added there with an em-dash goes red.
- When the backend model lands, the selection is a brand-level list of channel keys; do NOT infer it from `optimizationGoal` (that is the funnel's question) and do not wire a save before there is a column to write to. (#3178)

**The two marks are COMPONENTS, not icon maps inlined in a card — `components/marks/{acquisition-channel-mark,sales-funnel-mark}.tsx`.** The staff Campaigns table draws the SAME tile for the channel a campaign runs on and the funnel it optimizes for, so a second copy of either icon map is how two surfaces end up disagreeing about what a channel looks like. Both take `size` (`sm` for a table row, `md` for a settings card) and `dimmed`; both settings cards and the table render them, and the guards fail if `OWN_CHANNEL_ICONS` / `FUNNEL_ICONS` reappear in a card. Resolution lives in the two alias-free libs: `acquisitionChannelForWorkflowSlug` (a campaign has no channel field, its workflow IS the channel) and `campaignFunnel` (the funnel key the campaign itself states — never derived from its goal, see above). Both return `null` for anything the catalogue does not carry, and the caller then prints a plain word with no tile rather than borrowing a mark. (#3180, #3258)

## The 7 confirmed user-fields (the offer): a PUT that OMITS a key cannot clear it, so an emptied field MUST be sent

The brand offer fields (`USER_FIELD_KEYS` = `services` + the 6 Hormozi levers) are written through brand-service `PUT /orgs/brands/:brandId/user-fields`. Two backend semantics drive everything on the consumer side:
- **`upsertUserFields` REPLACES the value of each key the body CARRIES and leaves an OMITTED key untouched.** No merge, no partial-array append.
- **`buildUserFieldsView` falls back to the AI `suggested` prefill (from `brand_extracted_fields`) whenever a key has NO confirmed row.** So a key that was never confirmed keeps re-serving the AI suggestion.

Consequence — **a payload builder that omits empty values makes "clear this field" IMPOSSIBLE, and the deleted entry visibly comes BACK on the next read** (either the old confirmed value or the suggestion behind it). All three builders shipped with an `if (cleaned.length) out[key] = cleaned;` guard and a comment claiming it protected a confirmed value; that guard WAS the bug. They now send the key with `[]` / `""`, which writes a confirmed-EMPTY row — clearing the field AND stopping the suggestion resurfacing. The PUT body is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` with no minimum, so empties are accepted and **no backend change is needed**. (#3027, #3028)

**THREE copies of this builder exist and must stay coherent — fixing one is not fixing the bug:**

| App | File | Function | Scope rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| admin | `src/lib/user-fields-form.ts` | `profileToPayload` | every key of the card's `defs` subset (`cloneSubset` defaults them all) |
| dashboard | `src/components/strategy/strategy-page.tsx` | `profileToUserFieldsPayload` | all 7 (`cloneFields` defaults them all) |
| dashboard | `src/components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx` | `buildUserFieldsPayload` | a lever only when `key in profile` — a lever the offer step never rendered carries no user intent |

**Onboarding keeps a non-empty guard on `services` ONLY** (`if (cleanServices.length)`): that is the services step's own picked list, so an empty one there is an incomplete step, not a deletion. Do not "unify" it away.

**An "update from my website" button is a RESET, and it MUST send `regenerateFieldKeys` or brand-service hands back the user's own input.** brand-service does two things to a CONFIRMED user-field: it injects the value into the generation prompt as authoritative (*"prefer the profile value"*) AND overwrites the model's output with it before responding (`multiBrandFieldExtractionService.ts`, unconditional overlay). So a prefill on a brand with confirmed fields **cannot return anything new** — which is why the admin console's output read as disappointing for months while onboarding's looked good (onboarding runs on a brand-new brand where nothing is confirmed yet). Diagnose this by reading brand-service, never by re-tuning the prompt.

- `regenerateFieldKeys` (brand-service v0.62.0) suppresses BOTH halves, **scoped to the keys listed**. The offer button therefore lists the 6 levers and deliberately NOT `services`: confirmed services must keep reaching the model, because the levers are written FROM them. Guarded (`SERVICES_PREFILL_KEYS` must not appear in the levers mutation).
- **Two buttons, never one** (`lib/offer-prefill.ts`, alias-free twin in both apps → real unit tests): services are read off the site, the levers are written from the services. The dashboard's single-card offer button SAVES a dirty services field first, since only confirmed fields reach the prompt.
- **Both run `mode: "suggest"`.** `extract` is site-grounded and returns the literal string `"Unknown"`, which is not a value anyone wants to read in a field. `resetCache: true` stays (redundant with regenerate, but covers a pre-v0.62.0 producer that strips the key).
- **ONE shared description set** — `USER_PROFILE_FIELDS` in each app's `api.ts`, the set onboarding already uses. The admin's own `HORMOZI_LEVER_GUIDANCE` + `buildExtractDefs` + `EXTRACT_KEY_FOR_FIELD` are DELETED: it asked for `valueProposition` and mapped the answer onto `dreamOutcome` (the prompt names the requested JSON keys verbatim, so the model wrote a generic value proposition), and it prepended the services into all six lever descriptions — six copies of one sentence, duplicating the Hormozi framing brand-service's `suggest` prompt already applies. **`valueProposition` still exists as a backend-extract field** (features-service reads it as the `extractKey` for `valueForTarget`/`valueForVC`/`valueForAccelerator`) — do not rename that one, it is a different concept.
- A RESET, not a top-up: the fields a button owns take whatever came back, one the extraction did not answer is CLEARED, and keys outside that set are untouched. Values land in the DRAFT — nothing persists until Save — which is what makes a destructive reset safe. The old fill-blanks-only guard (`isEmptyField`) is REMOVED; it made the button read as dead on a populated card. Guards: `apps/{admin,dashboard}/tests/{offer-prefill,user-fields-clear}.test.ts`. (#3213, #3215, brand-service #419)

**The two offer editors (`components/brand-profile/field-editor.tsx`, byte-equal twins in both apps) are ONE mode, and the chips are deletable from the keyboard.** Its previous shape — a permanent pencil on every text row, and a list rendered as a comma-joined sentence behind an `Edit` button whose chips only grew an `×` inside a second mode you left via `Done` — was reported as *"c'est dur à delete, je ne sais pas quoi faire avec ça"*.
- **Pencil on hover only** (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`). A pencil printed permanently beside every value reads as a control you must find and press, which is the opposite of what an inline editor is for.
- **No `Edit`, no `Done`, no `+N more`.** Chips are live the moment they render.
- **Chip keys** (the pattern Material 3 / eBay / Telerik / PatternFly / CMS.gov / Angular Material converge on): `←` `→` Home End move between chips on a SINGLE roving tab stop (tabbing past seven services must be one press, not seven); `Delete`/`Backspace` removes the focused chip and focus lands on its neighbour, or on the add field when none remain; **`Backspace` in an EMPTY add field FOCUSES the last chip rather than deleting it** — the two-step is what stops a stray keypress destroying an entry. The `×` stays for the mouse at `tabIndex={-1}`, or every stop doubles.
- **The textarea grows from measured `scrollHeight`**, capped at `MAX_TEXTAREA_HEIGHT_PX`, resetting to `auto` before each measure (or `scrollHeight` reports the previous height and the box can only grow). The old `rows={value.split("\n").length}` counted HARD newlines, so a paragraph wrapping over five visual lines opened two rows tall.
- **Verify a change here by REPRODUCING it**, per the rendered-behaviour rule: bundle the real component with esbuild (`node_modules/.pnpm/esbuild@*/…/bin/esbuild --bundle --format=iife --jsx=automatic`) into a throwaway page and drive it with Playwright. A class grep proves the handler is spelled a certain way and says nothing about what a key press does — and it will not catch the two assertion traps this cost: a chip's `×` is an SVG so `textContent` carries no `×`, and a click landing while React re-renders from an input's `onBlur` hits a detached node (blur first). Guards: `apps/{admin,dashboard}/tests/offer-field-editor-ux.test.ts`. (#3213)

**A stored user-field's SHAPE does not track its declared `kind` — normalize by kind at the ONE clone boundary, never with a bare `typeof v === "string" ? v : ""` at a render or save site.** `kind` (text vs list, `ALL_FIELDS` in `components/brand-profile/field-editor.tsx`) is a DASHBOARD-ONLY concept: brand-service takes `{key, description}` and stores whatever JSON the model emits, so the SAME key comes back a bare string on one extraction run and a `string[]` of bullet points on the next (prod `brand_extracted_fields` for one brand: `riskReversal` array at 01:37 and 02:07, string at 03:40). Both directions therefore occur in prod, and BOTH break:
- **array in a text-kind lever** (`perceivedLikelihood`, `riskReversal`, `urgency`, `scarcity`, `dreamOutcome`) → the old `typeof value === "string" ? value : ""` rendered the placeholder, i.e. the Strategy page said **"not set" on a lever the user had filled in onboarding** — and, because the SAVE path applied the same coercion and #3027 sends every key including empties, **editing any OTHER lever then wrote a confirmed-EMPTY row over it** (silent deletion of a value the user never touched). Hit 6 brands each on riskReversal/urgency, 5 on scarcity, 3 on perceivedLikelihood, 1 on dreamOutcome.
- **string in a list-kind field** (`socialProof`) → the earlier, already-fixed half of the same bug (`coerceListField`).

The fix is a mirrored pair — `coerceListField` / `coerceTextField` (dashboard `lib/strategy-model.ts`, admin `lib/user-fields-form.ts`, byte-equal twins; array→text joins on `\n`, since `TextEditor` renders `whitespace-pre-line`) — applied **by declared kind at the single clone boundary each app already has**: `cloneFields` (dashboard `field-editor.tsx`) and `cloneSubset` (admin `user-fields-form.ts`). Normalizing there fixes render AND save at once and heals the stored row on the next save. Do NOT push it down into individual editors, and do NOT reintroduce a raw `typeof … ? … : ""` on a user-field value. `coerceListField` MUST return a NEW array (the clone is what stops an edit mutating the saved baseline). `isListLeverKey` (`onboarding/offer-levers.ts`) must stay equal to `ALL_FIELDS`' `kind` (guarded). Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/offer-lever-text-coercion.test.ts` + `apps/admin/tests/user-fields-text-coercion.test.ts`. (offer-levers-array-text-coercion)

**Testing note:** these modules import through the `@` alias, which vitest does not resolve in this repo, so the guards are source-substring over the function body (`src.slice(indexOf(marker), +1200)`), not unit calls. `lib/strategy-model.ts` and `components/brand-profile/field-editor.tsx` (dashboard) are the exceptions — they carry no runtime `@` import (types only / a relative `../../lib/strategy-model`), so they ARE runtime-importable and their helpers get real unit tests. Keep it that way: adding a runtime `@/…` import to either turns those unit tests into resolution failures.

## A brand domain belongs to whoever CHECKED OUT on it — and a backend error message is NEVER rendered raw

Two rules from the same prod incident (2026-07-29). A customer who signed up without a website tried to add it in Brand Settings and got, on screen, verbatim: `Could not save: {"error":"A brand already exists for domain \"steadyrecruitment.com\"","code":"DOMAIN_CONFLICT"}`. The blocker was their OWN abandoned brand: onboarding creates the brand row early, they abandoned twice, and one of those shells was squatting the domain. Their org held three brand rows for one business; only the third had a campaign and a daily budget.

**Ownership rule (owner-decided, enforced in brand-service).** A domain belongs to whoever has checked out on the brand holding it. If nobody ever paid on that brand, the domain is up for grabs and attaching it TAKES IT OVER, silently, no prompt. Only two refusals survive, and they are distinguishable: `DOMAIN_OWNED_BY_YOUR_PAID_BRAND` (this org already paid on a brand with that domain) and `DOMAIN_OWNED_BY_ANOTHER_ORG` (a paying org would lose its click destination). Cross-org sharing of one brand identity by domain is intentional and untouched — the rule only decides which row CARRIES the domain. Checkout state is NOT brand-service's to compute: **client-service owns it** (`GET /internal/brands/{brandId}/checkout-status` and its per-org sibling), because it owns the user journey and can join brand identity to money; brand-service throws rather than assume "nobody paid" when that answer is unavailable. Do NOT re-derive checkout state anywhere else, and do NOT reach into billing/stripe for it.

**Never render `err.message` from `apiCall` to a user.** `callExternalService` sets the thrown Error's `message` to the downstream response body VERBATIM, so any api-service route that rebuilt `res.json({ error: err.message })` stringified the whole downstream JSON INTO the message — destroying `code` for every consumer and putting a JSON blob in front of a customer. api-service now forwards the upstream status + body field-for-field (`respondUpstreamError`, `src/lib/upstream-error.ts`); the gateway does not own downstream shapes. On the dashboard side the invariant is permanent regardless: an error surface branches on the STATUS (and `code` when present) and prints its own copy, with the real error going to `console.error`. `brand-domain-card.tsx` is the reference — 409 names the conflicting brand when the org owns it (a display lookup over the shared `["brands"]` query, deduped with the tenant switcher), everything else gets one generic line. Guard: `apps/dashboard/tests/brand-domain-card-error.test.ts` (`not.toContain("error.message")`).

**Related, fixed in the same ship:** creating a no-website brand was not idempotent, so every re-onboarding minted another row. That is how one org reached three. (#3079, brand-service v0.57.1, client-service v0.24.4, api-service v0.91.1)

## `/onboarding` is ONE flow, built around the brand's SALES FUNNELS — and it WRITES them

`components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx` serves every signup. It ran for a while as a beta-gated variant beside the brand-level flow it replaced (`Onboarding({ variant })`, `?flow=ga`); both the gate and that second flow are GONE, so there is no `variant` prop and no `isV2` to branch on — a re-added branch would be a second money path nobody asked for.

- **Order:** welcome → url → loading → services → **audiences** → **funnels** → **primary** → consent → pricing → bonus → Stripe → celebrate → phone → **funnelStats** (one screen per picked funnel) → model → offer → launching.
- **`destination`, `objective`, `rates` and `ltr` are RETIRED but still in `Step` + `ALL_STEPS`.** They asked for ONE click destination, ONE goal and ONE set of rates — the brand-level model the funnels replaced. Their render blocks, `saveDestinationAndContinue`, `saveRatesAndContinue` and `saveLtrAndContinue` are deleted (guarded). The NAMES stay because removing one NARROWS what a persisted snapshot may carry, which strands a session that was mid-checkout when this shipped — the same reason `ONBOARDING_STATE_VERSION` stays at **8**, and why `clickDestinationUrl` stays on `PersistedOnboardingState` although nothing asks for it any more. `legacyStepFor` maps each retired name onto the step that asks the same thing now (`destination`→`audiences`, `objective`/`rates`→`funnels`, `ltr`→`funnelStats`) and a render fail-safe catches anything that slips past it.
- **The selection MUST survive the Stripe round-trip, which is NOT the same question as surviving a refresh.** The post-payment steps run on a FRESH page load, so state that only lives in React is gone by the time `funnelStats` renders: `detailFunnels` came back empty, the step hit its own `if (!funnel) → setStep("model")` escape and skipped itself SILENTLY, and `model` lost its primary-funnel card too ("il manque les étapes … après le checkout"). The fix is the `selectedAudienceIds` precedent, not a version bump: `selectedFunnelKeys` + `primaryFunnelKey` ride the **TOP LEVEL of the `PendingCheckoutLaunch` blob** (version-independent), and `applyRestoredFunnelSelection(pending)` puts them back on BOTH returns — success (walks the screens) and cancel (lands on pricing, where Back walks up through primary/funnels). They are read TOLERANTLY and are deliberately absent from the blob's validity check (an older blob must still LAUNCH), and `buildPendingLaunchBlob` writes them live-selection-first with the stored blob as fallback, so a re-checkout after a cancel carries what the user has picked NOW. `?from=add` never showed the bug — it launches in the SAME page session. Tell for this class: a post-payment step reads state the pre-payment steps wrote, and the wire between them is the blob, never the component.
- **TWO writes, each on the step that renders the values it sends.** (1) `saveFunnelsAndContinue` states the WHOLE set (`stateBrandSalesFunnels`, `PUT /v1/brands/:id/sales-funnels`) — distinct from declaring one funnel: it flips `declared`, removes a funnel the user unpicked, and is what features-service `/goal-arbitration` reads, so it lands BEFORE the budget step. (2) `savePrimaryFunnelAndContinue` writes NOTHING — it used to persist the primary funnel's goal on the brand row, and that goal is retired (features-service reads the declared funnel instead), so the step is now synchronous local state plus an advance. (3) `saveFunnelStatsAndContinue` prices each funnel through the SAME `getBrandSalesFunnels` → `buildFunnelPatch` → `declareBrandSalesFunnel` path the Settings card uses: the stored set is read from the wire on EVERY write and the patch is the DIFF against it, so a prefill the user left alone is never written and an emptied field really clears. A refusal STOPS the step and renders `funnelWriteErrorMessage(err)` (never `err.message` — that is the whole downstream body verbatim).
- **The brand-level click destination is MIRRORED, not asked.** A funnel owns its landing page now, but brand-service still serves `clickDestinationUrl` on the brand read and consumers link off it, so the page given to the FIRST funnel that lands a click on the site is written there too (`clickDestinationMirroredRef`, once per session, best-effort — the funnel's own page is already persisted, so a failure must not strand a paid user). Same value the user typed, never an invented one. Dropping this write entirely was the silent regression the flow would otherwise have shipped: every new brand would carry a null destination.
- **Everything the launch CREATES names the OFFER it is about, and the offer is READ, never created here** (`lib/launch-offer.ts` `soleOfferId`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests). A campaign is (offer × funnel × channel) and billing keys its ceiling on the same triple, so a launch that states no offer produces a campaign **no offer page can show** and a ceiling that addresses every offer selling that pair rather than the campaign it funds. Both were unstated until #3506: prod carried an `ongoing` campaign a customer could not see anywhere, on a brand whose single offer had existed 28 minutes before it was created. There is nothing to create — brand-service gives a brand its first offer on the first brand-scoped write (`resolveOfferForWrite`, get-or-create, race-safe), and the funnels step has already triggered one by the time the launch runs. **Zero offers and several offers both mean "we do not know" and both ship UNATTRIBUTED** — naming the wrong one files a campaign, and the money pacing it, under a proposition the customer never sold through. The read is best-effort by construction, logged loud on both branches: the customer has already been charged, and campaign-service and human-service each adopt an unattributed row on their own tick, so a failure here is a delay, not a dead end. ⚠️ **The AUDIENCES step is the one write that CANNOT name it** — it runs BEFORE the funnels step, so no offer exists yet and every audience a signup produces is born brand-wide (distribute.you#3454 filed exactly this; the same prod brand carried 12 unattributed audiences). Do NOT "fix" that by creating an offer from the browser to make the step nameable: naming a brand's first proposition is brand-service's call, and human-service attributes those rows on its own cadence. Guards: `tests/launch-offer.test.ts`.
- **`monthlyPipelineLabel` is the one browser-derived figure in the flow**, and it is on borrowed time: there is no served field for a budget-scaled monthly pipeline, so it divides. Filed as features-service#699; when that lands, delete the helper and render the field. It returns null (never a zero) when any input is missing — "we could not estimate this" and "it buys nothing" are different statements. Do NOT add a second browser-derived number beside it.
- Guards: `tests/onboarding-funnels-flow.test.ts` + `tests/onboarding-funnel-view.test.ts`. (#3167, onboarding-funnels-ga)

**A `min-height` is not a height, so a footer declared as pinned is not pinned — and the symptom is a CTA below the fold on every phone at once.** The onboarding shell is a `flex-col` column and `StepShell` splits it into a `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto` region plus a `shrink-0` footer, which reads as a bottom-pinned CTA over a scrolling body. It was neither. `min-h-[100svh]` sets a FLOOR: the column is free to grow past the viewport, so the flex children divide the GROWN height, the scroller's content always fits inside its own box, `overflow-y-auto` never triggers, the PAGE scrolls instead, and the footer rides below the fold. The tell is exact and worth memorising: measure the scroller and find `scrollHeight === clientHeight` while `document.documentElement.scrollHeight > innerHeight` — the region you declared as the scroller is not scrolling and the document is. The fix is to make the height DEFINITE (`max-h-[100svh]` + `overflow-hidden`, released at `sm:` where the shell becomes a centred floating card and a hard cap would clip a tall step); it is one edit in `app/(authed)/onboarding/layout.tsx` and it covers all 16 steps rather than the one that was reported. Measured on the welcome step before: the CTA sat at **926px on a 375x667 screen, on a 390x844 one AND on a 412x915 one** — identical, because the number is content height, not viewport-relative, which is itself the giveaway that nothing was being capped. After: 647px on the 375x667, and the body no longer scrolls at any size. Generalise: whenever a layout claims a pinned footer / sticky region / internal scroller, check that some ancestor actually BOUNDS the axis — `min-h-*` alone never does. **And measure it: a source guard proves the classes are spelled right and says nothing about whether the CTA is on screen.** Guards: `tests/onboarding-welcome-mobile.test.ts`. (#3482)

**Mobile sizes are their own decision on the welcome step, not an afterthought — one size at every width is what put the CTA off screen.** Three cards at `p-5` with a stacked 40px icon tile were **528px of the 926**, so the compaction is the other half of the fix above: the cards are ROWS on mobile (icon beside the text, `p-4`, `h-9 w-9`) and stack again from `sm:`, and the headline, intro, grid gap and CTA margin each step down. Nothing about the desktop layout changes. The copy is pinned by `beta-onboarding-flow.test.ts` and `onboarding-funnels-flow.test.ts` — resize, never reword.

**The website typed on the landing is carried in a COOKIE, not only in `?url=` — the param does not survive the trip and the fallback silently degrades it.** `?url=` rides `redirectUrlComplete` through Clerk and `/sso-callback`, and the first-run edge gate can bounce a signed-in user to a bare `/onboarding`; either hop drops the query. The field then falls through to `businessDomainFromEmail`, which returns a bare host BY CONSTRUCTION (`kevin@voozaa.app` -> `voozaa.app`), so a visitor who typed `voozaa.app/us/` lands on `voozaa.app` and the page they actually named is gone — with nothing anywhere reporting a failure. `?via=` needed exactly this treatment for exactly this reason (`partnero-via-capture.tsx` exists BECAUSE the param does not survive the Clerk redirect), so the precedent was already in the repo. `LandingUrlCapture` (root layout, sibling of the `?via=` one) parks it; onboarding recovers it in an effect ordered ABOVE the email guess (effects run in declaration order, and that ordering is guarded) and expires it once it has landed in the field, so a later `?from=add` flow does not inherit the first brand's website. `lib/landing-url-cookie.ts` is alias-free so it carries real unit tests; it refuses a non-http scheme (a `javascript:` input parses fine and must never be rendered back into a field someone then submits) and caps length, because the cookie rides every request to the origin including each `/api/v1/*` proxy call. **Generalise: any value carried from the landing through signup needs a cookie, never a query param alone.**

**The URL field SHOWS a URL, and that is a display decision that must not leak into identity.** A bare host in a field asking for a website reads as a token someone half-remembered; `https://voozaa.app/us/` reads as the page they gave us. Both sources (the landing carry and the email guess) are normalized for display through `normalizeLandingUrl`, on the SEED only — never on a keystroke, or the field could not be typed in. `extractDomain` is untouched, so `domain`, `hostname`, the org name, `BrandStepHeader` and `upsertBrand` all resolve exactly as before. Do NOT "simplify" this by normalizing at `extractDomain` or by storing the normalized value as the brand: this is what the field shows, not what the brand is.

**A step that is WAITING on an extraction and a step whose extraction FAILED must never render the same thing — and the thing they must not render is the success copy.** Onboarding's every-step pattern is `await extract(...).catch(() => null)`, which is correct (a failed read cannot strand a paid signup on the loading screen) and is exactly what made two steps lie. The services step kept its `We drafted these from <host>` line over an empty box, and the audience step kept a sentence it had assembled locally from the picked services, which is indistinguishable on screen from an ICP brand-service drafted. Neither said anything. Reported as *"during like 20s nothing was suggested… then 2 options just appear in the text input. WTF???"* and *"this is a placeholder when the backend is down right?? WTF it was down???"* — the surface never answered either question, so both had to be answered from prod logs.

- **Three states, and the two empty ones are different statements.** `servicesDrafted` (a list exists, from the extract or typed) / `servicesPending` (nothing yet, something still running that can deliver it) / `servicesUnread` (nothing, nothing left running — a verdict, so it says so and offers `retryServicesExtract`). A still-running read is a WAIT; a settled empty read is an ANSWER; collapsing them is the whole bug. `servicesExtractFailed` + `servicesHydrating` are the state that makes the distinction possible — the `.catch` keeps swallowing, it just stops swallowing SILENTLY.
- **The success copy is gated on the success.** `We drafted these from <host>` is a claim about an extraction that returned something, so it renders only when something is there; otherwise the step asks plainly.
- **A late background write must not land on a step already in use.** The heavy hydrate resolves tens of seconds after the fast extract, so BOTH writers go through `setServices((prev) => (prev.length ? prev : nextServices))`. `servicesEditedRef` protects a list the USER curated; `prev.length` protects one the SIBLING WRITER filled. Without the second guard the hydrate swaps the list out from under whoever is reading the step — which is the "2 options just appear" half of the report.
- **The audience seed reads live state through a REF, never the effect's closure.** `promptRef` / `fallbackPromptRef` are assigned during render; the seed runs inside a promise callback created at MOUNT, so reading `prompt` there read the mount render and its "never clobber a prompt the user already edited" guard tested a stale `""`. A failing ICP is what makes the prewarm settle late, so the two bugs fire together: the ICP dies, the prompt arrives seconds later, and it overwrites whatever was typed in between. `not.toContain("onPromptChange(prompt.trim() ? prompt")` pins it.
- **A dead prewarm auto-fires the suggest rather than looking idle.** The prewarm bails the instant the ICP throws, so `suggestAudiences` never ran and the step read as one that merely wanted a click. Both branches now self-fire off `seeded`. ⚠️ `loading` is HANDED OVER to `runSuggest`, not cleared under it — a shared `finally` drops the button to its idle label for the whole call it just started AND leaves it clickable, so a second suggest fires over the first.
- **`icpFallback` is the REASON, not the absence.** A locally-assembled sentence is labelled as one (`We couldn't read enough from <host> to draft this`); a reader who assumes it was drafted off their site edits it as if we had read the site.

Guards: `tests/onboarding-extraction-visibility.test.ts`. Root cause of the incident was NOT in this repo — chat-service's `disableThinking` floor was derived per GENERATION plus a `model.includes("pro")` test, so the 2026-08-14 `flash-pro` → `gemini-3.7-flash` upgrade sent `thinkingLevel: "minimal"` to a model that rejects it: `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`, deterministic, 10 days, killing brand-service's services extraction AND its ICP suggestion on every new signup (fixed chat-service v0.54.1, per-model floor table + a test walking every alias). The dashboard half is that none of it was visible: the fast path failed silently and the slow `url_map` fallback (~28s) looked like the product being slow. **Generalise: when a producer outage surfaces as "the product is slow", check whether the fast path is failing and the slow path is the fallback — and whether any surface said so.** (service-suggest-loading)

**Leaving the flow and coming back LATER is a different question from refreshing, and only a COOKIE can answer it — the wizard's progress is `sessionStorage`, so the tab closing takes it.** `onboardingComplete` is written only at the terminal launch, so a person who abandons mid-flow still fails the edge gate on their next visit; the gate sent them to a BARE `/onboarding`, which with no snapshot and no brand starts at the welcome screen. Everything they had typed was still in brand-service — the flow just had no way to know which brand to re-hydrate. Reported by a customer who reached the per-funnel budget step (the last screen before Stripe), left, and had to redo the whole flow.

The recovery path ALREADY EXISTED and was structurally unreachable for the case that needs it: `/onboarding?brandId=` fetches the brand, replays the loading-screen hydration and lands on `funnels` with services / funnels / rates / lifetime revenue pre-filled — but its only caller was `BrandSetupGate`, which fires for an org that is ALREADY `onboardingComplete` (the add-a-second-brand case). So the one thing that rescues a half-finished signup could never serve a first signup. **When a feature reads as absent, check whether it exists behind a gate that excludes its own use case** — the fix here was three call sites, not a new flow.

- **`lib/onboarding-brand-cookie.ts` is the model** (alias-free, so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way). The client writes it at BOTH brand-create sites (website and no-website), because the brand row is what the resume keys on; `proxy.ts`'s first-run gate reads it through one `onboardingHref()` helper used by both of its branches; `/api/onboarding/complete` clears it. ONE clear point, server-side, on the path the launch already calls — a finished user must never be sent back into the flow by a stale cookie.
- **Org-scoped (`onboarding-brand-{orgId}`), like `last-brand-{orgId}`.** Onboarding can create a brand-new org (`?new=1`), so an unscoped cookie would resume a brand abandoned under org A inside org B — landing someone on a brand that is not the one they came to create.
- **NOT httpOnly, deliberately** — the client creates the brand, so the client is what writes this; the value is a brand id the URL carries a moment later. Same call as `tenant-identity-cookie.ts`, and it mirrors that file's pure-`*CookieAssignment()`-builder split so the string stays unit-testable.
- **A week, then it expires.** An abandoned onboarding older than that is better restarted than resumed: the brand's extracted fields have gone stale and nobody remembers what they picked.
- **Audience picks and budget amounts are still lost cross-session, and that is accepted here.** Neither is persisted before payment (audiences activate at the terminal launch, per-funnel budgets are written to billing in `completeLaunchAfterCheckout`), so the resume lands on `funnels` and the user re-picks those two — 4 screens instead of 10, with everything typed earlier restored from the wire. Persisting them is a separate change; do not fake them from a snapshot that no longer exists.
- Guards: `tests/onboarding-brand-cookie.test.ts`. ⚠️ The DIS-111 gate guard (`onboarding-brand-gate.test.ts`) pinned the literal `new URL("/onboarding", req.url)`, so it goes red on any change to the redirect's destination — assert the helper, not the string.

**Gate a NEW preview on the BETA allowlist, never the staff one — the two are not interchangeable and picking the wrong one is a security decision, not a style one.** `isAdminEmail` is the PRIMARY security boundary on `/api/admin/*` in the dashboard (the god-mode org switcher, which enumerates and joins every tenant on the platform, with no edge gate behind it), and it must stay byte-equal across THREE copies (`apps/dashboard`, `apps/admin`, api-service `src/middleware/auth.ts`). So gating a preview on it means every person added for the preview also receives cross-tenant god-mode, and adding them costs a three-repo fan-out. `beta-allowlist.ts` grants nothing but UI visibility. Rule: a preview that only ever touches the VIEWER'S OWN data goes behind the beta gate; reach for the staff gate only when the surface genuinely reads across orgs. Cost 2026-07-31 (#3167 → #3170): shipped this flow behind `useIsAdminUser`, so `kevin@pressbeat.io` — already on the beta list — got the other flow and reported the click-destination step still showing.

**The funnels come from the shared catalogue through a DISPLAY ADAPTER (`lib/onboarding-funnel-view.ts`) — read the catalogue there, never field-by-field at a render site.** This is the general pattern whenever you consume a model a PARALLEL WORKSPACE is actively reshaping (the Conductor in-flight-workspaces query at O.0 is what surfaces one): put the shape-reading in a single alias-free module, map over it at the render sites, and the sibling's reshape becomes a diff to that one file. Proven in this ship — #3165 landed mid-PR and changed `steps` from a 3-tuple to a 4-step chain, replaced the `destination` object with `pageDestination`/`bookingLink` flags, moved rates onto per-arrow `legs`, added a fourth funnel and a `name` per funnel; **zero render sites changed**. Two details that make it work: the adapter's entry type is fully OPTIONAL/structural (a catalogue mid-reshape still renders), and rate LABELS are resolved by the catalogue's OWN `funnelRateFields` rather than re-listed, so a rate reads the same word in onboarding as on the settings card.

## The onboarding step header shows a company NAME and a domain-keyed LOGO — two values, never one, and there are two logo.dev keys

`BrandStepHeader` (`components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx`) is the first thing a customer sees after clicking "Analyze my product", and it is instant by construction: `headerDomain = extractDomain(url)` is parsed from the typed field with zero network, and `BrandLogo` hits `img.logo.dev` directly. It used to label itself `domain ?? hostname`, so it read `acme.com` for the rest of the flow while `Brand.name` sat unread on the wire — the brand-create response was destructured for `brandId` alone.

Four invariants, each of which has a way of silently breaking:

- **The logo is keyed on the DOMAIN, the label reads the NAME.** These were one `label` value (`domain={label}`), and `img.logo.dev/<company name>` resolves nothing — so collapsing them again empties the logo slot the moment a name is present, with no error anywhere. They are `logoDomain` and `label`; guarded by `tests/onboarding-brand-name-header.test.ts` (`not.toContain("domain={label}")`).
- **The domain stays the label until a name arrives** (`name?.trim() || domain || hostname`). The header is never blank, and a session that never saw the create response degrades to exactly the pre-#3116 look.
- **`resolvedBrandName` is deliberately NOT in the persisted snapshot.** Adding a field there means bumping `ONBOARDING_STATE_VERSION`, which strands an in-flight checkout; the cost of not persisting is only that a resumed session (and the post-Stripe `launching` step) shows the domain again. Do not "fix" that by persisting it.
- **It is DISTINCT from `brandName`**, which is what the user TYPES on the no-website path. `headerName` is null in `noWebsiteMode` — a brand with no domain has nothing to resolve a name from, and the typed name already identifies it. Conflating the two lets a resolved name overwrite the user's own input.
- **Every step that renders its own header must be threaded too.** The audience step (`OnboardingAudiences`) takes `brandName` for exactly this reason: left on the domain while its siblings show the company name, it is an internally incoherent header, not a cosmetic gap.

**Producer side (brand-service v0.58.0):** `brands.name` is resolved at brand-CREATE, priority `logo.dev company index → landing-page HTML (og:site_name / <title> / JSON-LD) → titlecased domain`. Only the index lookup is awaited, so the create never waits on a fetch of the customer's own site; the read path keeps a lazy-fill safety net and `name` is still never null on brand-detail. Before this, the derivation ran on the READ path bounded by a 5s third-party fetch, and the HTML heuristics yielded **"Home"** for a site titled `Home | Acme` and **"Emailtoolshub"** for `emailtoolshub.com`.

**Two logo.dev credentials, NOT interchangeable — this is the trap.** `logo-dev` holds the PUBLISHABLE token (`pk_...`) that signs the `img.logo.dev` image URLs; `logo-dev-secret` holds the SECRET key (`sk_...`), the only one that authenticates the server-side REST APIs (Search, Describe, Brand). Both are registered from `apps/{dashboard,admin}/src/instrumentation.ts` (`LOGO_DEV_TOKEN` / `LOGO_DEV_SECRET_KEY`) and both `platform-config-startup.test.ts` pin the list AND an exact count. Reaching for the publishable one on a REST call fails auth; the registration loop skips a missing env var with only a `console.warn`, so brand-service silently falls back to the HTML path and the whole feature reads as inert rather than broken.

**Which logo.dev endpoint to use, by meter** (the pricing surface is genuinely confusing): **Search** (`/search?q=&strategy=match` → `{name, domain, logo_url}`) and the image CDN are on the monthly REQUEST POOL, 500k/mo on the free Community plan. **Brand** (`/brand/{domain}` → the above plus colors, brandmark, social banners, blurhash) is metered on a SEPARATE prepaid credit grant — 5 credits ≈ $0.05 per call, 100 calls/mo on Community, a hard `402` when exhausted, and its own docs warn responses take 15-30s. **Describe** (same fields minus brandmark/banners) requires a PAID plan and is unavailable to us. So a name lookup uses Search; do not reach for Brand for it. Search returns up to 10 name-matched candidates, so a result is only trustworthy when its `domain` matches the brand's own. (#3115, #3116, brand-service #382 → v0.58.0)

## A CRM "source" is ONE crm-service CSV upload, and ONE source = ONE human-service audience bound via `crmUploadId`

For the CRM-outreach feature (`sales-crm-email-outreach`, the slug lives in ONE place: `apps/admin/src/lib/crm-outreach-feature.ts` — gate on `isCrmOutreachFeature`, never a scattered literal) the people are the brand's OWN imported contacts, not a provider search. Model as of `#3017`:

- **"Source" = a crm-service upload** (`GET /v1/orgs/contacts/uploads?brandId=`, one row per imported CSV). Contacts carry `sourceUploadId`. Listed for staff at `.../brands/[brandId]/services/crm/sources`.
- **Each source can be bound to its OWN human-service audience** via the audience field **`crmUploadId`** (human-service v0.33.0). Set at CREATION only, immutable afterwards, validated against the brand's uploads (400 if the upload is not this brand's). `null` = unbound ⟹ the serve covers the brand's WHOLE imported list, which is the pre-existing behaviour. Several bound audiences per brand are allowed and intended.
- **Why per-source and not one whole-brand CRM audience:** a single pooled audience gives no per-file cost-per-outcome and no way to stop sending to one file. Binding one audience per source makes each file independently pausable (audience `status`) and independently costed (per-audience attribution).
- **crm-service serves the restriction** (crm-service `#12`): `POST /orgs/contacts/serve-next` and `GET /orgs/contacts/serve-stats` take an optional **`uploadIds`** (absent = whole brand, unchanged). The restriction narrows the CANDIDATE pool only — **suppression stays brand-wide** (`contact_serves UNIQUE(brand_id, email)`, no upload dimension), so a person present in two files is still contacted at most once per brand.
- **api-service needs no route work here** — its `/v1/orgs/audiences*` routes forward `req.body` raw, so a new human-service field reaches the wire with zero gateway change. Verify before assuming a proxy PR is needed.
- **Staff UI:** the **Audiences** section under Sales Economics on `features/[featureSlug]/settings` (`components/settings/crm-source-audiences-card.tsx` + the api-free join in `lib/crm-source-audiences.ts`). ON creates-or-reactivates, OFF pauses. Two behaviours that are deliberate, not gaps: an unbound CRM audience is surfaced as a WARNING (while it is active it draws from every file, so the switches alone would not describe who gets contacted — an internally-incoherent surface is a bug, so it is stated rather than hidden), and a source with no filename cannot be switched on (an audience needs a name and we never fabricate one).
- **Audience names are unique per (org, brand)** — human-service 409s on a collision. The name is the CSV filename verbatim, so two imports sharing a filename surface that 409 verbatim; do NOT "fix" it with a client-side suffix, that is inventing a name.
- The three audience readers (`listAudiences` / `createAudience` / `setAudienceStatus`) now exist in **both** `apps/admin/src/lib/api.ts` and `apps/dashboard/src/lib/api.ts` — same contract, so they fall under the existing lockstep rule: migrate both when human-service reshapes it.

## Dashboard UI iteration — the real page, not a standalone mockup (previews are GONE)

Default: edit the real page with the existing component vocabulary (`bg-white rounded-xl border border-gray-200` cards, `focus:ring-brand-300` inputs, brand-500, `SparklesIcon`, `SectionCard`/`ScoreCard`, the `bg-brand-50`+`border-brand-200` segmented toggle). A standalone HTML mockup reads as "page vierge" and gets bounced — acceptable only as a fast layout sketch when explicitly asked, graduate to the real page ASAP. When rewriting a section, inventory existing interactive affordances (AI panels, billing guard, "Edit with AI" → `CampaignAIPanel`) and preserve them — silently dropping one is a regression. (#1239, #1242)

**Vercel previews no longer exist** (#3287 — see the release section): no branch other than `main` builds, so there is no `distribute-dashboard-git-<branch>-…vercel.app` to open. Verify a visual change by REPRODUCING it (esbuild-bundle the component + Playwright, per the rendered-behaviour rule) or on prod after merge. Dashboard project `distribute-dashboard` (`prj_nJn9Xr5D1fD5h7ug3eRPejEGsg2z`, team `team_lYmJIUH6q2rTY6dUfDiYtpAt`).

## Dashboard-wide reskin / rebrand = central via the `html.dark` remap + theme default, NEVER a per-page sweep

"Mets le dashboard au même design que la landing" / "full rebranding" = adopt the landing's **FULL aesthetic** — NOT a conservative shared-token pass (a brand-token-only pass reads as "je ne vois pas de changement" and gets bounced). **VERIFY the SERVED landing's real look FIRST — never trust this note's prior color/theme claim.** The served landing is the STATIC `apps/landing/public/landing/index.html` + `/landing/css/styles.css`, NOT the React `.dy-page` surface. 30-sec check before reskinning: `curl -s https://distribute.you/ | grep -E 'data-theme|styles.css'` (shows the default theme the HTML forces) + read `apps/landing/public/landing/css/styles.css` `:root` + `[data-theme="light"]` tokens (the real `--accent` hue, fonts, bg/text). **Ground truth as of #1644: served landing = LIGHT-default (`data-theme=light`), accent INDIGO-blue (OKLCH hue 264, `--accent: oklch(50% 0.250 264)` light / `oklch(64% 0.220 264)` dark — NOT pink), Inter + JetBrains Mono (no display face / no Fredoka), nav logo = blue `/landing/logo/logo-distribute.svg`.** Cost 2026-06-14 (#1635→#1644): reading only `styles.css`'s `:root` block (which IS dark) shipped a dark-default pink Fredoka dashboard against a page that forces `data-theme=light`. The dashboard reskins from ONE place — do not touch the ~71 pages / ~97 inline-Tailwind components individually:
- **`apps/dashboard/src/app/globals.css` already carries a full `html.dark` utility-remap layer** (`html.dark .bg-white {…}`, `.text-gray-* → …`, borders, inputs, divide, ring, prose, scrollbar). This is the same mechanism the landing uses with `.dy-page` — it remaps the inline Tailwind utilities to theme values, so retargeting those values reskins every page at once. To rebrand: (1) define the palette tokens in `:root` (the landing OKLCH set `--dy-bg/-surface/-border/-text/-sub/-muted`), (2) point `html.dark` base bg/fg at them, (3) retarget every `html.dark .*` remap to the tokens, (4) add the glow via `html.dark body::before` (the landing `.dy-page::before` signature).
- **Default theme** lives in TWO places that MUST stay in sync: the anti-flash inline script in `src/app/layout.tsx` AND `src/components/theme-provider.tsx`'s `initial`. Landing-match = **LIGHT-default** (`stored || "light"`, script adds `dark` only when `theme==="dark"`) — the served landing forces `data-theme=light` (#1644 flipped this from the wrong dark-default #1635 shipped). The in-app toggle + explicit stored choice still win — reskin is reversible (one PR + toggle).
- **Canonical card depth**: the `bg-white rounded-xl border` card pattern (~203 inline usages) gets its shadow from one `@layer base` rule (so explicit `shadow-*` utilities still win) — don't add shadows per-card.
- Accent vocab ported from landing lives in globals too: `.gradient-text` / `.gradient-text-subtle` / `.gradient-bg`, `.dy-eyebrow` mono pill + `.dy-dot`, `--font-mono` → JetBrains Mono. Opt-in (apply to headers/hero numbers).
- **The `html.dark` gray-text remap covers `text-gray-900` down to `text-gray-300` but historically SKIPPED `text-gray-950` — so `text-gray-950` (the token used for the most prominent text: card headings, big stat values) renders near-black + invisible on dark.** `apps/admin`'s remap had this exact gap; a big `+1100.0% CMGR` stat read black on the dark metrics page (#2702). Fix is ONE central line in that app's `globals.css` — `html.dark .text-gray-950 { color: #f8fafc; }` (fixes every `text-gray-950` at once, per the reskin-from-one-place doctrine) — NOT a per-component class swap.
