Fixed in `apps/admin`; **`apps/dashboard`'s `html.dark` layer likely still has the same `text-gray-950` gap** — add the line there if a prominent number/heading reads black on dark. When adding dark-visible prominent text, confirm the token is in the remap (grep `html.dark .text-gray-950` in that app's globals.css), don't assume `text-gray-950` is covered. (#2702)
- **Favicon is NOT covered by the `html.dark` remap — it's a separate surface a rebrand MUST update explicitly.** The dashboard favicon = the blue distribute mark `apps/dashboard/public/logo-distribute.svg` (same SVG the landing serves: `<link rel="icon" href="/landing/logo/logo-distribute.svg">`). It's wired THREE ways that must agree: file-convention `src/app/icon.svg` (auto-emits the tab `<link>`, applies to ALL routes incl. `/sso-callback`), `src/app/apple-icon.jpg` (iOS home-screen only, NOT the tab — SVG doesn't render on iOS so this stays a jpg/png), and `layout.tsx` metadata `icons` (→ `/logo-distribute.svg`). **Next file-convention icons OVERRIDE metadata `icons`** — so a stale `src/app/icon.jpg` beats a fixed metadata ref; to change the favicon you must replace the FILE (`icon.svg`), not just metadata. #1635/#1644 rebrand swapped the header mascot but left the old "d + rainbow-dot" jpg favicon for months — caught only on `/sso-callback` (Google-login tab). Favicon was identical (md5) on every route → never sso-callback-specific. (#1752) **FOURTH surface, runtime: on a brand page the tab mark is the BRAND's logo, not the distribute one** — `components/brand-favicon.tsx` (mounted once in the dashboard layout) reads `useTenantSwitcher()`'s `displayBrand.domain`, preloads `img.logo.dev/<domain>` with the shared publishable token, and on a successful decode BLANKS the `rel` of every app-owned `link[rel~="icon"]` (parked in `data-brand-favicon-parked`) before appending its own. So a rebrand that swaps `src/app/icon.svg` still looks unchanged on any `/orgs/:orgId/brands/:brandId` route — verify a favicon change OFF a brand page (billing, org root, `/sign-in`), not on the brand overview. Two invariants when touching it: the swap is gated on the image actually decoding (logo.dev answers an unknown domain with a generated monogram, and a brand can have no domain — both must keep the distribute mark, with the reason logged), and the parking is deliberate rather than appending a second icon link (which of several `<link rel="icon">` a browser picks is unspecified). `apps/admin` is NOT in scope — it keeps its distinct white/blue staff mark. Guard: `tests/brand-favicon.test.ts`. (#3094)
- **The dark accent-tint remap is a CLOSED SET — a new colored surface picks from it or it paints a bright block on the dark background.** `html.dark` remaps exactly seven `-50` tints (`blue`, `green`, `red`, `yellow`, `purple`, `orange`, `indigo`) plus `brand-50`/`brand-100`/`border-brand-200`. Tailwind ships dozens more (`violet`, `sky`, `amber`, `teal`, `rose`…) and every one of them is UNTOUCHED in dark, so `bg-violet-50` renders its light-mode near-white on the dark surface. This is the same class of gap as the `text-gray-950` one above, and it is invisible in light mode, which is the default — so a colour picked without checking looks perfect until someone toggles. Before shipping any new tinted chip / icon tile / callout, `grep "html\.dark \.bg-<colour>-50" src/app/globals.css`; if it misses, either pick a remapped colour or add the one-line remap in the same commit. Green and red stay semantic (good / error), so a decorative palette that needs several hues draws from purple / blue / orange / yellow / indigo. `SALES_FUNNELS` in `lib/sales-funnels.ts` carries its tones as whole class strings for the same reason Tailwind always requires (a class assembled at runtime is invisible to the compiler), and `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts` pins every tone to an existing `html.dark` rule. (#3146)
- **TWO icon sets, and the pick is by ROLE, not by habit: Heroicons for CONTROLS, Phosphor duotone for a SUBJECT.** `@heroicons/react` is the default and stays the default for everything that is an affordance — a chevron, a pencil, a close, a check, a nav glyph. It is single-weight by design, and inside a colored tile that reads flat: a thin stroke floating in an empty square. `@phosphor-icons/react` (9,000+ marks, 6 weights) is there for the other case, where the icon REPRESENTS the thing rather than the action on it — a funnel, an entity type, a card's subject. Use `weight="duotone"`: it lays a tinted fill under the stroke in the same `currentColor`, so a single `text-<tone>-600` drives both layers and the mark fills its tile. **Import per-icon from `@phosphor-icons/react/dist/csr/<Name>`** — the package root is a ~190KB barrel — and take the `<Name>Icon` export (the bare `<Name>` is deprecated). Reference: the four `SALES_FUNNELS` marks. When a redesign brief is specifically ABOUT the visual (Kevin 2026-07-31: *"les icones sont trop simplistes, cherche une lib plus avancée"*), "reuse the existing primitive" does not settle it — an icon set is a palette, not a behaviour, and the reuse rule protects behaviour primitives (`InfoTooltip`, `BrandLogo`, `ScoreCard`), not the choice of mark. (#3164)
- Caveat surfaced to the user: a user who previously toggled has `theme=light` stored → default change won't reach them until they toggle / clear the `theme` localStorage key. (#1631, #1635)

## Dashboard "size" requests = type scale (text-only), not zoom/spacing

When the user says "shrink / reduce the dashboard by N sizes", "size" means the **type scale** (text), tuned text-only via the `--text-*` tokens in `apps/dashboard/src/app/globals.css` — NOT root `font-size`, NOT `zoom`, NOT spacing/width/icons. Do not recommend a root-font-size or `zoom` "overall scale" lever; it gets corrected to text-only (incident #1305: recommended root-font-size zoom (A); user picked text-only (B) — "Non je voulais dure text only"). Keep #1297's approach: redefine font-size tokens only (line-heights inherit Tailwind defaults), rem-based so browser-zoom a11y is preserved. "−1 size globally" = shift every `--text-*` token one step down. A region asked to shrink MORE than the rest (e.g. "sidebar −2, rest −1") gets one extra step via its OWN text classes (`text-sm`→`text-xs`→`text-[10px]`), leaving `py-*`/icon/`w-56` untouched → zero layout shift, existing reveal skeletons still match. No new test (brittle rem-pin guards fight future tuning); verify with `tsc --noEmit` + the dashboard suite + Vercel preview. (#1297 round 1, #1305 round 2.)

## Every Save on brand Settings is `SettingsSaveRow` — it hides until the form is edited, and it sits at the END of the row

`components/settings/settings-save-row.tsx` is the one Save row for the page. Four cards (`brand-daily-budget-card`, `brand-click-destination-card`, `brand-sales-economics-card`, `brand-domain-card`) each carried a hand-rolled copy, and all four had drifted the SAME way — which is the tell that the abstraction was missing, not that four authors were careless. Two rules it exists to hold:

- **Nothing renders until the live form differs from what was saved** (`if (!dirty && !saved) return null`). The old copies rendered the button always and greyed it out via `disabled={!dirty}`, so every card offered a control that was dead until the user touched the form. A greyed primary button reads as a thing to press.
- **The row is right-aligned** (`justify-end`). A single primary action belongs where the eye lands last, and every card has to agree or the page reads as several forms sharing a background. This holds by CONSTRUCTION rather than by convention: all four rows sit directly inside their card's `p-5` box, so they align to the same edge.

`Saved ✓` outlives the button it replaces — it reports an action the user just took — and clears on the next edit, so the row shows while `dirty || saved` and the button only while `dirty`.

Notes for a new settings card:
- **Use the row; do NOT hand-roll a fifth copy.** `tests/settings-save-row.test.ts` fails if any of the four keeps a `{saving ? "Saving..." : "Save"}` button or the old `mt-5 flex items-center gap-3` row — extend that list when you add a card.
- **Your card's own gate goes through the `disabled` prop**, not a second condition around the row: the click destination stays locked until the brand has a domain (`disabled={domainLocked}`).
- **"Edited" is whatever your card can honestly compute.** `brand-domain-card` is a one-time setup on a brand with NO website, so its field starts empty by construction and a typed URL IS the edit (`dirty={value.trim().length > 0}`) — no new state. The others already track `dirty` against a saved baseline; keep it a LIVE compare, never a sticky boolean latch (see the Save/dirty-gate rule).
- **Accepted consequence:** the card grows by one row on the first keystroke. Reserving the height would cancel the effect the row exists for.
- **`apps/admin` keeps its own copies of three of these cards** and is deliberately NOT synced — it is the staff fork, and its tests are not a CI gate. If you do port this there, port the guard with it. (#3174)

## An info tip is ALWAYS `InfoTooltip` — a native `title=` attribute is a dead affordance on a phone

`InfoTooltip` (`components/visibility/metric-info.tsx`, ~20 call sites) is the dashboard's only (i). **Never hand-roll a `title=` for explanatory copy.** A native `title` waits ~1s, cannot be styled, and shows NOTHING on a touch device — a finger produces no hover — so on mobile the user gets only the `cursor-help` `?` and concludes the control is broken. That is exactly how it was reported (2026-07-30: *"quand je hover le (i) ça n'affiche pas de tip box, seulement un ?"*). Nine such tips existed across the leads timeline, both budget-tier pickers, best-model-card, the economics card and the strategy page; `best-model-card` was doubly dead, putting `title` on an `<svg>`, where most browsers render nothing even with a mouse.

Three things inside the primitive are load-bearing — each protects a whole class of call site, and the guards in `tests/info-tooltip-touch.test.ts` pin all three:

- **Hover opens ONLY for `pointerType === "mouse"`.** A tap emits `pointerdown → touchend → mouseenter → click`: the compatibility mouse events land BEFORE the click. So a bare `onMouseEnter` opens the bubble and `onClick` immediately toggles it shut — it flashes and vanishes, at EVERY call site (billing, audiences, strategy, sidebar, revenue). #2026 was titled "tap-accessible tooltips"; this ordering is what silently defeated it, and it survived undetected for months because the desktop path looked fine. Proven empirically with a Playwright `devices['Pixel 7']` tap: event order `mouseenter, click`, old bubble hidden after tap, guarded bubble visible.
- **The trigger is a `<span role="button">`, never a `<button>`.** It renders INSIDE clickable cards (the budget tiers in `onboarding.tsx` and `brand-status-control.tsx` are `<button>`s) and a nested button is invalid HTML: the parser closes the outer one early and the card breaks. `tabIndex` + `aria-expanded` + Enter/Space keep it operable. Its `onClick` also stops propagation, so tapping the (i) explains a number without also selecting that card.
- **Hit area padded to ~26px** (`-m-1.5 p-1.5`) around the unchanged 14px icon, per WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8's 24×24 minimum. The negative margin keeps surrounding layout put.

**A `title=` that is NOT an info tip stays a `title=`**: an exact timestamp behind a short date, a full string behind a truncation, a `Copied` confirmation on a button that already shows a check. Those carry no information the user needs, so a phone losing them loses nothing. Two known gaps deliberately left: `api-keys/page.tsx` explains a DISABLED button's reason via `title` (the right fix is rendering the reason as text, not bolting an icon onto a pill), and `theme-toggle.tsx` has no `aria-label` at all, so its button is unannounced to screen readers.

`InfoTooltip` and `ScoreCard` both still live under `components/visibility/` although those routes were deleted in #2196 — a misleading home, not dead code; both have live consumers. (#3103)

## A rendered behaviour is verified by REPRODUCING it, never by grepping the source that implements it

The repo already says this for CSS (verify a landing fix by the computed value, not by grepping the stylesheet). The interaction analogue bit twice in one day: a source-substring guard proves a handler is spelled a certain way, and says nothing about what a real tap does. When the bug IS an interaction (tap, hover, focus, drag, key), reproduce the **event sequence** headlessly — Playwright is a devDependency of `apps/dashboard` and `apps/admin`:

```js
const ctx = await b.newContext({ ...devices['Pixel 7'] });  // hasTouch + isMobile
await p.locator('#icon').tap();
await p.evaluate(() => window.__seq);   // ["mouseenter","click"] ← the whole diagnosis
```

Put BOTH the old and the new handler shape on one throwaway page and assert they differ — that proves the fix rather than restating it. The dashboard's own preview URLs are behind Vercel deployment-protection AND Clerk, so a preview tap is not reachable; a minimal `file://` page exercising the same handler contract is, and it is enough when the claim is about event ORDER rather than about app state. There is no jsdom / testing-library in `apps/dashboard`, so do not reach for a component test without adding deps.

**Mechanics that cost two failed runs whenever they are re-derived:** the specifier is `@playwright/test`, NOT `playwright` (the bare package sits in the pnpm store but is linked into no app), and the script must live INSIDE `apps/dashboard` or `apps/admin` — a file left in `/tmp` resolves against `/tmp` and dies `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. `apps/landing` does not have Playwright at all, so a LANDING check runs from the dashboard's directory against the landing URL. Write the script to `/tmp`, `cp` it into the app, run, delete. **A hand-built page with NO `<meta name="viewport">` lays out at 980px CSS on a mobile context, so every `sm:`/`md:` rule matches and the "390px" measurement is a desktop measurement wearing a phone's clothes** — the tell is a measured element sitting at an x far beyond the viewport width you passed (a `right-4` FAB reading `x=908` at 390px). Put the meta tag in the throwaway HTML, and sanity-check that a right-pinned element's `right` equals your viewport width before trusting any number. **Assert an animated value programmatically BEFORE screenshotting it** — a count-up caught mid-flight photographs a number that is true for 200ms and wrong forever, so the screenshot is the illustration and `expect(count).toBe(target)` is the proof. **The same trap ruins a GEOMETRY measurement, and there it reads as a bug in the code you just wrote**: recharts animates bars in, so measuring rect heights the moment the first bar appears returns a fraction of the true value and looks exactly like an axis scaled to the wrong domain — wait ~2s (or set `isAnimationActive={false}`) and re-measure before diagnosing anything. And `apps/landing`'s own vitest suite resolves workspace deps through `dist/`, so `pnpm --filter @distribute/content build` first or every `static-html` import fails to resolve.

**A throwaway page carrying REAL Tailwind class strings needs those classes COMPILED, and there is no `tailwindcss` binary in this repo — build the CSS through `@tailwindcss/postcss` in the probe script itself.** Hand-writing equivalent CSS re-implements the thing you are trying to verify, and the Play CDN is a different build than the one the app ships. Two resolution traps, one failed run each: `postcss` is not a direct dependency of either app, so import it from the store (`../../node_modules/.pnpm/postcss@<v>/node_modules/postcss/lib/postcss.mjs`); and the postcss ENTRY file must sit inside `apps/<app>` or `@import "tailwindcss"` fails with `Can't resolve 'tailwindcss' in /var/folders/...` — the resolver works from the entry's own directory, not from `process.cwd()`. Keep the probe HTML in `/tmp` and point at it with an absolute `@source`:

```js
const css = `@import "tailwindcss" source(none);\n@source "${htmlPathInTmp}";\n`;
writeFileSync(entryInsideApp, css);
const built = await postcss([tailwind()]).process(css, { from: entryInsideApp, to: outCssInTmp });
```

Then measure with `scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth` on the wrapper and `getComputedStyle(cell).display` per column, rendering the OLD markup on the SAME page so the numbers prove the fix rather than restate it (`md:min-w-[900px]` measured 358/358 at 360px against the previous markup's 1068/358). Delete the probe and its entry file before committing.

**A PERCENTAGE max-height (`max-h-full`) pins nothing when the parent's own height is indefinite — say the cap in VIEWPORT units, and check which way the overflow went before believing the class took.** This is the sibling of the min-height rule above (a `min-h` alone lets the column grow past the viewport): here the class is a `max-h`, it reads as obviously correct, and it applies to NOTHING. `max-height: 100%` resolves against the parent's computed height, and a `flex-none` item inside an `items-center` row has no definite height to resolve against, so the cap silently does not exist. The failure is worse than no cap: an `overflow-hidden` ancestor then clips the card at BOTH ends and centres what is left, so the step's own header lands at a NEGATIVE top and the content the user needs is off the top of the screen as well as the bottom. `calc(100svh - <the chrome above it>)` is definite by construction and cannot be defeated this way.

Diagnostic tell, and it is what makes this cheap to catch: measure the header's `top`, not only the CTA's `bottom`. A CTA below the fold is consistent with "no cap" AND with "cap applied, content too tall"; a NEGATIVE header top can only mean the element overflowed a clipping ancestor. Cost 2026-08-24 (#3502, onboarding desktop CTA): `sm:max-h-full` on the step card measured CTA bottom 996 on an 800px viewport with the header at **-179px**; the same markup with `sm:max-h-[calc(100svh-8rem)]` measured 757/60 and a short step kept its natural 310px height. Both numbers came from the compiled-Tailwind probe above — the class-string guard was green for the broken version.

## A hover `filter` re-shuffles the paint order — an overlapping badge on a `brightness`/`blur`/`drop-shadow` element needs an explicit `z-index`

A non-`none` CSS `filter` makes an element its own stacking context, which promotes it out of the ordinary flow layer and into the one positioned elements paint in. So a control that is static at rest and filtered on hover CHANGES paint order under the cursor: an absolutely-positioned sibling that sits on top at rest gets covered on hover, if the filtered element comes later in DOM order and neither carries a `z-index`. The bug reads as a hover glitch rather than a layout bug, which is why it survives review — at rest the screenshot is perfect.

The dashboard hits this wherever a small pill overlaps the top edge of a control: `LastUsedBadge` (`components/auth/last-used-badge.tsx`) over the `Continue with Google` buttons on `/sign-in` and `/sign-up`, both of which dim with `hover:brightness-[0.97]`. The fix is one `zIndex: 1` on the pill (same stacking context, so it beats the filter-induced 0), NOT removing the dim and NOT restructuring the markup. `transform`, `opacity < 1`, `will-change`, `backdrop-filter` and `contain: paint` all create a stacking context the same way, so the rule generalises: **when a hover state adds any of those to an element that something overlaps, give the overlapping thing a `z-index` in the same commit.**

Verify by reproducing, per the rule above — `document.elementFromPoint(x, y)` at a coordinate inside the overlap strip, read at rest and again after `page.hover()`, with the old and new markup side by side on one throwaway page. A class grep proves nothing about paint order. Guard: `tests/last-used-badge-stacking.test.ts`. (#3137)

## The support FAB occludes a BAND, not a corner — clearance goes on the axis it is PINNED to, and a bottom inset only fixes the scroll-end case

`SupportButton` (`components/support/support-button.tsx`) is mounted once on the dashboard shell as `fixed right-4 bottom-4` at `z-30`, so it covers the rightmost **64px (72px on `sm+`)** of the viewport at EVERY scroll position and paints above page content. A control pinned to the right edge of any page is therefore tappable only by luck — the leads pagination row is `justify-between`, which put `Next` flush against that edge.

The trap is that the report ("Next is hidden behind the WhatsApp button") reads as a bottom-corner problem, and a bottom inset makes it go away in the one state you check. #2764 fixed exactly this report with `pb-24` on the leads scroll column; that clears the FAB only when the column is scrolled to its end, so the same bug was re-reported 13 days later. **A right-pinned overlay needs RIGHT padding; a bottom-pinned bar needs bottom padding. Match the axis, and if the overlay is pinned on both, pad both.**

- **`SUPPORT_FAB_CLEARANCE`** (`pr-20`, exported from the FAB's own file) is the one home for the number, so it stays coupled to the button's size instead of being a literal copied per call site. 80px covers the 72px worst case AND leaves a gap — parking a control flush against the FAB (a bare 64px on mobile) still loses a fat-finger tap on its right edge.
- Padding, never `z-index` (the FAB must stay on top to be reachable) and never hiding the FAB on one page (support access is the point).
- **A gutter can WRAP the row it protects — measure the fit, don't assume.** 80px at 390px left the leads pagination row needing exactly the width it had, so `Page X of Y` wrapped to a second line; it is now `hidden sm:inline` (the range on the left already states the position). Measured 235px needed vs 294px available after hiding it.
- Only ONE pagination surface exists in the dashboard (`table-pagination.tsx` went with the conversions cluster), but the FAB is global — any NEW right-edge control reuses the constant rather than re-deriving a number.
- Verified by reproducing per the rule above: `document.elementFromPoint` at the centre of `Next` returned the FAB at 390 / 768 / 1280px on the old markup and the button on the new. Guard: `tests/leads-pagination-fab-clearance.test.ts`. (#2764 partial, #3158 complete)

## Link styling is a promise to NAVIGATE — a copy-to-clipboard value is plain text plus a glyph, never blue and underlined

`text-brand-600` + `hover:underline` on a value tells the user that clicking it goes somewhere. On an email address it reads specifically as a `mailto:`. So when the click actually copies to the clipboard, the affordance lies about what the control does, and the user learns that only by clicking it. Reported verbatim on the Leads right panel (2026-07-30): "tu l'as mis en bleu souligné quand on hover, ce qui fait penser que c'est un lien qui va ouvrir le mailto, alors que pas du tout, c'est juste pour copier".

The convergent pattern across Stripe's dashboard, [PatternFly](https://www.patternfly.org/components/clipboard-copy/design-guidelines) and [Shoelace](https://shoelace.style/components/copy-button) keeps the **value unstyled** and puts the whole affordance on a dedicated control beside it:

- value in the same `text-gray-800 font-medium` as every other panel value, no underline, no brand color
- a persistent copy glyph beside it, `text-gray-300` → `group-hover:text-gray-500` (a hover-only glyph is discoverable only by accident)
- a hover surface over the entire hit area (`-mx-1 px-1 py-0.5 rounded hover:bg-gray-100`) so the clickable region is visible
- `title` reads `Copy`, becomes `Copied` once it lands; a check + "Copied" for ~1.5s confirms
- `aria-label` names the action, plus a `focus-visible` ring — it is a button, not a link

Reference impl: `CopyableEmail` in `components/audiences/engaged-leads-page.tsx`. The other copy sites (api-keys, `brand-conversion-tracking-card`, `edit-with-ai-chat`, `context-sidebar`, onboarding) were already icon buttons — this was the only link-styled one. Guard: `tests/lead-panel-audience-and-copy.test.ts`, scoped to the function body because `text-brand-600` and `hover:underline` are legitimate elsewhere in the same file. Generalise it: a control that does not change the URL does not get link styling. (#3085)

**Same panel, the inverse rule — a card that summarises another PAGE's entity links there instead of restating its numbers.** The Leads right-panel "Audience" card printed Size and Remaining, duplicating figures the Audiences page owns while never showing the targeting filters, which is what a reader of that card actually wants. Both were dropped for a `?audienceId=` link — the deep-link seed `CustomerAudiencesPage` reads on first paint (`customer-audiences-page.tsx:198`, the same mechanism the brand-overview Top-3-audiences card uses), so the audience's detail panel opens with its colored targeting tags. Check for an existing deep-link seed before building a second summary surface. (#3085)

**Third rule from the same card, and it is the general one: a link to an ENTITY is built from where THAT ENTITY lives, never from the route the reader happens to be standing on.** `AudienceSection` assembled its href as `tenantBasePath(orgId, brandId, useParams().offerId)`, which reads as obviously correct and is a coin flip: it produces the right URL on any route that happens to carry the same depth as the target, and a nonexistent one everywhere else. Audiences moved down to the offer, so at brand level there is no offer segment to insert and the card's single affordance pointed at `/orgs/:orgId/brands/:brandId/audiences` — **a 404 on the brand Leads page for months**. The offer and campaign routes both carry an offer, so the link worked from either, which is exactly why nobody saw it: the two surfaces you would check to confirm the fix are the two that were never broken.

- **Read the entity's own scope off the wire.** `audience.offerId` (human-service serves it required + nullable) states which offer that audience belongs to; the route's offer stays only as the fallback for a lookup miss, so nothing regresses where the link already worked.
- **Unresolvable scope ⟹ NO link**, never one that 404s. 51 of 819 prod audiences are filed under no offer (rows predating the offer level) and have no page to open; the card states the audience and stops. Same render while the lookup is in flight — nothing is claimed before it is known.
- **The reason the correct source was unreachable is the too-narrow-schema bug one layer down**: `offerId` was on every audience response and `AudienceSchema` never declared it, so Zod stripped it at the parse boundary. Declared REQUIRED here, matching the producer — `.optional()` would read `undefined` forever and every link would quietly vanish, which is indistinguishable from "this audience has no offer".
- Generalise the diagnostic: whenever a hierarchy gains or loses a level, `grep` every caller of the path builder (`tenantBasePath`) and ask what each one does when the new segment is ABSENT. `tsc` cannot see it, the guard tests pinned the old form, and the broken surface is silent until someone clicks. Guard: `tests/lead-panel-audience-and-copy.test.ts`. (#3514)

## A status label states what HAPPENED, never what we ATTEMPTED — `contacted` is a QUEUE state and reads "Queued" on the customer dashboard

lead-service's `contacted` flag means the lead was handed to Instantly, NOT that anyone was emailed. Instantly's campaign schedule (instantly-service `src/lib/instantly-client.ts`, `timing 08:00-17:00`, `days` Mon-Fri, `timezone` = the recipient's when known, `America/Chicago` otherwise) holds the lead until its next weekday window, and instantly-service's own retry sweep waits **72 hours** before calling such a row stuck precisely because that queue legitimately lasts that long. So the gap between `firstContactedAt` and `firstSentAt` is routinely days, and every surface that printed "Contacted" during it asserted something false. Reported by a customer who had just been told we only send during business hours (we do, in Instantly) and read the dashboard as contradicting it.

- **`leadStatusLabel("contacted")` → `Queued`**, slate not teal (a wait, not a step cleared). The badge, the table Status column and the CSV `Status` column all read through it, so the word is defined once. (The `conversions-table` chip that also read it went with the retired conversion tabs.)
- **The timeline is GROUPED BY MESSAGE, because a bare `Sent` does not say sent WHAT and a lead receives several messages.** Each message (`kind: "message"`) is a demarcated, openable card — tint plus a full 1px border, per the repo's no-side-accent rule — and its own delivery rows sit INSIDE it. Reported as "c'est gênant que tu mettes juste Sent… plutôt une section colorée/démarquée pour l'initial email, avec dessous Sent, Delivered etc associés". The whole arc is worth keeping in view: `Initial email` was first a row pushed at `firstSentAt ?? email.createdAt` (so with nothing sent it read as a PAST `Initial email · <generation date>`); anchoring it at `sentAt` then printed one moment twice under two labels (`Initial email · Jul 30` directly above `Sent · Jul 30`); folding it into the `Sent` row fixed that and produced the bare `Sent`. Grouping is what satisfies all three at once — the message names itself, its rows carry the instants, and no instant is printed twice.
- **What nests is bounded by what the wire can PROVE, and the boundary is load-bearing.** lead-service forwards LEAD-level first-occurrence timestamps, not per-step ones. So `Sent` / `Delivered` nest under `Initial email` (the first send IS the initial email), while `Website visit` / `Replied` / `Bounced` / `Unsubscribed` stay TOP-LEVEL: a reply can land after follow-up 2, so filing it under the initial email would state something we never observed, and their position in the chronology already says which send preceded them. **Follow-up cards carry NO delivery rows at all** — there is no per-step delivery data, and inventing one is the "never work around missing backend data" trap; that gap is a lead-service / email-gateway feature request. `Delivered` is dropped when absent rather than rendered empty.
- **A message card prints NO date of its own when it has delivery rows** — its instant IS its `Sent` row's, and stating both is the duplication that caused the report. It still SORTS at that instant, so the chronology is unchanged. A scheduled follow-up has no rows yet, so it does state its projected date.
- **Row spacing keys on the INDEX (`i < sorted.length - 1 ? "pb-4" : ""`), never a `last:` modifier — and this is a general Tailwind trap, not a timeline detail.** `last:` compiles to `&:last-child`, which resolves against the element's PARENT. The timeline's content div is the second and FINAL child of its own `<li>` on every row, so `pb-4 last:pb-0` matched everywhere and the padding never applied at all: consecutive follow-up cards rendered edge to edge, and only the rows adjacent to the "Now" divider looked spaced (that divider carries its own `py-2`). The tell that you are in this trap is a `last:`/`first:`/`only:` modifier on an element that is NOT the repeated sibling — here the repeated sibling is the `<li>`, one level up. Verified by reproduction rather than by reading the class (Playwright, two rows, old markup computed `padding-bottom: 0px` on the FIRST row; index-keyed markup computed `16px`). It shares the connector line's exact condition so the line and the gap cannot disagree about which row is last. Guard: `lead-timeline-queued.test.ts`.
- **The queue row is now the initial card's only delivery row while `firstSentAt` is absent** (`initialEvents` is a ternary on `queuedOnly`, so `Queued for sending` and `Sent` can never both be on screen), and it still carries `SEND_WINDOW_NOTE` on an `ⓘ`.
- **`servedAt` gets no footer.** A `Served: <locale string>` line sat under the timeline in a different date format than every row above it, naming an internal pipeline step. The one place it is worth showing is the leads-table Status/Date pair while the lead still reads `Processing` (`leadDateForStatus`).
- **Timeline instants read `Today at 8:45am` / `Yesterday at 10:45pm` / `Jul 28, 2026 at 4:23pm`** (`lib/friendly-datetime.ts`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests; `now` is injectable for the day-boundary cases). Three things there are deliberate: the day is a LOCAL-midnight diff, not a 24-hour one, so 11pm yesterday still reads `Yesterday`; the time is assembled by hand rather than via `toLocaleTimeString`, which separates the meridiem with U+202F in current ICU so the obvious `.replace(" ", "")` leaves a stray gap; and there is **no `Tomorrow`** — a FUTURE instant gets its plain calendar date with NO clock time (`isFuture ? friendlyDate : friendlyDateTime`), because Instantly sends inside a weekday 8am-5pm window, so both an exact minute and a firm day name would be invented (owner-picked: "pour le future on ne met pas l'heure"). The `title` keeps the full `toLocaleString` on hover.
- **A timestamp DERIVED from a generation time is stated as an offset, never as a date.** Unsent follow-ups carry `estimate: "~Nd after the first email"` (from `deriveEmailRows(email, anchor, estimated)`) and print it in place of the absolute date, which would otherwise drift by however long the queue lasts. The `title` attribute is dropped on those rows too — no precise instant to claim.
- **`forceContacted` is gone.** It painted the queue badge on every row of the Outreach tab, so a lead we really had sent read as waiting in the table while its own detail panel said `Sent` — an internally-incoherent surface, which is a bug, not a simplification. Every row states its own most-advanced status on every tab. The tab's MEMBERSHIP is unchanged (`if (lead.contacted) outreach.push(lead)`): "Outreach" deliberately covers queued + sent + bounced + unsubscribed.
- **The leads CSV uses the dashboard's words, not the wire's.** `contacted` IS the Outreach tab's predicate and `clicked` the Website-Visits one, so the columns are `Outreach` / `First outreach at` and `Website visit` / `First website visit at`. `Replied` keeps its name: the flag includes negative replies, so the Positive-replies tab is a SUBSET of it and renaming the column would overstate it.
- **`apps/admin` keeps `Contacted` in all four of its copies** — the staff console shows the raw technical state, and re-syncing it to the customer vocabulary would hide the distinction staff need.

Guard: `apps/dashboard/tests/lead-timeline-queued.test.ts` (source-substring, scoped per function — `Contacted`/`Clicked` stay legitimate as wire field names and in admin). Generalise it: before naming a status, ask whether the value proves the event or only proves we tried. (#3089)

**Same split one layer down, on the WIRE: `apps/admin`'s leads reads send `status=all`, the dashboard's send nothing — and which app asks for which population is a rendering fact, not a preference.** lead-service's leads list defaults to the ACTIONABLE population (`buffered,claimed,served`); `skipped` must be asked for by name (comma-separated list, or `all`; an unrecognised value is a 400). The dashboard buckets purely on ENGAGEMENT evidence (`coveredLeads`), so a lead that was never served and carries no delivery evidence cannot appear under any of its tabs — for one prod brand that was **53,760 skipped rows out of 53,777**, ~82% of a body polled every 30 seconds, and features-service scores them at zero. Not sending them is the whole point, so **do NOT filter client-side and do NOT add a status param to a dashboard read**. `apps/admin` is the one app that renders them (Skipped / Buffered / Claimed / Served tabs on its brand, feature and campaign leads views, plus the public report's own Skipped state), so all four of its reads carry the parameter — `ALL_LEAD_STATUSES` in `lib/api.ts` names it once, and `report-api.ts` spells it out rather than importing, because the report tree stays free of the Clerk-authed api client. Ordering is consumer-first by construction: a lead-service that ignores the parameter makes this a no-op, while producer-first empties the staff tabs. The api-service `/v1/leads` proxy forwards the caller's query string VERBATIM off `req.originalUrl` (api-service #826, prod `8bb3e00d`), so a new lead-service filter needs no gateway PR — unlike `/revenue`, whose whitelist is a fixed list and still misses `funnel`. ⚠️ **Its OpenAPI `parameters` block still enumerates a closed list (`brandId, campaignId, limit, offset, view`), so the prod api-registry reads as if `status` were stripped when the code forwards it.** That doc is a description of a passthrough, never its contract — a registry param list is only authoritative for a route that re-declares its query, and reading it as one cost a false "blocked on the gateway" line in #3411's own PR body. Check the route's code, or the deployed sha, before calling a passthrough param unforwarded. Guard: `apps/admin/tests/leads-all-statuses.test.ts` pins the parameter on all four admin reads AND its absence on every dashboard one. (#3411)

**Same row, the date half: a status and a date printed side by side are read as ONE statement, so they must describe the SAME event — the leads table's Date column is per-TAB and the badge is per-LEAD, which is not the same event on any tab but "Positive replies".** The Outreach tab lists every contacted lead and dated all of them at `firstContactedAt`, while each row's badge states its own most-advanced state — so a lead that had replied read `Replied` beside the day we handed it to Instantly, and a reader has no way to know the two cells are about different moments. Neither number is wrong on its own, which is why this survived the #3089 pass that fixed the words.

- **`leadDateForStatus(lead, status)` (`lib/api.ts`, beside `getLeadConsolidatedStatus`) is the one map from a badge to the timestamp that PROVES it** — `firstRepliedAt` / `firstClickedAt` / `firstDeliveredAt` / `firstSentAt` / `firstBouncedAt` / `firstUnsubscribedAt` / `firstContactedAt` / `servedAt`. It is an exhaustive `switch` over `LeadConsolidatedStatus`, so a new status cannot ship without deciding which timestamp proves it, and it lives in `api.ts` (alias-free) so it gets real unit tests rather than a source-substring guard.
- **The fix is the Date column's VALUE — `leadDateForTab` is deleted, not demoted.** The row already has exactly one place for a date; the bug was which timestamp went in it. Adding a SECOND date under the badge and leaving the column on the tab's event is the wrong shape: it puts two differently-sourced dates on one row and asks the reader to reconcile them, which is the original complaint with an extra number. Ship #3152 did that and was reverted in the same evening ("je t'ai demandé de changer la valeur de la date dans la colonne date"). One row, one date, sourced from the row's own status.
- **`skipped` / `claimed` / `buffered` carry no timestamp on the wire → `null` → the cell renders its `-`.** A column must hold its cell shape, so the dash stays here (unlike a value hanging under a badge, where a dash reads as a date we looked for and found empty).
- **The sort key moves WITH the column** (`sortByStatusDate`, replacing `sortByTabDate`): a column that displays one field and orders by another reads as unordered. The tabs differ in MEMBERSHIP, never in what a date means, so all three engagement tabs share the one comparator. It reads the UNLATCHED `getLeadConsolidatedStatus` because it decides row ORDER, while `useMonotonicStatuses` exists to keep a rendered BUCKET from bouncing on a poll.
- **The outcome tabs are the one exception**: their date is the realized-outcome instant from the `/revenue` join, because a signup has no delivery status to date. That is a different column meaning, deliberately.
- Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/lead-status-date.test.ts` (real unit tests on the map + source-substring on the cell and the comparator). Generalise it: two values printed together are one statement — before putting a number next to a label, check they are about the same event, and fix it by correcting the number that is there, not by adding another one.

## Currency display = adaptive by magnitude (`<$10` → cents, `≥$10` → whole), EXCEPT billing

Every USD amount on the **user dashboard** formats by magnitude: `abs(usd) < 10` → 2 decimals (`$4.27`), `≥ $10` → 0 decimals rounded, thousand-sep (`$1,240`). Canonical helpers `formatUsdAdaptive(usd)` / `formatCentsAsUsdAdaptive(cents)` in `lib/format-number.ts`; `lib/format-stat.ts` currency branch + every live per-component currency formatter (revenue cards, strategy CPC/cost tiles, audiences, brand-status unit-cost, brand-info) implement the same `Math.abs(usd) < 10 ? 2 : 0` inline. **Billing is deliberately EXEMPT** — `billing/page.tsx` + `lib/billing-guard.tsx` keep the explicit-decimal `formatUsd`/`formatCentsAsUsd(…,2|0)`/`formatBillingCents` (exact charged amounts must never round). **DAILY BUDGET is a further carve-out — it ALWAYS renders as WHOLE dollars (no cents at all, not even under $10), a daily budget being an exact whole-dollar config value where cents read wrong (`$7.50/day` → `$8/day`).** Whole-budget surfaces: `brand-status-control` (`fmtUsdWhole` — the status pill's `budgetLabel`; the unit-cost-per-outcome beside it stays adaptive), `revenue-cost-summary` (`formatBudgetCents` — the `/$budget` denominator), `billing-guard` (the "Brand daily budget cap / day" line only; its "Required" credit amount stays exact), onboarding + the Sales Funnels card (the per-funnel `$ /day` fields, integer-only input). A new dashboard currency site → use the adaptive helper (or the inline `Math.abs(usd) < 10 ? 2 : 0`), NOT a hardcoded `minimumFractionDigits: 2`; a new DAILY-BUDGET display → render whole (`$${Math.round(usd).toLocaleString("en-US")}`). (#2593, daily-budget-whole #2596)

## Release flow (distribute.you specifics)

No `release.sh hotfix` here (that targets the backend semver services). The Hetzner box pulls `main` and rebuilds, unattended, within about 7 minutes of a merge — no tag, no bump, and nothing to promote.

**⚠️ The BACKEND fleet no longer deploys from a merge the way Railway used to — it is pulled onto the Hetzner box, and for a day after the migration nothing pulled at all.** Railway's git integration WAS the deploy path; when it was deleted (2026-08-08) nothing replaced it, so `release.sh` kept tagging, CI kept greening, PRs kept merging, and production kept running older code — two real fixes sat merged-and-unshipped, one of them customer-blocking. **So a merged backend PR is NOT in production until the box has rebuilt it** — the deploy-landed discriminator is behaviour, never the PR state.

How it works now (`/root/distribute` on `167.233.196.79`, SSH key `~/.ssh/oracle-distribute`, user `root`): every service has its OWN git clone under `repos/<service>` and `docker compose` builds from that directory (`build: ./repos/<service>`), so deploying is fetch → `reset --hard origin/main` → `compose build` → `up -d`. Postgres is a container on the box (`distribute-postgres-1`), and Windmill's server + worker are too — Neon and Railway are both out of the backend path.

- **`./deploy.sh <service> | --all | --status`** does that, plus two things: it asks the service `/health` from INSIDE the container (Node 18 `fetch`, so nothing to install and no dependency on Cloudflare being healthy) and **returns the clone to its previous commit and rebuilds when that check fails**. A build failure never touches the running container.
- **`deploy-cron.sh` runs `--all` every 5 minutes** (`flock`-guarded, logs to `deploy.log`). Deliberately a PULL from the box, not a push from CI: a CI-triggered deploy needs this machine's key in GitHub secrets, which is a root shell on all of production for every workflow run, and it needs a workflow file in each of 23 repos. Nothing leaves the box this way and a new service is covered for free.
- **`deploy-admin.sh` is a ONE-LINE WRAPPER around `./deploy.sh admin-app --force`, so the staff console gets the same health check and the same rollback as every service.** It kept the name because that is what the runbooks say. It used to be a separate implementation, on the belief that admin could not be health-checked without widening its staff-email allowlist; `proxy.ts` already lists `/api/public(.*)` as public, so only the health route was missing (#3314). Being separate was the real hazard: all three Next apps build from ONE clone, and two scripts that each `git reset --hard` it can pull the tree out from under the other's build. One deployer, one lock, one clone. `deploy.sh` writes `env/admin-app.build.env` into the clone as `.env.production` before building, which is how the build-time `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` still get inlined. **⚠️ The CADENCE claim that used to sit here — "admin is NOT on `deploy-cron.sh`, so a merge alone changes nothing" — is WRONG and was repeated into three PR bodies before anyone checked.** `deploy.sh` has `APPS=(landing-app dashboard-app admin-app)` and deploys them as ONE group (they share a clone, which is exactly why), `--all` calls `deploy_apps`, and `*/5 * * * * /root/distribute/deploy-cron.sh` runs `--all`. So **a merge to `main` reaches `admin.distribute.you` within ~5 minutes on its own**, health-checked with rollback, like every other service. `--force` is only needed when a build-time `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` changed with no commit to trigger a rebuild. Verified 2026-08-15 by reading `deploy.sh` + the crontab on the box and watching `admin-app: healthy on 0ca97c5a` land unattended. **Generalise: a cadence/ownership claim in this file is a point-in-time assertion like any other — confirm it against the box before repeating it to the user, especially when you are about to tell them a manual step is required.** Telling someone to run a deploy that already ran is the same class of error as reporting a healthy deploy as stuck.
- **Waiting for YOUR fix to land: match the MERGE commit or a content marker, never the sha your PR branch carried.** The box does `reset --hard origin/main`, so its clone HEAD is the merge commit; an `until … rev-parse HEAD | grep <branch-sha>` never fires and reads as a stalled deploy on a deploy that already succeeded.
- **A CONTENT marker must be a code construct, not a string your own explanatory comment can contain — this is the source-substring-guard trap wearing a deploy hat, and it lies in the SCARIEST direction: it reports a healthy deploy as stuck.** Verifying "did my change ship?" by grepping the deployed file for the literal you REMOVED fails the moment your commit explains the removal, because a good comment names the thing it retired. Grep for the construct instead — the `WHERE` clause, the function signature, the absent parameter — or read `deploy.log` for `<service>: healthy on <sha>`, which is unambiguous and free. Cost 2026-08-13 (brand-service v0.68.0): a `grep -c 'backfilled_from_goal IS NOT NULL'` returned 1 against a container running the new code, because the new code's doc comment says *"The first run also required `backfilled_from_goal IS NOT NULL`"*. Reported to the user as a silently-stuck deploy, then retracted; the deploy had been healthy for 40 minutes. The same trap bit three more times the same day on ordinary test guards, so treat any `not.toContain(<retired literal>)` as needing its own source comment reworded in the SAME commit.
- **Confirm the deploy LANDED before probing its outcome, or you will report your own change as ineffective.** A prod probe run between the merge and the box's next 5-minute tick returns the OLD behaviour, which reads exactly like "the fix does not work" and sends you off diagnosing shipped-and-correct code. Gate the probe on `grep '<service>' deploy.log | tail -1` showing `healthy on <merge-sha>`. Cost 2026-08-13 (features-service v0.126.0): probed ~2 minutes early, saw the pre-change pipeline unchanged, and started reading the merged diff for a bug that was not there; the numbers were exactly right one tick later.
- **`--force` exists ONLY for the three Next apps, never for a backend service.** `deploy_apps` parses it (`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is inlined at build, so an env change needs a rebuild no commit will trigger); `deploy_one` does not, and short-circuits on `already on <sha>, nothing to do` by comparing the CLONE's sha — not the running image's. So a backend service whose clone advanced without a successful rebuild cannot be pushed through by `deploy.sh` at all. If you ever genuinely land in that state, rebuild through compose directly (`docker compose build <svc> && docker compose up -d <svc>`) — but first make sure you are not looking at a false marker, per the rule above, because that state is rarer than a bad grep.
- Accepted trade: the build runs ON the box, competing with production for CPU, and a red `main` reaches that build (the health check + rollback is what bounds the damage, not the merge gate).

**NOTHING IS ON VERCEL ANY MORE — `landing` and `dashboard` cut over on 2026-08-09 and both projects are DELETED.** Both ship as containers (`apps/{landing,dashboard}/Dockerfile`, `output: "standalone"` with `outputFileTracingRoot` at the repo root), `deploy.sh` deploys them as one group off the shared `repos/distribute.you` clone, and the 5-minute cron picks them up like every service. `CUTOVER.md` on the box keeps the record ids and the undo, but the undo now restores DNS to projects that no longer exist — recovery is fixing the box, not going back. Verify what serves a hostname with the response header (`via: 1.1 Caddy`), never by which host you happen to know about. `docs` and `sales-cold-emails-landing` are STATIC (`output: "export"`) and served by **Cloudflare Pages** — built and uploaded by `.github/workflows/deploy-static-sites.yml`, which is free because the repo is public. Their `vercel.json` files are vestigial, kept like `apps/admin`'s in case anything is ever moved back. **A change to either one does NOT reach production through a Vercel deploy**, so do not wait for one, and do not verify it by reading `vercel ls`; watch the Actions run instead. `admin` is a container on the Hetzner box and rides the SAME 5-minute cron as landing and dashboard — `deploy.sh` lists all three in one `APPS` group off the shared clone, so a merge to `main` reaches `admin.distribute.you` unattended (this paragraph used to say a merge changed nothing there; it was wrong — see its own section). **every `mcpfactory.org` domain was removed on 2026-08-07** (retired brand), so do not re-add one and do not treat a `mcpfactory.org` 404 as a regression — its DNS still lives on a Gandi account outside the tokens in `~/.agents/skills/gandi-mail/secrets.env`, so the records dangle at Vercel until someone with that access clears them. Two things that bite when editing `docs`/`sales`: `sitemap.ts` and `robots.ts` need `export const dynamic = "force-static"` or the export FAILS the build, and anything needing a request-time API (a route handler, a DB read, `cookies()`) cannot go in them at all — which is exactly why `landing` and `dashboard` are containers rather than static exports.

**Three things a self-hosted Next build does NOT inherit from Vercel, each of which fails silently:**
- **`export const maxDuration` is a Vercel function limit and does nothing to a standalone server.** The `/api/v1` catch-all and the chat route legitimately run for minutes, so the only ceiling is now Caddy's, set per vhost (`read_timeout 310s`). The chat route also streams, so its vhost carries `flush_interval -1` — without it Caddy buffers the whole response and a token-by-token stream arrives as one silent wait then the entire answer.
- **`request.url` in a route handler is the address the process BINDS to, not the address the visitor typed** (`http://0.0.0.0:3000/...`). So `NextResponse.redirect(new URL("/somewhere", request.url))` — which reads as obviously correct and works perfectly on Vercel — emits a Location pointing at the container. `/how-it-works` shipped exactly that. Emit a **relative** Location instead (`new NextResponse(null, {status: 308, headers: {Location: "/#how"}})`); the browser resolves it against whatever host it asked, so no origin is needed at all. Middleware is NOT affected — `NextRequest` is host-aware there and already emits relative Locations.
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` and anything the build READS are two different lists.** The landing PRERENDERS `/blog` and `/sitemap.xml` out of `DATABASE_URL`, so a build without it produces a blog with zero articles and a sitemap missing 28 URLs — no error, no warning, just a smaller page. It reaches the build as a **BuildKit secret** (`--mount=type=secret`), never a build arg or a line in `.env.production`: both of those are recorded in the image, and it is a real credential rather than a publishable token.

**A deleted platform takes its secrets with it, and only the ones with a second source come back.** The cutover recovered every value it could from key-service, the box, the live bundle or the database; two had no second source at all (`PARTNERO_API_TOKEN`, `OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) and a human had to fetch them from the vendor. Generalise before retiring any platform: list what only IT holds and pull those out FIRST, while it still answers. A `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` value never qualifies — it is inlined in the live bundle, so it can be read off the served page rather than asked for.

**The `ignoreCommand` / `scripts/vercel-ignore.sh` machinery is HISTORICAL — there is no Vercel project left to run it.** It existed because all four projects rebuilt on every merge (2032 builds in 30 days for 494 that had anything to build) and it cut that by deciding from turbo's dependency graph, on the production branch only. The `vercel.json` files that still carry it are vestigial. Two things it leaves behind: **there is no preview URL** (verify locally, or on prod after merge), and its one durable trap is worth remembering if it is ever revived — Vercel inverts the shell convention (`exit 0` SKIPS, `exit 1` BUILDS), so getting it backwards silently stops every deploy. (#3287)

- **The generalisable lesson from `distribute-docs`: a note saying a deployment is broken has a shelf life.** It was documented as having an inverted ignore command whose production had "never" built; #3068 fixed that and it shipped READY deploys for a week while the note still read as current. Check the platform before acting on any claim in this file that something is already broken.

**Which host actually serves a domain is answered by a RESPONSE HEADER, never by a cloud provider's CLI still having the service.** This is written from the Railway era, when its CLI kept returning values and a green `SUCCESS` for every backend service long after DNS had moved — so `RAILWAY_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` read plausible and stale, and `railway logs` tailed a container nothing reached, which looks exactly like a service that logs nothing. Railway is gone now and the CLI with it; the rule outlives it, because the next dead platform will read just as alive. One `curl -sI` settles it: **`via: 1.1 Caddy` = the Hetzner box**, which is now every hostname we serve, and a Cloudflare `server:` header is on all of them and says nothing. An `x-vercel-id` on one of our domains would mean something had been moved back. Do this before hunting for a service's logs or quoting an env var as live config. What made the wrong model survive so long was that the stale credentials still MATCHED, so a probe built from them worked.

**And WHAT a component does is answered by its ACTIVITY, never by its CONFIGURATION — an env var naming a peer is a statement of intent that survives the peer's death, so reading config tells you what someone once meant, not what runs.** Config is the seductive source because it is complete, instantly greppable and reads as authoritative; activity takes a query. The failure is asymmetric and lands on the destructive side: config says a thing is load-bearing, so you leave it running (cheap mistake) — or, far worse, config says a dead component is wired to a live one, so you "reconnect" it and wake a second copy of a system that is supposed to be a singleton. **Before concluding anything about a component, ask what it has DONE in the last N hours** — jobs executed, rows written, requests served, events emitted — and prefer the count over any URL. **When a probe returns a status the code can produce for two different reasons, name which one** — a gateway route answering `500 fetch failed` proves the downstream is unreachable; a `404` on a path you guessed proves nothing at all.

Cost 2026-08-08 (Railway teardown): the Railway fleet's env carried `*_SERVICE_URL = http://<svc>.railway.internal:8080` for ~20 services, so I read the Railway containers as load-bearing for campaign execution and proposed repointing Windmill's URLs at the public hostnames. Those resolve to Hetzner, so the change would have woken a **second full execution engine** beside the live one — the double-send it was meant to prevent, and only harmless today because Railway talks to stopped containers. The activity numbers said the opposite of the config: **0 Windmill jobs in 6h, 0 runs in 18h, 0 Instantly events**, against 180 runs per 30 min on Hetzner. Worst part: I had already read the worker logs, where every single pull said `empty: true` — the queue being empty was on screen, and I read "the workers are alive" instead of "the workers have nothing to do".

**Conductor workflow — create a new workspace per task, never `git checkout -b` manually.** Conductor creates each workspace as a git worktree on its own branch and fetches `origin` first, so the branch already starts from the latest remote commit. Branch prefix: `adam/<workspace-name>` (configured via `.conductor/settings.local.toml`). If the workspace has been open a while and `origin/main` has advanced, run `git fetch origin main` before creating a PR.

**Before REBUILDING an existing dashboard/admin PAGE, `git fetch origin main && git log origin/main -20 -- <page dir>` FIRST — the workspace branch lags `origin/main`, and a PARALLEL rebuild of the SAME page (or its backend contract) may have already merged.** The in-flight-workspace + `gh pr list --search` dup-checks are NOT enough: they don't surface a page rebuild that already landed on THIS repo's `origin/main`. Cost 2026-07-05 (admin CRM contacts rebuild): built the entire page + spawned a 4-repo backend fan-out against the OLD server-fetch CRM page, but `origin/main` had already merged a client-side rebuild (#2391 — `GoogleCrmClient` + typed safeParse readers) AND the backend contract landed in parallel autonomous workspaces (google-service silver + `contact-links` + `?participant`, brand-service `/internal/brands/all`, api-service proxies). Had to `git reset --hard origin/main` and rebuild the feature (contacts table + right-panel status editor) ON TOP of the merged base, adopting its readers. Two corollaries: (a) adopt the merged base's typed readers/types and EXTEND them (add `links`/`participant`/`saveContactLinks`), never re-declare a parallel set; (b) an "overlapping" in-flight workspace editing the SAME files is NOT necessarily a conflict — additive changes that merge sequentially are fine (the silver + contact-links tasks both edited `orgs-google.ts` and both merged clean), so don't alarm/discard a sibling workspace prematurely.

- **Hotfix** → branch from `origin/main`, PR → `main`, `gh pr merge --auto --squash`.
- **Bugfix / Feature** → branch from `origin/staging`, PR → `staging`. Promotion to `main` via the staging→main PR flow.
- **Exception (`apps/dashboard/` + `apps/landing/` only):** dashboard-only and landing-only PRs merge straight to `main` in recent practice. Touching ONLY those dirs → follow the last 5 merges on the file; when in doubt, staging. Cross-cutting (shared/, packages/, multi-app) MUST go through staging.
  - **A NEW app under `apps/` (anything other than dashboard/landing), or ANY edit to root `package.json` / `pnpm-lock.yaml` / `pnpm-workspace.yaml` / `turbo.json`, is cross-cutting → prefer STAGING by blast-radius POLICY (not a hook block).** ⚠️ The `guard-branch.sh` protect-main hook does NOT inspect paths/lockfiles — rule 5 only checks `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main "$CURRENT"` and ALLOWS `gh pr create --base main` for ANY branch descending from `origin/main`. So a new-app / root-manifest / lockfile PR to main is mechanically ALLOWED (verified #2113: removed `apps/app` + edited `pnpm-lock.yaml`, PR to main, hook passed). The staging routing is a deliberate blast-radius CHOICE for additive/cross-cutting work, NOT something the hook enforces — and for an ISOLATED change (e.g. deleting one self-contained app) where a `promote` would drag unrelated divergent commits, main-direct is the CORRECT call. Don't refuse main-direct citing a hook block that doesn't exist; decide on real blast radius. (Stale prior claim from #1561 said the hook BLOCKS this — it does not, at least on the current hook.)
- **Carve-out — copy-only `shared/content` is prod-direct-eligible.** A marketing/positioning COPY change to `shared/content/src/*` (brand.ts taglines/hero, content strings, README regen) bundled with a landing/dashboard hero edit does NOT force staging, even though it touches `shared/`. `BRAND.tagline`/`hero` and the content strings have no runtime JSX consumer (only `generate-readme.ts` reads them at build), so blast radius = text on already-prod-direct surfaces. Treat like landing-only → `main` direct on Kevin's "prod". The "Cross-cutting MUST go through staging" rule still binds when the shared/ edit changes CODE/TYPES/a runtime-imported export, not just copy. Don't pre-defend staging for pure-copy shared/content. (#1312: hero distribution→lead-gen + brand.ts; flagged staging, Kevin said "prod", shipped to main, zero blast.)
- **The protect-main hook allows dashboard→main ONLY while the branch descends from `origin/main`.** `origin/main` drifts as you work → hook flips to deny. Fix: `git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main --no-edit` (NOT rebase — force-push is blocked; a merge keeps the push fast-forward), confirm `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD`, push the same explicit refspec, then `gh pr create --base main`. Squash collapses the merge commit. Don't retarget staging (it's ~140 commits behind for dashboard work). (#1288)
  - **The local `origin/main` ref advances even when you never fetch it — so an `--is-ancestor` check that passed a minute ago proves NOTHING at `gh pr create` time, and a `BLOCKED: Cannot create PR targeting protected branch 'main'` right after a green check is drift, not a broken hook.** Background tooling (the RTK wrapper, a sibling worktree, `gh`) refreshes remote refs, which makes the execute skill's "do NOT `git fetch` between the merge and `gh pr create`" insufficient here: not fetching does not freeze the ref. On this repo main lands several merges an hour, so expect to run merge → push → `gh pr create` more than once. On a BLOCKED, do not re-diagnose: immediately `git merge origin/main --no-edit`, re-push the same refspec, retry the create — and re-run `pnpm --filter @distribute/dashboard test` after each merge, because what you pulled in is other people's code (a merge that brings in a new guard test is exactly the case a stale local run misses). Observed twice in three minutes on 2026-07-30 (#3098). (onboarding-email-domain-prefill)
- **`--auto` squash does NOT guarantee the merge — a PR can silently stall as `DIRTY` (merge conflict) and sit OPEN forever, prod unchanged.** When two dashboard→main PRs edit the SAME hot lines (the feature-gating `Live flags` CLAUDE.md line, `feature-gates.ts`, `context-sidebar.tsx` Database block), whichever merges second goes `DIRTY` the moment the first lands. Armed auto-merge only fires on a CLEAN+green PR; it will NOT auto-resolve a conflict. So after arming auto-merge, the monitor MUST poll `gh pr view N --json mergeStateStatus` and treat `DIRTY` (or a long-lived `BLOCKED`) as an action signal — a `state`-only watch (MERGED/CLOSED) never fires on a stalled PR and you'll falsely report "monitoring → will merge" while nothing happens. On `DIRTY`: `git merge origin/main` (resolve the conflict, usually the `Live flags` line — keep BOTH PRs' flag additions), re-push the same refspec. (#1343: armed auto-merge, PR went `DIRTY` vs #1338's CLAUDE.md edits, sat OPEN until Kevin reported "aucun changement en prod".)
- **Cut a NEW branch off `origin/main` before each change — never commit onto the just-merged branch.** `git fetch origin main && git checkout -b KevinLourd/<topic> origin/main`. Recovery if you slip: `git checkout -b <new> origin/main && git cherry-pick <sha>`. The branch tracks `origin/main` → push explicit refspec `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/X` to dodge the hook. (#1248)
  - **Uncommitted working-tree edits AUTO-FOLLOW `git checkout -b NEW origin/main` — do NOT then `git checkout - -- <path>` to "carry" them.** `checkout -b` keeps your dirty edits on the new branch already. Running `git checkout - -- <path>` afterward restores that file from the PREVIOUS branch's committed HEAD, silently REVERTING your uncommitted edit (the Edit tool's in-memory file-state still thinks it's applied → a re-Edit fails "File has not been read"; re-Read shows the old code). After `checkout -b`, just `git diff --stat` to confirm the edit rode along, then commit — never re-checkout the file. (#1966: lost the edit this way, had to re-Read + re-apply.) **And the recovery reflex is worse than the mistake: copying the file out to `/tmp` and copying it back after the branch switch CLOBBERS whatever the new base added to it.** `origin/main` moves while you work (a sibling workspace merges), so a whole-file restore from a snapshot taken against the OLD base silently deletes their section — the diff reads as pure deletions, which is the tell. Re-apply the EDIT against the fresh file, never restore the FILE. Caught at `git show --stat` (`1 file changed, 9 deletions(-)` on a commit whose message promised an addition) 2026-08-24, one commit away from deleting a just-merged sign-in section. `git reset --hard` is hook-blocked, so the recovery is `git checkout -b <new> origin/main` + re-edit.
- **Chain `git commit && git push` with `&&`, never `;`/newline — Conductor worktrees hit `index.lock` contention.** A concurrent git process (other agent, IDE) can make `git commit` fail with `Unable to create '.git/worktrees/<ws>/index.lock'`; if the next line is an unconditional `git push`, it pushes the **un-advanced HEAD** → an empty branch at `origin/main`, and the `* [new branch]` output reads like success. Always `git commit … && git push …` so the push only fires on a committed HEAD; if you hit the lock, `rm -f .git/worktrees/<ws>/index.lock`, re-commit, verify `git log --oneline -1` advanced, then push. (#1367)
- **Base-branch prerequisite check before `checkout -b … origin/staging`:** if the feature builds on code merged to `main` but not yet promoted to `staging` (typical for dashboard), cut from `origin/main`. Check: `git log origin/main ^origin/staging -- <path>` returns commits → prereq missing on staging. (#1186)
- **`release.sh promote` conflict here is usually BIDIRECTIONAL divergence (main +N unique, staging +M unique), NOT the release-skill's "staging is a clean superset" case.** Because dashboard/landing PRs merge **main-direct** (the carve-out), `main` routinely drifts ahead of `staging` with commits staging never got (admin/Google-Ads/design-system/$5-credit), WHILE staging carries its own feature backlog → a `promote` PR goes `DIRTY`. The release-skill's `--ours` fast-forward recovery is WRONG here (it would drop main's unique work). Correct recovery = **merge main INTO staging, resolve per-hunk by INTENT, push staging (now a true superset), re-promote**: (1) `git checkout -B sync/promote-resolve-vX origin/staging && git merge origin/main`; (2) resolve each conflict by the NEWER intended direction — for landing/app COPY the staging reframe wins (the de-channel/de-email rewrites #1581-1615 are deliberate; main-sides are often EMPTY = staging-added blocks, or the older copy the reframe removed); **keep HEAD per-hunk** (`sed -e '/^/,/^>>>>>>> origin\/main$/d'`), do NOT `git checkout --ours <file>` (that discards main's non-conflicting auto-merges inside the same file — e.g. main's CLAUDE.md Google-Ads doc); (3) verify markers 0 + `apps/dashboard` tsc + a dangling-import grep (an `icons` import dropped with its usage must hit 0 refs); (4) `git push origin sync/...:staging` (staging not protected → allowed), confirm `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main origin/staging`; (5) `gh pr close <stale-promote-PR>` + re-run `release.sh --yes promote distribute.you`. CI on the fresh promote PR gates the resolution. (2026-06-14, v0.80.0: 4 conflict files, all resolved keep-HEAD toward the staging reframe; the re-promote then merged clean.)

## Architecture

**Monorepo** — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo. Roots: `apps/`, `packages/`, `shared/`.

**Apps:** `apps/dashboard/` (port 3001, Next.js 15 App Router, Clerk) · `apps/docs/` · `apps/landing/` · `apps/sales-cold-emails-landing/`.

**Extracted services (separate repos):** [api-service](https://github.com/shamanic-technologies/api-service) · [mcp](https://github.com/shamanic-technologies/mcp).

**Packages (published MCP servers, tsup ESM):** mcp-sales-outreach · mcp-google-ads · mcp-influencer-pitch · mcp-journalist-pitch · mcp-podcaster-pitch · mcp-reddit-ads · mcp-thought-leader (all `@distribute/*`).

**Shared libs:** `shared/auth` · `shared/content` · `shared/pictures` · `shared/runs-client` · `shared/types`.

**Content sync:** all marketing/docs content lives in `shared/content/src/` (`urls.ts`, `mcps.ts`, `pricing.ts`, `features.ts`, `brand.ts`); public surfaces import `@distribute/content`. When changing: update src → `pnpm generate:readme` → verify build → commit the regenerated README. **README.md is GENERATED — never edit directly.**

**Landing copy lives in MULTIPLE surfaces — a copy sweep MUST grep ALL or it ships half-done.** The *served* prod landing is the STATIC set `apps/landing/public/landing/**.html` (`apps/landing/src/app/route.ts` → `staticResponse` via `static-html.ts` — ~20 SEO pages: index/index-pro/index-agency, performance, pricing, how-it-works, use-cases + `cold-email-*` sub-trees), NOT the React `apps/landing/src/**` components alone. A grep of `src/` only misses the served HTML (sign-up buttons ×27, hero, `<meta>`/OG/twitter, JSON-LD, animated stat-counter, price-checks). **`/` serves `index-agency.html`, `/pro` serves `index-pro.html`, `/agency` serves `index-agency.html`** — the default homepage is the AGENCY variant, so verify `/` against `index-agency.html` (the variants drift: #2032 found agency's `Organization` JSON-LD missing `sameAs`/`legalName` while pro+index had them). **GEO/AI-search surfaces are part of the sweep too: `apps/landing/public/llms.txt` (curated index) AND the inline JSON-LD (`SoftwareApplication.description`, WebSite description).** Also sweep `apps/docs/src` + `shared/content/src`. When reframing a word/claim across the landing, `grep -rl '<term>' apps/landing/public apps/landing/src apps/docs shared/content`. Watch for a SECOND meaning of the same token. (#1516, #2032)

**Product is COLD EMAIL ONLY — NOT multi-channel.** Do NOT describe distribute as running PR / hiring / VC / journalist / podcaster / influencer / Reddit Ads / Google Ads as product channels. `llms.txt` + JSON-LD `SoftwareApplication.description` drifted multi-channel for months (#2032 de-channelled them). The product is cold email outreach done for you (agency-domain model). The cold-email-vs-linkedin SEO pages discuss "multichannel" as a topic — that's fine content, NOT a product claim. (Kevin 2026-06-22: "We are not multi channel.") **The React LEGAL pages `apps/landing/src/app/terms/page.tsx` + `privacy/page.tsx` are part of the positioning sweep too — they drift independently of the served HTML/llms.txt/JSON-LD and were the multi-channel STRAGGLER (Terms §3 Description, §5 Outreach, §8 third-party still listed PR/VC/hiring/journalist + LinkedIn/X/social months after #2032 de-channelled everything else; de-channelled #2084). Grep them on any positioning reframe.** (#2084)

**The PRODUCT is a productized ACQUISITION AGENCY that sells SALES MEETINGS at a MEASURED cost. Cold email is the CHANNEL, never the category — describing distribute by its channel on any public surface files it under tooling and is a positioning bug.** The rule above ("cold email only, not multi-channel") is about which CHANNELS we claim; this one is about what we SELL. Both hold. Concretely, on every public surface (`meta`, `og`, JSON-LD `SoftwareApplication.description`, FAQ, footer, `llms.txt`): the offer is named **Autonomous Sales Meetings Acquisition**, we are an agency that runs the campaign on the client's behalf, and the deliverable is an interested reply from a buyer. Never "a cold email tool", never "done-for-you cold email outreach" as the self-description. Three facts that must survive any rewrite:
- **The cost per meeting is MEASURED, never QUOTED.** We invest the client's budget through paid APIs, take a margin, and report what each interested reply actually cost. The only legitimate figures are the live cross-client rate (`__CAC_PRICE__`) and the client's own stats. NEVER write "predictable cost", "a cost you know in advance", or any phrasing implying a rate card. The differentiator is that we are the ones who measure it, not that we can promise it.
- **THE COST IS THE CLIENT'S, NEVER OURS. This is the one that gets inverted.** The client authorises a daily budget cap and is charged **the budget the campaign spent, and nothing else** — one number, no breakdown. Our margin (fixed, 75%) sits INSIDE that spend and is invisible to them. So every "$X per interested reply" on any surface is **a price the client pays**, and we have no cost-per-meeting to show a buyer. Banned: "what a meeting costs us", "our cost per reply", "we deliver a reply at $X" (reads as our rate). Correct: "what it cost you", "what our clients are paying", "a positive reply costs $X". Equally banned in the other direction: **"at cost", "pass-through", "no markup", "you only pay for X"** — all false, the margin is in there. "You pay the budget you authorised, nothing else" is true and is the actual selling point (no seat, no retainer, no subscription). Cost 2026-07-29 (#3059 → #3064): shipped the ROI calculator headlined "against what a meeting **costs us**"; Kevin: "non ce n'est pas cost us, c'est cost au client!!! Mais tu me fais peur… reformule le business model." An audit found exactly one offending phrase page-wide (mine) — the rest already said "cost you" — but the inversion was live on the homepage. Guarded by `homepage-offer-story.test.ts` (prose-only: a whole-file `margin` check fires on the inline stylesheet's `margin:` declarations).
- **Do NOT qualify the live rate with "best model" / "best rate" / any model vocabulary.** Every client is served the best model, so the number needs no qualifier — it is simply what it costs. A "best rate" hedge is worse than useless: it invites "so which one will I get?" for nothing, and it leaks the workflow-model detail the landing deliberately hides (Kevin 2026-07-29: "ne rentrons pas dans le détail du meilleur model blabla. On donnera au client le meilleur model de toute façon"). The producer-side rule that the fleet figure is cross-org + BEST workflow (never pooled) is unchanged — it just never surfaces as words on the page.
- **The software is OPTIONAL.** A client who never wants to log in can send their website and we set the whole thing up at the same price, with interested replies arriving in their normal inbox. This is a load-bearing objection-killer; do not let a rewrite make the dashboard sound mandatory.
- **The guarantee is on the MEASUREMENT, not the result** (owner-picked, 2026-07-29). Copy may commit to: the real cost always visible, the whole picture always visible, spend always controlled by a daily cap and an immediate pause, and our own numbers published. It may NOT promise meetings. The FAQ's "results are not guaranteed" stays.
(Kevin 2026-07-29: "on vend du sales meeting. A du cout predictible. On est des experts du CAC" then the correction "les gens ne connaissent pas leur CAC exactement en avance… la seule chose qu'on peut donner ce sont les moyennes des autres clients et leurs propres stats".)

**Homepage (`index-v1.html`) is an OFFER ARGUMENT, one idea and one PRO VISUAL per section, in story order — do NOT re-add a text-only section.** Rebuilt #3051 after an audit against the Hormozi value equation the product itself sells to clients: 5 of the 8 sections had been pushing perceived-likelihood, time-delay had NO section, and the value stack was a list with no prices. Current order and what each section OWNS: hero (dream) · `#testimonials` (the real dream, Doc Dinners **$50 per sales meeting, 10x ROI**) · `#proof` (price anchor vs the $700 market meeting) · `#measure` (per-segment cost of acquisition, dashboard preview) · `#engine` (**the split-and-test tree** — sub-segments by geography/size/role, A/B angles inside each, Hormozi-built templates) · `#how` (30-second setup) · `#speed` (time before the first email) · `#stack` (what's included, priced against documented market rates) · `#pricing` (what you risk + the measurement guarantee). Rules when editing:
- **`#engine` is the section that justifies the price.** It was absent from the site for months while being the entire reason a reply costs what it does. Never delete it to "simplify".
- **A number shown twice on the page must come from ONE token.** The `#measure` dashboard headline reads the same `__CAC_PRICE__` as the hero, so the two cannot diverge. Do not hardcode a second copy.
- **`#measure`'s three segment rows read that same rate, and their BAR LENGTHS are decorative — leave them at `38% / 61% / 92%`.** Each row states its own cost per reply (`__SEG_COST_LOW__` / `_MID__` / `_HIGH__`, built by the unit-tested `segmentCostBand` as the live rate ∓ $5, low leg clamped above zero) — a hardcoded price there would freeze while the headline kept moving, so the same card would end up disagreeing with itself. The bars are NOT a scale for those prices, and making them proportional was considered and rejected (owner-picked): at a ±$5 spread the three would render nearly identical and the row would lose its shape. So this is the case where a length that does not track the number beside it is intentional, NOT the self-contradictory-surface bug — do not "fix" it. Guarded by `segment-cost-band.test.ts`. (#3134)
- **Market-rate figures are only the DOCUMENTED bands** (agency $1,500-5,000/mo + $80-200 per qualified reply, SDR $4,000-7,000/mo + $50-120, $700 per booked meeting, 2-4 weeks DIY setup, 3-6 months SDR ramp). There are no documented per-TOOL prices, so the stack prices the ALTERNATIVES, not the individual tools. Do not invent a price for "a lead database" or "a warmup tool".
- **This page carries its CSS INLINE and links no `css/styles.css`**, so the `?v=N` cache-buster rule does not apply to it (it does apply to its sibling static pages). Guarded by `homepage-offer-story.test.ts`.
- The four-model comparison grid (`#category`) and the post-launch step list were deleted as text-only sections carrying no idea of their own. The nav's "Why distribute.you" now points at `#engine`. (#3051)
