**A mutation whose only visible effect renders BELOW the fold must report its own result next to the control — and a run that legitimately produced NOTHING is the case that must not be silent.** The onboarding audience step appends new candidates UNDER the ones already picked, so a user holding a full selection sees nothing move and reads the click as a no-op. Worse, `runSuggest` had a branch that wrote no message at all: when every returned candidate collided with one already stored (`added === 0` while `keep.length > 0`) the cards were unchanged, no error fired, and the run was indistinguishable from a dead button. A real user clicked "Find new audiences" five times into that silence before abandoning (org `d3367008`, 2026-07-29 12:29→12:34 — human-service re-serves existing audiences by name, so repeat clicks on the same prompt converge to zero new rows). `notice` now reports the count of genuinely-new candidates (`merged.length - keep.length`) beside the button, the zero-new run gets its own amber line, and both clear at run start and in the catch. RULE for any similar "generate more / add / refresh" control: derive the count from what CHANGED, never from the response length, and give the produced-nothing branch an explicit message. (#3078)

**The best-model step edits the economics its ROI is computed from — `roiMultiple` is lifetime revenue ÷ cost per outcome, and showing the quotient while hiding both operands makes a sub-1x return unexplainable.** The fields are the PRIMARY FUNNEL's own chain (`funnelRateFields(def)`) plus its lifetime revenue, rendered ABOVE `BestModelStats` and highlighted amber when `resolved.roiMultiple < 1`. **The lifetime revenue is load-bearing in that set — a sub-1x ROI is almost always a small LTR rather than a bad model, so a block showing only the conversion rates would hide the actual driver** (exactly the #3039 incident, where a placeholder $2,500 LTR printed a flattering ROI that collapsed to 0.6× once the real $100 landed). `saveModelEconomics` runs `saveFunnelStatsAndContinue`'s discipline verbatim — `getBrandSalesFunnels` → `buildFunnelPatch` → `declareBrandSalesFunnel`, so the stored set is read from the wire on every write and the patch is the DIFF against it — then `setBestModelLadder(null)` BEFORE `fetchBestModelLadder` so a stale ROI never sits beside freshly typed inputs. That wire-first discipline matters more here than anywhere else in the flow: **the step runs on a FRESH page load after Stripe, where the client copy can be a reconstructed snapshot full of placeholders.** The button arms off a live compare against the baseline captured on entering the step, never a sticky edited latch. (#3078, retire-brand-level-rates)

**And it asks features-service on the FUNNEL, never on a goal — because a goal-keyed request does not merely mislabel the answer, it RANKS the wrong workflow.** `/workflow-projection` takes `?funnel=` and wins it over `goal`/`objective`; the two meeting funnels both echo `meetingBooked`, so a goal-keyed request is priced from BOTH channels at once (its own docs: the outcome count is `clicks·visitToMeeting + replies·replyToMeeting`). Per dollar that buys ~86× more clicks than replies at fleet prices ($2.47 CPC vs $212 CPPR), so the click leg supplies nearly every projected outcome — and `recommendedWorkflowDynastySlug` is an argmin on that same mixed cost, so the workflow crowned `BEST` is whichever is cheapest **per CLICK**, with its cost per reply incidental. That is the Osprey-vs-Dawn split from the audiences-vs-strategy incident recurring one surface over: prod showed `Cost / positive reply $212` for a reply→meeting brand whose reply-cheapest workflow was a third of that. **Diagnostic tell, and the reason it reads as a producer bug: the numbers are arithmetically impossible against the rates on the SAME screen.** $93 per paid client under a $212 first step (the chain gives $4,711 and 2.1×, not $93 and 26.8×) — no paid client can cost less than one of the replies that buys it. So when a projected figure is challenged, divide it out by the visible rates before looking anywhere else; a *plausible* wrong number needs a data hunt, an *impossible* one localises to the request. Sibling rule for the render: `BestModelStats` gates every tile on `stepsFor(null, funnelKey)`, so a reply chain shows no cost per website visit, and its outcome noun comes from the step's own `outcome.noun` (`goal-steps.ts`) rather than `outcomeNoun(goal)` — one goal covers two chains, so it cannot say what was bought. ⚠️ `funnelKey` must be DECLARED on the reader's zod schema or it is stripped, which throws away the producer's own statement of what it priced. The deprecated `goal`/`objective` params stay on `getWorkflowProjectionLadder` for the brand-level callers that state no funnel BY DESIGN (a brand sells through several at once): removing them would not make those callers funnel-aware, it would drop them onto the producer's `meeting-booked` default — a worse wrong number. They go with the last sender. Still goal-keyed and awaiting a per-surface decision: `stepsFor`'s fallback, `goalForOptimizationGoal` → audience-stats, `audienceRankMetric`, `OUTCOMES`/`GOAL_LABEL`/`OUTCOME_UNIT`, the outcome digest, `first-outcome-reassurance`, `workflow-projection-choice`'s unit-cost branches. Guards: `tests/onboarding-funnel-scoped-projection.test.ts`. (#3491)

**Two smaller rules the same screen proved.** (a) **A form under copy saying "we prefilled this" must actually seed, and the seed goes through the SHARED helper.** The funnel detail step seeded `rates: {}` while its own paragraph promised a prefill, so every conversion field was blank and the user retyped what we already knew — then the model step showed those typed values, which is why it read as a one-screen bug rather than a missing seed. It now reads `funnelDraftFromBrand`, the same helper the Settings card uses (its param is the structural `FunnelSeedEconomics`, so the EFFECTIVE economics read — a subset of the columns, and the better guess for a brand-new brand because it folds cross-brand averages — can seed it too; a rate it does not carry seeds BLANK, never 0%). The economics live in STATE as well as `econRef`: a ref lands with no re-render, so the form would stay blank until a keystroke. (b) **A RANK or a SUPERLATIVE over a set of one states nothing, and reads as a missing sibling.** `1 of 1`, a `Primary` tag with nothing to be primary over, a `1` numeral in an icon tile, "your most profitable path" and "your primary goal" on a brand selling through one chain — each promises a comparison the page cannot show. Gate all of them on `length > 1` and state what the single thing IS instead (the numeral gives way to the funnel's own mark). Same family as the `1 of 1` counter fix that shipped before it: when you gate one of these, grep the screen for its siblings, because they were all written together.

**Onboarding checkout return — exactly ONE effect may own a `?launch_checkout=` redirect; the GENERIC resume must no-op on it.** A checkout return is owned end-to-end by the dedicated effect (`resumeCheckoutLaunch` for `success` → step `"launching"` → `completeLaunchAfterCheckout` → push to dashboard; the cancel branch for `cancelled` → restore pricing). The trap: the restored checkout snapshot's `step` is `"pricing"`, so the GENERIC resume (`resumeTargetRef` → `runResume(...)`, which re-hydrates the brand and lands on the snapshot step) computes target `"pricing"` and fires TOO — both effects run on mount and race, flashing the budget modal over the real `"launching"` flow (~2s while the stale `createBrandAndFetchServices` re-fetches), plus a wasted concurrent brand re-hydration. Rule: `resumeTargetRef` MUST be null whenever `searchParams.get("launch_checkout")` is set (covers `success` AND `cancelled`), and the first-paint `step` goes straight to `"launching"` on `success` so the budget step never renders. (#2173)

**Bumping `ONBOARDING_STATE_VERSION` must NEVER lose an in-flight checkout — the pending-checkout blob (`CHECKOUT_PENDING_KEY`) carries a NESTED `onboardingState` that is version-guarded, so a bump that lands while a user is at Stripe kills their cancel/success RETURN.** The nested `onboardingState` is only a convenience for re-rendering the deeper wizard; the blob's TOP-LEVEL fields (`brandId`/`orgId`/`brandUrl`/`hostname`/`outcome`/`budgetUsd`/`workflowSlug` + the lifted `selectedAudienceIds`) are version-INDEPENDENT and are the real source of truth for a launch. So `readPendingCheckoutLaunch` validates the top-level fields strictly, then — if `parseOnboardingState(nested)` fails (stale version) — `reconstructCheckoutOnboardingState` rebuilds a minimal CURRENT-version snapshot from those top-level fields (logged loud, keep-resolved not silent-fallback) instead of throwing the whole blob away. Before this fix, a mid-deploy return threw in `readPendingCheckoutLaunch` → the cancel handler's `catch` reset to `pricing` with no restore → the brand + wizard history were lost. Rules when touching this: (1) any field the checkout RETURN needs to survive a version bump lives at the TOP LEVEL of the pending blob, not only in the nested `onboardingState`; (2) never make `readPendingCheckoutLaunch` throw on a stale NESTED state — reconstruct; (3) the `_OrNull` opportunistic reader still purges on a TOP-LEVEL-invalid blob. Guards in `tests/onboarding-resume-persistence.test.ts`. (checkout-version-resilient)

**Audience activation happens ONLY at the TERMINAL launch (`completeLaunchAfterCheckout`), and it makes the picked set the brand's EXACT active set — the audience step does NOT activate.** Earlier the audience step ALSO activated on Continue (`setAudienceStatus(c.audienceId,"active")`), additively + never deactivating — so a re-roll (Back → re-suggest → re-pick → Continue) or a re-onboarding STACKED stale `active` rows, and the audiences page then showed more active audiences than the user picked ("picked 2, page shows 5"; confirmed in prod — one brand/org accumulated 5 active across two activation bursts). Fix (#2239): the audience step `saveAndContinue` only records `selectedAudienceIds` (no DB write); `completeLaunchAfterCheckout` is the SOLE activator — it (1) `listAudiences(brandId,{status:"active"})`, (2) demotes any currently-active audience NOT in the picks back to `"suggested"` (recoverable, hidden from the page), (3) activates the picks. So the picked set BECOMES the active set: re-rolling / re-onboarding OVERRIDES the prior selection instead of accumulating. Still fail-loud on an empty pick (the dashboard's hard "No active audience yet" blocker would otherwise hit an already-PAID campaign), and `beginCheckoutAndLaunch` blocks checkout up front when no audience is picked. Done BEFORE avatar gen (server-side on-activate gen, human-service #144). FOLLOW-UP (human-service #151): `/orgs/audiences/suggest` still persists every candidate as a permanent `"suggested"` row and never supersedes the prior batch per (org,brand) → orphan suggested rows accumulate (hidden from the page, DB pollution). (#2173, #2239)

## Agency-domain sending model — distribute sends from OUR domains, on the client's behalf (LOAD-BEARING positioning)

**distribute is a marketing AGENCY: all outreach goes out from inboxes/domains that WE own, warm, and operate — NEVER from the client's domain.** Think PR firm pitching journalists from the agency's own contacts. This is a core differentiator, not an implementation detail — copy across landing/dashboard/docs must reflect it and must NEVER imply the client connects/owns/configures the sending domain. The five client benefits (the canonical framing — reuse this vocabulary in copy):
1. **Zero reputation risk** — the client's domain never touches cold outreach; deliverability damage (if any) lands on OUR domains.
2. **Zero setup** — no DNS / SPF / DKIM / DMARC / inbox-warming / mailbox purchase on the client side. We own the full sending stack; the client only drops a URL.
3. **Zero inbox to babysit** — replies route to OUR inboxes first; we screen + qualify and forward only the positive ones to the client's Gmail.
4. **Full CRM visibility, still theirs** — the client keeps the whole view: who we contacted, opens, replies (incl. negative). We send on their behalf, nothing is hidden.
5. **Test demand before revealing the brand** — for niche/finite prospect universes, we can validate the need WITHOUT naming the client, then forward warm prospects — so they re-engage a limited list without saturating their brand.

**Copy discipline:** any line about sender/from/domain/inbox/warmup/deliverability/setup MUST attribute the infrastructure to distribute ("our sending domains", "we configure", "nothing to set up on your side"), never to the client ("your sending domain" ❌). The misleading-line fixes + the dedicated "Sent on your behalf" section live in `apps/landing/public/landing/how-it-works.html` (full 5-card section) + `index.html` (condensed 5-item strip). Terms of Service (`apps/landing/src/app/terms/page.tsx` §5) + the dashboard email previews (`From: distribute.you`, below) are the gold-standard surfaces — match their precision. (Agency-model copy sweep + dedicated section: KevinLourd/agency-domain-sending)

**NOTE — served landing path is `apps/landing/public/landing/**.html`, NOT `landing-v2/`.** `src/app/<page>/route.ts` → `staticResponse(...)` → `static-html.ts:233` joins `public/landing`. The repo CLAUDE.md notes that say "served set is `public/landing-v2/**`" are STALE (no `landing-v2/` dir exists). Edit `public/landing/`.

**⚠️ ACCENT IS BLUE, NOT GREEN — green is DEPRECATED, do NOT re-introduce it (Kevin 2026-07-21: "le nouveau design est blue, delete partout le green").** Every brand accent is `#2563eb` / OKLCH **hue 258**: landing `index-v1.html` `--green` VALUE is `#2563eb` (var misnamed, value blue), `css/styles.css` + React `globals.css` `--accent`/`--dy-accent`, the dashboard/admin `@theme --color-brand-*` ramp, every favicon and nav logo, and the `/brand` kit (SVG + PNG + `favicon.ico` + the zip, regenerated blue-D). Treat every "GREEN charter" claim below as HISTORICAL. SEMANTIC success-green is KEPT (KPI up-delta, positive status, "good" bars — green=good, not brand green). **The EMAIL templates were the straggler this line failed to catch, and the reason is worth keeping: the green-charter reskin (#2671, 15 Jul) landed six days BEFORE the revert, and the sweep swept the apps but not the template strings, so every button and link mailed to customers stayed green for three weeks.** Nothing surfaced it — no test renders those to a screen and nobody reads their own dunning mail; it only appeared when the admin fork was found overwriting them with its own older-but-coincidentally-blue copies. They now read `EMAIL_ACCENT` / `EMAIL_ACCENT_TEXT` / `EMAIL_DOT` (`#2563EB` / `#1A4FC3` / `#3D80FF`, contrast at or above the greens), and a guard in `email-templates-startup.test.ts` fails on any `#00xxxx` or on an accent hex repeated outside its declaration. **Generalise: when a charter sweep runs, the surfaces that render OUTSIDE a browser — email, PDF, OG images, anything with inline hexes — are the ones it misses, because none of them is covered by a screen test.** Still-green FOLLOW-UP: the dashboard/admin decorative rainbow-gradient `#10b981` stop. (#2939, #3008, #3013, #3304)

**A landing favicon/brand sweep is NOT just `<link rel=icon>` — it MUST cover: (1) the dynamic OG `ImageResponse` routes (`src/app/{,investors/,performance/}opengraph-image.tsx`, which drive `og:image` for the homepage + all 28 static pages), (2) `src/app/apple-icon.png` + `public/favicon.ico` binaries, (3) `seo.ts` `BRAND_LOGO_PATH` (React Org JSON-LD logo+image = Google Panel + investors OG), (4) static-HTML Organization JSON-LD `logo`/`image` (`public/landing/**.html`), (5) `layout.tsx` `openGraph.images`/`twitter.images`. Grepping only `rel="icon"` misses the OG cards + Panel logo.**

**The `apps/landing` React `navbar.tsx` nav = `How it works`/`Pricing` links + `Log in`/`Launch campaign` CTAs (parity with the static sub-page nav + homepage); footers carry a `Blog` link (`/blog`) — #2936/#2939.**

**HOMEPAGE (2026-07): `/` serves `index-v1.html` (the "5-minute-positioning" design), NOT `index-agency.html`.** The old agency homepage is stashed at `/v0`; the `/agency` route was RETIRED. `index-v1.html` is the graphic-charter baseline: DARK `--bg #070a0f` + Space Grotesk (display) / Inter (body) / IBM Plex Mono (mono), logo = glowing dot + "distribute" wordmark. This SUPERSEDES the #1644 "LIGHT-default indigo" note **for the homepage**. The same charter fonts + brand ramp were ported to `apps/dashboard` + `apps/admin` (`globals.css`), LIGHT default kept, byte-equal across the two apps; neutral gray tints (chroma <= 0.018) stay at hue 264. **The accent VALUE is BLUE per the correction above, not the green those PRs shipped.** The static SEO-cluster HTML + React landing pages are still un-reskinned. Brand kit at **`/brand`** (self-contained dark), assets under `public/brand/`. (#2595, #2600, #2605, #2607)

**HOMEPAGE-ERA ARCHIVE MAP + non-indexed `/v1` `/v2` reference routes (#2890, #2895).** Three generations, newest first: current `index-v1.html` at `/`; **Adam blue agency** `index-agency.html` at `/v0`, archived as the self-contained `public/landing/archive-blue.html` at `/v2`; **pre-Adam multicolor/beige**, which is the **React `page.tsx`** homepage (Fredoka + rainbow pastel + old "d" logo, last live `f23b1866e`), archived as the React route `src/app/v1/` with era components under `_c/` and every era rule scoped under `.v1-scope`. Both archives are `noindex` and out of `sitemap.ts`. **Identify an era by its DESIGN SIGNATURE in `globals.css` (font/palette/logo), NOT by which file `/` pointed at** — the beige era is a React page and cannot be inlined the way the static archives are. Navbar shrink on `index-v1.html`: constant `nav{height:64px}`, morph the pill only (max-width/radius/shadow) past `scrollY>60`; do not re-add the min-height/logo-mark resize, it reads jumpy.

**`index-v1.html` also carries the `__TICKER_BOARD__` token** (a live `#outcomes` cost section added #2600, resolving 7 dead `#outcomes` anchors) — same server-render mechanism as below. NOTE: the ticker currently renders LAST-KNOWN-GOOD fallback because the public `cost-per-outcome-trend` endpoint has been timing out (identical fallback on `/v0`, so pre-existing/backend, not a landing bug); real numbers appear automatically once the features-service public endpoint responds within budget.

**An SSR `replaceAll(TOKEN, injectedScript)` placeholder token in a served landing HTML MUST be UNIQUE vs every client global it injects — a bare token that also appears in the page's own JS gets rewritten too, producing invalid JS that silently kills the whole `<script>` IIFE.** The homepage CAC chart (`data-cac-chart`, under the `$52` live-CAC price) was DEAD in prod for exactly this: `withCacBoot` (`static-html.ts`) did `replaceAll("__CAC_BOOT__", "<script>window.__CAC_BOOT__=…</script>")`, and `__CAC_BOOT__` ALSO matched the client reader `var boot=window.__CAC_BOOT__;` → rewrote it to `var boot=window.<script>window.__CAC_BOOT__=…</script>;` (SyntaxError) → `renderCac()` + the live refetch never ran → blank chart, even though the injected boot payload had live data. Fix (#2832): the HTML placeholder is a DISTINCT token `__CAC_BOOT_SLOT__`; the injected script sets the `window.__CAC_BOOT__` global the reader reads. RULE: name any `replaceAll` slot token so it can not be a substring of the runtime global/identifier it feeds (`__X_SLOT__` placeholder → sets `window.__X__`). Also: `resolveCacBoot` bakes a deterministic `fallbackCacSeries(best)` when the live trend fetch is empty/slow (#2831), so the chart paints instant + never blank and the client swaps in live points when the endpoint responds. (#2831, #2832)

**NEVER surface a cross-org + cross-WORKFLOW POOLED metric on the landing — the wanted metric is always the BEST single model (MIN over per-workflow ratios), cross-org.** Cross-ORG aggregation is fine; blending N workflows into one average is banned (Kevin: "je ne veux JMS de cross org + cross workflow"). Two pooled public endpoints are therefore OFF-LIMITS to landing consumers: `cost-per-outcome-trend` (fleet-wide moving average) and `cost-projection`/`avgCostPerOutcomeByObjective` (fleet-mean). The BEST-model number comes from `workflow-cost-per-outcome` (per-workflow, take the MIN of `costPerOutcomeUsd` over workflows whose objective outcome was observed > 0 — never a 0-outcome husk) — that's the `$52` headline and everything on the page must be coherent with it (`fetchBestModelUsd` in `static-html.ts`). The CAC chart series + ticker sparklines are a deterministic best-shaped curve ENDING on the live best-model price (`fallbackCacSeries`/`fallbackSeries`) until a **non-pooled best-model DATED trend** endpoint ships in features-service (spawned `KevinLourd/best-model-cost-trend`); the client rescales the baked curve to the live best. A new landing surface that needs a $/outcome number reads `workflow-cost-per-outcome` MIN, NEVER a pooled trend/projection. (#2861 chart, #2863 ticker+pricing-budget, issue #2864)

**Any build-time fetch on a served landing page MUST be `AbortSignal.timeout`-bounded + fall back to last-known-good**, else a slow cold endpoint hangs the prerender past Vercel's 60s budget and the whole deploy ERRORs. Reference: the `/v0` ticker's `fetchTrendSeries` (8s) and the `/benchmarks` bounded fetch. (#2530, #2533)

**Changing how often a served landing page re-renders means moving TWO values, and moving one alone does nothing.** `export const revalidate` on the route governs Next's own data cache; `staticResponse()` sets an explicit `cache-control: s-maxage=…` on the `Response`, and THAT is what the CDN obeys — so the edge keeps coming back to the function on the header's schedule no matter what `revalidate` says. Both read `86400` today. The failure is silent and expensive: #3287 raised the 22 routes' `revalidate` from 300 and shipped green while all 21 pages kept re-rendering a ~127KB document every 5 minutes, which is exactly what Fast Origin Transfer bills for ($15/month, 459K ISR writes over 3 months). Crawlers keep every SEO page warm enough to hit the window continuously, so a nominally traffic-driven cost behaves like a timer. **Verify by reading the header off the live page** (`urllib`, per the curl-truncation rule), never by grepping the route file — the route file is the value that does not win. `stale-while-revalidate` stays long: it is what keeps a reader served instantly from the edge during a revalidation. (#3287, #3289)

**The REACT landing pages (`apps/landing/src/app/<page>/page.tsx` — investors, benchmarks, privacy, terms, blog) render on a HARDCODED-WHITE body (`globals.css` `body { background: var(--background) }`, `--background: #ffffff`, NEVER themed) while `layout.tsx` sets `<html data-theme="dark">` by default — so the `--dy-*` design tokens resolve to their DARK-theme values (`--dy-text` = light text). Net: using `text-[var(--dy-text)]` / `bg-[var(--dy-surface)]` for CONTENT on a React landing page gives LIGHT text on a WHITE body = washed-out/unreadable on a fresh (no-localStorage) visit. These pages must use PLAIN dark Tailwind grays (gray-900 headings / gray-600 body / gray-500 muted / white|gray-50 cards / gray-200 borders) — theme-INDEPENDENT, correct regardless of `data-theme` — exactly like the working siblings (benchmarks/privacy/blog headings compute to `rgb(26,26,46)` = the body `--foreground`, NOT a dy token). The `--dy-*` tokens are for the STATIC served HTML (`.dy-page` mechanism) + the home React surface that ride the theme attribute, NOT for these white-body content pages. **VERIFY the RENDERED COMPUTED color (headless Playwright `getComputedStyle(h2).color` on the live URL — preview deploy URLs are behind Vercel deployment-protection and return the auth interstitial, so probe PROD after merge) BEFORE declaring done — a class grep proves nothing about contrast.** (#2299 swapped investors' hardcoded grays → dy tokens and shipped STILL washed-out because dy-text=light; #2300 forward-fixed to plain light grays. Two merge+promote cycles burned by not probing the computed color before the first merge.)
- **#2303 then flipped `layout.tsx` to `data-theme="light"` default** (the React layout was the last straggler still defaulting `dark` after the #1644 light-default landing rebrand). So the dy-themed CHROME (navbar/footer/buttons) is now coherent-light on the white body. Content pages still use plain grays (theme-independent, unchanged) — but new dy-themed chrome now resolves correctly. Footer stays intentionally dark (`bg-gray-950`, hardcoded).
- **`src/components/navbar.tsx` (the React landing nav, shared by investors/benchmarks/blog/privacy/terms) had referenced class names that DON'T EXIST in `globals.css`** (`dy-chip`, `dy-nav-actions`, `dy-button-ghost`, `dy-button-primary`, `dy-nav-menu`, `dy-nav-mobile`, `dy-shell`) → unstyled + jammed action buttons ("Sign inStart free"). The real design-system classes (what the STATIC nav in `public/landing/js/components.js` uses) are `dy-nav-chip`, `dy-nav-right`, `dy-btn dy-btn-g`, `dy-btn dy-btn-p`, `dy-nav-toggle`. #2303 corrected them. When touching `navbar.tsx`/`benchmark-cta.tsx`, the class vocabulary MUST match `globals.css` (`grep '\.dy-' globals.css`) — the static `components.js` nav is the reference for correct names.

**Email-preview `From:` is ALWAYS the literal `distribute.you`, never the client's company.** The outreach is always sent by the marketing agency distribute.you on the client's behalf (the agency-domain model above), so the 3 email-preview pages (`brands/[brandId]/emails`, `features/[featureSlug]/emails`, `features/[featureSlug]/campaigns/[id]/emails`) hardcode `From: distribute.you`. Do NOT re-introduce `selectedEmail.clientCompanyName || 'Your Company'` (or any brand-name-derived From) — Kevin: "c'est toujours envoyé par l'agence marketing qui s'appelle distribute.you." (#1403)

### React Query mutations: write the response to the cache, don't just invalidate

Mutation returns the fresh entity → `queryClient.setQueryData(["entity", id], data)` (not just `invalidateQueries`). The GET can 5xx, and with `placeholderData: keepPreviousData` an invalidate-then-failed-refetch leaves the stale pre-mutation row → user thinks the click did nothing. `invalidateQueries` still correct for LIST caches the mutation can't rebuild (`["campaigns"]`, `["leads"]`). (#1090)

### Dashboard data fetching — ONE framework (React Query v5 SWR)

TanStack React Query v5 on every client component. No SWR, no mixing. Server components for the shell only.

**Global config (`src/lib/query-provider.tsx`):** `staleTime: 60_000` · `gcTime: 30min` (= persister `maxAge`, see below) · `placeholderData: keepPreviousData` (GLOBAL — never re-set per query) · `refetchOnWindowFocus/Reconnect: true` · `retry: 1` queries / `0` mutations.

**Poll cadence (`src/lib/query-options.ts`):** ONE cadence — `pollOptions` = `POLL_INTERVAL` = **30s everywhere** (the 5s base + the `pollOptionsSlow` 10s / `pollOptionsSlower` 30s tiers were collapsed to a single 30s in #2196). Import + pass; never a local `const pollOptions`. Idle/hidden-tab polling is paused globally by `installIdleFocusManager`, so an AFK tab stops polling regardless of cadence.

**Idle/visibility polling pause (`src/lib/idle-focus-manager.ts`) — load-bearing, do NOT remove.** A foreground-but-AFK dashboard tab that keeps polling every 5s re-renders lists/charts/badges → DOM mutation → feeds PostHog's rrweb session recorder an unbounded mutation stream → Chrome **renderer OOM** ("Aw Snap") on long-lived tabs (confirmed: 15–20h recordings with <100s real activity). `installIdleFocusManager()` (mounted once in `query-provider.tsx`) installs a custom `focusManager` event listener reporting the app **unfocused when the tab is hidden OR the user is idle >2min** (`IDLE_MS`); with `refetchIntervalInBackground` at the v5 default `false`, this pauses EVERY poll, and `refetchOnWindowFocus:true` refreshes on the next interaction. This is the ONLY thing pausing idle polling — do not re-introduce an always-on global poll, and keep session recording ON (the fix removes the *cause* of the OOM, not the recording). posthog-js kept current (≥1.386.6) for the rrweb canvas/Safari/memory fixes (PostHog/posthog-js#3273). (#1514)

**V5 render rule:** `isPending`/`isLoading` (no data) → SKELETON. `isFetching && !isPending` (background refetch with cached data) → CONTENT, never skeleton. Background refetches are SILENT — no top-bar/spinner/flash (matches Linear/Vercel/GitHub). Button-scoped spinner only for mutations. **A mutation button's in-flight label must stay FULL-opacity — a disabled button carries `disabled:opacity-40`/`opacity-50`, which fades the very "Saving…/Launching…" text meant to signal work and reads as a DEAD button.** Gate the fade class on `!isMutating` (`${isMutating ? "cursor-wait" : "disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed"}`) and render a `<Spinner/>` next to the label, so the working state is at 100% while the genuinely-unavailable state still fades. For a multi-second create-then-navigate flow, also pre-seed the destination (`setQueryData(["entity", id], response)` before `router.push`) AND show a full-screen "Launching…" overlay during the POST — the new id doesn't exist until the POST returns, so you cannot route earlier; reassure in place. (#1408: the launch page — then `campaigns/new`, now `brands/[brandId]/launch` — Launch button showed a faded `Launching…` for ~3s → "blanc" the user read as a bug.)

**Carve-out — `isPlaceholderData` on a KEY CHANGE → SKELETON, not stale content.** The "never skeleton when data exists" rule is about a background refetch of the SAME key. When a panel's query key carries an entity id (`["workflowExamples", workflowSlug, brandId]`, `["x", selectedId]`) and the user switches entity, the GLOBAL `keepPreviousData` keeps the PREVIOUS entity's data in `data` → `isPending=false`, `isFetching=true`, `isPlaceholderData=true`. Rendering that = showing entity A's content under entity B's selection for the whole cold fetch (~2s). `isPlaceholderData` is the discriminator: gate the skeleton on `isPending || isPlaceholderData` so a switch paints a skeleton (not the wrong entity), while a warm re-select (cache hit < `staleTime`) stays instant. Pair with `queryClient.prefetchQuery` for every visible entity on container/modal open → switching becomes instant after warm-up. (#1383: workflow example-emails panel showed the prior workflow's 3 emails for ~2s on switch because the `"Loading…"` text was gated on `isPending`, which never fires under `keepPreviousData`.)

**Detail-page not-found gate — gate on `isPending`, NEVER `isLoading`.** A detail page is tri-state (skeleton / not-found / content). The not-found branch must fire ONLY once the query has RESOLVED empty: `!isPending && !data` for not-found, `isPending` for skeleton. Do NOT use `isLoading`: `useAuthQuery` suspends a query (`enabled:false`) via the org-consistency gate until Clerk's active org resolves and matches the URL org, and a disabled v5 query reports `isPending:true` but **`isLoading:false`** (`isLoading = isPending && isFetching`, `isFetching` false while idle). Gating not-found on `isLoading` → `!isLoading && !data` is `true` during the org-settle window → a red-cross "X not found" flash for ~2s on every cold load before the fetch runs. `getX` throws on 404 → query error → `isPending` false → genuine not-found is preserved. The source-substring guards (`brand-404-handling.test.ts`) double as the site INVENTORY for this gate — run the dashboard suite, a failing guard pins a site you still need to migrate. Fixed across the detail surfaces (brand overview, brand-info, workflow page, press-kit, workflow-detail-panel; the campaign detail surface via `campaign-context` was removed in #1768). (#1384)

**Same bug in TWO more forms — `isLoading && !data` skeleton guards, and context-exposed loading flags.** The not-found gate is only the loud version; the identical org-settle flash hits any TERMINAL UI gated off `isLoading`: (a) **list empty-states** — `if (isLoading && !data) return <skeleton>` then `items.length===0 → "No X yet"`: during settle `isLoading` is false so the skeleton is skipped and the empty-state flashes before the fetch runs. **Key equivalence: `isPending` ≡ `isLoading && !data` in the *enabled* case (skeleton on first-load-no-data, content on refetch-with-data) and differs ONLY in the disabled/settle case** — so swapping `isLoading && !data` → `isPending` is a strict, zero-regression improvement. Do NOT read `isLoading && !data` as "already safe" (it is the bug, not the fix — a subagent audit mis-classified it as safe). (b) **context loading flags** — a context built on `useAuthQuery` (e.g. `features-context`'s `["features"]` query) must expose its loading via `isPending`, else every consumer that gates "Feature not found"/skeleton on it flashes; fix once in the context (`isPending: isLoading` alias) and all consumers inherit it. Swept ~28 surfaces at the time: `features-context` + (the now-removed, #1768) `features/[featureId]/{page,new,workflows}` "Feature not found" guards + all `isLoading && !data` list guards (leads/emails/articles/outlets/runs/logs/quote-requests/quote-pitches/press-kits/workflows — the feature- and campaign-level variants were removed in #1768; the brand-level ones remain + brand-usage/leads-stats-panel/visibility-runs/api-key-preview/workflow-selection-table). (#1392)

**Third form — a list/tabbed surface that splits ONE entity across SEVERAL status queries must gate each tab/section on ITS OWN query, never a COMBINED `isPending`.** When a page fans one list into N parallel reads (e.g. Audiences = `["audiences",brandId,"active"|"paused"|"archived"]`, a status-tab table, anything with per-status fetches), a single `const isPending = aPending || bPending || cPending` + `&& combined.length===0` skeleton guard fires ONLY when ALL N are empty. The instant the FIRST query resolves, the guard passes — so a still-loading sibling tab renders its empty-state ("No archived audiences") instead of a skeleton. The producers cold-start seconds apart (human-service 0.25 CU), so this is the steady-state look, not a rare race. Fix: a per-tab loading flag (`archivedTabLoading = archivedPending || (archivedRows===0 && archivedFetching)`) and skeleton that tab while ITS query is pending OR fetching-while-empty — `isFetching` is load-bearing because the per-query persister can RESTORE an empty snapshot (e.g. archived=[] from before anything was archived, and it does not expire for 30 days) then revalidate in the background, where `isPending` is false but the empty is stale. Empty-state shows only after that tab settles with 0 rows. Mirror the sibling that's already right (`EngagedLeadsPage` gates on `isPending || isPlaceholderData`). (#2230)

**Page composition:** shell+nav+header render instantly (no query gates them). Each card owns its `useAuthQuery` + skeleton, parallel. Skeleton matches the real layout (zero shift). No in-page `<Suspense>` to gate dashboard content (React Query + per-card skeletons handle within-page loading; in-page `<Suspense>` is public-marketing only). **This ban does NOT cover route-segment `loading.tsx` — see "Instant nav transitions" below.**

**Injecting a full-width row (stat cards, banner) at the TOP of a list/detail SPLIT page → put it INSIDE the inner padded content column, NEVER as a direct child of the `flex flex-col md:flex-row` split root.** The brand-level entity pages (leads/emails/journalists/outlets/articles/companies; the campaign-level variants were removed in #1768) return a root `<div className="flex flex-col md:flex-row h-full relative">` whose children are the list column + the detail panel. A row dropped as the FIRST child of that root becomes a flex ROW sibling on desktop → it renders as a squished LEFT COLUMN beside the list, not a full-width band above it (on mobile `flex-col` masks the bug, so it passes a quick mobile check). `tsc`+tests stay green — it's a pure layout defect. Correct placement: first child of the inner `p-4 md:p-8` content column (scrolls with the list, full-width when no detail panel). Reusable component for the outreach stat row = `components/revenue/outreach-stat-cards-auto.tsx` (`OutreachStatCardsAuto`, self-contained: reads brand/campaign scope from route params, fetches its own featureStats + sales-economics funnel, reuses existing query keys to dedupe). (#1689)

**Instant nav transitions (`loading.tsx` route boundaries) — REQUIRED, distinct from the in-page `<Suspense>` ban.** A dashboard route is DYNAMIC (Clerk `auth()` + `ClerkProvider dynamic`); Next 16 does NOT prefetch a dynamic route and BLOCKS on the old page until its full RSC render returns — **unless** the segment has a `loading.tsx`. Without one, a sidebar click shows "nothing for several seconds." Fix is global: one `loading.tsx` → `<DashboardPageSkeleton/>` at each segment level **below** the persistent `(dashboard)` layout (org · brand · feature · campaign — NEVER at `(dashboard)/` itself, that would blank the sidebar). Boundaries live at `orgs/[orgId]/loading.tsx`, `…/brands/[brandId]/loading.tsx`, `…/features/[featureSlug]/loading.tsx`, `…/campaigns/[id]/loading.tsx`. **Load-bearing invariant: the intermediate layouts (`[orgId]`/`[brandId]`/`[featureSlug]`/`[id]`) MUST stay empty server passthroughs — no `cookies()`/`headers()`/uncached `fetch` in a layout, or `loading.tsx` can't show a fallback for it and navigation blocks on the layout render (Next `loading.js` "Good to know" caveat).** New dashboard route level → add its `loading.tsx`. (#1338)

**Body reveal — use the `CoordinatedReveal` component, not a hand-rolled ternary.** `src/components/coordinated-reveal.tsx` wraps `useCoordinatedReveal`: `<CoordinatedReveal flags={[a !== undefined, …]} skeleton={<Skel/>}>{() => <Content/>}</CoordinatedReveal>`. `children` is a RENDER FUNCTION — content only evaluates once revealed (it dereferences now-resolved data). Declare every `useAuthQuery` at page top (parallel fire); this gates only the paint. Same warm/cold rule as the hook: group cold-coherent siblings, never AND a warm cache with a cold sibling.

**Static-shell-first reveal (3-tier hierarchy) — the DEFAULT for any card with static structure.** Loading is NOT binary (all-skeleton → all-content). Three tiers, validated against the React `<Suspense>` docs ("reveal nested content as it loads"), Next PPR (static shell + dynamic holes), TkDodo (anti-popcorn) and NN/g ("real content replaces skeletons as available; don't frame-only-skeleton"): **(0) static shell** — card frame, **title, subtitle, labels, static copy** — render on the FIRST paint, never skeletoned; **(1) coordinated values** — the data regions only (stat numbers, charts, donuts, table cells) show a `<Skeleton/>` and reveal together (the `pending`/`revealed` latch); **(2) progressive rows** — list/table rows stream within an already-shown frame. Pattern: a data card takes a `pending?: boolean` prop, renders its frame+title+labels always, and wraps ONLY each value region in `{pending ? <Skeleton className="…" /> : <value>}`. The PAGE computes `const valuesRevealed = useCoordinatedReveal([...])` and passes `pending={!valuesRevealed}` — it does NOT whole-body-gate a card that has a static shell. Co-locate the value-skeleton sizing with the real value (Aurora Scharff: skeleton lives next to its component → zero drift). Reference impls: `BrandMetricsHeader` (MetricCard frame+title always, value children gated on `!revealed`), `RevenueCostSummary`/`RevenueOverviewSection`, the `campaign/*` charts. Whole-body `CoordinatedReveal` (above) is the fallback ONLY for content with NO static shell (e.g. a bare list, where the frame IS the rows). Guard: `tests/static-shell-reveal.test.ts`. **Skeleton imported into a test-IMPORTED component must be RELATIVE (`../skeleton`), not `@/components/skeleton` — vitest has no `@` alias config, so an aliased import in an import-tested module fails to resolve.** (#1347-follow-up)

**Mutations:** `setQueryData(["entity", id], response)` FIRST, then `invalidateQueries(listKey)`; return the invalidation promise from `onSuccess`.

**A Save/dirty gate MUST be a LIVE compare against a baseline snapshot, NEVER a sticky boolean latch.** `const dirty = !formsEqual(form, baseline)` where `baseline` = the last-SAVED (or hydrated) form, set at init (cache/defaults), on hydration (server read), and on save-success (`formFromEconomics(res.salesEconomics)`). A `useState<boolean>` latch flipped `true` on any edit stays `true` forever, so a change-then-revert — flip a segmented-toggle/goal-tab and switch back, or edit a field and undo it — leaves Save wrongly ENABLED though nothing changed. Reference: strategy page's `offerDirty = draft !== null && !fieldsEqual(draft, baseline)`. `brand-sales-economics-card.tsx` was the sticky-latch offender (goal-tab flip kept Save armed) → fixed to derived-compare. (#2450, #2452)

**Forbidden:** local `const pollOptions` · `refetchIntervalInBackground: false` (v5 default) · per-query `placeholderData: keepPreviousData` · skeleton on `isFetching` when data exists · in-page `<Suspense>` to gate dashboard content (route-segment `loading.tsx` is REQUIRED, not this) · **a gate that ANDs a WARM section (warm-cache, e.g. `["features"]`) with a COLD sibling** — group at the finest coherent level, barrier WITHIN a group, independent ACROSS groups. (#1257) · **a reveal barrier (`useCoordinatedReveal`) that gates on a SLOW / inline-compute query** — a get-or-refresh POST that scrapes on cache-miss, or any cold source that can take seconds — freezes the WHOLE group in skeleton ("shows nothing") until that one source returns, holding the fast siblings hostage. Gate the barrier ONLY on fast cache-read queries; a slow cold source reveals WITH the group and renders its OWN card-local skeleton while pending (`metricPending ? <Skeleton/> : …`). A fast GET cache-read is barrier-safe; a POST that triggers a scrape is NOT. (#1330→#1331: card-4 ahref Brand-Radar `ai-visibility` get-or-refresh POST in the barrier held all 4 brand-overview cards in skeleton until the Apify scrape returned.)

**Coordinated reveal** (`src/lib/use-coordinated-reveal.ts`) — reveal a coherent all-cold group together in one paint, then keep it revealed (a poll / token rotation / transient error must not send a shown group back to skeleton). The reveal-layer analog of `keepPreviousData`.
- `useCoordinatedReveal(readyFlags: boolean[]): boolean` — barrier (false until every flag true once) + monotonic latch (true stays true for the mount). Call unconditionally at top level (uses `useRef`).
- One flag per query: `data !== undefined` or `!isPending`. **A disabled query stays `isPending` forever** — gate its flag `!enabled || !isPending`.
- **Group at the finest COHERENT level, nest coarse→fine** (body group ⊃ sidebar nav-items group ⊃ badge-numbers sub-group). Never span warm+cold.
- **Prepend an inputs-loaded flag (`defsReady`) FIRST** when queries are conditionally enabled off another query — else the first paint has every query disabled, every flag true, the barrier passes instantly and latches an EMPTY group. `useCoordinatedReveal([defsReady, ...flags])`. (#1260)
- **Gate each section on the query that produces ITS data, never a context's coarse `loading`.** A shared context's single `loading` often tracks only its primary query; the list a page renders (`emails`, `leads`) resolves later → gate on `emailsLoading`/`leadsLoading`. (#1264)
- **Sidebar primitives** (`context-sidebar.tsx`): gate a nav level on `defsReady` + render `SidebarNavRowSkeleton`; reveal count badges together via `badgePending={!badgesRevealed}` on `SidebarLink`. (#1261)
- **Standard for every multi-query surface** — page bodies + all data-dependent sidebar levels (`BrandLevelSidebar`; the `CampaignSidebar`/`McpSidebar`/`AppFeatureLevelSidebar`/feature-level sidebars were removed in #1768). Static-only nav levels need no gate. ~40 pages still un-adopted = a phased sweep (first-load stagger only; the return-flash is fixed globally by persistence) — do NOT bundle into a hotfix. (#1259/#1260/#1261, DIS-133)
- **ONE barrier PER visually-distinct card — never a single page-wide `revealed` that ANDs heterogeneous card-queries.** A page that funnels all its queries into one `useCoordinatedReveal` holds EVERY card in skeleton until the SLOWEST query returns (a slow features-service revenue/stats cold-chain sibling freezes the fast campaign-LIST that only needs campaign-service). The user's rule: within a card (or the sidebar badge group) everything reveals together; BETWEEN cards it's independent. So compute one latch per card and gate each card on ITS OWN query — per-card `trafficRevealed`/`drRevealed`/`aiVisRevealed` on `BrandMetricsHeader` (queries sharing ONE source share a latch — visits+revenue both read traffic), `platformReady`/`providersReady` per section on `api-keys/page.tsx` (the `campaigns/page.tsx` list latches `listRevealed`/`heroRevealed`/`chartsRevealed`/`costRevealed`/`statsRevealed` were removed with that page in #1768). The SIDEBAR is the one coherent multi-card unit that reveals its badges together — but its nav ROWS still gate on `defsReady` alone (instant), badges as a finer `badgePending` sub-group (`BrandLevelSidebar` already does this right). Guarded by `static-shell-reveal.test.ts` + `brand-metrics-getorfetch.test.ts` (pin the per-card latch names). (#1551: full per-card sweep; brand-metrics/api-keys fixed, sidebar/overview were already correct; the campaigns-list + campaign-detail surfaces it also touched were removed in #1768.)

### "API returned a non-JSON response" = the Vercel function was OOM-KILLED — diagnose the PROXY, not the endpoint

**That client error string never means the endpoint is broken. It means the `/api/v1` proxy replied with something that isn't JSON — in practice Vercel's own HTML error page after `Vercel Runtime Error: instance was killed because it ran out of available memory`.** Diagnose with `mcp__vercel__get_runtime_logs` on the app's project (`distribute-admin` / `distribute-dashboard`, team `team_lYmJIUH6q2rTY6dUfDiYtpAt`) around the failure minute — the OOM line names the killed request. **Under Vercel fluid compute ONE instance serves several concurrent invocations, so the request that dies is usually the VICTIM, not the cause**: a long-running POST (an LLM extraction) gets killed by whatever heavyweight GET blew the instance seconds earlier. Tell you're in this case: the failing request 500s FAR too fast for the work it does (a `POST /v1/brands/extract-fields` LLM prefill dying ~2s after the click). Look for a second OOM on a big list endpoint in the same window before blaming the endpoint you clicked.

Two invariants that came out of it (#3021):
- **The catch-all proxy (`apps/{admin,dashboard}/src/app/(authed)/api/v1/[...path]/route.ts`) MUST stream `res.body` through — NEVER `await res.text()` then re-wrap.** Buffering holds every payload TWICE (the JS string + the re-encoded `NextResponse` body). `GET /v1/emails?brandId=` returns every email for the brand with `bodyHtml` + `bodyText` + the `sequence[]` bodies + the full `generationRun` cost tree (`costs[]` + `descendantRuns[]`), so the doubling is what kills the instance. Guarded by `tests/proxy-stream-no-buffer.test.ts` in BOTH apps (`not.toContain("await res.text()")` — so never write that literal in a comment there either).
- **`apps/admin` now runs the SAME single 30s poll cadence as `apps/dashboard` (#2196).** `lib/query-options.ts` `POLL_INTERVAL = 30_000`; `pollOptionsSlow` / `pollOptionsSlower` are ALIASES of `pollOptions` (kept so the ~60 call sites don't churn) — do NOT re-introduce a faster tier, and do NOT hardcode `refetchInterval: 5_000` inline (the guard test fails on any `refetchInterval: 5_000` left in `apps/admin/src`). The one sanctioned exception is the workflow fork-detection poll (`workflows/[workflowId]/page.tsx`, 3s) because it drives an immediate navigation on a tiny payload. Root cause of the burst: `context-sidebar.tsx` polled FIVE unbounded brand list endpoints (emails/leads/outlets/journalists/articles) every 5s purely to render count badges.
- **The badges still download the full lists** — a served count is the real fix (issue #3023, backend). Until then the cadence + streaming are what keep the instance alive.
- **The non-JSON `ApiError` message now carries HTTP status + content-type + a 200-char body preview** (`readJsonResponse` in both apps' `lib/api.ts`). Most call sites render `err.message` alone, so a bare "non-JSON response" hid whether the platform 500'd, the gateway timed out, or the route was missing. Keep the diagnostics in the MESSAGE, not only in the error body.

### Monotonic status latch (`useMonotonicStatuses`) — status-tab tables must not flap on a poll

Keeps a per-row derived bucket across refetch (the only family member addressing a valid CHANGING success). A status-tab page derives each row's tab from a delivery/engagement overlay re-fetched every poll; a transient drop (status under a different shape, or empty) sends every row back to "Processing", leaving the viewed tab → the table empties then repopulates. `keepPreviousData`/coordinated-reveal can't fix it (valid 200, content legitimately changed).
- `src/lib/use-monotonic-status.ts` → `useMonotonicStatuses(entries: {id, status}[], priority, label?)`: a per-mount latch on the page's "most-advanced-first" priority (`LEAD_STATUS_ORDER`, or `STATUS_PRIORITY` from `outlet-status.ts`). Outreach is append-only (`contacted→sent→delivered→opened→clicked→replied`) so a less-advanced later poll is a stale read — keep the most-advanced, `console.error` the suppressed downgrade (fail-loud, real invariant). The returned Map is the single source for tabs + row badge + detail panel.
- **Rule:** every CLIENT-POLLED status-tab surface MUST latch via `useMonotonicStatuses`. Static/ISR tables aren't susceptible. Wired into the brand-level status-tab surfaces (brand × leads/journalists/outlets) + added missing `safeParse` on `listBrandLeads`/`listCampaignLeads`. (#1270, DIS-149. NOTE — #1768 removed the feature-level + campaign-level variants; only the brand-level surfaces remain. `listCampaignLeads` survives as the launch modal's self-fetch.)

### Local-first SWR cache (per-query IndexedDB persister) — open a page → its content NOW

**This is the dashboard's named architecture: a LOCAL-FIRST, stale-while-revalidate (SWR) cache.** Local-first = the on-disk cache is the source the UI paints FIRST, network is secondary. SWR = show the cached (stale) data instantly, refetch in the background, swap fresh in silently (TkDodo: *"stale data is better than no data, because no data means a loading spinner = perceived slow"*). It is the 4th "don't revert resolved state on a transient" layer and the only one that survives cache EVICTION/RELOAD (the first three keep warm-in-memory state across refetch only): 1. `keepPreviousData` (data) · 2. `useCoordinatedReveal` (reveal) · 3. `useMonotonicStatuses` (row bucket).

**Mechanism (`src/lib/query-provider.tsx` + `src/lib/persist-cache.ts`, unit-tested):** the **PER-QUERY persister** `experimental_createQueryPersister` (from `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client`), wired as a default query option (`queries.persister = persister.persisterFn`) on a plain `QueryClientProvider`. Storage = **IndexedDB** via `idb-keyval`. NOT the old whole-client `PersistQueryClientProvider` + `createSyncStoragePersister` (localStorage).

**Why the migration (2026-06-25, KevinLourd/dashboard-instant-cache) — the 2026-06-23 "persist-everything into localStorage" policy was SELF-DEFEATING, causing the exact two symptoms it meant to fix ("30s skeletons all the time" + "site lourd/lent"):**
- **localStorage's hard ~5 MB per-origin cap** → a heavy brand's `brandLeads`/`brandEmails` blew the cap → `removeOldestQuery` evicted the small overview queries (cached first) → the overview cold-skeletoned on the slow Neon chain. The persist-all policy evicted the very page it was meant to keep.
- **Whole-client `dehydrate()` re-serializes the ENTIRE cache on EVERY mutation** (TanStack #9775). ~12 overview queries polling 5s → constant multi-MB `JSON.stringify` + sync write on the MAIN THREAD → jank/"lourd".
- The per-query persister fixes BOTH: each query is written **separately, keyed by its query hash, only when IT changes** (no whole-cache re-serialize) AND IndexedDB has **no 5 MB cap + async off-main-thread writes**. Verified against TanStack `createPersister` docs + TkDodo #5169.

**`admin` ≠ `dashboard`: separate Vercel apps = separate ORIGINS = separate storage.** `admin.distribute.you`'s heavy Outlets/Journalists/Articles cache NEVER touches the `dashboard.distribute.you` cache. Don't diagnose dashboard slowness via admin's big lists; the dashboard customer never even loads those (alpha/staff-gated). Admin is allowed to stay heavy.

**`apps/admin` runs the SAME per-query IndexedDB SWR persister (ported from the dashboard, #2779) BUT with a DENYLIST, not the dashboard's curated allowlist — do NOT re-introduce a `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` allowlist in admin, and do NOT re-sync admin's `persist-cache.ts` to the dashboard's.** admin is staff-only on a separate origin ("allowed to stay heavy"), so `isPersistableQueryKey` persists EVERY successful root except `SENSITIVE_QUERY_ROOTS` (`apiKeys`/`byokKeys`/`keySources` — key material only). This is deliberate: any new admin page auto-SWRs with zero allowlist maintenance (a new dashboard page still opts in via the allowlist). Two more admin-specific divergences from the dashboard's `query-provider.tsx`: (1) the cache **bucket = `urlOrgId ?? "platform"`** (`bucketForPath`) — admin has TWO page classes: `/orgs/[orgId]/…` god-mode customer pages (bucket = URL org id, DIS-143 isolation preserved) AND cross-org FLEET pages (`/metrics`, `/audit`, `/feature-stats`, `/workflows`, `/features` — platform-global data owned by no org, bucket = fixed `"platform"` so they're SWR regardless of the staff user's Clerk active org); (2) the nav reseed + remount key derive from that URL bucket (no monotonic active-org latch — the URL is stable, never blinks). Storage key namespace is `distribute-admin-cache:{bucket}` (vs `distribute-dashboard-cache:`). (#2779)

**Load-bearing rules:**
- **`maxAge` is a LIE without `navigator.storage.persist()` — request persistent storage at boot, or the browser EVICTS the whole IndexedDB store after a few idle days and every `useAuthQuery` surface cold-skeletons again.** Best-effort IndexedDB (the default) is evictable under disk pressure / prolonged non-use, so the "instant across days" the per-query persister promises silently decays — this is the recurring "instant right after a deploy, infinite skeleton a few days later" report on audiences/leads. `QueryProvider` calls `requestPersistentStorage()` once at mount (feature-detected, idempotent via `storage.persisted()`, never throws). Do NOT remove it; a rebrand/refactor of `query-provider.tsx` must keep the boot call. Pairs with the org-gate latch below — eviction UNMASKS the disabled-query hang. (KevinLourd/leads-skeleton-persist-orggate)
- **Disk retention (`maxAge`) and memory (`gcTime`) are DECOUPLED, and BOTH are finite.** The decoupling is why per-query beats whole-client: a heap-GC'd query **stays on disk**, so staying instant across sessions needs NO `gcTime: Infinity` → no heap blowup. (The whole-client persister mirrors disk⇆memory, so `maxAge: Infinity` there would force `gcTime: Infinity` = the #1273 OOM.) `PERSIST_MAX_AGE_MS = 30 days`, `PERSIST_GC_TIME_MS = 30min`. The old "`gcTime` MUST equal `maxAge`" rule (TanStack #5169) applies ONLY to the whole-client persister.
- **A read of the persisted cache is SCOPED TO ONE BUCKET, and the store is SWEPT — `maxAge` was `Infinity` and nothing ever deleted anything, so opening any page loaded every response the app had ever cached into the heap.** The store is one flat key space shared by every bucket (each god-mode org, plus admin's `platform`), and the persister's own `restoreQueries` / `persisterGc` call `storage.entries()` and only THEN filter on `key.startsWith(prefix)` — so a whole-store `entries()` decodes every org's cache to keep one page's. It ran twice per mount (the mount `restoreQueries` plus the nav reseed, whose effect also fires on mount) and again on every navigation. Reported as a single admin tab holding **3.5 GB**. Three parts, all in `lib/idb-bucket.ts` + `lib/persist-cache.ts`, byte-equal across both apps: (a) `bucketEntries(prefix)` reads the bucket's CONTIGUOUS key range with a bounded `getAll`, and is what the storage adapter's `entries()` returns — the persister then finds its prefix filter already satisfied; (b) the mount `restoreQueries` is GONE (the nav reseed is a superset AND cold-guarded, while `restoreQueries` calls `setQueryData` unconditionally and can stomp fresher memory) — do NOT add it back; (c) `sweepStaleEntries` walks a CURSOR at boot, in constant memory, across every bucket, deleting what `PERSIST_MAX_AGE_MS` and the buster say is past. Do NOT reach for the persister's own `persisterGc()`: it calls `entries()`, so garbage collecting would re-create the allocation this exists to avoid, and it only ever looks at the current bucket. `reclaimLegacyStore` clears the pre-bounded store once ever (the sweep cannot reach it — most of those bytes are recent enough not to be stale), guarded by `RECLAIM_MARKER_KEY`, which the sweep must never delete or the reclaim runs on every boot. Guards: `tests/persist-bucket-scope.test.ts`.
- **Org-scope via the persister `prefix`** = `persisterStorageKey(orgId)` (`distribute-dashboard-cache:{orgId}`); each query's storage key is `${prefix}-${queryHash}`, so org A's keys never collide with org B (DIS-143). **`storage: undefined` while `orgId` is null OR on the server** (`persistEnabled = typeof window !== "undefined" && !!orgId`) — no anon-bucket cross-org bleed. The keyed remount (`key={orgKey}`) still resets the in-memory cache on switch.
- **`buster` = MANUAL version constant** (`persistCacheVersion()` → `PERSIST_CACHE_VERSION`, currently `"1"`), bumped BY HAND only on an incompatible shape change — NEVER the git commit SHA (the SHA flips every deploy, ~12×/day → wiped the disk cache on ~every visit, defeating persistence). Expiry is a coarse safety net at 30 days, so `buster` is the practical forced invalidation; cross-deploy shape drift is otherwise tolerated by `safeParse`/`z.coerce` on list readers + keep-last-good `structuralSharing`. Bump when you rename/remove a field on a response type consumed straight from cache with no safeParse guard (additive optional fields do NOT need a bump).
- **Persist filter = success + non-sensitive + ALLOWLISTED** via the persister `filters.predicate` → `shouldPersistQuery` (reused from the old `shouldDehydrateQuery`). `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` is the INVENTORY of every live non-sensitive root (big lists included); default-OFF for an unlisted root. Secrets EXCLUDED (`apiKeys`/`byokKeys`/`keySources` via `SENSITIVE_QUERY_ROOTS` — key material never on disk).

**Irreducible:** the genuine FIRST-EVER load on an empty cache still waits on the cold Neon backend (~20-30s, features/api/runs scale-to-zero 0.25 CU). Killing that needs backend warm-up (other repos), NOT a dashboard cache change.

**Backend-DOWN must degrade to STALE, never to a skeleton — and the persister has TWO disk-restore paths, each with a hole that a down backend un-masks.** The per-query persister self-restores a query from disk ONLY when that query actually FETCHES (i.e. `enabled`); the org-consistency gate (`use-auth-query.ts`) disables every `useAuthQuery` while Clerk's active-org is still settling, so during that window the disabled queries never fetch → never self-restore. The other path — the mount seed `persister.restoreQueries` (query-provider.tsx) — is ONE-SHOT per provider mount. So a page reached while the gate is momentarily closed, OR a sub-page whose memory was GC'd (30-min `gcTime`), cold-skeletons even though its stale snapshot sits on disk. Backend-HEALTHY hides this (the network answers within seconds); backend-DOWN turns the transient into a STUCK multi-minute skeleton on a page the user opens 10×/day — the local-first cache silently not painting. Diagnose by reading the disk directly in the browser console: `(await indexedDB.databases())` + a `keyval-store` getAllKeys — if `distribute-dashboard-cache:<org>-["<root>",…]` entries EXIST for the affected org/brand while the page skeletons, the data is on disk and NOT being painted (a restore-path bug, NOT eviction; eviction = the store/entries are absent, a different failure fixed by `requestPersistentStorage` #2336). **Fix (#2574): `reseedColdQueriesFromDisk` re-seeds COLD queries from this org's on-disk snapshot on EVERY org-scoped navigation, decoupled from `enabled`/network** — pure guarded helper `coldRestorablePairs` (org-prefixed = DIS-143, buster-checked = never paint stale-shaped data, COLD-GUARDED = `getQueryData!==undefined` skip so a reseed never stomps fresher live memory), read-only `setQueryData`, zero network. Any new org-scoped restore/reveal work must keep the invariant: painting a present disk snapshot is decoupled from the gate + the network, so a down backend always shows stale. (#2574, sibling of the #2336/#2337 eviction+org-gate fix — distinct: there the disk was empty, here it's present-but-unpainted.)

**Rule:** persistence is GLOBAL — one per-query persister wired as a default query option, never a second provider / per-page persister. `experimental_createQueryPersister` is `experimental_`-prefixed (stable behavior, API may shift — pin to the installed `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client` version). Keep `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` in lockstep with live queries. (#1273 small-allowlist origin; #1278/DIS-170 30min+allowlist; 2026-06-23 persist-all-localStorage regression; 2026-06-25 KevinLourd/dashboard-instant-cache = per-query IndexedDB; 2026-08-02 investor-update-ram-leak = bucket-scoped reads + a bounded store, the current design.)

### keep-last-good (`structuralSharing`, cache-write boundary) — a SUCCESSFUL refetch returning VALID-but-degenerate data must not collapse the UI

5th "don't revert resolved state on a transient" layer — and the ONLY one for a **successful** refetch that legitimately returns **worse** data. The other four all assume the new data is good: 1. `keepPreviousData` (key-change / error→undefined) · 2. `useCoordinatedReveal` (reveal decision) · 3. `useMonotonicStatuses` (status downgrade *ordering* on a poll) · 4. persisted cache (eviction). **The gap:** a 200 whose nullable field flips non-null→null (a cold Neon chain — api→features→workflow/runs/email-gateway/brand, all scale-to-zero — answers a poll/refocus refetch with zeroed unit costs or fewer rows while half-warming). `keepPreviousData` can NOT help — the null IS the new valid query data, so React Query installs it and any UI **derived** off it (budget cards, a `disabled` gate, a badge, a $ amount) collapses mid-session.

**Industry-aligned fix (researched: TkDodo / TanStack docs / SWR all agree there is NO built-in for successful-but-worse — you intercept at the cache-write boundary).** TanStack's hook is `structuralSharing(prev, next)` (SWR's is `compare`, Apollo's is a `merge` field policy). Helpers in `src/lib/keep-last-good.ts`: `keepLastGoodFields(prev, next, fields, label)` (per-field coalesce: keep prev's non-null when next nulls it) + `keepLastGoodList(prev, next, {keyFn, fields, label})` (keyed merge: per-item coalesce + RETAIN a prev item that vanished from next). Both `console.error` the suppressed downgrade (fail-loud — a persistent real downgrade still logs; this is keep-resolved, NOT a silent fallback). Compose per query (e.g. `keepLastGoodWorkflowProjection` in `api.ts`, co-located with the type) and pass it as `structuralSharing` on the `useAuthQuery` — protects EVERY consumer of that query at ONE place, not per-component (a per-component latch is opt-in-and-forgettable, which is exactly how the collapse shipped).

**Five load-bearing rules:**
- **OPT-IN per query, NEVER a global default.** A null can legitimately mean "deleted/gone"; blanket last-good would mask real deletions. Apply ONLY where a null means "transient/not-ready" (cold compute, in-flight aggregation), not "removed".
- **Put it at the QUERY (`structuralSharing`), not at each consumer.** One merge → cards + button + badges all inherit it. The per-component `useLastGoodValue` latch is strictly weaker (forgettable).
- **List ONLY the fields whose null = transient.** Fields outside the allowlist let next win (incl. when null) — so a genuinely-cleared field still clears.
- **CUT the *steady-state* poll on econ-INDEPENDENT slow-changing queries — but keep a BOUNDED cold-start warm-up poll.** The workflow-projection unit costs are "fetched ONCE" by design (#1528/#1536) — a perpetual 5s `...pollOptions` on them only re-hits the cold Neon chain for zero steady-state benefit (cards update from the client `econLive` recompute). BUT "fetch-once + `refetchOnWindowFocus`" alone is NOT correct: **keep-last-good cannot heal the FIRST fetch** (no prior good value to keep), and the cold Neon chain most often returns its degenerate-200 (null unit costs / empty workflows) on exactly that first request — so a cold load STICKS on the empty-state ("No workflow has website-visit cost data yet") until a manual window-refocus. The right shape is a **conditional `refetchInterval`**: poll (~4s) ONLY while the payload has no usable data (e.g. no workflow with non-null `costPerCloseUsd`), capped at N tries (so a genuinely-empty brand stops), `false` the instant it warms. This self-heals cold-start without perpetual polling. (#1557-regression: #1542 dropped the poll entirely calling it "pointless"; it was the cold-start self-heal — keep-last-good #1548 only covers a later bad refetch of an already-good cache, never the first fetch.)
- **TanStack types `structuralSharing` params as `unknown`** — pass `(prev, next) => keepLastGoodX(prev as T | undefined, next as T)`.

**Rule:** any **polled / refetch-prone** query with **nullable fields that GATE or compute UI** (disabled-button, empty-state, $ amount, recommended pick) MUST pass a keep-last-good `structuralSharing`. Pure read-display surfaces that already degrade to "—" on null are lower-risk (phased-sweep, not a hotfix). (#1542-regression: the launch-page budget cards + Launch button — then `campaigns/new`, now `brands/[brandId]/launch` — collapsed when the polled `workflowProjection` caught a valid-but-degenerate 200 mid-session; `econLive`/§2 inputs were NOT the cause — they're latched. The poll predated #1542; #1542 widened it by recomputing cpc client-side. Fix: keep-last-good `structuralSharing` on the query + drop the pointless 5s poll. Other projection/revenue surfaces — conversions/workflows/brand-overview/report + top-workflows card — are the phased follow-up; the campaign-LEVEL variants (`campaigns/[id]/overview`, top-campaigns card) were removed in #1768.)

### A form seeded from a query must RE-SEED when the payload changes — a once-per-mount latch reads the DISK snapshot and ignores the server (7th "don't get stuck" layer)

The six layers above all keep RESOLVED state from being reverted. This one is the mirror image: local component state copied OUT of a query, which then never hears about the fresher answer. Any editable surface does this — it cannot bind inputs straight to `data`, so it seeds a `useState` from the query and lets the user type.

**The trap is that the FIRST payload to settle is the on-disk one.** With the local-first per-query IndexedDB cache, a restored snapshot resolves before the network does, so a `hydrated.current` boolean latch seeds the form from the PREVIOUS VISIT's copy and the fresh server payload that lands a moment later updates the cache and nothing else. Compounding it: **`setQueryData` after a mutation does NOT go through the query function, so it is never persisted** — the disk keeps the pre-write copy, and the write the user just made is the thing that gets overwritten on the next mount.

The symptom is the worst kind: the value IS saved, the server has it, and the screen shows blank. It reads as the product dropping the save, so the bug gets reported against the write path where there is nothing to find.

- **Re-seed when the payload is a DIFFERENT OBJECT than the one the form was built from** (keep the last-seeded reference in a ref). Identity, not deep equality: React Query returns the same reference when nothing changed (structural sharing), so an unchanged refetch costs nothing and cannot loop.
- **Two things outrank the server: a field the user has EDITED, and the card/row they have OPEN.** A form that rewrites itself mid-edit is worse than a stale one. The mutation's own `onSuccess` seeds those from its response, which is also what guarantees the card shows exactly what persisted.
- The `hydrated` flag stays, but only to answer "has this ever been seeded" for the settle gate — never as "seeding is finished".

Reference impl: `brand-sales-funnels-card.tsx` (`seededFrom` ref + the `touched || openKey === def.key` guard). Guards in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`. (#3342 — a saved `meetingBookedToAttendedPct` rendered blank on the customer's Settings page while brand-service had held the value for hours.)

### Reveal on SETTLE, never success-only — an ERRORED gate query must not eternal-skeleton (6th "don't get stuck" layer)

**A `useCoordinatedReveal` / skeleton barrier gated on `data !== undefined` (success-only) SKELETONS FOREVER when its query ERRORS — this is the #1 cause of the recurring "page still shows skeleton" reports, and it is INVISIBLE to every cache/persist/reseed fix.** The five layers above (keepPreviousData, coordinated-reveal, monotonic-status, persisted cache, keep-last-good) all assume the query eventually SUCCEEDS. None cover the case where the gate query keeps FAILING: a cold Neon chain (features→downstream, all scale-to-zero) intermittently returns an aborted fetch / 5xx / `ECONNRESET`, React Query exhausts `retry:1`, `data` stays `undefined` forever, and any barrier keyed on `data !== undefined` never latches → permanent skeleton, no error UI, no recovery. The fast sibling cards are held hostage if they AND the failing query.

**Rule — reveal on SETTLE (resolved OR errored):** destructure `isError` from every `useAuthQuery` that feeds a reveal barrier, and gate on `data !== undefined || isError`, never `data !== undefined` alone. On error the region paints its null-safe "—"/stale content (a DISPLAY fail-soft — the error still logs loud via React Query + the reader's safeParse throw, this is keep-resolved not a swallowed fetch); the monotonic latch keeps it revealed while the next poll recovers real data. **This is ORTHOGONAL to the org-gate + disk-reseed work (#2336/#2574):** a query DISABLED by the org-consistency gate has `isError:false` → NOT settled → still (correctly) waits for reseed-from-disk; reveal-on-settle covers the DIFFERENT case where the query is enabled, fires, and FAILS.

**Two co-defects that turn one failing query into a whole-page skeleton (both must be fixed together):**
- **Single load-bearing gate.** Do NOT gate heterogeneous cards on ONE query's reveal (the "ONE barrier PER card" rule). The brand Overview gated the entire section + cost card + the fast `featureStats` outreach cards on the slow `/revenue` reveal — so `/revenue` failing skeletoned everything. Gate each card on ITS OWN query's settle.
- **Defensive `!data` RE-GUARD in the child component.** A `const loading = pending || !data` inside the presentational component (`revenue-overview-section.tsx`) RE-LOCKS the skeleton even after the page forces reveal-on-error (page passes `pending=false` but `data` is undefined → `!data` wins). Drop the `!data` re-guard; make sub-fields null-safe (`data?.x` → "—"). The child must trust the page's `pending` flag, never re-derive loading from `!data`. **A component carries ONE gate per query, so fixing the reported one and leaving its siblings ships the bug with a smaller blast radius — grep the whole file for `pending || !` and fix every hit in the same commit.** #2650 fixed `revenueLoading` and left `activityLoading = activityPending || !pipelineActivity` two lines under it, so the identical eternal skeleton reappeared 2026-08-08 the moment `pipeline-activity` 502'd. **Where a gate genuinely cannot take an absent payload** (`PipelineActivityChart` reads `data.days`), the answer is a branch STATING the reason, never a skeleton — and inline the `!data` check at the render site, since a derived `const` boolean does not narrow the prop for TypeScript.

**And check each region against the query that actually FEEDS it — a region sharing a neighbour's gate goes blank on an outage of an endpoint it never reads.** The Outcome card's cumulative line is `pipelineActualSeries` off `/revenue`, which was serving fine; only its dashed forward projection comes from pipeline-activity, and that degrades to nothing. It was grey for 20 minutes purely by association.

~100 prior "skeleton" PRs all touched cache/reseed (#2074/#2107/#2129/#2336/#2574); none gave the gate query a failure exit. Guarded by `overview-reveal-on-settle.test.ts`. (#2650, #3303)

### Public marketing pages — SEO + AI-scraper rendering

Public landing/marketing/docs (`apps/landing/`, `apps/sales-cold-emails-landing/`, `apps/docs/`): default **ISR + `unstable_cache` + edge cache**, NEVER Suspense streaming on indexable content. AI scrapers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Bingbot AI) parse raw HTML only — a Suspense fallback means they index the skeleton, not the data.
1. No `<Suspense>` on indexable content (hero, h1/h2, body, JSON-LD, leaderboards, tables, blog cards, stats — SSR-sync). Suspense only for user-specific non-indexable widgets.
2. Never combine `force-dynamic` with `revalidate` (force-dynamic wins, ISR silently off). Prefer ISR (`export const revalidate = N`) alone.
3. `headers()`/`cookies()` in a page forces dynamic rendering — drop them, resolve hostname from `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`.
4. Wrap every fetch/DB call in `unstable_cache` with explicit tags.
5. Invalidate via `revalidateTag(tag, profile)` — Next 16.2+ requires the 2nd arg (`"default"` for ordinary invalidations).
6. `unstable_cache` at module top level breaks vitest — mock `next/cache` globally in `tests/setup.ts` (identity pass-through; `revalidate*` → `vi.fn()`), declare `setupFiles` in `vitest.config.ts`.
7. Validate scraper output after any caching change: `apps/landing/scripts/seo-snapshot.sh` + `measure-ttfb.sh`; pre/post snapshots in the PR. (#1153)
8. **Skeleton-leak check (seo-snapshot does NOT catch this).** `seo-snapshot.sh` counts h1/h2/article/JSON-LD — it does NOT count `animate-pulse`, so a page that ships data behind `<Suspense>` passes the snapshot while serving skeletons to LLMs. Add `curl -sA "GPTBot" https://distribute.you/<page> | grep -c animate-pulse` — **>0 = the data is behind Suspense/client-fetch and invisible to AI scrapers**, fix it. Canonical fix = `/investors` + `/pricing` (#1520): both were ISR + `<Suspense fallback={Skeleton}>` around async server components → 314/102 skeletons in raw HTML. Removing the Suspense (the async sections await straight into the static prerender, mirroring `/benchmarks`) dropped both to 0. **A page that's already `revalidate=N` ISR gains nothing from `<Suspense>` on its own data — drop it.** When de-Suspensing, make the data fetch **fail-soft** (try/catch → zeros) so a build-time fetch throw can't abort the prerender (`data-sections.tsx` `EMPTY_INVESTOR_METRICS`). Guarded by `investors-page-ssr.test.ts`. **Measuring page byte-composition: never `grep -oE 'ld+json.*'` on minified single-line HTML — `.*` grabs the whole doc, not the block; use a real parser (python non-greedy per-`<script>`).** (A grep artifact once mis-reported a 4 KB JSON-LD block as "2.27 MB"; the real bloat was the 2.2 MB RSC flight payload `self.__next_f`, which scrapers ignore.)

**Fixing an Ahrefs / external site-audit: target the SERVED HTML, never reconstruct from source metadata exports.** The audit overview gives issue COUNTS, not per-URL data. Pull the per-URL row export (Ahrefs MCP `site-audit-issues` / API / UI CSV) OR crawl the live served HTML — do NOT reverse-engineer which page is flagged from the Next `metadata` source. Source reconstruction produces false positives because: (a) `title` templates compound (`page title "X — distribute"` + layout `template "%s | distribute Y"` = doubled brand + over-length — the served `<title>`, not the raw string, is what Ahrefs measures); (b) Next metadata **inherits** down the layout tree (a child page with no `openGraph`/`twitter` is NOT missing them — it inherits the root layout's); (c) a layout `title.default` is **overridden** by any child `page.tsx` `title` (the default may never be served); (d) `description` constants (`PAGE_DESCRIPTION`/`SITE_DESCRIPTION`) and JSON-LD `description` fields are NOT the HTML meta description — a 166-char `Service.description` in `application/ld+json` is structured data, not the `<meta name="description">`; (e) a `<html>` literal inside a JSDoc comment fools a naive `grep '<html'`. Incident 2026-06-04 (#1310 / DIS-69): 4 such false positives caught only by direct served-HTML / template-aware verification. Ship ONLY directly-verified served defects; defer count-only issues (OG-incomplete, structured-data, redirects) to DIS-69 pending the per-URL export. The recurring SEO-audit home is **DIS-69** (Ahrefs project 9700515) — update it, don't open a new issue.

### Public pages — no horizontal overflow on mobile

Every public app ships **`html { overflow-x: clip }`** in `globals.css` — never `overflow-x: hidden` (`hidden` makes html/body a scroll container, breaks `position: sticky`; `clip` doesn't). It's a safety net, NOT the fix — real spills are fixed at the element level. Wide legit content (code, perf tables) scrolls internally via its own `overflow-x-auto` (else `clip` HIDES it). Decorative `absolute -inset-N` glows must stay in-viewport: a full-bleed component whose `max-w-*` box reaches the edge bleeds the glow past it → body scroll; keep every home-page component inside the standard `max-w-* mx-auto px-4 sm:px-6 lg:px-8` container. Verify with `apps/landing/scripts/overflow-audit.mjs [BASE_URL] [--app=landing|sales|docs]` (headless Playwright at 360/390/414px, separates escaping spills from contained); subagent/static analysis is NOT reliable for overflow. (#1284) **The audit auto-discovers React routes ONLY — it SKIPS the static-served landing pages (`src/app/<page>/route.ts` → `staticResponse("….html")`: `/pro`, `/agency`, `/use-cases`, `/how-it-works`, the whole `cold-email-*` cluster).** A "mobile review" that trusts the audit's pass count misses those pages — pass the explicit static routes too, or audit `public/landing/**.html` directly. Two real overflows hid there for months (`/cold-email-cost-guide` `.guide-channels` stayed 2-col at ≤640px → 2×202px spill; `/cold-email-for-saas-founders` inline `repeat(4,1fr)` stat grid couldn't collapse → 538px spill) because auto-discovery never loaded them. Distinguish REAL overflow (body `scrollWidth` > viewport) from contained element-spill (`scrollWidth == vw`, masked by `clip`) — only the former is a bug. (#1745)

### Landing voice — sell scale, not solitude
