The ICP ("The Serial Builder", `/investors`) is a solo / 1–3-person team running 3–10 products — keep "solo founder" as an SEO keyword + persona. **But never celebrate the solo lifestyle in copy** ("Stay solo. Go big.", "$0→$1M MRR solo", "you don't need a team" demotivate a founder who wants to grow + undersell the platform). The dream we sell = variable CAC + measurable channels per product across a portfolio. The investors dream quote stays "whether I stay 1 person or grow to 10." (#1162)

### Landing billing-truth invariants — the model is GOOGLE ADS, so "No subscription" is TRUE; sell RESULTS not the per-email price

- **The billing model is Google Ads: the customer sets a daily budget, is charged for what the campaign spent, and the balance tops itself up at a threshold so the campaign can keep running.** Nobody calls Google Ads a subscription, and neither do we. So **"No subscription", "No seats, no retainer", "Pay-as-you-go", "Cancel anytime", "Pause anytime" and "No monthly fee" are all TRUE and shippable** — there is no seat, no retainer, no term, no recurring fee, and stopping is a budget you set to zero. What stays banned is denying the **RECHARGE**, which is a different fact and is genuinely mandatory on a recurring campaign: never write "Credits never auto-renew", "no auto-renew", or "no surprise charges", because the balance really does top itself up. **Subscription ≠ auto-recharge.** The previous version of this rule conflated the two ("the product is a per-day SUBSCRIPTION with mandatory auto-topup → never write no subscription") and that conflation stripped a true claim off the homepage twice — #1739, then #3434, reverted the same day it shipped. Kevin 2026-08-18: *"il n'y a aucune subscription!!! On est comme Google Ads!!! Et oui ils peuvent cancel anytime."* The auto-topup fact that started this is a DASHBOARD launch-gate rule (recurring → auto-topup mandatory) and says nothing about what to call the model on the landing. Note the two halves of this file already agreed: the business-model section above calls "no seat, no retainer, no subscription" the actual selling point. (#1738, #3434)
- **Sell the RESULT, not the means.** Do NOT quote distribute's per-email/per-contact price ($0.07/contact, $1.75/reply). Quote industry-benchmarked **result economics: $4–$7 per click, $70–$250 per positive reply (= a buyer conversation worth engaging)**. Keep COMPETITOR figures untouched (LinkedIn $8–15/click, InMail $3–5/msg, agency $3–10k, SDR, DIY $0.65). Consequence accepted: distribute is no longer visually "cheapest per contact" — comparison visuals position it as mid-market done-for-you, not 1000× cheaper. The OFFER is **"$25 free credits"** = a $1-per-$1 match up to the first $25 spent (NOT a no-card free trial — a match needs a card). (#1732, #1728) **Same principle for the MECHANISM: landing copy never exposes internal model/workflow vocabulary ("best-performing playbook", "best model", "cheapest workflow that delivers", "cross-org workflow") — a visitor does not care which model produced the number. State the practical live price a user gets ("what a positive reply for a sales meeting costs right now, live"), not how we produce it. Kevin 2026-07-19: "on ne veut pas rentrer dans le détail technique de quel est notre model… c'est ce que ça donne en user en pratique." (#2810/#2811)

### Landing logo discipline — borrow trust from providers

distribute = "Stripe of Distribution" (thin wrapper over Apollo, Anthropic, Resend, LinkedIn, Muck Rack, Featured, Adobe, Gartner…). Wherever a provider/source/tool is named on a public page, show its logo via `apps/landing/src/components/provider-avatar.tsx` (`logo.dev` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOGO_DEV_TOKEN`, initial-letter fallback). NEVER hand-roll a provider SVG, NEVER ship the initial fallback as the intended UI — set the token in every env. New Channels-grid mappings in `apps/landing/src/data/feature-providers.ts`; stat/study mappings via `SourcedStat.providerDomain` / `ExternalStudy.providerDomain`.

### Feature maturity gating (alpha / beta / ga) — dashboard

Immature features stay in prod but are hidden from non-staff via **PostHog feature flags**, NOT `NODE_ENV`. `alpha` → staff only (flag on person-property `email = kevin.lourd@gmail.com`) · `beta` → opt-in cohort · `ga` → everyone, NO flag.
- Single source: `src/lib/feature-gates.ts` `FEATURE_GATES` (surface key → `{ flag, maturity }`). Flag name `<maturity>-<surface>`. **Each distinct gated surface gets its OWN flag — do NOT reuse an adjacent surface's flag for convenience** (couples graduation; the `<maturity>-<surface>` convention is one-flag-per-surface). When gating a new surface, create a dedicated PostHog flag mirroring an existing alpha flag's targeting (e.g. `alpha-brand-database` = Outlets/Journalists/Articles Database rows, staff-only, ≠ `alpha-brand-features`). (#1343: recommended reusing `alpha-brand-features`; Kevin picked "go dedicated".)
- `useFeatureFlag(flag): boolean` (`src/lib/use-feature-flag.ts`) — **default-hidden** (false until PostHog loads → no flash); subscribes `posthog.onFeatureFlags` to re-resolve after `identify`.
- `<MaturityBadge level>` (`src/components/maturity-badge.tsx`) — amber alpha / violet beta.
- Pattern: call `useFeatureFlag` at component top (hooks rule), gate the render, attach the badge:
```tsx
const ok = useFeatureFlag(FEATURE_GATES["services-crm"].flag);
{ok && <SidebarLink item={{ ..., maturity: FEATURE_GATES["services-crm"].maturity }} />}
```
- Graduation = widen flag targeting in the PostHog UI (no redeploy). Flags created/edited via PostHog MCP (`mcp__posthog__exec` → `create/update-feature-flag`). "Disappear for everyone" = hard JSX removal, NOT a flag defaulted-off.
- **EVERY surface that LISTS features must apply the brand-features filter `features.filter((f) => GA_BRAND_FEATURES.has(f.slug) || featuresAlphaOk)` — not just the sidebar.** There are THREE such surfaces and they must stay in lockstep: `BrandLevelSidebar` (sidebar), `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx` (overview grid), AND the `breadcrumb-nav.tsx` feature switcher (BOTH the app-level `appFeature` dropdown AND the brand-level `feature` dropdown render `visibleFeatures.map`, not `features.map`). A new feature-listing UI defaults to leaking alpha to non-staff if it maps the raw `useFeatures().features` — gate it. (#1410: breadcrumb switcher shipped without the filter; sidebar+overview had it, breadcrumb was the forgotten third surface.)
- **Deleting/renaming a flag the CURRENTLY-DEPLOYED prod code still reads HIDES the gated surface for EVERYONE (incl. staff) during the deploy gap.** `useFeatureFlag(<deleted>)` resolves false. So when a rename ships (`alpha-X` → `alpha-Y`, or an alpha→GA that drops a gate), delete the OLD flag only AFTER the new code is live in prod (Vercel deploy READY), never during the PR. Order: ship code → confirm prod READY → delete old flag. Deleting early = the gated entry vanishes until the new build lands. (#1338: deleted `alpha-feature-settings` while #1337 prod still gated the Feature Settings entry on it → entry disappeared for everyone for ~2 min until #1338 deployed.)
- **In the DASHBOARD app `useFeatureFlag()` is HARD-DISABLED — it returns `false` for everyone (alpha/beta live only in the admin app since the 2026-06-14 split). So a NEW dashboard surface that must ship to a small set of people (a beta) CANNOT gate on `useFeatureFlag` — it would be invisible to everyone, silently.** Gate on the email allowlist instead: `src/lib/beta-allowlist.ts` (`BETA_ALLOWED_EMAILS` + `isBetaEmail`) + `useIsBetaUser()` (reads the Clerk `useUser()` email), mirroring `apps/admin/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`. The gate applies to BOTH the sidebar entry AND the page body (page returns the "not available" card for non-beta). Drop the gate (not widen the list) to graduate to GA. Reference: the Signups / Booked Meetings / Sales outcome pages (#1654; the current cohort is in `BETA_ALLOWED_EMAILS` — Kevin + pressbeat). (The `FEATURE_GATES`/PostHog path below is admin-app-only now.)
- **The mirror of that rule, and the one that rots silently: an EXISTING dashboard surface still gated on `useFeatureFlag` is not "staff-only", it is DEAD — invisible to everyone, reachable only by typing its URL.** The hook returns `false` unconditionally, so `{flagEnabled && <SidebarLink/>}` renders for nobody. That reads in the diff as a staged alpha and behaves as an orphaned page, which is why it survives: nobody looks at it, so nothing about it gets fixed, and its contents drift out of date unnoticed. So when a task touches a dashboard surface, `grep` its nav entry for `useFeatureFlag` FIRST — if it is gated, the surface has been showing to no one since 2026-06-14 and "graduate it" means DELETING the gate (plus its `FEATURE_GATES` entry, which is then dead), never widening a PostHog flag. Treat everything on that page as unverified: it was last exercised by whoever wrote it. Cost 2026-08-16 (#3400, the org API-key page): gated on `alpha-keys`, so the owner did not know the page existed; three of its four install snippets had rotted into commands that fail on paste (see below).
- **A surface that prints a COMMAND or a PACKAGE NAME for a person to paste must have that command run before it ships — `npm view <pkg>` is five seconds and the failure is total.** This is the "prove the artifact WORKS through the real consumer" rule (see the investor-update markdown case) applied to install instructions: rendering the snippet proves nothing, because the consumer is a shell on someone else's machine. The org API-key page advertised `npx @distribute/mcp` and `@distribute/api-client`; **neither exists on npm** (the MCP repo is `@mcpfactory/mcp-service`, `private: true`, retired brand), and its curl authenticated with `X-API-Key`, which is api-service's separate ADMIN path — an org key sent that way is answered `401 Invalid admin key`. A user key is a Bearer token (`src/middleware/auth.ts`, path 2). Shipping a GA page whose commands 404 is worse than shipping no page, so the honest move is to delete every snippet that does not resolve and restore it when the package is real. (#3400)
- **The org API key page (`/orgs/:orgId/api-keys`) is GA and hands out exactly ONE thing: a Distribute key.** The second section, where an org pasted its own third-party credentials, was REMOVED — we run every vendor ourselves, so `listByokKeys` / `setByokKey` / `deleteByokKey` / `listKeySources` / `setKeySource` must not come back to this page (guarded: `tests/org-api-keys-ga.test.ts`; those readers survive in `lib/api.ts` with no caller). A key authenticates as its org through key-service and then reaches every route the dashboard does, with **no scopes and no expiry** — revoking is the only limit, accepted deliberately as of #3400. `/provider-keys` stays a redirect so old links resolve.
- **STAFF/GOD-MODE preview gating (distinct from beta) = `useIsAdminUser()` (`src/lib/use-admin-user.ts`, reads Clerk email → `isAdminEmail`), mirroring `useIsBetaUser`.** Use it (not the beta allowlist) for a surface only STAFF should preview. Gate BOTH the nav entry and the page body; badge rides the nav entry (per the whole-page-gate corollary below). Since the dashboard has NO edge allowlist, a page gated only on this hook is a UI-visibility gate over the viewer's OWN org data, never a data-security boundary. **The campaign-centered v2 Campaigns surface is the case this rule used to name, and it is now GA — every gate and every beta badge is GONE from it** (`campaignsOk = isRevenueFeature(featureSlug)` in `context-sidebar.tsx`, both sidebar levels badge-free, and neither `campaigns-page.tsx` nor `campaign-overview-page.tsx` reads `useIsAdminUser`; guarded by `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` + `tests/context-sidebar.test.ts`, which now fail if the hook reappears in the sidebar or either page). **Campaign re-intro is MULTI-STEP (owner-approved, #2761/#2762) — the campaign concept is being progressively re-added despite the "#1768 removed campaigns" notes elsewhere in this file; step 1 is READ-ONLY (list + per-campaign pipeline/$CAC/ROI/%CAC from features-service `getFeatureRevenueByCampaign` groupBy=campaignId + right panel + global header). Today = 1 campaign/brand so the list shows 1 row until multi-campaign creation lands. Before editing the brand sidebar or "campaign removed" surfaces, know this surface exists — extend it, don't re-invent or delete it.** (#2761, GA'd 2026-08-02)
  - **Campaign v2 step 2 (#2771) — per-campaign OVERVIEW + LEADS drill-down + a campaign SIDEBAR level.** `.../campaigns/[id]` renders `components/campaigns/campaign-overview-page.tsx`, a PARALLEL of the brand Overview rather than a shared body, to keep the GA page clear of staff-preview risk and its source-substring guards green. Revenue + `/stats` scope to `campaignId`; pipeline-activity / audiences / economics / pause stay brand-level (features-service serves those brand-scoped by design). `getNavigationLevel` gains a `"campaign"` level (`segments[4]==="campaigns" && segments[5]`) → `CampaignLevelSidebar`; `EngagedLeadsPage` takes an optional `campaignId`. Query keys are byte-equal to `OutreachStatCardsAuto`'s, so the polls dedupe.
  - **Campaign v2 step 3 — the per-campaign budget is DEPRECATED as a concept: money is funded per SALES FUNNEL and a campaign runs one funnel, so the ceiling belongs to the funnel.** campaign-service keeps a per-campaign figure only as a MIRROR of that funnel ceiling, because `gate-check` is the first node of every run and cannot call billing hot on a scale-to-zero compute. The campaign Overview keeps the brand-level read for the monthly outcome forecast, which asks a different question ("what does the money buy per month") and is answered with the SUM of every funnel's ceiling — a number that is right for the forecast and wrong under one campaign's name. Audiences + sales-economics stay BRAND-WIDE; per-campaign audience stats via `fetchFeatureAudienceStats(campaignId)`. ⚠️ This line used to end "so there is no per-campaign pause" and that was FALSE the whole time: `PATCH /campaigns/:id` has always taken `{status: "activate" | "stop"}`, proxied by api-service, and step 6 below now uses it. A note in this file saying a capability does not exist is a point-in-time assertion like any other — check the deployed contract (`api-registry`) before building around it, because the reflex when one is missing is to design a workaround, and here the workaround (defund the campaign) was destructive.

⚠️ **What stays deprecated is campaign-service's `maxBudgetDailyUsd` MIRROR, which nothing edits and nothing displays — not the idea of a per-campaign figure.** A campaign is (offer × funnel × channel) and billing keys a ceiling on exactly that triple (#3455), so the campaign's own money exists and three surfaces now state it: Campaign Settings EDITS it (#3456, the only writer), the Campaigns table states it per row, and **the campaign Overview states it read-only at its top right, beside the campaign's own status pill** (#3462) — a line, not a heading: the page states no campaign identity of its own (the top bar names the open campaign, once per screen), so this says only whether it is running and what it may spend while it does. That figure is NOT the run-status bar coming back: the bar printed the BRAND's sum next to a retired goal word, this is the campaign's own row at the campaign's own grain, and the pill and the whole-dollar formatter are the ones every other campaign surface uses so a campaign reads the same way in the list and once it is open. Several readers, one shared narrowing — so they cannot diverge. Guard: `tests/campaign-budget-once.test.ts`.
  - **Campaign v2 step 6 — PAUSING is a STATUS, not a budget of zero, and the same modal states and changes it at all three grains.** A campaign carries TWO independent answers to "is this running, and how hard": campaign-service's own `status`, and billing's `(offer x funnel x channel)` ceiling. Collapsing them (pause = set the ceiling to 0) is what this replaces, and it was never merely inelegant: zero throws the amount away, and billing's per-funnel floor lets a funnel funded UNDER its minimum be kept or raised and nothing else, so a grandfathered campaign stopped that way could never be restarted at the figure it was running. Nothing needed building for it — `PATCH /v1/campaigns/{id}` with `{status: "activate" | "stop"}` has been live all along (campaign-service maps them to `ongoing` / `stopped` and stamps `stopReason: manual`, which its own code documents as NOT auto-resumable — a person stopped it, a person restarts it), so this is one `setCampaignStatus` wrapper and no backend PR.
    - **ONE modal, three entry points, and its rows are always CAMPAIGNS.** `CampaignControlsModal` mounted through `CampaignControlsTrigger`, filtered by `{}` at brand grain, `{offerId}` at offer grain, `{campaignId}` on a campaign's own Overview. A brand and an offer are SCOPES, not things billing or campaign-service fund, so an editable figure at either grain would have to be split back across the campaigns and no split the customer did not state is honest. One campaign is a one-row modal, not a branch.
    - **The MONEY on the trigger is read, never recomposed.** Brand grain passes billing's own served total (`budgetData.dailyBudgetCents`, which the Overview already fetches for the cost card); offer grain has no served figure, so `scopeTotalCents` adds up the offer's own campaign ceilings — the same shape as the funnels card's per-offer total, and for the same reason. Do NOT sum the rows to obtain the brand's number: that is how two surfaces come to state one number two ways.
    - **The roll-up is three states, ordered and exhaustive** (`rollupStatus` → `active` / `partially-paused` / `paused`, plus `none` for a scope with no campaign). A mix is neither running nor stopped, and printing either word for it states something false about half the rows. `statusLabel` in the Campaigns table now reads `stopped` as **Paused** for the same reason: the row in the list and the pill on that campaign's page must say ONE word about one campaign, and from the customer's side a stopped campaign is one waiting to be turned back on. `isActiveStatus` is untouched, so the colour and the table's sort key are unchanged.
    - **Only what CHANGED is written, and status and budget never restate each other** (`controlsDiff`). The writes are a FAN-OUT — there is no bulk endpoint — so a failure is reported per row, the modal stays open, and it never claims a success it does not have. The floor is checked against the FUNNEL total across every row of that funnel the form is editing at once (`projectedFunnelTotalsUsd`), because this modal can move several of them in one go; billing holds the same rule and its 400 decides.
    - ⚠️ **`activate` FIRES THE WORKFLOW IMMEDIATELY** (`executeCampaignWorkflow` + `wakeScheduler`), so restarting spends now rather than at the next daily tick, and the summary above Confirm says so. `activate` also 400s without `x-brand-id` / `x-feature-slug`, which `setCampaignStatus` therefore sends rather than assumes.
    - The campaign Overview header is now the way IN to this modal, which is a deliberate change to the "editing stays on Campaign Settings" line above: the header still renders no field and holds no mutation, and every window writes through the SAME narrowing in `lib/campaign-budget.ts`. Several windows onto one number are fine; a second NARROWING is not. `lib/campaign-controls.ts` is alias-free so it carries REAL unit tests — keep it that way. Guards: `tests/campaign-controls.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-controls-surface.test.ts`.
  - **Campaign SETTINGS is the daily budget and NOTHING else (#3456)** — `components/settings/campaign-settings-card.tsx`, guard `tests/campaign-settings.test.ts`. It carried the campaign's name, its audiences, its services and its click destination; every one of those is a statement about the OFFER, which has its own Settings page one level up, so the screen was four editable copies of the offer's answer one click below the offer itself. They are gone, and `updateCampaign` / `CampaignSettingsPatch` went with them rather than being left unrendered — there is NO per-campaign settings write. Four things a future edit must hold: it writes `saveBrandFunnelBudget(brand, funnel, cents, channel, offer)` (billing's row, never the mirror); **ZERO is the point of the screen** and is an ordinary value, the way a customer stops one campaign without losing anything they stated about how it sells; the floor binds the FUNNEL total (`projectedFunnelTotalUsd`, computed only to check the form — nothing displayed derives from it, and billing's 400 decides); and a campaign naming no funnel or channel says so rather than pointing at a guessed row. **The narrowing that turns billing's per-pair figure into ONE campaign's money is `offerScopedCents` in `lib/funnel-channels.ts`, read by EVERY budget surface** — Offer Settings through `funnelChannelBudgets` for every channel of a funnel at once, and the three per-campaign surfaces through `lib/campaign-budget.ts` (`campaignBudgetScope` / `campaignSavedCents` / `campaignBudgetCents` / `fmtDailyBudgetUsd`), which Campaign Settings, the Campaigns table and the campaign Overview header all import. A second copy of it is how those pages would start disagreeing about the same campaign's money. `lib/campaign-budget.ts` is alias-free, so it carries REAL unit tests (`tests/campaign-budget.test.ts`) — keep it that way; a runtime `@/…` import there turns them into resolution failures.
  - **Campaign v2 step 4 (#3180) — the surface is called CAMPAIGNS again (#2902 had renamed it to Channels), it CREATES nothing, and it names the open campaign in the TOP BAR.** Route back to `.../brands/[brandId]/campaigns`; nav entry, page heading, empty state and breadcrumb crumb all read `Campaigns` (the campaign sidebar's back-link is gone, see the no-back-link rule below). **`NewCampaignModal` is DELETED, not hidden** — a campaign is set up with us, not spun up from a table row, so the create control and the clone-a-campaign modal behind it are gone (guarded: the page must not mention `New channel` / `NewCampaignModal`, and the file must not exist). **`HeaderPageContext` (`components/header-page-context.tsx`) is the ONE exception to "the top bar carries no breadcrumb"**: tenant identity (org, brand) stays in the sidebar switcher, but a campaign has no name anywhere in the sidebar and several sit under one brand, so the bar names the one you drilled into — on `.../campaigns/[id]` and nowhere else, on the `["campaign", id]` key the overview already polls (one deduped poll), with a skeleton rather than a placeholder word while the name lands. Do NOT extend it into an org/brand breadcrumb. The table's Channel and Goal columns read the brand-Settings catalogues through the shared marks (see the Acquisition Channels section). (#3180)
  - **Campaign v2 step 5 — STRATEGY + AUDIENCES live under the campaign, and the rule for that whole level is: show the campaign's own value where campaign-service stores one, else the brand's.** They used to link out to the brand pages (`${basePath}/…`), so drilling into a campaign and clicking Strategy silently left it. Now `campaigns/[id]/strategy` + `campaigns/[id]/audiences` render the SAME `StrategyPage` / `CustomerAudiencesPage` through an optional `campaignId` prop — the `EngagedLeadsPage` precedent, NOT a parallel body: the prop is absent on the brand routes, so the GA path is byte-identical and the existing source-substring guards stay green. `getNavigationLevel` (`segments[4]==="campaigns" && segments[5]`) and `campaignIdFromPath` (`parts[5]`) already ignore depth, so the campaign sidebar and the top-bar campaign name carry over to the sub-routes with no change.

Nothing here is saved yet.` Inline editing still works as a local draft (with Discard) — that is the "see how it would work" surface Kevin asked for. Fixed by adding the root (#2776). Rule of thumb when extending this surface: grep the new `useAuthQuery([...])` root, confirm it's in the allowlist; the reveal-on-settle gates only stop an ETERNAL skeleton on error, they do NOT make a non-persisted query fast. (#2776)
- **EVERY beta/alpha-gated surface MUST carry a VISIBLE `<MaturityBadge level="beta|alpha" />` next to it — gating WITHOUT a badge is a bug, not a "clean" hide.** A surface that only beta users can see still needs the badge so the viewer KNOWS it's beta (not GA) — otherwise even the people who can see it (incl. Kevin) don't realize it's gated and treat it as shipped. This applies to a whole gated PAGE/nav entry (`MaturityBadge` on the `SidebarItem.maturity` / page header) AND to a beta sub-element living inside an otherwise-GA page (e.g. the "Edit with AI" button on the GA brand-profile page — `{isBeta && <button>…<MaturityBadge level="beta" /></button>}`). When you add ANY `isBeta`/`isBetaEmail`/`useFeatureFlag` gate, add the badge in the SAME edit. Rule of thumb: if you can't point at the badge, the gate is incomplete. (Kevin 2026-06-25: "All beta gated must have the beta tags. I didnt know brand profile was beta gated!!!" — the brand-profile page was beta-gated with no badge for months.) **Corollary — NEVER beta/alpha-gate a WHOLE page + its nav entry silently. A full-surface gate (`if(!isBeta) return <not available>` + hidden `SidebarItem`) hides the surface ENTIRELY from the excluded user, so by definition they see NO badge (a badge inside the page is invisible when the page is blocked), and even an allowlisted owner doesn't notice the surface got gated → reads as "shipped/GA". So a whole-page/nav gate is NOT a code default you apply on your own — it needs explicit owner sign-off, and an in-page `MaturityBadge` does not satisfy the badge rule for it (the badge must ride the nav entry / page header that the gate still renders). Gating only a SUB-ELEMENT of an otherwise-GA page (Edit-with-AI button) is the safe form: the page stays visible, the badge rides the gated element. (Kevin 2026-06-25b: Audiences page+nav + Leads nav were beta-gated with the badge buried inside the blocked page — "Je suis enragé que tu as mis deux pages en beta gated sans me le dire"; #2190 made all three GA, kept only the Edit-with-AI sub-button beta + threaded `isBeta` so its badge never shows without its gate.)
- **The signup wizard REUSES the real feature editors — never re-inline them.** `components/brand-profile/field-editor.tsx` (`FieldEditor` + `SECTIONS`) was extracted from its page for exactly this; edit the shared module and both surfaces move together. Rate inputs are `type=text` with comma separators and decimals, NOT `type=number` (which cannot show commas and re-introduces the leading-zero `01` bug), and a prefilled rate is rounded to ONE decimal at the seed site — round the `rates` VALUE, not just its display text, or what is shown and what is saved diverge. (The `beta-onboarding.tsx` variant this described is DELETED; the live flow is `onboarding.tsx`, documented in its own section above.)
- **Adding an AI-EXTRACTED, display-only brand-profile field is CLIENT-SIDE ONLY** — a new `SALES_PROFILE_FIELDS` entry (`key` + AI `description`) flows through the generic `extract-fields` endpoint and the generic `Record<string, string|string[]>` storage, so do NOT spawn a brand-service workspace for it. **A USER-CHOSEN config value is the opposite**: if it needs a Brand Settings editor OR a backend consumer reads it (the click-destination URL outreach clicks land on), it is a first-class brand-service field — typed nullable column + `PUT /v1/brands/:brandId/<thing>`, mirroring `sales-economics` / `current-goal` — never the generic profile bag. (#2103, brand-service#295, api-service#611)
- **A workflow projection is server-derived from the brand's SAVED economics, so it must be refetched AFTER the save**, not once at a loading screen that runs before the rates are entered (stale default econ reads `$1/day` and `~0 signups`). The refresh is best-effort and keep-last-good: adopt it only when `costPerCloseUsd` and the counts are non-null, since a direct `getWorkflowProjection` call has none of the `structuralSharing` protection the query gets. (#1766)
- **POST-onboarding first-visit modal series + blocker surfaces use `driver.js` (MIT, React-19) — this is the dashboard's onboarding-tour / product-spotlight lib; reuse it, do NOT add react-joyride/shepherd/intro.js.** Mounted ONCE in `(authed)/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` alongside `CreditAlerts`, distinct from the SIGNUP `onboarding.tsx` wizard (that's the pre-dashboard flow; this fires AFTER a user reaches the dashboard). Three surfaces, all gated client-side off existing query keys (`["billingAccount"]`, `["audiences", brandId]` — dedupe, no new fetches):
  - **`WelcomeTour`** (`components/onboarding/welcome-tour.tsx`) — 5-card driver.js sequence shown ONCE per user via `localStorage distribute:welcome-seen:{userId}` (NOT a Clerk-metadata routing gate, reassurance only). Steps: why / $25-match credits / we-email-on-your-behalf (+ example email) / what's-next timeline / find-your-best-audience (spotlights the Audiences nav on desktop, centered card on mobile). Copy lives in the pure `lib/onboarding-content.ts` (`WELCOME_STEPS`), humanizer-clean + em-dash-guarded by `tests/onboarding-content.test.ts`.
  - **`OnboardingReminders`** (`components/onboarding/onboarding-reminders.tsx`) — state-based blocker modals after the tour, ONE at a time, priority topup then audience (`nextReminder` in `lib/onboarding-reminders.ts`): auto-topup off (`!has_auto_topup` opens `showPaymentRequired({proactive})`) then 0 active audiences (routes to audiences page). Session-dismissable per brand (`sessionStorage distribute:reminder-dismissed:{kind}:{brandId}`); re-shows next session while unresolved, never once resolved.
  - **`NoAudienceBanner`** (`components/onboarding/no-audience-banner.tsx`) — persistent red bar (mirrors the urgent credit-runway banner) when the brand in view has 0 active audiences; NOT dismissable (hard blocker, outreach can't run). Reads URL `brandId` via `useParams` (null off a brand route).
  - Gating logic + storage keys are PURE in `lib/onboarding-reminders.ts` (unit-tested); the React components own storage reads + fetches. `OnboardingFlow` (`components/onboarding/onboarding-flow.tsx`) sequences tour then reminders. driver.js popover is brand-themed via `.distribute-tour` in `globals.css`. (#2115)
- **The audience-step prewarm must NOT couple the ICP fetch with the audience-suggest call — `suggestAudiences` is FLAKY in prod (~57% fail: human-service `/orgs/audiences/suggest`, human-service#100) and `suggestBrandIcp` is healthy (~78% OK).** The prewarm IIFE (`hydrateOnboardingInBackground` → `audiencePrewarm`) ran `await suggestBrandIcp(id)` then `await suggestAudiences(id, prompt)` in ONE `try`, and the `catch` returned `{ prompt: "", candidates: null }` — so an audience-suggest failure AFTER the ICP already resolved DISCARDED the real ICP, and the audience step adopted `"" || fallbackPrompt` → always showed the generic `Find the ideal customers for <brand>` line instead of the real brand-service ICP. Fix: hoist `prompt` out of the `try` so the `catch` returns the already-resolved ICP (`candidates` stay null → manual "Find my perfect audiences" retry). Rule: when a prewarm chains a HEALTHY producer then a FLAKY one, the flaky one's failure must never nuke the healthy one's result. The ICP 422 ("Empty brand profile") is NOT the cause here — `brandProfileService.getByBrandId` derives a virtual v1 from `brandExtractedFields` (written by `extractBrandFields`, awaited first), so the profile is non-empty by the time icp/suggest runs. (#2096)
- **Outcome/metric/probability/EV surfaces compute SERVER-SIDE in features-service, dashboard renders only — extend `GET /v1/features/:slug/revenue?lens=` (`getFeatureOutcomes`), never re-derive client-side.** The Signups/Booked Meetings/Sales pages pass `?lens=signups|booked-meetings|sales`; features-service filters leads to the lens signal and returns per-lead `conversionProbabilityPct` + lens-specific `expectedRevenueUsd` (= proba × brand LTR; Sales = combined-OR `orP(pClick,pReply)` paid-close). A new outcome lens = a new enum value + backend math, not a client formula. Detail-panel data (status booleans, sent emails) is a legit client JOIN of existing endpoints (`listBrandLeads` + `listBrandEmails`) — a display affordance, not derived metrics. (#1654, #1660; features-service #283 / v0.47.0.)
- Live flags: `alpha-services-crm` (195453), `alpha-keys` (195454), `alpha-brand-info` (195479, Brand Info), `alpha-brand-features` (195480, every brand feature except GA exceptions), `alpha-conversions` (Revenue/Conversions), `alpha-workflows` (200358, Workflows page + its sidebar entries; the Feature Settings sub-level that hosts it is GA), `alpha-brand-database` (200359, brand Database rows Outlets/Journalists/Articles; Leads+Emails stay GA), `beta-campaign-activity` (200595, Live activity feed on the campaign overview — `CampaignActivity` self-gates + carries the beta badge; first BETA flag, opt-in cohort per `BETA_ALLOWED_EMAILS`). GA-exception slugs in `feature-gates.ts` → `GA_BRAND_FEATURES` (currently `sales-cold-email-outreach` only — `pr-cold-email-outreach` went alpha in #1339), consumed by `BrandLevelSidebar` + `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx` + `breadcrumb-nav.tsx` (the three feature-listing surfaces — keep in lockstep, see the filter rule above).

**Onboarding field extraction warms ONLY the 7 user-facing fields (`USER_PROFILE_FIELDS` in `api.ts` = services + the 6 Hormozi offer levers = the exact `USER_FIELD_KEYS` set) in `mode:"suggest"` — NEVER the full 19-field `SALES_PROFILE_FIELDS`.** The offer step reads these via `getBrandUserFields` and needs a best-effort value for every lever (`suggest` = generative Hormozi/top-3-expert persona, infers where the site/pasted-context is silent, never "Unknown"; the `suggest → user-fields GET` path is proven by the admin offer-levers card #2996). Two load-bearing rules a future edit must not regress: (a) **`dreamOutcome` MUST be in `USER_PROFILE_FIELDS`** — it is a user-field key that legacy `SALES_PROFILE_FIELDS` never listed (it kept the old `valueProposition`), so before this fix the Dream-outcome lever had no extraction source and always prefilled empty; (b) **do NOT warm the 12 backend-only `SALES_PROFILE_FIELDS`** (funding/competitors/leadership/…) during onboarding — the flow never reads them and the brand-info **alpha** page regenerates them on demand via its own Generate button; re-adding them is a pure LLM-cost waste. `extractBrandFields` takes an optional `mode` forwarded in the body (default absent = `extract`, api-service passthrough). General lesson (mirror of the dead-job "audit readers first" rule): before changing the MODE/behavior of a multi-field extraction/fetch, audit which fields the consumer actually READS and DROP the unread ones — do not preserve the call's scope just because it was there. (#2999)

### Auth / first-run gating — key on the real product signal, NOT org existence

**Routing/auth decisions belong at the EDGE (`proxy.ts`) via JWT claims — pre-paint, zero fetch. SWR is for DATA, never routing.** This governs EVERY landing/default redirect (onboarding, "last-visited brand", any "bare URL → entity" default): the FIRST move is the edge, not a client page. Remembered state goes in a **server-readable cookie** (the edge reads it pre-paint), NOT localStorage (client-only → client round-trip → flash). A client `useEffect`+fetch+`router.push` is the flash anti-pattern. A bounded client fallback is OK only where no remembered state exists yet (nothing to flash). (#1298: org-scoped `httpOnly` `last-brand-{orgId}` cookie set on brand URLs in `proxy.ts`, bare `/orgs/:orgId` redirects on it; the org Overview page is now an empty-org-only fallback — `return null` + redirect to last/first brand whenever the org has ≥1 brand. Don't restore an org-overview-first landing.)

**The cookie half of that rule is NOT limited to routing — it governs any REMEMBERED VALUE that must be on screen in the first frame, including a plain display label.** The sentence above lives in the auth section, so it reads as being about redirects, and a placeholder-flash bug does not look like a routing bug. It is the same bug. The test is one question: *is this value needed before hydration?* If yes, no client store can supply it — IndexedDB's restore is asynchronous and even a synchronous localStorage read happens at hydration, by which time the placeholder HTML has been on screen for the whole TTFB + JS-parse window. Only a cookie reaches the server render. So a "we already cache it, why is it still flashing" report is never answered by caching harder; re-derive WHEN each source becomes readable relative to paint. Worked example + the two-layer split (cookie owns the first frame, React Query owns freshness): the tenant-switcher section below. (tenant-identity-sync-paint)

**Don't gate on org existence** — a Clerk org is auto-created at signup, so `if (!hasOrg) router.push("/onboarding")` never fires. (#1229, DIS-91: 0 onboarding pageviews in 60d.)

**Custom auth flows (`sign-in`/`sign-up`/`forgot-password`) MUST import `useSignIn`/`useSignUp` from `@clerk/nextjs/legacy`, NOT `@clerk/nextjs`.** Clerk v7 ships TWO different hooks under those names: the bare `@clerk/nextjs` export is the NEW signals-based API (`{ signUp, errors, fetchStatus }` — NO `setActive`, NO `isLoaded`, different shape), while `@clerk/nextjs/legacy` is the classic `{ isLoaded, signUp/signIn, setActive }` API that the custom email/password + OAuth flows are built on. Grab the wrong one and `setActive`/`isLoaded`/`signUp.create` are `undefined` and the whole custom flow breaks. All three auth pages import from `/legacy`; keep it that way. Email/password uses `signUp.create` → `prepareEmailAddressVerification({strategy:"email_code"})` → `attemptEmailAddressVerification` → `setActive`; sign-in uses `signIn.create({identifier,password})` → `setActive`; reset uses `signIn.create({strategy:"reset_password_email_code"})` → `attemptFirstFactor` → `setActive`. `/forgot-password` is in `proxy.ts`'s public + auth matchers (public = signed-out can reach it; auth = signed-in bounce to `/orgs`). Email/password requires the Clerk Dashboard "Email address + Password" toggle to be ON (backward-compatible with Google). (#2754)

**An auth-failure PostHog event MUST carry the Clerk error CODE via `authFailureProps(err, ctx)` (`src/lib/clerk-error.ts`) — a bare `posthog.capture("x_failed")` (or one carrying only `{stage}`) makes the signup funnel undiagnosable, and "why did this user churn?" becomes unanswerable after the fact.** All five failure events (`signup_email_failed`, `signin_email_failed`, `password_reset_failed`, `signup_google_oauth_failed`, `signin_google_oauth_failed`) go through `authFailureProps`, which emits ONLY `{code, codes}` plus the caller's `stage`/`provider` — **never the Clerk `message`**, because Clerk interpolates the identifier into it (`"Couldn't find your account for x@y.com"`), so forwarding the message ships an email address into analytics. A non-Clerk throw reports `code:"unknown"` rather than omitting the field, so unclassifiable failures stay visible instead of blending into the pre-instrumentation events. `src/lib/clerk-error.ts` is the ONE home for `clerkErrorMessage` / `clerkErrorCode` / `clerkErrorCodes` / `isGoogleOnlyAccountError` / `sanitizeVerificationCode` — all three auth pages carried drifting local copies; do NOT re-inline them. **A source-substring guard asserting a Clerk error-code literal (`strategy_for_user_invalid`) must now read `src/lib/clerk-error.ts`, not the page** — the extraction rotted exactly two such guards in `email-password-auth.test.ts`, fixed in the same commit.

**Verification-code inputs (sign-up verify + forgot-password reset) MUST run `sanitizeVerificationCode` (digits only, sliced to `VERIFICATION_CODE_LENGTH`) plus `maxLength={VERIFICATION_CODE_LENGTH}`.** A code copied out of a mail client routinely carries a trailing space or newline and Clerk compares the string verbatim, answering "Incorrect code" on a CORRECT code, which reads to the user as a broken product. This is input normalization on a field that only ever holds a 6-digit number, NOT a silent-fallback / fail-loud violation. The verify screen owns its own recovery affordance too: a resend that CONFIRMS (`New code sent to <email>`) with a `RESEND_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (30s) disabled window, and a `form_code_incorrect`-gated hint that only the newest email's code is live. Origin: a real Google-Ads-sourced signup (Nigeria, Android mobile, 2026-07-27) failed verification twice in 45s then left; PostHog held only `{stage:"verify"}` so the cause was unrecoverable, and the plain grey "Resend code" text link read as dead (never clicked). Base rate then: 6 email signups started since the 20 Jul launch, 3 verified. Guards: `tests/auth-failure-diagnostics.test.ts`. (signup-code-incorrect)

**A sign-in status that is not `complete` is a STEP the flow owes the user, never one dead end — and the branch that handles it MUST capture, or the failure is invisible in the funnel that exists to catch it.** `signIn.create({identifier, password})` returns a `SignInStatus`, and only `complete` yields a session. The page collapsed every other value into a single `else` that printed *"Additional verification required. Try Google sign-in."* and captured nothing. Both halves were wrong, and they compounded:
- **This Clerk instance requires an emailed code as a SECOND FACTOR after a correct password**, so `needs_second_factor` is what every correct password returns — the happy path, not an edge case. The comment above that `else` asserted the opposite (*"needs_second_factor / needs_new_password etc. are not enabled on this instance"*), which is the assumption that shipped the bug. **Email + password sign-in therefore could not succeed for anyone, ever**, from its 20 Jul launch until 25 Aug: all 5 password accounts either reset their password (`reset_password_email_code` completes in ONE step, so `/forgot-password` was the only door) or used Google.
- **The copy named a provider the account may not have.** The customer who reported it (`andrew@voozaa.app`) has `password_enabled: true` and `external_accounts: []` — no Google identity at all — so the advice was a dead end. Naming Google is correct for EXACTLY ONE code, `strategy_for_user_invalid`, which means the account carries no password (verified against prod: a password attempt on a Google-only user returns it, an unknown email returns `form_identifier_not_found` instead).
- **The silence is why it lasted a month.** A non-complete status throws nothing, so `authFailureProps` never ran and PostHog held `signin_email_started` with no outcome — a stuck user is indistinguishable from one who wandered off. Every non-complete branch on all three auth pages now captures its own event carrying `status` (`signin_email_incomplete` / `signup_email_incomplete` / `password_reset_incomplete`), plus `signin_second_factor_started` / `_failed` for the new step. Fleet-wide there had been exactly ONE `strategy_for_user_invalid` ever and 12 `form_password_incorrect`, which is how a state-dependent bug reads when its main branch reports nothing.

Rules for the second-factor step (`sign-in/[[...sign-in]]/page.tsx`): it reuses the sign-up verify screen VERBATIM — same `sanitizeVerificationCode` + `maxLength={VERIFICATION_CODE_LENGTH}`, same 30s `RESEND_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` with a confirming notice, same `form_code_incorrect` hint — because a second implementation of a code input is a second place for the trailing-newline bug above to come back. `advanceSignIn` takes Clerk's `setActive` as an ARGUMENT (`activate`): extracting it out of the handler loses the discriminated-union narrowing `useSignIn` provides, so a closure over `setActive` is `possibly undefined`. **`prepareSecondFactor` is guarded on `!secondFactorPending`** — a second prepare mails a fresh code and silently invalidates the one being typed, which reads as "the code never works". Remaining statuses get their own sentence from `SIGN_IN_STATUS_MESSAGE` (a `Partial<Record<...>>`, so an unlisted one falls back to a line quoting the raw status rather than to Google).

⚠️ **The instance's MFA toggle can be turned off in the Clerk dashboard, which would make password sign-in one step and leave this branch dormant. Do NOT delete it if that happens** — the branch is what makes the page correct regardless of how the instance is configured, and the deleted comment proves how a config assumption baked into code ages. Verify a claim about instance auth config against Clerk (the FAPI response's `supported_second_factors`), never against a comment. Guards: `tests/sign-in-second-factor.test.ts`. (sign-in-second-factor)

**Current architecture (#1236, DIS-111):** the durable signal is `org.publicMetadata.onboardingComplete`, set on first brand via the in-repo server route `/api/onboarding/complete` (dashboard has `CLERK_SECRET_KEY`; `publicMetadata` is writable server-side, not from the browser). Surfaced as session claim `orgMeta = {{org.public_metadata}}`; `proxy.ts` redirects to `/onboarding` when `sessionClaims.orgMeta?.onboardingComplete !== true`. The same `orgMeta`/`email`/`firstName`/`lastName` claims drop the proxy's per-request `currentUser()`.

- **`onboardingComplete` MUST be written at the flow's TERMINAL step, NOT at brand creation.** The edge gate keys dashboard access on this single org-level flag, so the instant it flips `true` every dashboard URL passes. The beta guided flow (`beta-onboarding.tsx`, welcome→url→loading→objective→rates→personas→profile→consent→pricing) created the brand at the LOADING step and used to mark complete right there — 6 steps early — so a mid-flow **refresh or manual dashboard-URL nav** slipped past `proxy.ts` onto a half-set-up dashboard (no rates/personas/consent/campaign). Fix (#1770): the `POST /api/onboarding/complete` + `getToken({skipCache})` pair fires in `launch()` AFTER `createCampaign` succeeds (the genuine end); a mid-flow refresh now correctly bounces back to `/onboarding`. The DEFAULT flow (`default-onboarding.tsx`) is fine — its complete-write sits at brand creation which IS its last step (3-step flow). Rule for any new/edited onboarding flow: the complete-write goes at the LAST step only. Gate stays org-scoped (a per-brand check would need a fetch at the edge → violates JWT-claims-only routing; onboarding = first-brand setup so org ≈ brand). Abandon-before-terminal → re-runs onboarding next visit (brand upsert idempotent, no dupe) — correct, they didn't finish. (#1770)
  - **The org-scoped edge gate CANNOT catch an unfinished ADD-BRAND on an already-onboarded org ("org ≈ brand" breaks here) — a per-brand CLIENT gate covers it, and that is NOT a DIS-111 regression.** When an EXISTING org (already `onboardingComplete`) adds a NEW brand via `/onboarding?from=add` and abandons before the terminal launch, the brand row exists (created early at the loading step to prefill services) but its campaign does not (created only at `launch()`), yet the org flag is already `true` so `proxy.ts` opens every `/orgs/:id/brands/:brandId` URL → a half-built dashboard (no campaign/budget/active audience) is reachable. The edge fundamentally can't gate this (per-brand needs a FETCH; routing is JWT-claims-only). Fix (#2729): `components/brand/brand-setup-gate.tsx` (`BrandSetupGate`) — a CLIENT gate mounted in the brand layout (`orgs/[orgId]/brands/[brandId]/layout.tsx`, which STAYS an empty passthrough so `loading.tsx` still works; the gate is a client child, CreditAlerts pattern). It reads the brand's campaigns via `listCampaignsByBrand`; **zero campaigns = never finished** (1 campaign/brand, #1768) → `router.replace('/onboarding?from=add&brandId=<id>')`. **Fail-soft, mirroring the not-found gate:** redirect ONLY once the query SETTLES empty (`!isPending && !isError && length === 0`) — never on `isPending` (org-consistency gate / cold load) nor `isError` (a cold/failed campaign-service read must not bounce a COMPLETE brand). A brand with a campaign never redirects → god-mode / existing brands untouched. This does NOT re-introduce the flash-prone #1229 client brand-COUNT gate that DIS-111 removed (that was an ORG-level first-run gate on the dashboard layout, replaced by the edge claim) — this is a per-BRAND completeness gate for a case the edge can't see; the `onboarding-brand-gate.test.ts` DIS-111 guards (which assert the *dashboard* layout does no `listBrands`/`router.push`) stay green. **Resume:** onboarding accepts `?brandId=` (cross-session, when the sessionStorage snapshot is gone) — fetch the brand to seed its URL, replay the loading-screen hydration (idempotent `upsertBrand` + economics/projection/audiences from backend via `runResume(target, urlOverride)`), land on the GOAL step (not budget: pre-terminal audience picks + budget tier live only in the snapshot, so they must be re-picked; everything typed earlier is saved in backend + re-hydrated). A live same-tab snapshot still wins and resumes at the exact step (e.g. pricing). (#2729)

- **"New organization" / "New brand" / "Add brand" buttons MUST run the REAL `/onboarding` flow — never a stripped-down create modal.** Kevin's expectation: creating a brand or org from inside the app goes through the SAME onboarding as signup (services/goal/rates/personas/consent/budget/card), minus only the org-creation first step for a new brand. A `create-flows.tsx`-style mini-modal (just a URL field → `upsertBrand` → reload) was shipped once (#1599-era) and explicitly rejected. The flows already handle both cases natively — `/onboarding?from=add` (reuse active org = New Brand) and `/onboarding?new=1&from=add` (`forceNew` = New Org); `?from=add` skips only the welcome/booking intro and lands on the URL step. `/onboarding` is exempt from the first-run edge gate, so an already-`onboardingComplete` org is NOT bounced out. Don't re-introduce a stripped create-brand/create-org modal. (create-buttons-onboarding, #1985)
  - **ESCAPE HATCH for the add/new flow — an EXISTING user in `/onboarding?from=add` / `?new=1` MUST be able to return to a live org×brand mid-flow; only TRUE first-run signup is trapped.** #1985 keeps the REAL flow (don't strip it to a modal); it does NOT mean trapping an existing user with no exit. The trap (isolated full-screen layout, non-clickable logo, no breadcrumb) is correct ONLY for first-run signup (nothing to return to + the edge gate pins an incomplete org on `/onboarding` anyway). For `from=add`/`new=1` the active org is already `onboardingComplete` → the edge does NOT bounce them, so the no-escape was purely a UI omission. `components/onboarding/onboarding-top-chrome.tsx` (client, reads `useSearchParams`) renders, when `from=add||new=1`: a sticky header with a clickable logo + the dashboard `BreadcrumbNav` (org→brand switcher = jump to ANY existing tenant) + a Cancel link (logo+Cancel → `explicitHierarchyHref("/")` → last-visited brand) + the account widget; else (first-run) just the floating `OnboardingAccountWidget`, unchanged. `BreadcrumbNav` mounts on onboarding with ZERO new providers (it uses only global Clerk hooks + `fetch`, no OrgContext/Features/React Query). The flow body is untouched. Multitenant-onboarding UX leaders (Vercel/Linear/Stripe) keep the switcher + exit visible during a create flow — never a full-screen takeover for an existing user. Don't re-trap the add/new flow. (onboarding-escape-chrome)
    - **STAFF (`isAdminEmail`) ALSO get the escape chrome on BARE `/onboarding` (no `?from=add`/`?new=1`) — the god-mode org switch can drop a super-admin onto an incomplete/never-onboarded tenant as active org, which the edge gate then pins on `/onboarding` with NO exit.** Unlike the add/new case (active org already `onboardingComplete`), a staff-trapped org IS incomplete, so the trap fires for real — but staff have live tenants to return to, so trapping them is wrong. `onboarding-top-chrome.tsx` computes `isStaff = isAdminEmail(useUser().email)` and renders the escape chrome when `isAddFlow || isStaff`. `BreadcrumbNav` gates its all-orgs god-mode switcher on the same `isStaff`, so switching to any complete org clears the edge gate → escaped. Genuine first-run trap for NON-staff is unchanged. **A non-staff user who ALREADY has a COMPLETED org also escapes** — same edge-gate pin, but they have a live tenant to return to. Detected from Clerk memberships: `useOrganizationList({userMemberships:{infinite:true}})` → `hasCompletedOrg = data.some(m => m.organization.publicMetadata.onboardingComplete === true)`. Escape when `isAddFlow || isStaff || hasCompletedOrg`. A genuine first-run user has exactly one org and it's incomplete → `hasCompletedOrg` false → trap stays. Loading is trap-first (false until memberships resolve → no escape-flash on real first-run). (#2746, #2749)
    - **The onboarding ORG crumb label distinguishes add-BRAND from add-ORG — do NOT show the active org on `?new=1`.** `BreadcrumbNav` resolves the org crumb from the URL `/orgs/[orgId]` segment, which `/onboarding` lacks → `displayOrg` undefined → the `"Dashboard"` placeholder (the reported bug). The off-/orgs fallback to `useOrganization()` (active org) is gated by FLOW: `?from=add` (new **brand**) reuses the existing active org → show its name ✅; `?new=1` (new **org**) targets a brand-new org that is NOT the active one → the active-org name would MISLEAD, so it keeps the neutral placeholder. So `displayOrg` off-/orgs = `isNewOrgFlow ? undefined : (organization ?? undefined)`, where `isNewOrgFlow = useSearchParams().get("new") === "1"`. This is the ONE sanctioned `useOrganization()` use as an off-/orgs fallback (org-switching item 5) — still never bind the crumb to active org ON a `/orgs/:id` route (multi-tab oscillation). (#2653)

**Edge-claim gate migration — 3 traps:** (a) **Backfill** every existing entity that should pass BEFORE shipping (else claim-absent bounces all users; `apps/admin/scripts/backfill-onboarding-flag.mjs`). (b) **Stale token** — a claim is frozen at JWT mint, force `session.getToken({ skipCache: true })` before navigating. (c) **Exempt the loop + in-flight routes** (the onboarding route, all `/api/*`, the `?autoCreate` hop). Gate on "user COMPLETED the thing", not "container exists".

**Readiness gates MUST be monotonic** — never blank a mounted subtree on a transient auth-loading flip. Clerk's `isLoaded` flips back to `false` and `organization` blinks `null` during background JWT rotation (~1/min + focus/reconnect), so `show = !isLoading && hasOrg` blanks the body every rotation. Latch:
```ts
const resolved = useRef(false);
if (!isLoading && hasOrg) resolved.current = true;
const show = resolved.current || (!isLoading && hasOrg);
```
The data framework handles query data; a separate app-shell auth gate needs its own latch. (#1255)

### Org switching — cross-org isolation framework (defense-in-depth)

4th "don't act under the wrong context" member (after data/reveal/bucket). Keeps every API call + the whole cache bound to the viewed org. **Failure:** the proxy forwards the Clerk session active org (`auth().orgId`), decoupled from the URL `/orgs/[orgId]` (Clerk `organizationSyncOptions` syncs URL→session on page routes, NOT `/api/*`); after `setActive` the JWT rotates async → a lag window carrying the OLD org → a stale write commits under the old org (later 404) or a stale poll 404s. campaign-service isolation is correct; the 404 is the symptom. (DIS-143). Doctrine converges (Clerk + OWASP Multi-Tenant + tkdodo): derive tenant from JWT claims not client headers, validate every request, fail CLOSED, remount on switch.

Framework (every layer central, one file — never per-page):
1. **Server choke point — fail-closed proxy guard** (`src/lib/proxy-org.ts` `checkProxyOrg`): the client sends `x-active-org-id` (the URL org); the proxy compares it to `auth().orgId`, returns **409 `org_desync`** on mismatch, refuses to forward. The JWT stays the sole authority (client value detects the desync only — OWASP fail-closed, not trust-the-header). Applied to ALL proxy routes; a new route → call `checkProxyOrg` right after the `clerkOrgId` check. Constants in dependency-free `src/lib/org-desync.ts`.
2. **Client cache choke point — keyed remount** (`src/lib/query-provider.tsx`): inner `QueryClientProvider key={org.id}` → org switch = new client = empty cache atomically. REPLACES `queryClient.clear()` (raced). It is now the ONLY org-switch cache reset: the tenant switcher's org/brand labels are ordinary persisted queries under this org's persister prefix, so the remount clears memory AND the disk key space at once. The `OrgCacheInvalidator` component — which existed solely to clear the switcher's module-level caches, and whose cross-tab `router.push` was already removed in #1944 — was DELETED along with those caches; do not re-add a side cache that needs clearing. **The remount key MUST be MONOTONIC — latch it to the last RESOLVED org id, never raw `orgId ?? "no-org"`.** Clerk's `useOrganization()` blinks `organization: null` transiently during background JWT rotation (~1/min) + tab focus/reconnect; a raw key flips `realId→"no-org"→realId` on every blink → spurious remount → empty in-memory cache. Persisted queries rehydrate from IndexedDB silently (ALL live roots, big lists included, no 5MB cap since the 2026-06-25 per-query IndexedDB migration — so this blink-skeleton is gone). Fix: `const lastOrgId = useRef(null); if (orgId) lastOrgId.current = orgId;` then key + `orgId` prop both off `lastOrgId.current` — a null blink keeps the last key (no remount); a real switch to a DIFFERENT org still advances the id → remount + fresh per-org persister prefix, DIS-143 intact. Same monotonic-gate rule as the app-shell auth latch. (#1767)
3. **Source — `await setActive` THEN `await session?.getToken({ skipCache: true })`** (`breadcrumb-nav.tsx` `handleOrgSwitch`) before `router.push`. `await setActive` alone is NOT enough: it resolves on the CLIENT before its `Set-Cookie` propagates, so the very next navigation reaches `proxy.ts` `organizationSyncOptions` carrying the STALE token (active = previous org / not-a-member of the target). Clerk bounces the URL back → `OrgActivator` re-syncs the client to the previous org → **the switch reverts on its own** (loudest in STAFF god-mode, where the target is a just-joined customer org whose membership the stale JWT lacks). The `getToken({ skipCache: true })` re-mint forces the fresh token (new active org + new membership) into the cookie BEFORE the middleware reads it — same proven fix as `beta-onboarding`'s onboarding-complete hop (CLAUDE.md "Stale token — force `getToken({ skipCache: true })` before navigating"). Guarded by `org-switch-cache.test.ts` (getToken must sit between setActive and router.push). (#1940)
   - **That correct ordering is also SLOW and entirely INVISIBLE, so the click has to be answered locally — a switch that shows nothing reads as a dead button, and reverting the ordering to "feel faster" is the one thing that must never happen.** Two or three Clerk round-trips run before `router.push`, and every label on screen is keyed on the URL org, which has not moved yet. Reported as *"je change d'org depuis le Org selector, il ne se passe rien, des fois je dois attendre 30s"*. Four invariants in `use-tenant-switcher.ts` + `tenant-switcher.tsx`, guarded by `tests/org-switch-instant.test.ts`: (a) `setSwitchingOrg` is set SYNCHRONOUSLY, before the first `await` — set after, the spinner appears at the moment it stops being needed, so the guard asserts the index, not the presence; it carries the NAME because a god-mode target is routinely in none of the lists we hold; (b) the staff join round-trip is SKIPPED when Clerk's already-loaded client-side membership list has the membership — authoritative-POSITIVE only, since that list is paginated, so an absent membership still goes through the idempotent route (the paged-precheck trap the join route itself documents); (c) an org row does NOT go through `go()` — closing the menu first removes the only surface that can show the switch running, so the ARRIVAL closes it (`useEffect` on `t.orgId`); (d) the pending flag clears on ARRIVAL, not when the promise resolves — `router.push` returns long before the destination renders.
   - **The permanent-hang half was a swallowed error, and it is the reason the fail-loud rule applies to click handlers too.** A failed join did `console.error` and CONTINUED into `setActive`, which REJECTS for a non-member org; that rejection was unhandled inside a click handler, so `router.push` never ran, nothing was rendered, and the switcher stayed dead until a reload. A non-ok join now throws, the whole handler is wrapped, and the refusal is stated in the menu. `getToken({ skipCache: true })` likewise lost its `.catch(() => {})` — a failed re-mint IS the self-reverting switch of #1940, so it belongs where the user is told, never swallowed.
4. **Read gate** (`src/lib/use-auth-query.ts`): `enabled: caller && urlOrg === activeOrg`. **LATCHED against Clerk's transient `organization: null` blink** (JWT rotation ~1/min, tab focus): the gate compares `urlOrgId` to `effectiveActiveOrgId = activeOrgId ?? lastResolvedActiveOrgId.current` (a `useRef` that only ever stores a NON-null active org). Without the latch a null blink flips the gate false → v5 disabled query → `isPending: true` FOREVER → any `isPending`-gated skeleton (leads page) hangs, and once the IndexedDB cache is evicted (see persist section) it hangs PERMANENTLY = the infinite-skeleton report. The latch fills ONLY a null — a genuinely DIFFERENT non-null active org (real switch / cross-tab) STILL closes the gate, so a null-from-cold never fires a read and DIS-143 cross-org isolation is intact (never fire a URL-org read under another org's token → proxy 409). NO `|| !activeOrgId` escape (a read under a truly-null active org lands in a null-org cache entry, TanStack #3743). `useOrganization` import allowed here (powers the gate, not token-passing). Guarded by `org-switch-cache.test.ts`. (KevinLourd/leads-skeleton-persist-orggate)
5. **MULTI-TAB — scope every request by the PER-TAB token, NEVER the shared cookie; never navigate a tab on the shared active-org signal.** Clerk supports a per-tab active org, BUT **the session cookie is one global singleton for the whole browser** — it reflects whichever tab was focused last ([Clerk: Organizations → "multiple browser tabs"](https://clerk.com/docs/guides/organizations/overview)). So scoping data off the cookie (`auth()` with no Bearer) makes a background poll / navigation from a NON-focused tab run under the WRONG org → cross-org bleed + 409 churn; and any code that NAVIGATES a tab when the (shared) `useOrganization()` active-org changes yanks tab A onto tab B's org the instant tab B switches — the "org switches by itself across tabs" bug. Two-part fix (#1944), per Clerk's own multi-tab guidance + WorkOS/SuperTokens "the token IS the tenant context":
   - **Data path:** `api.ts` `apiCall` attaches THIS tab's token — `Authorization: Bearer ${await window.Clerk.session.getToken()}` (via `getTabSessionToken()`, a non-hook read of the per-tab `window.Clerk` client) — on every `/api/v1/*` proxy call. Clerk's `auth()` honors a Bearer over the cookie ([Clerk: making authenticated requests](https://clerk.com/docs/guides/development/making-requests)), so the proxy scopes off the per-tab org. Optional-chained → before Clerk loads / signed-out it falls back to the cookie, and `checkProxyOrg` (item 1) still fails closed, so there's NO new exposure even if a Bearer is ever absent.
   - **Navigation path:** nothing navigates on an active-org CHANGE (#1944 stripped `router.push`/`useRouter`/`usePathname` from `OrgCacheInvalidator`, which has since been deleted entirely). The URL `/orgs/[id]` is the per-tab source of truth; in-tab switches navigate via the switcher (`handleOrgSwitch`), URL→active alignment via `OrgActivator`. Do NOT re-add a navigate-on-active-org-change effect — it re-introduces the cross-tab self-switch.
   - **Display / cache-key path (#1948 — the one that actually stops the VISIBLE oscillation):** #1944's data+nav fixes were necessary but NOT sufficient — the org name kept flipping between tabs because the CLIENT UI still keyed on `useOrganization()` (the shared active org that flips cross-tab). Every per-tab client surface must key on the **URL org** (`/orgs/[id]`), never the active org: (a) `query-provider.tsx` remount key = `urlOrgId ?? organization?.id` (URL is per-tab-stable → no remount storm when a sibling tab switches; falls back to active org only OFF the /orgs/ tree, e.g. onboarding); (b) the tenant surfaces render the URL org via a per-orgId snapshot (the `["orgIdentity", orgId]` query, written only when `organization.id === orgId`) so the name/avatar never flip; dropdown highlight compares `orgId === o.id`, not the active org. RULE: in a multi-tab org app, treat the URL as the per-tab source of truth for ALL of {data scope, cache key, displayed org, nav}; `useOrganization()` is shared/global and may only feed (i) the cross-tab-stable parts via a per-URL-org cache, or (ii) off-/orgs/ fallbacks. Don't bind a per-tab surface directly to `useOrganization()`.
   - Guarded by `org-switch-cache.test.ts` (per-tab Bearer guard + no-cross-tab-navigation guard + URL-keyed remount guard) + `breadcrumb-hierarchy`/`feature-overview-polish`/`last-brand` (URL-org display). Note the cookie-era item 3 (`getToken({skipCache})` after `setActive`) is STILL correct for the focused-tab navigation; item 5 makes the DATA + DISPLAY paths immune to the cookie regardless of focus.
   - **BRAND crumb — resolve the label by a per-URL-id FETCH (`getBrand(brandId)` → `GET /api/v1/brands/:brandId`), NOT by `find()` in the dropdown LIST.** The brand crumb long resolved only via `brands.find(b => b.id === brandId)` over a module-level, non-org-keyed, 60s-cached `/api/v1/brands` list (`brandListCache`) that cleared only in `handleOrgSwitch`. Any nav skipping that path (god-mode deep-link, cross-tab, direct URL) served a DIFFERENT org's list → `find` missed → the crumb stuck on the `"Brand"` placeholder PERMANENTLY. #2364's `brandDisplayCacheRef` keep-last-good could NOT save it — the brand never resolved once, so there was no first value to keep (keep-last-good fixes flicker, not a never-resolve). Fix (#2416): a by-id read on `brandId` (same source the overview page uses) feeds `displayBrand` when the list misses, resolving under the same active org the page already renders the brand with. RULE (same as the org crumb): a per-URL-entity crumb LABEL must come from a by-id read of that entity, never from membership in an org-scoped list that may be cached under the wrong org. (#2416, follow-up to #2364. The org-keyed `brandListCache` that #2416 also introduced is gone — both reads are React Query now, see the SWR item below.)

6. **A flow that CREATES its org cannot live under an org-keyed provider — `/onboarding` opts out via `<QueryProvider scope="onboarding">`.** The keyed remount in item 2 is a cache reset in the dashboard, where the org changes only when the user switches to one that already exists. Onboarding is the opposite case: `?new=1` calls Clerk `createOrganization` + `setActive` INSIDE `createBrandAndFetchServices`, so `useOrganization()` flips to a brand-new id in the middle of the loading screen. Under an org-derived key that remounts `OrgScopedQueryClientProvider` and therefore the whole `<Onboarding/>` subtree (the onboarding layout mounts the provider ABOVE it) — every `useState` resets, `resolveResumeStep("loading", null)` sends the restored snapshot back to `"url"`, and the user lands on the step BEFORE the one they were on. The `createBrandAndFetchServices` promise keeps running in the detached closure, so the brand IS created in the new org, invisibly; prod ended up with one `claude.ai` brand row linked to three orgs from three attempts, and the org-desync warnings in the Vercel logs named a different new org each time. `?from=add` (new brand, existing org) never hit it — `reuseOrg` is true there, so nothing flips. Two things the fix must keep: the scope is a PROP passed by the layout, never a `usePathname` check (the pathname updates while the terminal `router.push("/orgs/<new>/…")` is in flight, which would remount the wizard one last time on its way out); and it passes `orgId={null}`, not the stale previous org — the persister is org-prefixed, so holding the old id would file the new org's queries under the old org's disk keys, which is the exact bleed the prefix prevents. Onboarding therefore runs on an in-memory cache, which costs nothing (transient route; first-frame tenant identity comes from the server-read cookie). Isolation is unchanged: the terminal push leaves this subtree for the dashboard's own provider, which keys on the new org. Guard: `tests/onboarding-org-remount.test.ts`.

**Self-heal:** on 409 `org_desync`, `apiCall` waits 500ms + retries once. **Rule:** JWT authority server-side every request fail-closed; cache resets by remount not `clear()`; remount-surviving nav lives above `QueryProvider`. (DIS-143)

### Dashboard content width — content-driven, never cap-without-centering

No global width knob: the authed shell `<main>` is full-width; each page sets its OWN `max-w-*` wrapper. A cap WITHOUT `mx-auto` left-hugs with an asymmetric right gutter (the bug — brand overview was `max-w-4xl` = 896px, looked "stops short / weird"). Industry consensus (Atlassian fluid/fixed grid · Material body-region · Baymard/WCAG line-length) = width is **content-driven**:
- **Dense pages** (dashboards, card grids, lists, tables) → `p-4 md:p-8 max-w-7xl mx-auto` (fluid up to ~1280px ceiling, then centered). Tailwind v4 here → `max-w-7xl` is the largest named (`max-w-screen-2xl` was REMOVED in v4; use `max-w-7xl` or arbitrary `max-w-[96rem]`).
- **Reading / forms** → keep narrow (`max-w-2xl`/`max-w-3xl` ≈ 65–80ch) for line length — widening a form to 1280px hurts readability.
- ALWAYS `mx-auto` when capped (Material fluid-margins) so there's no asymmetric gutter.

Applied to the dense surfaces (brand overview, org overview, brands list, brand-info). Forms (`brands/[brandId]/launch`, settings, billing, api-keys) stay narrow. (#1300-follow-up)

### Dashboard mobile — tables scroll, never crush; breadcrumb is mobile-visible

- **A dense `<table>` MUST live in an `overflow-x-auto` wrapper with a `min-w-[Npx]` on the table** — NOT the copy-pasted `<div className="bg-white rounded-xl border border-gray-200 overflow-hidden">` wrapper, which makes columns squeeze/wrap into mush on a phone instead of scrolling. The `overflow-hidden`+`w-full` pattern was duplicated across several surfaces (brand quote-requests, brand leads, visibility prompts/competitors/runs/run-detail; the campaign-level quote-requests/leads variants were removed in #1768) — when adding a new table, start from the `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w` form. (#1746)
- **BUT the `min-w` floor must be BREAKPOINT-GATED (`md:min-w-[720px]`), never unconditional — an unconditional floor OVERRIDES every `hidden sm:table-cell` you added and the phone scrolls anyway.** The two halves fight each other: hiding columns narrows the row, then a bare `min-w-[720px]` re-widens it past the viewport, so the visible columns are pushed off-screen and the reader concludes the data is missing rather than off to the right. The leads table shipped that way for months — Status sat at `hidden sm:table-cell` AND behind a 720px floor, so a phone showed neither the tag nor a reason. Gate the floor at the breakpoint where the folded columns come BACK (`hidden md:table-cell` columns ⟹ `md:min-w-[…]`), and state the mobile column set explicitly.
- **A column carrying free text needs `table-fixed` + an explicit width to truncate — `truncate` ALONE does nothing in a table.** The default `table-auto` sizes a column to its content, so `truncate` has no bounded width to work against and one long value (an audience name, a company name) widens the whole row: measured 649px for a single cell in a 360px viewport, i.e. the row overflowed even with only two columns left. Fix is `table-fixed md:table-auto` plus a percentage width per mobile column (`w-[62%] md:w-auto` / `w-[38%] md:w-auto`), and `min-w-0` on the flex wrapper inside the cell. Reference: the leads table + `AudienceCell` in `components/audiences/engaged-leads-page.tsx`.
- **A column FOLDED into a neighbour on mobile reads its value from ONE binding, and the two placements are strict complements.** The leads table folds Contact / Audience / Date away below `md`: the first cell shows a 40px company mark beside the company name with the audience under it, and the date sits under the status tag, each `md:hidden`, while the columns themselves stay `hidden md:table-cell` — so a value is on screen exactly once at every width. The date is computed once into a `dateNode` const rendered by both the stacked line and the column; recomputing it per placement is how two spots start disagreeing. A row whose folded value is absent (a lead attributed to no audience) simply has no second line — a dash there would read as a value. **The company leads that cell, not the audience** (owner-picked, and it also keeps the row identifiable: the audience repeats across rows while the company does not). `CompanyLogo` takes a `size` prop applied as a STYLE, because a Tailwind class built from a prop is invisible to the compiler — the same reason `AudienceAvatar` does it that way; it requests `size * 2` from logo.dev for retina and carries `shrink-0` since the sibling text truncates.
- **Verify a table fits by MEASURING the render, never by reading the classes** — a source guard proves the class is spelled right and says nothing about whether the content fits. Playwright at 360px and 412px, asserting `wrapper.scrollWidth <= wrapper.clientWidth` plus `getComputedStyle(cell).display` per folded column, with the OLD markup on the same page to prove the fix. Both of this change's real defects (the unconditional floor, then the un-truncatable free text) were invisible to green source guards and to `tsc`. (leads-table mobile fit)
- **The header breadcrumb (`components/header.tsx` → `BreadcrumbNav`) is visible at ALL widths** — do NOT re-add `hidden sm:block` (it left mobile users with only the distribute logo: no org/brand name, no org switcher). The `<nav>` in `breadcrumb-nav.tsx` scrolls horizontally on narrow screens (`overflow-x-auto`) and flips to `overflow-visible` while a dropdown is open so the absolutely-positioned switcher panel isn't clipped by the scroll container. (#1746)
- **A page-by-page mobile/load AUDIT by subagents over-reports** — it invented a `min-w-[500px]` and a 7-col table in a page that actually delegates to a shared component. Confirm each flagged defect against the EXACT served string (grep the literal) before sweeping; the "split-panel `absolute inset-0` detail overlay" is the intended mobile drawer, NOT a bug. (#1746)

### Dashboard chrome = L-shaped shell + sidebar-top tenant switcher (GA, single path)

**The header carries NO product mark, NO `distribute.you` wordmark and NO breadcrumb — that is deliberate, not an oversight; do not "restore" them.** The shell is L-SHAPED: the sidebar is a FULL-HEIGHT column to the LEFT of the header column (`layout.tsx`), so the sidebar's top block sits level with the header row. Tenant identity + switching live in `components/tenant-switcher.tsx` `<TenantSwitcher>`, passed to `SidebarSection` via the `topSlot` prop; the top bar keeps only universal actions (theme, account), per Atlassian. There is exactly ONE chrome — the pre-switcher path was deleted at GA, not gated off, and `tenant-switcher.test.ts` fails if `useIsBetaUser` reappears in `context-sidebar.tsx` or the dashboard `layout.tsx` (the header may still read it, but ONLY for the unrelated beta Profile entry — never to branch the chrome).

**The two rows sit SIDE BY SIDE, so their height must match by CONSTRUCTION — one shared token, never a hand-measured pixel value, and the border never on the sized element.** Both the header's inner row and the switcher button read `CHROME_ROW_HEIGHT` from `src/lib/chrome-row.ts` (`h-14`). Two traps, both hit on the first attempt: (1) the header's height is NOT its `py-*` plus the obvious icon — it's `py-2.5` around whatever control is tallest, and that's the account button (`p-1` + a 28px avatar = 36px), so it renders ~57px, not the 49px a quick read suggests; hardcoding the miscount left a visible seam. (2) Tailwind is `box-sizing: border-box`, so a `border-b` ON the `h-14` element eats into the 56px while the header's `border-b` sits on `<header>` OUTSIDE its `h-14` inner row — a 1px mismatch. The switcher therefore puts its border on the WRAPPER (`className="relative border-b border-gray-200"`) and keeps the button pure `h-14`. Guard: `tenant-switcher.test.ts` pins both to the token and forbids a literal `h-[49px]`.

**ONE implementation of tenant identity + switching: `src/lib/use-tenant-switcher.ts` (`useTenantSwitcher`).** Both `breadcrumb-nav.tsx` and `tenant-switcher.tsx` consume it — org/brand path parsing, the three identity reads (`["orgIdentity", orgId]`, `["brands"]`, `["brand", brandId]`), the staff god-mode all-orgs list, and `handleOrgSwitch` (join → `await setActive` → `getToken({skipCache:true})` → `router.push`, the DIS-143 race close). `OrgAvatar` is likewise a shared component (`components/org-avatar.tsx`). Do NOT re-inline any of it into a surface — the guards for the org-switch race now read the HOOK file (`org-switch-cache.test.ts`, `clerk-org-sync.test.ts`, `breadcrumb-hierarchy.test.ts`), not `breadcrumb-nav.tsx`. **`breadcrumb-nav.tsx` is NOT dead code and must not be deleted**: the dashboard no longer mounts it, but `onboarding-top-chrome.tsx` still does (guarded by `onboarding-escape-chrome.test.ts`) — a `grep` for `<BreadcrumbNav` that only covers the dashboard tree will wrongly read as zero consumers.

**Every identity read in the switcher goes through React Query, so the org name / brand name / brand logo paint from disk on the FIRST frame — a raw `fetch` or a module-level cache here is a UX bug, not an optimization.** The switcher was for months the ONE dashboard surface bypassing the local-first per-query IndexedDB persister: `/api/v1/brands` + `/api/v1/brands/:id` via raw `fetch` behind module 60s caches, and the org label in a `useRef`. None of those survive a page load, so every hard navigation (org page, brand page, billing) showed `Dashboard` / `Brand` and an empty logo slot for as long as the cold gateway → brand-service chain took — reported as "le logo et le nom passent quelques secondes en placeholder". The three reads are now `["orgIdentity", orgId]` (a snapshot of Clerk's org, written only when `organization.id === orgId` — Clerk is the ONLY source of an org name and it hydrates async, so without the disk snapshot the label cannot beat Clerk), `["brands"]` → `listBrands`, and `["brand", brandId]` → `getBrand`.
