Load-bearing details: the latter two are the SAME keys the org-overview / billing / brand pages already use, so they DEDUPE instead of doubling; all three roots must stay in `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` (an unlisted root is default-OFF and silently loses the instant paint); the list's loading flag is reveal-on-settle (`isPending && !isError`) so a failed read falls to the empty state rather than an eternal "Loading…"; and going through the shared api client means each request carries THIS tab's Clerk token instead of the shared session cookie (the raw `fetch` could resolve a label under another tab's org). Guarded by `tenant-switcher-swr.test.ts`. (brand-crumb-swr-placeholder)

**That fix was necessary and NOT sufficient: no client-side cache can paint the first frame, because IndexedDB is ASYNCHRONOUS. The identity therefore ALSO lives in a server-readable COOKIE, and both layers must stay.** The per-query persister restores in a `useEffect` (`persister.restoreQueries` / `reseedColdQueriesFromDisk`) — strictly after paint — so a hard refresh was *architecturally guaranteed* to render `Brand` + the globe first and swap the real values in a moment later. "Cache harder" cannot fix an ordering problem: the browser has already painted the server's HTML before any client store is reachable. The repo's own doctrine already said so under the first-run/auth section ("remembered state goes in a **server-readable cookie**, NOT localStorage — client-only means a round-trip and a flash"), and localStorage is no better than IndexedDB here for the same reason: even a synchronous read happens at *hydration*, long after the placeholder HTML is on screen.
- `src/lib/tenant-identity-cookie.ts` — the pure blob (`distribute-tenant`, version-tagged, LRU-capped at 4 orgs / 8 brands, single-letter keys). It rides EVERY request to the origin including each `/api/v1/*` proxy call, so the caps are load-bearing; a size guard pins it under 4KB. Deliberately alias-free so vitest can run it (`tenant-identity-cookie.test.ts` is real unit tests, not source-substring) — do NOT add an `@/…` import.
- `(authed)/layout.tsx` reads it with `cookies()` and passes it to `TenantIdentityProvider`, so the real name + logo are in the SSR HTML and the client hydrates against the identical value (no mismatch). Safe there specifically: `ClerkProvider dynamic` already opted that subtree out of static rendering, `/report/*` sits OUTSIDE it (the ISR split is untouched), and the `loading.tsx` boundaries live under `(dashboard)`, well below — the CLAUDE.md ban on `cookies()` in a layout targets the INTERMEDIATE `[orgId]`/`[brandId]` layouts, which stay empty passthroughs.
- Deliberately NOT httpOnly: the client is what learns the identity (Clerk for the org name, brand-service for the brand) and therefore what writes it back, via `remember` in `use-tenant-switcher.ts`. Contents are display labels the user is already looking at — no token, no id the URL doesn't carry.
- **Precedence is live Clerk → React Query → cookie seed, and the seed must stay LAST.** Ordering it earlier pins a renamed brand to its old name. Guarded by an index-comparison assertion, not a substring.
- **An UNKNOWN tenant renders a skeleton, never `Brand` + a globe.** A fabricated identity reads as the product having lost the brand — which is exactly the reported complaint — so `orgKnown` / `brandKnown` gate both switcher surfaces on the tenant the URL is on (a brand page must not fall back to the org's name). With the cookie in place this only appears on a first-ever visit to a tenant on that browser.
- The write-back effect depends on PRIMITIVES (`rememberedBrandName`/`rememberedBrandDomain`), never on the `displayBrand` object — that object is rebuilt every render, and `mergeTenantIdentity` returning the SAME reference on a no-op is what stops a poll re-writing the cookie each tick.
Guards: `tenant-identity-cookie.test.ts` + the "first-frame identity" block in `tenant-switcher-swr.test.ts`. (tenant-identity-sync-paint)

**The switcher panel is the whole IA, and it is capped at TWO tiers by design** (Carbon: "the left panel does not support three tiers"; GitLab Pajamas: "there is not a third level of depth"). Org row → brand row (drawn as its child via a **1px** connector rail + a disclosure chevron — indent alone is not hierarchy, and a colored side-border accent >1px is banned repo-wide), then org-scoped **Billing**. Only ONE submenu is ever open (`useState<"org" | "brand" | null>`), which is what makes a third tier structurally impossible. "New organization" / "New brand" sit at the bottom of their section and route into the REAL `/onboarding?new=1&from=add` / `?from=add` flows (never a stripped create modal, #1985).

**Each submenu opens as a FLYOUT to the right of the parent panel on `md+`, and STACKS under its row below `md`** (`components/tenant-switcher.tsx` `<Submenu>` — unprefixed classes are the stacked mobile case, `md:absolute md:left-full md:top-0` is the flyout). Side-by-side is Atlassian's app-switcher shape and the one NN/g points to (it rates a nested two-tier dropdown "frustrating"); a flyout is impossible below `md` though, because the sidebar is 224px and becomes a drawer. When asserting "no bare `absolute`" in a guard, remember `\babsolute\b` MATCHES inside `md:absolute` (`:` is not a word character) — match on whitespace/quote boundaries instead.

**Mobile: `<MobileTenantChip>` in the header is a FULL switcher, not a drawer trigger.** It renders the same `<TenantMenu>` anchored under the header (viewport-bounded + scrollable). Routing it through the drawer would cost two taps and put the flyout inside a 224px panel. Consequence to keep in mind: the chrome mounts `useTenantSwitcher` up to THREE times at once (desktop sidebar + the drawer's sidebar + the chip), so a raw `fetch` added to that hook fires three times per navigation. Every read in it goes through React Query, which dedupes by key — keep it that way.

**There is NO org nav level.** Its only item was "Overview", which was just a brand picker, and the switcher is the brand picker now. `OrgLevelSidebar` renders the switcher plus, on a settings path only (`/billing`, `/api-keys`, `/provider-keys`, `/services/*`), an "Organization" group. **Billing KEEPS its URL `/orgs/[orgId]/billing`** — `billing-guard`, `credit-alerts` and onboarding all deep-link to it, so a move is pure link rot. Reached from the switcher, which is where every multi-tenant leader checked puts it (Linear "click your workspace name → Settings > Administration > Billing", Notion, Vercel, GitHub); **never** the avatar menu (that means "me", the tenant name means "this tenant") and **never** a primary sidebar nav item (zero of eight products do that). `/orgs/[orgId]/page.tsx` is untouched and still the last-brand resolver + empty-org state; it just renders switcher-only chrome.

**NO sidebar level carries a back link at its top — the switcher IS the way up, so a second one competes with it.** Every level used to draw one above the switcher (`< Brand` on the offer level, `< Campaigns` on the campaign level, `< Back to dashboard` on org settings), and the switcher already names where you are and moves you anywhere. The affordance is DELETED, not hidden: the `BackLink` component and the `backHref` / `backLabel` props on `SidebarSection` are gone, so a new level cannot re-add one by passing a prop. Consequence accepted: on Billing / API Key / CRM the way back is the switcher's brand row. `apps/admin` keeps its own copies — staff fork, deliberately not synced. Guard: `tests/context-sidebar.test.ts` fails if `backHref`, `backLabel`, `function BackLink` or `Back to dashboard` reappears in the file. (#3499)

Research behind the redesign (fetched, not recalled): NN/g rates breadcrumbs "not useful for sites 1-2 levels deep" — org › brand is exactly two, so the breadcrumb carried no orientation value; Atlassian and Vercel both migrated product nav OUT of the top bar into the sidebar for information density; a topbar product logo is weakly defended for a SINGLE-product app (Carbon mandates it for multi-product suites — Notion/Slack/Linear drop it). Guards: `tests/tenant-switcher.test.ts`.

### Dashboard sidebars are SHARED per nav level — one edit covers every page at that level

`src/components/context-sidebar.tsx` → `ContextSidebar` switches on the URL to ONE sidebar per level. No per-page sidebar. A sidebar change on "the X page" applies to EVERY page at that level — map all levels + surface the blast radius before scoping. (#1223)

**The campaign concept was removed from the dashboard UI (#1768 + follow-up) — one subscription per brand (1 campaign under the hood), single feature flattened into the brand. There is NO feature level and NO campaign level anymore; the launch funnel lives at `brands/[brandId]/launch`, everything else is brand-level.** Nav is now App → Org → Brand → Brand Settings only (plus the brand entity Database rows).

| Level | Component | File | Groups |
|---|---|---|---|
| App / Org | `AppLevelSidebar` / `OrgLevelSidebar` | `context-sidebar.tsx` | — |
| Brand | `BrandLevelSidebar` | `context-sidebar.tsx` | Overview · Customer Personas / Brand Profile (beta) · **Database** (entity rows Outlets/Journalists/Articles/Leads/Emails, kept FLAT) · Brand Settings |
| Brand Settings | `BrandSettingsLevelSidebar` | `context-sidebar.tsx` | Brand Settings · Brand Info (alpha) · Workflows (alpha) |

Section-header style `<h4 className="px-3 pb-1 text-xs font-semibold text-gray-400 uppercase tracking-wide">`.

- **(HISTORICAL — #1768 removed the campaign + feature sidebar levels; `campaign-sidebar.tsx`/`mcp-sidebar.tsx` are gone, the brand-level slug-gating idea below still applies via `BrandLevelSidebar`.)** A surface that "mirrors the campaign sidebar" used to enumerate BOTH Outcome entities AND Settings buttons — `feature.entities` alone undercounts; the old `CampaignSidebar` = `outcomesItems` (entities × registry) PLUS `settingsExtra` slide-over buttons (e.g. `Prompt` → `CampaignPromptPanel`, `Inputs` gated on `featureInputs`). (#1286)
- **A feature-SPECIFIC surface added to a SHARED sidebar must be slug-gated by default, NOT shown on every feature.** The (brand-level) sidebar renders for ALL brands/features, so a raw `topItems` entry appears everywhere. If the page only makes sense for some feature(s) (e.g. a revenue/conversions page that needs sales-economics), gate it on a shared slug registry (`isRevenueFeature(slug)` → `lib/revenue-feature.ts`), the same way the page + any embedded section gate. "We'll reuse the formula for other features later" ≠ "show the page on all features now" — scope to the slug(s) that actually work today; new features opt in by adding their slug. Don't default to all-features just because the sidebar is shared. (DIS-229)
- **(NOTE — #1768 removed the campaign + feature levels. The campaign-detail revenue page (`CampaignRevenueSection`, `campaigns/[id]/page.tsx`), the `TopCampaignsCard`/`CampaignBudgetCard`/`TopWorkflowsCard` cards, and the `campaigns/page.tsx` list are all GONE. The SURVIVING revenue surface is the brand Overview (`RevenueOverviewSection` + `RevenueCostSummary`, gated on `isRevenueFeature`). The per-campaign / per-workflow ROI machinery below is historical.)** Revenue surface render: the brand **Overview** (`RevenueOverviewSection`) gated on `isRevenueFeature`.

  **The Organizations / Leads conversion tabs are DELETED — do NOT rebuild them, and do not look for a per-lead table on the Overview.** `ConversionsTabs` + `OrgConversionsTable`/`LeadConversionsTable`/`EventConversionsTable` + `ConversionDetailPanel` + the shared `components/table-pagination.tsx` (whose only consumer was that table) all sat behind a `conversions` prop that BOTH callers of `RevenueOverviewSection` — the brand Overview and the campaign Overview — passed as `null`, so the branch was unreachable and the whole cluster rendered nowhere. Every guard test that pinned its internals was retired with it (`conversions-cluster-retired.test.ts` now asserts the files are gone and the prop is absent from both call sites). The live per-lead surface is `components/audiences/engaged-leads-page.tsx` at `brands/[brandId]/audiences/leads`. Two consequences: (a) a "the Overview table shows X" report is about the LEADS page, not the Overview — check which surface actually renders before diagnosing; (b) `RevenueOverview.organizations` / `.events` are still parsed off the `/revenue` wire (the schema guard in `conversion-org-logos.test.ts` keeps `orgDomain` honest) but nothing reads them — a follow-up may drop them from `revenue-view.ts` + `revenue-parse.ts`. `getFeatureRevenue(slug, brandId, campaignId)` is still campaign-scopable on the wire (pass `campaignId`), but no UI renders a campaign-scoped view anymore. **Funnel-chart steps are backend-owned** (features-service `seed/features.ts`, re-registered on boot) — to add a step (e.g. the Clicks / website-visit step `recipientsClicked`, already a served stat) edit the seed there, never client-inject. (DIS-229)
  - **(HISTORICAL — #1768) The campaign-detail/top-card `RevenueCostSummary` `bottomCard` overrides (`TopCampaignsCard`, `CampaignBudgetCard`, `TopWorkflowsCard`) were removed with the campaign + feature levels.** The Overview consumer remains; grep `RevenueCostSummary` usages before assuming any `bottomCard` override renders. (#1376, #1388)
  - **(HISTORICAL — #1768) Canonical per-campaign ROI reader was `getFeatureRevenueByCampaign(slug, brandId)`** → `GET /v1/features/{slug}/revenue?brandId=&groupBy=campaignId`, used to rank campaigns by ROI for the (now-removed) `TopCampaignsCard`. No surviving UI ranks/lists campaigns by ROI (one subscription per brand). (#1376)
  - **Per-WORKFLOW ROI is PROJECTED only** (no realized per-workflow revenue stat exists): `getWorkflowProjection({featureSlug, brandId, objective, budgetUsd})` → `GET /v1/features/{slug}/workflow-projection`. ROI = revenue ÷ budget = `100 / projection.cacPct`, **budget-invariant** (pass `budgetUsd:1` only to populate `projection`). `objective` is deprecated/objective-agnostic backend-side (still in the dashboard helper sig; pass `meeting-booked`, echoed + ignored). `getWorkflowProjection` survives (the launch page + beta onboarding consume it for the recommended budget + per-outcome counts); the `TopWorkflowsCard` that ranked workflows by ROI was removed in #1768. (#1388)
- **Deleting a page route ⇒ delete its nav scaffolding in the SAME PR** or the link 404s (NOT caught by `error.tsx` — a missing route ≠ a render throw). `context-sidebar.tsx` wires each level in THREE places: the `SidebarItem` `href`, the `NavigationLevel` union member + its `getNavigationLevel` branch, the `XLevelSidebar` + its `switch` case. Invisible to `tsc` + source-substring tests. (#1226)
- **A backend-declared feature entity needs a brand-level route page** — built from the registry `pathSuffix` (`feature.entities` × `entityRegistry[name].pathSuffix`, features-service `stats-registry.ts`). Declaring an entity emits a sidebar link to a maybe-missing route → 404 (registry is backend-owned → blind to `tsc`/tests). The `quote-pitches/quote-requests/visibility-runs/prompts/competitors` routes were **DELETED in #2196** — they had no nav entry / no link (orphaned after the #1768 campaign+feature flatten), so they shipped unreachable; their components, `api.ts` readers (`listAll{QuotePitches,RankedOpportunities}`, `listVisibilityRuns`, …), libs (`quote-pitch-*`, `visibility-detail`, `use-latest-visibility-run`), persist roots and tests went with them. **The whole brand-level Database entity-page cluster `leads/emails/outlets/journalists/articles` was ALSO orphaned by the #1768 sidebar flatten (Database section removed → 0 nav links, URL-only dead code) and DELETED in #2593** along with its coupled source-substring tests (`email-signature`, `email-recipient-display`, `outlet-*`, `journalist-*`) + trims to the mixed ones (`run-error-display`, `dashboard-mobile-responsive`, `brand-tools`). The live **Leads** surface is `brands/[brandId]/audiences/leads` (`EngagedLeadsPage`), NOT `brands/[brandId]/leads`. The orphaned `api.ts` readers (`listBrandEmails/listBrandOutlets/listJournalistsEnriched/listBrandArticles/listBrandLeads`) were left in place (harmless, `listBrandLeads` still used by `EngagedLeadsPage`). Do NOT reintroduce any of these routes without a nav entry. **still 404: `companies`** (blocked on a brand-scoped companies API) + **`press-kits`**. (#1276, DIS-104, #2593. NOTE — #1768 removed the feature + campaign levels; #2196 + #2593 then deleted every orphaned brand-level entity page.)
- **The Leads page counts the population its TABS can reach (`coveredLeads`), never the raw `listBrandLeads` list — and a lead that reads "not contacted" while the provider says it was sent is a BACKEND bug, never a display one to paper over.** Every tab on `EngagedLeadsPage` buckets on an ENGAGEMENT step (contacted / clicked / replied / the goal's outcome), so a lead that lead-service served with no delivery evidence belongs to no bucket and is unreachable from the page; counting `leads.length` in the title therefore advertised rows the table could never show (reported: `Leads (6 leads)` above a 5-row Outreach tab whose stat card also read 5). `coveredLeads` = union of the visible tabs' buckets, deduped (the tabs are NESTED SUBSETS — positive replies ⊂ outreach — not a partition) in the base `sortedLeads` order; the title count, the CSV export (`buildLeadsCsv`) and the "No leads yet" card all read it, so the page states one population. Guarded by `tests/leads-covered-count.test.ts`. **Do NOT "fix" a future count gap by widening a tab's predicate or by re-reading `leads.length`** — the reported case was lead-service serving a lead whose email was already attached to another lead row (global one-email-one-lead uniqueness rejected the attachment, the serve continued anyway), so `contacted` could never resolve; 103 such rows fleet-wide, fixed in `sales-lead-service#394`. Diagnostic path when a lead is missing from a tab: call the prod reader directly (`GET lead.distribute.you/orgs/leads?brandId=` with a valid-UUID `x-run-id`) and look at the row's `email` — an EMPTY email is the tell, because the delivery overlay is keyed on the lead's registered email (`lead_contact_methods`), NOT on the provider payload the lead row stores. (#3071, #3072)
- **The Leads page counts PEOPLE and says "Contacted"; the brand Overview counts email SEQUENCES sent and says "Outreach" — two grains that must never share a word.** `sequences.total` (features-service `/revenue`, undeduped by lead, so it tracks the spend charted beside it) against the count of lead rows whose `contacted` is true: on one prod brand, one afternoon, **9,915 against 7,895**. Both correct, and the pair read as one broken number for as long as both cards said "Outreach". The rename covers the base tab (`LEAD_TAB_LABEL.outreach`), the stat card above the table, and the CSV columns (`Contacted` / `First contacted at`) — a page whose export disagrees with its screen is the same bug one file over. The card label is a PROP (`outreachLabel`, defaulting to `"Outreach"`) on the SHARED `OutreachStatCards`, threaded through `OutreachStatCardsAuto`: a second copy of that component is how the two surfaces would drift again. The campaign Leads page renders the same component, so it says "Contacted" too — correctly, it counts people as well. ⚠️ The tab still holds rows badged **Queued** (`contacted` means handed to Instantly, not sent — see the status-label rule above); that gap is stated by the row's own badge, deliberately, rather than by weakening the tab's name. **A campaign-scoped count now answers for the campaign IDENTITY, so on a brand whose campaigns are one identity it EQUALS the brand's — a divergence there IS a bug.** A campaign as the customer knows it is not one stored row: campaign-service used to mint a new row on every workflow switch, so one campaign lives in storage as many (measured brand: 48 rows, 47 `stopped`, and 35 distinct `campaign_id`s over 20,039 `leads_campaigns` rows — all one identity). features-service already totalled the identity; lead-service answered for a single row, so the page showed `$5,745 / 2.7×` beside `5,081 leads` while the brand read 7,895. Fixed in `sales-lead-service#412` → v0.50.0 (identity resolved from campaign-service, delivery evidence read brand-wide then narrowed — passing the single id returns the older members with NO evidence, i.e. "never contacted", which drops them from the tabs and is the same bug inverted — deduped one row per person, loud fallback to the single row if resolution fails; `/stats` had the same gap). Verified in prod: both scopes return 8337 rows / 7895 contacted / 45 clicked / 29 replied. Guard: `tests/leads-contacted-label.test.ts`.
- **A CAMPAIGN-scoped leads read is BRAND-SIZED, so it is sized like one — both readers send `view=basic`, and neither is a "small" read.** `listCampaignLeads` went without the slim projection for as long as a campaign scope meant one stored campaign row and a handful of leads. `sales-lead-service#412` made a campaign read answer for the whole campaign IDENTITY (see the rule above), which silently turned it into a brand-sized read: measured on one prod brand, `?campaignId=` returned **53,777 rows / 156 MB / 54 s** against **102 MB / 6.6 s** with `view=basic`, and `EngagedLeadsPage` polls it every 30 s per open tab. Both readers now ask for the slim projection, pinned in `tests/campaign-leads-endpoint.test.ts`. Generalise: when a producer widens what a scope RESOLVES TO, every consumer sized for the old scope is now mis-sized, and nothing goes red — the reader still parses, the page still renders, it just moves ten times the bytes.
  - **The failure mode this produced is worth recognising on sight: an infinite skeleton whose cause is the GATEWAY dying, not the page.** api-service buffered lead-service's already-streamed response into JS objects and re-serialized it (`await response.json()` → `res.json()`), so a 156 MB body OOM-killed the process (`FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit` inside `JsonStringifier`, 5 restarts). An OOM kills every in-flight request for every org, not just the one that triggered it. The page's own reveal-on-settle gates cannot help: the request never returns at all. Diagnose by reading `docker logs distribute-api-service-1` on the box before touching the component. Fixed in api-service v0.98.4 (`pipeExternalService`, raw bytes) — do NOT reintroduce parse-then-re-serialize on that route.
  - **`GET /v1/leads` forwards its query VERBATIM as of api-service v0.98.6** — it used to rebuild the upstream query from a fixed whitelist (`brandId, campaignId, limit, offset, view`), silently dropping anything else, which is the same gateway-strips-a-field bug documented for the features `groupBy`/`funnel` whitelist. So a new lead-service filter reaches it with no gateway PR. (`limit`/`offset` were in that whitelist and `/orgs/leads` implements neither — a gateway's copy of a downstream's vocabulary rots.)
  - **A `skipped` lead is invisible on the CUSTOMER page and rendered on the STAFF one, which is why the two apps ask for different rows.** `skipped` matches no engagement tab, so `coveredLeads` never counted it and the customer page could not display it; lead-service's default therefore excludes it (`sales-lead-service#416`: 53,760 of 65,956 rows for one brand). `apps/admin` DOES render Skipped / Buffered / Claimed tabs, so its leads views send `status=all` (#3411). Keep that asymmetry in mind before "syncing" the two apps' readers — it is deliberate, and dropping admin's `status=all` empties three of its tabs.
- **Entity-count badge MUST mirror its page's data source (`listingFallback`), not a feature-stat** — badge≠page is a DASHBOARD bug even when the empty page traces to an upstream gap. Resolution order: `listingFallback[entity.name]` (the page's count) → `fStats[entity.countKey]`. An entity missing from `listingFallback` falls through to a `countKey` counting a DIFFERENT data layer. Unify BOTH the page render AND both badges on ONE canonical endpoint. (#1231)
- **(REMOVED #2196) The canonical quote-requests / quote-PITCHES readers (`listAllRankedOpportunities`, `listAllQuotePitches`) + their pages and sidebar badges are GONE** — the quote routes were orphaned (no nav) and deleted along with the readers, the `["rankedOpportunities"]`/`["quotePitches"]` query keys, and the `/orgs/{opportunities,quote-pitches}` consumer wrappers in `api.ts`. The public-report quote surface is unaffected (it never imported these authed readers). If quote/HITL is rebuilt, re-add the paged reader (never a hardcoded `limit`) AND a nav entry. (history: #1287, #1367)
- **A feature SLUG re-version (`-opportunities` → `-outreach`, any `X-v1`→`-v2`) rots EVERY hardcoded-slug consumer — gate the FAMILY via a shared helper, never a per-slug `===`.** ~9 slug special-cases across the authed + report trees (campaign HITL queue, `PROMPT_EDITABLE_SLUG`, report draft/reply handlers, report quote-requests page, `REDIRECT_TO_FIRST_ENTITY`, report sidebar, `REPORT_ENABLED_FEATURES`, report header labels). `tsc` + tests stay green on a stale literal. Fix: `grep -rn "<old-slug>" apps/dashboard/src`, replace with ONE helper (`slug.startsWith("<prefix>")`, e.g. `lib/expert-quote-feature.ts` → `isExpertQuoteFeature`) + a unit test over the whole family + a hypothetical `-v2`. Thread the REAL `featureSlug` (not a constant) into per-feature calls (prompt-fork) or the new feature uses the old prompt. (#1292, DIS-197)

### Clerk avatars — `imageUrl` is ALWAYS set; gate real logos on `hasImage`

Clerk's `organization.imageUrl` / `user.imageUrl` is **never null** — when nothing is uploaded Clerk returns a generated gradient-initials avatar hosted on `img.clerk.com`. So `imageUrl ? <img> : <initial>` ALWAYS renders Clerk's default, and an `onError → initial` fallback never fires for it (the default image loads fine). To show a real uploaded logo only, gate on the **`hasImage: boolean`** discriminator (true only for a deliberate upload). For orgs without an upload, prefer a logo.dev logo keyed on the org's domain (onboarding sets org `name` = brand domain → `OrgAvatar.orgDomainFromName` in `breadcrumb-nav.tsx`), else the initial badge — never Clerk's default gradient. (#1321 shipped the default by mistake; #1327 fixed it.)

### Public marketing pages — SEO + AI-scraper rendering

For any public landing/marketing/docs page (anything under `apps/landing/`, `apps/sales-cold-emails-landing/`, `apps/docs/`), the default rendering strategy is **ISR + `unstable_cache` + edge cache**, NEVER Suspense streaming on indexable content.

Why: Next.js streaming SSR injects content into `<template>` blocks resolved by client-side JS. Googlebot (Chromium-headless) executes JS and sees the resolved content, but **AI scrapers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Bingbot AI, curl-like crawlers) parse raw HTML only** — they receive the skeleton fallback, not the data. Putting leaderboards, benchmarks, blog lists, stats behind `<Suspense>` kills AI Search indexing for the very content that earns AI Search traffic.

Concrete rules:

1. **No `<Suspense>` on indexable content.** Hero copy, h1/h2, body text, JSON-LD blocks, leaderboards, benchmark tables, blog cards, stats — all SSR-sync in the byte stream. Suspense reserved for user-specific, non-indexable widgets (logged-in dashboards, real-time counters, chat).
2. **Never combine `force-dynamic` with `revalidate`.** `force-dynamic` wins and ISR is silently disabled. Prefer ISR (`export const revalidate = N`) alone; let `unstable_cache` + graceful build-time empty-result handle the missing-config case.
3. **Calling `headers()` or `cookies()` in a page forces dynamic rendering** even with `revalidate` set. Drop them if the page can be ISR-prerendered (resolve hostname-dependent URLs from `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` env vars instead).
4. **Wrap every fetch / DB call used by a public page in `unstable_cache`** with explicit tags. Edge cache (`s-maxage / stale-while-revalidate`) provides instant HTML; `unstable_cache` provides instant data on the rebuild after invalidation. Both stack.
5. **Invalidate via `revalidateTag(tag, profile)`** in the publish/update flow (webhooks, admin forms). Next 16.2+ requires the 2nd arg `profile: 'default' | 'seconds' | 'minutes' | 'hours' | 'days' | 'weeks' | 'max' | CacheLifeConfig`. Pass `"default"` for ordinary invalidations.
6. **`unstable_cache` at module top level breaks vitest** because the wrapping runs at import time before `vi.doMock` can intercept. Mock `next/cache` globally via `apps/landing/tests/setup.ts` (`unstable_cache` → pass-through identity, `revalidatePath` / `revalidateTag` → `vi.fn()`); declare `setupFiles: ["tests/setup.ts"]` in `vitest.config.ts`.
7. **Validate scraper output after any caching change** with `apps/landing/scripts/seo-snapshot.sh [BASE_URL]` (counts `<h1>`, `<h2>`, `<article>`, JSON-LD blocks under a `GPTBot` user-agent) and `apps/landing/scripts/measure-ttfb.sh [BASE_URL]` (warm/cold TTFB per route). Both pre- and post-change snapshots in the PR description.

Incident 2026-05-24 (distribute.you#1153): pre-refactor, `/blog` TTFB was 1.5–2.3s because `force-dynamic` overrode `revalidate = 60` and every visit hit Neon directly. `/benchmarks` was dynamic because `headers()` was called for hostname resolution. `/investors` took 6–8s end-to-end because metrics were `cache: "no-store"`. Three loading.tsx files existed but were never reachable (page components weren't async-suspended). Single PR flipped 8+ routes from `ƒ Dynamic` to `○ Static` ISR with `unstable_cache` + tag invalidation; no Suspense or skeleton was added on any indexable content.

### Adding a brand optimization goal touches ~8 dashboard surfaces — wire ALL or it ships half-done

`BrandOptimizationGoal` (the "Maximising X" goal a brand optimizes for) has repeatedly shipped INCOMPLETE — a new goal added to some surfaces but not others, leaving an internally-incoherent UI (goal selectable in Settings but absent from the Overview picker; strategy header defaulting a real goal to "Sales meetings"; unit cost falling back to the meeting cost). #2402 (website_visits/positive_replies), #2424 (form_submissions), #2475 (purchase) each had to backfill gaps a prior goal PR left. When adding/renaming a goal, wire EVERY surface below in ONE PR (grep the goal literal across `apps/dashboard/src` to find stragglers):

1. **`lib/api.ts`** — `BrandOptimizationGoal` union · `normalizeBrandOptimizationGoal` (WIRE value → local; e.g. brand-service snake `combined_sales` ↔ local `sales`, `website_purchase` 1:1, legacy `sales`/`purchase` → `website_purchase`) · `serializeBrandOptimizationGoal` (local → wire; `sales` → `combined_sales`) · `isVisitDrivenGoal` · `SalesObjective` union + the `WorkflowProjectionResponseSchema.objective` z.union · `salesObjectiveForOptimizationGoal` · **AND `RuntimeGoal` — the goal a CAMPAIGN carries. campaign-service forwards the funnel's goal VERBATIM from brand-service, so it is the SAME canonical vocabulary, and `optimizationGoalForRuntimeGoal` therefore DELEGATES to `normalizeBrandOptimizationGoal` rather than re-deciding: a second mapping is a second place to go stale, which is exactly what happened (it was a three-token union ending in a bare `return "sales_meetings"`, so `formSubmission` printed "Sales Meeting from Conversation" on a brand that had declared only Form Magnet — #3253). `runtimeGoalForOptimizationGoal` is DELETED with `NewCampaignModal` (#3180); do not re-add a brand→campaign goal mapping.** The audience-stats row type+Zod also carry per-goal evidence/metrics (`evidence.sales` + `metrics.cpsaleCents` for the sales/website_purchase goals — note lowercase `cpsaleCents`; revenue spend block uses `salesCount`/`cpSaleCents`).
2. **`lib/strategy-model.ts`** — its LOCAL copy of `isVisitDrivenGoal` (vitest has no `@` alias, so it can't import api's) · `goalForOptimizationGoal` (→ `FeatureAudienceStatsGoal`) · `isVisitDrivenStatsGoal` (the stats-goal sort-metric family) · `outcomeNoun` · `objectiveForOptimizationGoal`.
   - **A goal that BORROWS a neighbouring family instead of sending its own native goal is a WRONG NUMBER, not a rendering approximation — features-service ranks workflows BY the goal you send, so borrowing returns a different outcome's winner and then prints THAT workflow's cost for YOUR outcome.** `goalForOptimizationGoal` mapped `positive_replies → meetingBooked` and `website_visits → signup` (a shim from before features shipped the single-step goals). Prod, one brand, one moment: Strategy sent `objective=positive_replies` → Dawn, cheapest per reply, **$61.73**; Audiences + the Overview top-audiences card sent `goal=meetingBooked` → Osprey, cheapest per CLICK, whose cost per reply is **$183.85**. Both labelled "fleet benchmark", 3× apart. `FeatureAudienceStatsGoal` now carries `positiveReply` + `websiteVisit` (features' canonical enum, already served) and every goal maps to itself. When a producer ships a native goal, MIGRATE the borrow — a stale shim is silent and only shows up as two prices on one screen.
   - **All three audience-stats call sites derive the goal from `goalForOptimizationGoal(optimizationGoal)`, never a local ternary** (brand Overview `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx`, `campaigns/campaign-overview-page.tsx`, `audiences/customer-audiences-page.tsx`). Two of them had drifted to a 2-branch copy (`isVisitDrivenGoal(g) ? "signup" : "meetingBooked"`), so `sales` / `form_submissions` / `website_purchase` brands were ALSO asking for the wrong goal there. Sort metric comes from `isVisitDrivenStatsGoal`, not a `=== "signup"` literal.
   - **A goal-gated COLUMN must test the goal FAMILY.** `showMeetingCols === "meetingBooked"` alone hides the Positive-replies + CPPR columns on a `positive_replies` brand — the one whose outcome IS the reply. Guards: `tests/audience-stats-goal.test.ts`. (cppr-mismatch-audiences)
3. **`lib/workflow-projection-choice.ts`** — `serverOutcomeUnitCost` + `workflowOutcomeUnitCostFromRates` need a branch, or the goal silently falls to the meeting-cost `else`. A goal with no dedicated grain field reads `resolved.costPerOutcomeUsd` (captured onto `WorkflowProjectionItem.costPerOutcomeUsd` in `ladderRowToWorkflowItem`) — the native per-goal cost features-service ranks on.
4. **`components/brand/brand-status-control.tsx`** — the Overview modal: `GOAL_LABEL` Record + the `GOAL_OPTIONS` picker array (both keyed on `BrandOptimizationGoal`; `beta: true` gates via `useIsBetaUser`).
5. **`components/settings/brand-sales-economics-card.tsx`** — `OPTIMIZATION_GOALS` array + `PCT_FIELDS[].goals` (which conversion-rate fields show) + `REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_GOAL` Record. A goal with no dedicated backend rate REUSES an existing pair (purchase reuses `visitToSignupPct × signupToPaidClientPct` — brand-service has no purchase-specific rate).
6. **`components/strategy/strategy-page.tsx`** — the "The plan" header (Objective label + conversion-rate rows) must cover the goal, else it defaults to the sales-meetings branch.
7. **The three `Record<BrandOptimizationGoal, …>`** (GOAL_LABEL, REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_GOAL, strategy-page OBJECTIVE_LABEL) are exhaustive → `tsc` CATCHES a missing key there, but the ternary/switch surfaces (picker array, unit-cost branches, strategy conversion rows) compile fine while silently mis-defaulting. Those are the ones that ship broken.
8. **Backend is usually already ahead** — verify the goal's enum on brand-service `PUT /orgs/brands/{id}/sales-economics` (`optimizationGoal`) + features-service `GET /features/{slug}/workflow-projection` (`goal`) via api-registry BEFORE assuming a backend gap; these goals were dashboard-only wiring. (#2402, #2424, #2475)
9. **`components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx`** — the sales-goal step is ALSO a goal-listing surface. `Outcome === BrandOptimizationGoal`; `OUTCOMES` (label + unit + `beta`) is the picker, and the restore validators + `ONBOARDING_STATE_VERSION` must accept any new goal key. There is NO per-goal rate list here any more — the old `RATE_KEYS_FOR_OUTCOME` held the entry legs of DIFFERENT funnels, so it asked for numbers belonging to no single path; every rate the flow collects comes from the funnel catalogue's own chain. Beta goals are `useIsBetaUser`-gated in the funnel with a `<MaturityBadge level="beta"/>` (currently only signups/sales_meetings ungated). Google-Ads conversion-category LABELS (Sign-ups / Book appointments / Page views / Contacts / Submit lead forms / Purchases) live here, distinct from the settings card's `# Signups`-style labels. (#2508)
10. **`components/audiences/customer-audiences-page.tsx` + `components/revenue/top-audiences-card.tsx`** — the two per-audience RANKING surfaces. Both lead with the goal's OUTCOME cost, and both read that choice from ONE helper: `strategy-model.ts` `audienceRankMetric(goal, trackerSetUp)` → `cppr | cps | cpfs | cpsale | cpc`, with `AUDIENCE_RANK_METRIC_{LABEL,INFO,OUTCOME_NOUN}` carrying the words. The table also renders a count column beside each cost (`showMeetingCols` / `showSignupCols` / `showFormSubmissionCols` / `showSaleCols`; `isPositiveReplies` hides the visit cols). **The card MUST NOT read the response's `sortMetric`** — features-service classes `websitePurchase` + `sales` as reply-driven and returns `cppr` for them, so the card printed "CPPR $319 / 0 replies" on a goal whose funnel has no reply step while the Audiences page hid the reply columns for that same brand. Same reason neither Overview sends `limit` (the server would pre-pick its top 3 on ITS column); the card sorts + slices on the shown metric. The tracker gate is load-bearing: no tracker → no attributed signups/form-submissions/sales → fall back to the measured visit cost, never an all-`-` column. Every number is features-service-owned via `fetchFeatureAudienceStats`: `evidence.{websiteClicks,positiveReplies,formSubmissions,signups,sales}` + `metrics.{cpcCents,cpprCents,cpfsCents,cpsCents,cpsaleCents}`, the outcome fields present ONLY for their own goal and `.optional()` on the wire (render `-` until the producer ships). A new outcome goal → a branch in `audienceRankMetric` + its 3 word entries + the table's `showXCols` pair + optional `evidence.X`/`metrics.cpXCents` on the api.ts type+Zod, and ask features-service for the per-audience attribution. Guards: `tests/audience-rank-metric.test.ts`. (#2632; features-service #554; top-audiences-goal-metric)

**The goal DISPLAY surfaces (Leads tabs, top stat cards, Outreach-activity graph bars, the leads table's per-tab column) read ONE source — `lib/goal-steps.ts` `goalSteps(goal)` — NOT a per-surface `isVisitDrivenGoal(...)` binary.** `goalSteps` returns the goal's ordered funnel steps (base→outcome), each declaring its per-surface mapping: `tab`/`signal` (Leads tab, outcome-first via `goalLeadTabs`), `chartKey` (activity bar via `goalChartMetricKeys`), `outcome` (stat-card count/cost fields via `goalOutcomeStep`). The `switch` is **exhaustive over `BrandOptimizationGoal`** → `tsc` catches a goal added to the config surfaces (items 1–7) but not wired here, so a new goal can't half-ship on the display side. Extend `goalSteps` (add the step + its surface bindings), never re-introduce a `goal === "x" ? … : …` branch inside `outreach-stat-cards`/`pipeline-activity-chart`/`engaged-leads-page`. A step has a `tab`/`chartKey` ONLY where the data exists per-lead/per-day: signup + form-submission outcomes are brand-level aggregate-only today (stat card yes; Leads tab / per-lead surface no) until features-service attributes them per-lead. (#2482)

**A CAMPAIGN-scoped surface keys those steps on its FUNNEL, never on the goal — `stepsFor(goal, funnelKey)`, and the goal is only the fallback.** The goal is the lossy vocabulary: `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` both answer to `sales_meetings`, so `goalSteps("sales_meetings")` has to cover BOTH paths (`[Outreach, Website Visits, Positive replies, Sales Meetings]`) and a goal-keyed campaign surface therefore hands the website-visit leg to a campaign whose chain starts at a positive reply. That shipped: a *Sales Meeting from Conversation · Sales Cold Email Outreach* campaign showed **Outreach 9,802 · Website Visits 44 · Cost per website visit $51 · Sales Meetings** — the wrong funnel's steps under the campaign's own name, and the reply it actually buys nowhere on the page. `funnelSteps(funnelKey)` in the same module is the fix, mapping each funnel onto the SAME step constants (identity, so a label/colour/rate cannot fork): `reply_meeting` → Outreach · **Positive replies · Sales Meetings**; `visit_meeting` → Outreach · Website Visits · Sales Meetings; `visit_signup` → Outreach · Website Visits · Signups; `visit_form` → Outreach · Website Visits · Form submissions. Five rules:
- **`stepsFor` / `leadTabsFor` / `chartMetricKeysFor` / `outcomeStepFor` / `outcomeTabFor` are what every funnel-aware surface reads.** An absent-or-null `funnelKey` returns the goal's answer byte-identically, so the brand Overview (a brand runs several funnels at once, so it states none) and a pre-funnel campaign (`funnelKey: null`) are untouched. That is what makes the prop safe to add everywhere.
- **Reads the WIRE spelling** (`SalesFunnelKeyWire`, via `normalizeSalesFunnelKey`), so the canonical rename lands for free.
- **Every funnel chain ends on its own terminal outcome**, so a campaign always carries an outcome pair — unlike the 1-step goals, which have none.
- **The stat cards decide each pair from the STEP LIST, not a `goal === "x"` test**: `showVisitPair = hasStep("website_visits")`, `showReplyPair = hasStep("positive_replies") && !isPositiveReplies`, and `isPositiveReplies = hasStep("positive_replies") && outcomeStep === null` (a reply that is TERMINAL — the `positive_replies` goal — versus a reply that is a MID-chain signal above its own Sales Meetings pair). The old `showReplyPair = goal === "sales"` is exactly what starved the reply→meeting funnel of its first step.
- **The chart, the Leads tabs and the Outcome line follow the same funnel or they contradict the row above them** — a Website-Visits bar under a card row that no longer mentions visits is the self-contradictory-surface bug one component over. The Leads page reads the funnel off `["campaign", campaignId]` (the key the campaign Overview and the top bar already poll → one deduped request), and its one-shot auto-tab latch WAITS for that read (`if (campaignId && campaignData === undefined) return;`) or it parks the user on a tab the chain does not offer.
Guards: `tests/campaign-funnel-steps.test.ts`. (campaign-funnel-vocabulary)

**The activity chart's first bar counts SEQUENCES LAUNCHED, never emails sent — it reads `New leads contacted` for that reason, and a run of zero bars beside real sending is the honest picture, not an outage.** `sequences` (features-service#416) counts a lead handed to the sending provider on the day it was handed over; the emails that leave the building that day are the queue plus the follow-up steps of leads launched days earlier, and Instantly holds a lead until its next weekday window. So the two series routinely disagree by everything: prod brand `75d7e3e8` sent 293 emails on 2026-08-17 and 6 on 08-18 while launching zero new leads on both days, and under the old `Outreach` label that read as "no email sent for two days" — the report that opened the investigation. The label is local to `pipeline-activity-chart.tsx` (its own `MetricDef` map, NOT `goal-steps.ts`'s `OUTREACH_STEP.label`, which the stat cards and Leads tabs read), so changing it moves the chart alone. Guard: `tests/pipeline-activity-chart.test.ts`.

⚠️ **Zero bars there DO also mean nothing new was launched, so check whether that is intended before closing the question** — the same two days were a real halt: chat-service `/complete` 429'd on Gemini free-tier quota (`limit: 0`, every model), so brand-service field-extraction failed, the workflow failed, no email was drafted, and no lead was pushed. The chain to walk, in order, is `runs` by `service_name`/`status` for the org (a failure spike names the failing service), then that service's `docker logs`. `campaign_service.campaigns.parent_run_id` and `instantly_campaigns.created_at` are the two tables that say whether a lead was launched at all. (#3425)

**The goal→outcome-NOUN (the plural word shown in "X <noun> / mo" budget pickers + "cost per <noun>" copy) is a SEPARATE label family from `goalSteps` and it DRIFTS — there are FOUR user-facing noun maps that MUST stay coherent, each keyed on `BrandOptimizationGoal`: (1) `onboarding.tsx`'s copy of `OUTCOME_UNIT` (inherited from the retired brand status bar, which owned it until the bar was deleted); (2) `strategy-model.ts` `outcomeNoun`/`outcomeNounPlural` (strategy card, best-model card, outcome-digest email, onboarding launch); (3) `onboarding.tsx` `OUTCOMES[].unit`/`label` (onboarding budget step + Google-Ads category labels); (4) `new-campaign-modal.tsx` `RUNTIME_GOAL_LABEL` (RuntimeGoal, not BrandOptimizationGoal).** Only `OUTCOME_UNIT` and `outcomeNoun*` are `Record`/exhaustive-`switch` (tsc-checked); a `ternary`/partial map silently mis-defaults a new goal (the Overview modal shipped a 4-branch ternary sending `website_purchase`/`form_submissions`/`sales` to **"meetings"** — count was grain-correct via `workflowOutcomeUnitCost`, only the noun wrong). **Canonical wording: `website_purchase` = "website purchases" everywhere (the `purchase`→`website purchase` rename, admin #2803) — NEVER "purchases" (stale) nor "sales" (that's the DISTINCT `sales` goal; `outcomeNounPlural` had `website_purchase`→"sales" colliding with it).** When adding/renaming a goal, grep the goal literal across ALL FOUR maps + prefer a `Record<BrandOptimizationGoal,…>` over a ternary so tsc forces the branch. (#2921)

**Corollary — tsc guards the KEYS of those maps, never the VALUES, so a wrong noun rots silently and is only ever caught by reading the rendered screen.** `OUTCOMES[]` is an array of object literals, so a nonsense `unit` typechecks forever: `positive_replies` shipped `unit: "contacts"` and the onboarding budget modal read **"50 contacts / mo"** for a goal that buys 50 interested replies — naming the people we email rather than what the budget buys, understating the tier by an order of magnitude, and contradicting the row's own label (`"Positive replies for sales meetings"`) two lines above. Fixed to `"positive replies"` in #3078, byte-equal with `OUTCOME_UNIT`. Same pass dropped `outcomeNounPlural(positive_replies)` from `"replies"` to `"positive replies"`: **a plain "reply" includes the negative ones, which are not the outcome the brand optimizes for** — the same distinction the OPENS-are-deprecated rule draws for the funnel. Test for a noun value: *does it name the thing the budget buys, or the people/action upstream of it?* (#3078)

**Fifth noun map, and the one that also owns a GATE: the Overview learning-window banner (`components/brand/first-outcome-reassurance-banner.tsx`). A reassurance banner names an outcome, so it must also DISAPPEAR on that outcome — the sentence and the gate are one decision, never two.** It shipped hardcoded to "the first website clicks" for every goal, which described a funnel step a `positive_replies` brand does not buy, and then hid on the first click while that brand was still waiting for its reply. Four things are load-bearing:
- **The noun comes from `outcomeNounPlural(goal)`**, so the banner joins the four maps above as a fifth goal-aware surface. It is the only one carrying a gate, which is why the drift is worse here than a wrong label: the wrong noun and the wrong disappearance ship together.
- **The gate reads the SAME goal's count** via `goalOutcomeCount(goal, spend, websiteClicks)` (`lib/first-outcome-reassurance.ts`) — `positiveRepliesCount`, the goal-steps `countField`, or clicks for `website_visits`. A count ABSENT from the wire falls back to clicks (exactly the pre-#3143 behaviour) rather than pinning the banner open forever. `0` is a real value.
- **The learning window is stated as a MULTIPLE of the brand's own cost per outcome, and NO dollar total is ever rendered** — `Plan on about {LEARNING_WINDOW_OUTCOMES}x the expected cost per {outcomeNoun(goal)} before you judge the results.` A multiple reads right for a $2 visit and a $700 meeting alike, as a flat "spend $500 first" never would, AND it survives the case the dollar version could not: a computed total (`Plan on about $2,579 of spend…`) reads as a bill on a screen that has produced zero outcomes, and it forced the whole line to VANISH whenever the unit cost failed to resolve. The multiple needs no unit cost, so the line is unconditional. `recommendedLearningSpendUsd` still exists but is now GATE-ONLY (below) — do not re-render it, and do not fall back to total spent or the daily budget (the `EXPECTED_COST_NOTE` / stat-card rule, one layer down). (#3143 shipped the dollar figure; the multiple replaced it.)
- **The banner retires itself** once spend passes that recommendation. It claims results typically take 2 to 4 weeks; left up at month three it is a promise the product already broke — a self-contradictory surface, not a harmless empty state.
The brand Overview (`brands/[brandId]/page.tsx`) and the staff campaign Overview (`components/campaigns/campaign-overview-page.tsx`) shipped BYTE-PARALLEL copies of this banner, which is how a goal-aware sentence ends up correct on one page and stale on the other; they now render one component with a `subject` prop ("Your campaign" / "This campaign"). The unit cost lives in its own `outcomeUnitCostUsd` memo on each page because the expected-monthly-count and the banner both read it — a guard in `pipeline-activity-chart.test.ts` pins `monthlyBudgetUsd / outcomeUnitCostUsd`. Guards: `tests/first-outcome-reassurance.test.ts` (real unit tests — the lib is alias-free apart from type-only imports, keep it that way) + `tests/first-outcome-reassurance-banner.test.ts`. (#3143)

**A daily budget is funded PER SALES FUNNEL, and the brand total is the SUM — nothing in the dashboard sets a brand-level budget any more.** billing stores a ceiling per `(org, brand, funnel)` and answers the EXISTING brand daily-budget read with their sum, which is why the launch gate (`billing-guard`), the credit runway, the credit alerts and the Overview tile never changed a line. Three consequences a future edit must not undo: there is no brand-level budget surface left at all (the status pill that STATED the sum is deleted along with its modal, its tier list and the whole `workflowProjection` query that only ever priced them — see the run-status-bar rule above); brand Settings has no daily-budget section, the field lives on the funnel it pays for; and no surface states the brand-wide SUM under a narrower thing's name — the campaign Overview's header states the CAMPAIGN's own ceiling (billing's `(offer, funnel, channel)` row) read-only, and Campaign Settings is the only writer of it (#3456, #3462). Zero is an ordinary value everywhere — it is how a funnel is put down without forgetting how it sells — and the per-funnel floors (`FUNNEL_MIN_DAILY_BUDGET_USD`, $24 meetings / $1 purchase + form) live once in the catalogue while billing's 400 decides. The dashboard NEVER sums the ceilings itself: a consumer that recomposes the total is how two surfaces start disagreeing about one number. (per-funnel-budgets)

### Dynasty-First Display Rule (Workflows Only)

Always display `dynastyName` for workflows, never the versioned name — exception: settings/debug panels (show the version + versioned name alongside). Applies to titles, rows, cards, breadcrumbs. Features have no dynasty — they use `slug` + `name` directly.
